### Q115 — The wasm32 lane's warnings are invisible

- Milestone: M2
- Size: XS
- Deps: P14 (the wasm32 lane), Q1 (CI)
- Do: `cargo test -p antseal-core --lib --target wasm32-unknown-unknown`
  currently emits `unused import: error::all_code_exemplars`
  (`crates/antseal-core/src/anchor/ots/mod.rs:70`) and the lane stays green.
  The import is used by `error_universe` and `tamper_coverage`, neither of
  which compiles on the wasm32 dev edge (`test-vectors` only), so on that
  target it really is unused. The finding is not the import — it is that
  **`wasm32-core-tests` runs `cargo test`, not clippy**, so no warning on
  that target can fail anything, and a target-specific `unused`/`dead_code`
  regression can accumulate silently. Decide between: adding `-D warnings`
  to the lane (and fixing whatever it surfaces), or gating the import so the
  warning is genuinely absent rather than merely tolerated. Prefer the
  first: the second fixes one instance of a class.
- Accept:
  - The wasm32 lane is warning-clean, and a planted warning turns it red
    (self-test, the pattern every other lane in this tree uses).
  - Whichever route is taken, the reason is recorded — a `#[cfg]` that
    silences a warning without explaining which lane could not see it is
    the same defect one layer down.
- Notes: Pre-existing; observed 2026-08-06 by A21's lane while running the
  wasm32 suite for the tamper rows, and unrelated to that work. Recorded by
  D96 §Discovered work.

### Q116 — The `strict v1 schema` needles carry a version number
- Milestone: M2
- Size: XS
- Deps: D97 R9
- Discovered by: **D97 R9** (2026-08-06).
- Problem: two committed assertions match error strings containing the literal `v1` — the anchor-record needle in `pipeline/anchors/tests.rs` and its twin at `journal.rs:401`. Every future `SEAL_JOURNAL_VERSION` bump silently invalidates them, and the cheapest repair on the day of the bump is to edit the needle, which is how an assertion quietly stops asserting anything. The counter-example is in the same file: `a_future_version_refuses_distinctly` needs no edit at a bump, because it is written **against the constant** — which is the shape the other two should have had.
- Do: drop the version token from both messages, or assert them against the constant.
- Accept: a bump requires no edit to either needle.
- Notes: **partly discharged 2026-08-07** by the D97 foundation lane, which landed the journal-version bump: the anchor needle now reads `(strict schema)`. The `journal.rs:401` copy remains, and this row stays open for it — the half that was discharged is the half whose bump was already in flight, which is exactly the half a bump-driven repair would have caught anyway.

### Q117 — `commands.rs`'s `list` rationale names a failure mode the lock cannot produce
- Milestone: M2
- Size: XS
- Deps: none
- Discovered by: **D99 §1.2** (2026-08-06).
- Problem: `commands.rs:297-305` justifies `list`'s lock-free read by saying that taking the lock would make `list` *"the one command that hangs"*. `VaultLock::acquire` is a **try-lock**: under contention `list` would **fail** with `VaultLockHeld`, exit 15 — not hang. The conclusion is unchanged and D99 R3 reaffirms it (and after U24 it is the **hook**, not `list`, that takes the lock — late, and declining silently when contended), but the recorded rationale describes a mechanism the code does not have, and a future reader reasoning from it will reason wrongly about every other command's lock cost too.
- Do: correct the sentence to name the real cost.
- Accept: the doc names `VaultLockHeld`/exit 15; the decision to stay lock-free is unchanged.
- Notes: D99 §9 files this as **Q118**; reconciled to Q117 at bookkeeping, where three lanes' overlapping id blocks were resolved.

### Q118 — Routes to the binary that an enumeration rule cannot see
- Milestone: M2
- Size: S
- Deps: Q16, U24; D99 R4, D99 §8
- Discovered by: **the D99 R4 lane** (2026-08-07), while landing the rule the finding is about.
- Problem: D99 R4.3 specifies a static rule — the only construction naming `CARGO_BIN_EXE_antseal` in the workspace lives in the spawn helper, and that helper sets `ANTSEAL_NO_REAL_ANCHOR_NETWORK`. Landing it found two routes to the shipped binary the rule **as specified** could not see.
  - `e2e_restore.rs`'s `run_clean_machine` re-executes the test binary with `.env_clear()`, which strips the arming **even in CI**, and it never names `CARGO_BIN_EXE_antseal` at all — so the rule as written would have missed it entirely. It was fixed, and `env_clear()` became R4's **fourth arm**.
  - `option_env!("CARGO_BIN_EXE_antseal")` is a second spelling of the same reach. The landed regex covers it, but **no test names it**, so that coverage is incidental rather than asserted, and the next edit to the regex can remove it without anything going red.
  This is D99 §8's own revisit trigger arriving before the ink dried: the rule refuses the shapes it enumerates, and the venue that reaches the binary by a route nobody enumerated is precisely the one that matters. The premise is not hypothetical — the same lane measured that a bare `cargo test -p antseal-cli` **reached freetsa.org**, completing DNS, TCP and TLS and taking a real 403 from its nginx in 0.83 s.
- Do: enumerate the property — *"this reaches the shipped binary"* — rather than the macro spelling. Add a planted-fault case for the `option_env!` form so its coverage is asserted, and decide whether an environment-clearing re-exec can be refused **structurally** rather than by a named arm; a fifth arm per newly discovered route is the shape this task exists to escape.
- Accept:
  - A planted `option_env!` construction reddens the rule by name, and a planted `env_clear()` without re-arming reddens; both directions executed, as R4's other arms already are.
  - What the rule still cannot see is recorded beside it — D99 §8 already concedes that a new crate driving the hook by an unforeseen route is out of reach of a static check, and the runtime gate remains the only thing that fails a call.

### Q119 — A `secret_hygiene.rs` flake that is not attributable
- Milestone: M2
- Size: XS
- Deps: U21
- Discovered by: **the D99 R4 lane** (2026-08-07).
- Problem: `secret_hygiene.rs` failed once on its first run of the wave — 9 passed, 1 failed — and **could not be attributed**. A/B measured on this host: after the change, five further passes including runs under deliberate 2-core load; before the change, at `HEAD`, three of three under the same load. The suite polls `/proc` and is timing-sensitive, which is the mechanism a flake of this shape would have, but **the specific failing test was not identified**, so this row records an observation and not a diagnosis.
- Do: make the `/proc` polling insensitive to scheduling, or make its failure name what it timed out waiting for, so that a second occurrence is attributable in one run instead of costing another A/B. A hygiene suite that flakes is worse than most flakes: its red means *"a secret escaped"*, and a reader who has seen it flake will discount the one time it is right.
- Accept: the polling has a stated bound and a failure message naming the process and the field it was waiting on; a deliberately slowed child produces that message rather than a bare assertion failure.
- Notes: not reproduced since. Recorded now so that a second occurrence is a second data point rather than another first one.

### Q120 — D98 rider 3c is honoured by `status` and unenforced across surfaces
- Milestone: M2
- Size: S
- Deps: U23, U25; D98 rider 3c
- Discovered by: **the U23 lane** (2026-08-07).
- Problem: D98 rider 3c names three predicates that share the word UNANCHORED and rules that they must not be collapsed — `WorkRow.unanchored` is the `--no-anchor` shaping flag, `NagState::Unanchored` means *"no anchors at all"*, and MVP-SPEC.md line 137's UNANCHORED means *"zero headline-eligible anchors"*. `status` computes the spec's version, as ruled. `list` badges off `WorkRow.unanchored`. The nag reads `NagState`. **U23's fixture work 3 makes the disagreement concrete**: one work, all three predicates answering differently, each correctly by its own definition. The ruling is honoured at the one surface that was being built and is enforced at none — nothing mechanically stops a fourth reader picking whichever predicate is nearest to hand, which is how the three become interchangeable in a reader's memory and then in the code.
- Do: give the spec's predicate a name and a single home, so a surface that wants *"zero headline-eligible anchors"* cannot spell it as either of the other two by accident, and so the two that are **not** the spec's say what they are at their own definition.
- Accept: each of the three predicates is reachable by a name that states which question it answers; a test pins work 3's three different answers, so a future collapse of any two of them reddens.
- Notes: revisit trigger is **R18/R22** — M3's authoritative wording set is where this either becomes one word with three qualifiers or three words, and D98 already flags rider 1b's marker as owing the same surfaces.
- **Closed 2026-08-09, and this row's own fixture turned out unable to discharge its own Accept.** `status_command.rs::the_three_unanchored_predicates_answer_three_questions_and_may_not_collapse` reads `list`'s row and `status`'s report off **one vault**. On U23's work 3 the three predicates measure (a) `false`, (b) `AttestedOnly`, (c) `true` — three different answers, which is what made the row look sufficient. It is not: **(a) and (b) both answer *"not unanchored"* on work 3**, so rewriting `WorkRow.unanchored` to `nag == Unanchored` would have collapsed exactly that pair and stayed green. Separating them needed a **sixth work** — a `--force-degraded` seal that obtained no anchors (`evaluate_seal_gate` → `ProceedDegraded` from a `None` submission), journaled by the test alone so `status-report.txt` is untouched. The generalisable finding: *"the three answers differ on some work"* is a weaker property than *"no two of the three are the same function"*, and only the second is what rider 3c forbids.
- **The prohibition is asserted as functional dependence over all six rows, not as a table of constants.** A table of expected values is re-blessable — a lane that collapses two predicates can update the table and stay green — and it is blind to a **negated** collapse: the fourth planted fault, `is_unanchored() { !self.anchors.is_empty() }`, disagrees with (b) on **every** row and would pass any *"they differ somewhere"* check. Asserting that no column is a function of another catches it, and did. Four planted faults, all red. Also landed: `list_command.rs`'s *"three predicates, three answers"* comment asserted two of the three and now computes the third; **two `NagState` variant docs defined themselves as the wrong predicate** — `Unanchored` read *"No anchors at all: the `--no-anchor` seal"*, which is predicate (b) written as predicate (a), rider 3c's own collapse at doc level, and `AttestedOnly`'s *"every OTS anchor is already attested"* is false for work 3, whose OTS is neither attested nor pending. The recon's `engine.rs:616` drift claim was **rejected as false** (*"four different meanings"* is correct — four of five states do not nag; the stale `three` was `listing.rs`'s quotation of it). Renames are **deferred, not skipped**: `NagState::name()` emits the wire string `"unanchored"` and `json-envelopes.txt` carries three senses of the word, so re-recording is blocked by **Q113**, and `AttestedOnly`'s over-claiming name rides the R18/R22 revisit. A **fourth** sense of the word, in frozen normative text, is **Q129**.

### Q121 — A test whose vehicle is "a still-unimplemented command" breaks every time a handler lands
- Milestone: M2
- Size: XS
- Deps: U19, U23, U24; before U27
- Discovered by: **the U24 lane** (2026-08-07), on this test's second migration.
- Problem: `config_file.rs::binary_resolves_network_through_the_config` needs a command that reaches config resolution and then stops, so it picks one that is **still a stub** — and a stub is by definition the thing the next wave removes. It has now migrated twice: `list` → `status` at U19, `status` → `show` at U24. It will break again at U27, and the repair each time is to hunt for the next stub, which reproduces the defect rather than closing it.
- Do: derive the vehicle instead of naming it — `machine::ALL_COMMAND_NAMES` minus the implemented set yields the current stub without a lane having to notice — or remove the vehicle question entirely by asserting the envelope's `network` field rather than pinning an exit code, since network resolution is what the test is about and the exit code is only how it currently observes it.
- Accept: landing a handler for any command requires no edit to this test; the property it asserts — that config resolves the network before the command's own failure — is still red when that resolution is broken.
- Notes: U19's execution note 8 recorded the same shape for four sibling suites (`cli_surface`, `exit_codes`, `config_file`, and `machine_mode`'s abort table each used `list` as their *"a stub command"* exemplar) and re-pointed each at a still-stubbed command — which re-armed the defect rather than disarming it. This is the instance that has been re-pointed twice since and is therefore the one worth giving a derived vehicle first.

### Q122 — Rule whether the body of a resolved decision gets dated corrections
- Milestone: M2
- Size: S
- Deps: none — the row states no `after:` clause.
- Spec: D84's dated `Correction` sections (the mechanism the row points at); D95 §4 (the forcing case).
- Discovered by: **the R73/R74 lane** (2026-08-07).
- Problem: a lane that measures a resolved decision and finds it wrong has nowhere to put the correction. The forcing case is concrete: D95 §4's *"`is_verified` has 3 repo-wide hits, all definition-or-test"* was true when written and went stale one wave later — measured **8**, two of them production, added by U23 under D98 rider 1c. The stale figure is now live in **four** documents: D95 §4 and its register row, `docs/decisions/README.md:81`, D98:240, and `TODO.md` at :470 and :704. Until this is ruled, every such measurement either edits a resolved record with no rule permitting it, or is dropped.
- Do: **rule whether a resolved decision's body takes dated corrections, or whether only its register row moves** — and say which, in a form later lanes can apply without re-deriving it.
- Accept:
  - The ruling is recorded with its reason and with the mechanism spelled out: where a correction goes, how it is dated and attributed, and what happens to the sentence it corrects (replaced, struck, or left with the correction beside it).
  - It states explicitly whether the **four stale `is_verified` sites** are in scope of this row or a follow-on. They are the reason the row exists and leaving that unsaid reproduces the defect.
  - It answers the case where the correction and the original disagree about a **count**, since both known instances are counts and a count is the thing that goes stale silently.
  - At least one real correction is made under the new rule, so the mechanism is exercised rather than described. An unexercised procedure is the shape D94 §4 already found blind once.
- Notes:
  - **The row is wrong about where D84's mechanism lives.** It says *"D84 §2 already has a dated-corrections mechanism that would fit"*. Measured 2026-08-10: D84 §2 is *"Why introducing these limits at M2 cannot break line 123"* and contains no correction machinery. The mechanism is D84's trailing **`## Correction — 2026-07-28 (A27 implementation)`** and **`### Correction to the correction — 2026-07-28 (M0 wave 7)`** sections, pointed at from §4 and §7 (*"see the 2026-07-28 correction below"*). The substance survives and is stronger than the row claims: D84 corrected its own resolved body **twice**, so the practice already exists without a rule — which is the argument for ruling it rather than inventing it.
  - **This row blocks two known corrections.** D115 §3.7 declines to correct **D109 §8 (iii)** (which attributes A106's history to A105 and A108) precisely because Q122 is unruled; and A108's entry parks a measured correction to **D101 §3.4** for the same reason. Both are waiting on this row, which is the sharpest available evidence that it is real work rather than bookkeeping.
- **Entry authored 2026-08-10 under Q134 from `TODO.md`'s Q122 row alone — no other source existed.** Transcribed from the row: `Milestone` (its M2 section), `Size`, `Discovered by`, the `Problem`, and the `Do` — the row is itself decision-shaped and supplies its imperative. `Deps` reads `none` because the row carries no `after:` clause and protocol rule 7 makes those clauses authoritative; it is not an assertion that nothing precedes this. **Authored here, not transcribed: the whole `Accept` and both `Notes` bullets** — the row names no acceptance criterion, and in particular does not say whether the four stale sites are this row's or a follow-on's; the `Accept` requires the ruling to answer that rather than answering it here. The first `Notes` bullet is a correction to the row, measured on 2026-08-10; the second is a finding of this wave and is not in the row.

### Q123 — The capture script must refuse to finish without all four provenance fields
- Milestone: M2
- Size: S
- Deps: after A25, Q99
- Spec: `docs/anchors/real-smoke-runbook.md` §1 (consent) and §4 (recording the run).
- Discovered by: **the A25 lane** (2026-08-07).
- Problem: `testdata/anchors/A16-A17-live/CAPTURE.log` violates runbook §1 and §4 — no consent record (it leans on the previous day's M2-wide permission, which §1 explicitly rejects), no SHA-256, no per-request UTC. `testdata/anchors/A25-bootstrap/D60-CAPTURE.log` also lacks per-file SHA-256. Nothing enforces the fields, so the next capture can omit them the same way.
- Do: make the capture script **refuse to finish** without all four provenance fields, rather than leaving them to the operator. The 2026-08-07 header capture (`testdata/anchors/A25-upgrade-headers/CAPTURE.log`) is the format to match.
- Accept:
  - A capture run that is missing any of the four fields — consent record, per-file SHA-256, per-request UTC, and the fourth §4 field — does not produce a finished log: the script fails rather than warning.
  - The refusal is demonstrated for each field independently, so a single guard cannot pass by covering one of them.
  - A run supplying all four succeeds and its log matches the 2026-08-07 capture's shape.
  - The two existing logs are **not** back-filled. Hashing the files today would prove nothing about what arrived over the wire while looking exactly like the field §4 asks for; their gaps are recorded where they are, not repaired.
- Notes: the no-back-fill rule is the row's own and is load-bearing — it is why this row is about the script and not about the logs. A16-A17's missing consent record is unrecoverable by construction: consent is an event, and the event either happened or it did not.
- **Entry authored 2026-08-10 under Q134 from `TODO.md`'s Q123 row alone — no other source existed.** Transcribed from the row: `Milestone` (its M2 section), `Size`, `Deps`, `Discovered by`, the `Spec`'s two runbook sections, the `Problem`, the `Do`, and the `Notes` — the row supplies all of them, including the no-back-fill reasoning verbatim in substance. **The `Accept` is authored**, though only as the observable form of the `Do`'s own refusal: the row states the refusal and not a criterion, and the per-field independence clause is this entry's addition, not the row's. The paths were confirmed to exist on 2026-08-10; **the row names "all four provenance fields" and enumerates only three** (consent, SHA-256, per-request UTC), so the `Accept`'s fourth field is left to be read off runbook §4 by the executing lane rather than guessed at here.

### Q124 — The vectors README's post-Q14 rule contradicts three committed sources and instructs a misfile
- Milestone: M2
- Size: XS
- Deps: before A22 (D101)
- Discovered by: **D101** (2026-08-07), while ruling where the M2 anchor golden vectors live.
- Problem: `testdata/vectors/README.md`'s *"Add a kind"* row stated, for the post-Q14 (frozen) column, *"new kinds land under a new format version"*. Three committed sources say the opposite, and one of them is executed rather than argued: `testdata/vectors/v1/FROZEN.sha256`'s `storage-address` preamble records a post-freeze kind addition as *"the sanctioned append path (the same one the M2 `anchor` kind is reserved for): new `#! kind` line + new digest line; no existing digest moves"*; the README's own freeze section says the same; and `storage-address` itself was appended that way at S4. The confusion is a category error — a vector's `format_version` names the format it **pins**, not the vintage of the kind that pins it. On paper it blocked A22's freeze append, because a lane reading the table would have concluded that landing `#! kind anchor` required a `v2`.
- Do: correct the row's frozen-column cell to state the append path, per D101 §5.1, and land it **before** `#! kind anchor` — that ordering is D101 §5.1's own clause, not a preference, because the table is what A22's lane would read first.
- Accept: the frozen-column cell states the append path and names the runner's refusal of a misfiled vector; a dated note under the table quotes the former wording verbatim, per the house rule that a corrected row quotes itself; and no existing digest moves.
- Notes: the correction is a rewording of one table cell plus a dated note, in a file that is not itself frozen — `FROZEN.sha256` covers the vectors' digests, not the README's prose — so it is an XS with no freeze-procedure cost.
- **[2026-08-09 DONE]** Landed per D101 §5.1, before `#! kind anchor`, as that ruling's ordering clause requires. The cell now states the append path and names `FormatVersionMismatch` as the runner's refusal of a misfiled vector; the dated note quotes the former wording. **Two deliberate adjustments to D101's supplied text, both to keep it true at landing**: D101's *"Executed twice: `storage-address` (S4), `anchor` (A22)"* would have been a prediction on the day it landed — the shape D101 itself calls *"a prediction that ages into a lie"* two paragraphs earlier — so it reads *"Executed once"* plus the `FROZEN.sha256` reservation, and A22's append takes it to twice; and the correction is dated **2026-08-09** (landing) while citing D101 §5 (2026-08-07, ruling), so the two dates are not conflated. The stale D87 budget figure in the same file was corrected in the same pass (590 280 B / 28.15 % → measured), and **A22 then moved it again to 695 365 B / 33.16 %**, which is what the file reads today. `cbor_crosscheck_contract` 4/4 green.
- **The row's closing claim about line 418 is FALSE, and it was wrong when written rather than stale.** The row asserts *"the amended row is **still line 418**, so D101's `:418` citations did not go stale."* Measured: at `f60d628`'s **parent** the row was at `:418`; `f60d628` — the commit that landed this very correction, `+17/−5` on that file — moved it to **`:423`**, with the dated note at `:426`; and it is still at `:423` at `95fcee0`, the file being byte-identical to `HEAD` in the working tree. So the correction did not preserve `:418`, it consumed it: the author verified the **pre-edit** location and recorded it as the post-edit one. D101 cites `testdata/vectors/README.md:418` in two places — its §5 ruling heading and its edit-set table — and **both were made stale by the act of executing D101**. Not corrected here: `D101`'s body is out of this row's scope, the sites are owned by **Q169** (which already names D101 among its three known stale line citations and asks whether the no-line-number rule extends past the frozen registry), and D117 §2.3(e) reserves a correction to the lane that owns it. Recorded here so the next reader of this row does not re-trust the sentence. This is the second measured instance today of a correction's own locators rotting faster than the claim they corrected, D117 §1.9 being the first.
- **Entry authored 2026-08-10 under Q135 from `TODO.md`'s Q124 row alone — no other source existed.** Transcribed from the row: `Size`, `Deps`, `Discovered by`, the `Problem`, and the `[2026-08-09 DONE]` outcome. **Authored here, not transcribed: the whole `Do`, the whole `Accept`, and the `Notes`** — the row is a closed row and states an outcome, not an acceptance criterion, so the criterion is reconstructed from what the outcome reports as delivered and is **not** an independent judgement of what should have been required. `Milestone` is not in the row and is taken from the `## M2 — Anchors` block it sits in. The line-418 bullet is **measured here, not transcribed** — the row asserts the opposite — by reading the file at `f60d628^`, `f60d628` and `95fcee0`; the three committed sources named in `Problem` and the `695 365 B / 33.16 %` figure were re-read in the working tree on 2026-08-10 and are exact.

### Q125 — The local gate compiles for `wasm32` and never runs the wasm32 tests
- Milestone: M2
- Size: S
- Deps: none
- Discovered by: **CI run 31251119065** (2026-08-08).
- Problem: `scripts/local-gate.sh`'s `wasm32` lane is `cargo build -p antseal-core --target wasm32-unknown-unknown`. The lane that *executes* wasm32 tests is `scripts/wasm-tests.sh --check`, and only CI runs it. So a green local gate plus a green native `cargo test` — the exact pair wave 9 verified on — **structurally cannot** catch an architecture-dependent assertion, and did not: `size_of::<x509_cert::Certificate>()` is **376 on wasm32 against 512 on x86-64**, and two new equality rows in `caps.rs` reddened `wasm32-core-tests` at `6f69e1a`. The gate printed `wasm32 PASS`, which is exactly how it was read.
- Do: either fold `wasm-tests.sh --check` into the gate, or make the gate say out loud that its wasm32 lane is compile-only, so the next lane does not read `wasm32 PASS` as *"the wasm32 tests pass"*. Note the sibling asymmetry: `heavy-features` already carries a spelled-out n/a state for the same class of reason.
- Accept: either the executing lane runs locally, or the gate's own output distinguishes "compiled for wasm32" from "tested on wasm32"; and the sentence claiming the gate runs the lanes CI enforces is true of whatever lands.
- **DONE 2026-08-09 — both halves, plus the sentence that licensed the misreading.** The compile lane is now named `wasm32-build`, and its name says what it does. A new **diff-triggered `wasm32-tests` lane** runs `scripts/wasm-tests.sh --check` on the `heavy-features` pattern: `--needs-run` decides from the diff (`ANTSEAL_GATE_BASE`; `ANTSEAL_GATE_WASM=1/0` forces; exit 2 is a **visible SKIP**, never a silent one). Validated **rc 0 against `6f69e1a` in a detached worktree at that commit** — all seven `antseal-core` files selected, `caps.rs` among them, so the trigger demonstrably fires on the change that caused the red — and rc 1 against wave 8's 49-file `09a81c2`. `--self-test` gained planted change-sets in **both** directions, with the positive arm pinned to `anchor/caps.rs` **by name**, and it **runs unconditionally before the trigger**: behind the trigger, a path list that stopped matching `crates/antseal-core/` would render `n/a` for ever and silence its own guard. `local-gate.sh:2`'s *"the same lanes CI enforces"* now enumerates the contexts a green gate does **not** reproduce, and four `feature_pins.rs` arms pin the wiring, each shown red before it was trusted.
- **The cost figure that kept the executing lane out of the gate was unsourced, and its stated cause was wrong too.** Measured here: **3 m 42 s cold, 2 m 10 s warm**, of which **2 m 15 s is node *executing*** the PQC suite in a 1.3 GB linear memory — the compile is cached at **0.31 s**. So no cache and no build tuning shrinks it; the runtime *is* the cost, which is why the answer is a diff trigger and not a `run` line. Any future argument about this lane's price must start from the execution number, not from a build number.
- Notes: **`wasm-bitmatch` stays out deliberately, and not for cost** — measured 32 s plus 53 s of self-test, *cheaper* than this lane. Its `build.rs` walks `testdata/vectors/`, which is the one path this trigger must **not** match, so folding it under the same predicate would install a lane that silently never fires for its most important input. It needs its own predicate → **Q128**.

### Q126 — The F4 registry's changelog has never recorded anything
- Milestone: M2
- Size: S
- Deps: after A27 (D104 §5)
- Discovered by: **the D104 lane** (2026-08-09).
- Problem: `docs/format/anchor-artifact-limits.md` §6 Changelog has taken **zero entries across seven document-changing commits** — measured, `git log --oneline 1620543..HEAD -- docs/format/anchor-artifact-limits.md` returns seven. It is not missing an entry; it has never been used. That matters because §5's Update rule routes F4's auditability *through* it: *"a dated note in §6 saying which release lowered it and why"* is the evidence step that makes *"has this ever been lowered?"* answerable from the tree, and it depends on a section with a 100 % miss rate.
- Do: either enforce §6 with a lint of the `--freeze-boundary` shape, or delete it and move the lowering note into the `lowered` cell itself, where the reader already looks. Do **not** write seven retrospective entries: a changelog composed by lanes that did not make the edits is fabricated provenance, which this project refuses elsewhere.
- Accept: a document-changing commit that owes a §6 entry cannot land without one, or §6 does not exist and the `lowered` cell carries the history; either way the question *"has this ever been lowered?"* is answerable without reading seven diffs.
- Notes: this conditions the `MAX_OTS_DEPTH` lowering window **D104 left open** — whoever exercises that window closes this row first, or the ceremony §5's Update rule prescribes produces an entry in a section nothing maintains. It is **not A106's** (scoped to two provenance citations) and **not Q64's** (the F1–F4 mirror lint).

### Q127 — Report enums are not enumerable, so a value can land unexercised
- Milestone: M2
- Size: S
- Deps: after R69 (D105 §6)
- Discovered by: **the D105 planner** (2026-08-09).
- Problem: nothing enforces *"a new value in report v1's value space must be exercised by a committed assertion"*. R69 was that gap, and it was found by inspection rather than by a red lane — `SupportingEvidenceResult::ArbitrumReceipt` shipped post-freeze, legally, with **nothing rendering it inside a whole report**. Of the report's five enums only `AnchorState` is immune, and for an unrelated reason: it has a `const ALL`, a wildcard-free `wire_name` match, and a sweep over `ALL` that asserts the serialized spelling, so an eighth state is a **compile error**. `SignatureScheme`, `AnchorKind`, `StorageLinkageResult` and `SupportingEvidenceResult` have none of that; their coverage is *incidental*, holding only because some fixture happens to render the value.
- Do: give the other four the triple `AnchorState` already has — `const ALL`, a wildcard-free spelling accessor, a sweep over `ALL` asserting the serialized bytes — so a variant that is named and never rendered reddens on the day it is named. `SupportingEvidenceResult` needs a hand-written arm for its struct variant; the point is that the sweep is **exhaustive**, not that it is uniform. Then state **R-VAL** (*every value report v1 can serialize is exercised by a committed assertion that renders it inside a whole canonical report*) wherever the report's format discipline is written, so the "in composition" half has a home rather than living only in R69's snapshot.
- Accept: adding a variant to any report enum without rendering it is a compile error or a red test, not a silent value addition; the four enums' `ALL` arrays are swept; and R-VAL is written down where a lane adding a variant will read it.
- Notes: the shape worth carrying is D105 §4.3's — **zero moved pins is a correct *format* signal and an empty *coverage* signal, and the tree has no second signal.** A value addition nothing emits moves zero pins; a format event moves all 21 report cases and all 26 `REPORT_DIGEST_BY_SHAPE` rows. That is why `arbitrum-receipt` shipped with nothing red and nothing wrong, and why the pin count cannot be the instrument here. **Not Q120**: Q120 is cross-surface agreement between `list`, `status` and `WorkRecord` on UNANCHORED; this is the report's own value space being a checkable set. Do not merge them. Adjacent and not ruled: **R70**, the converse gap (a value report v1 carries that the pipeline may no longer produce), which this work would make visible. Also recorded by D105 §6 and **not edited into its owner**: D94's three-class vocabulary has **no class for adding a case to a frozen report document**, and **Q107** as scoped only splits the existing row into the three classes rather than adding a fourth.

### Q128 — `wasm-bitmatch` is Q125's defect one file over
- Milestone: M2
- Size: S
- Deps: after Q125
- Discovered by: **the Q125 lane** (2026-08-09).
- Problem: `wasm-bitmatch` is a second wasm32-**execution** lane that the local gate does not run, carried only as a CONTRIBUTING checkbox. Q125 closed the `wasm32-tests` half and deliberately left this one out — **not for cost**: measured **32 s plus 53 s of self-test**, *cheaper* than the lane that did land. The reason is the trigger. `crates/wasm-bitmatch/build.rs` walks `testdata/vectors/` by design (*"no hardcoded file lists"*), so a **vectors-only change is its mandatory case** — and that is precisely the case Q125's `--needs-run` predicate must not match, since matching it would fire the expensive PQC lane on every vector edit. Folding this lane under that predicate would install a gate that silently never runs for its most important input.
- Do: give it its own predicate, keyed on `testdata/vectors/` and on the harness itself, with its own planted change-sets in both directions — the positive arm pinned to a vector file by name, the way Q125's is pinned to `anchor/caps.rs`.
- Accept: a vectors-only change fires this lane locally; a change that touches neither vectors nor the harness skips it visibly, not silently; and the self-test runs unconditionally before the trigger, so a path list that stops matching cannot render `n/a` for ever.
- Notes: A22 is the worked example of why this matters — it added a whole vector kind, and the lane that certifies native↔wasm32 byte-identity over it is the one the local gate does not run.

### Q129 — A fourth sense of UNANCHORED, in frozen normative text
- Milestone: M2
- Size: XS
- Deps: after Q120
- Discovered by: **the Q120 lane** (2026-08-09).
- Problem: `docs/format/registry-v1.md:664` says *"an UNANCHORED bundle is **both** anchor arrays empty"*. That is `NagState::Unanchored`'s **artifact-presence** sense wearing the spec's name: a bundle holding one pending OTS anchor is **MVP-SPEC.md:137's** UNANCHORED — zero *headline-eligible* anchors — and is not this one. D98 rider 3c already forbids collapsing the three predicates Q120 separated; this is a fourth spelling of the word, in normative text, saying the wrong one.
- Do: decide whether the sentence is corrected, annotated, or kept as a **scoped term of art** for the wire schema — and if it is kept, say at the line that it is the array-emptiness sense and not line 137's. Note the cost before choosing: the file is SHA-256-pinned by `scripts/format-freeze.sh`, so correcting it is a **format-freeze event**, not a drive-by edit.
- Accept: the registry line either states the spec's predicate or names which of the four senses it means; the decision is recorded with its freeze cost either way.
- Notes: this is the one instance of the word Q120 could not reach — Q120's mechanism is a test over `list`, `status` and `WorkRecord`, and a frozen document is outside it. The renames Q120 deferred are blocked by **Q113** (`NagState::name()` emits the wire string `"unanchored"`, and `json-envelopes.txt` carries three senses of the word); this row is blocked by a different mechanism, which is why it is separate.

### Q130 — The CBOR cross-check's scope rule is written twice, and checked against nothing
- Milestone: M2
- Size: S
- Deps: after A22; F31 owns the registry flag (D112 R-A); Q54 extends the same directive
- Discovered by: **the wave-10 gate run** (2026-08-09); scoped by **D112** (2026-08-10).
- Problem: `testdata/vectors/v1/crosscheck_cbor.py` decides a vector's scope by sniffing a field name, and A22 proved the rule wrong in both directions. It took "in scope" to mean `"diagnostic" in case`; the `anchor` kind commits no CBOR but names a field `diagnostic` for something else entirely — `AnchorDiagnostic` as a code string or `null` (D101 §3.5), 7 cases, 6 of them `null` — so every anchor case was pulled in and failed for having no `*_bytes` field. **Only the full gate caught it**: A22's lane verified `cross-check.sh`'s *generator* half and never reached this checker, which is `.py` and reads the committed documents rather than the Rust. Narrowed 2026-08-09 to `isinstance(diagnostic, dict)`, the weakest predicate under which `check_case` is defined. **The predicate lived in TWO places and the first repair fixed one, which CI caught within the hour**: `self_test` selects a document *and* a case by the same rule, and once `run` was narrowed the bare form there kept selecting `anchor/anchor.json` — which sorts before `bundle/` — and died on `StopIteration`. **Green `--check`, red `--self-test`, because CI runs them as separate steps and only the second one selects.** **The duplication is still live**: `_in_scope` is a closure *inside* `self_test` (`:958-959`), `run` carries its own inline copy (`:905-909`), and what holds them in step is a comment (`:949`). Two further defects in the same function: `self_test` plants each fault **inside** the `try` that judges it, so a mutation it cannot plant is reported as `RED as required`; and it has no positive non-vacuity assertion — three bare `next(...)` calls whose `StopIteration` is not a `CheckFailure` and never reaches `main`'s handler.
- Do: per **D112 R1–R4, R10**. One module-level `scope_of(path)` — the only expression of scope in the file — called by both `run` and `self_test`; delete `_in_scope` and `run`'s inline comprehension. `self_test` sweeps **every** in-scope document rather than the first (D31 §6 forbids hard-coding a vector or case name, and sweeping removes the sort-order dependence that caused the incident rather than working around it), with the `*_bytes` and ≥2-layer preconditions hoisted **out** of the mutation loop, planting moved out of the `try`, and the summary's fault counts computed instead of hardcoded. Make the out-of-scope message distinguish "no `expect.cases` container" from "cases without a sidecar" — five of thirteen kinds carry `expect.vectors` or named sub-objects and today all print the same false reason. Add `the_cbor_scope_predicate_is_written_exactly_once` to `cbor_crosscheck_contract.rs`, in the idiom that file already uses to ban `cbor2.dumps`, prose included. Rewrite `docs/testing/cbor-cross-check.md` §7 bullet 1 and §8.1, routing the registry flag to **F31**.
- Accept:
  - The scope predicate occurs **exactly once** in `crosscheck_cbor.py`, prose included, and a second copy turns a named test red — shown red both ways (deleted, and duplicated).
  - `--self-test` selects the same set `--check` checks, not a sample of it, and raises a diagnosing `CheckFailure` — never `StopIteration` — when that set is empty.
  - A mutation that cannot be planted aborts the self-test; it can no longer be reported as a fault the checker caught.
  - Every count in the self-test's summary is computed from the run.
  - `docs/testing/cbor-cross-check.md` names **F31** as the flag's owner and no longer says "Tracked as Q130".
- Notes: **The registry flag is NOT this row's** — D112 R-A. **F31** has owned it since 2026-07-28 with a fuller `Do` (the `#! kind` home, the must-exist biconditional, `docs/testing/cross-check.md:221-229`'s *"discovery may return a superset, never a subset"*) and a **measured** instance this row lacks: `crypto/manifest-aead.json`'s `manifest_bytes` is CBOR-shaped, **not well-formed** (`5820` declares 32 bytes over a 30-byte literal; `581e` was meant), sidecar-less, frozen, and checked by nothing — so the false **negative** is the live defect and the false positive is the hypothetical, which is the reverse of the ordering `TODO.md:560` gives. **Q54** then forbids building it here: *"two columns on one directive line and one parser, not two mechanisms over the same registry."* **The freeze cost is zero and was the brief's central worry** — `FROZEN.sha256`'s own bytes are pinned by nothing (its `EntryPolicy` admits `.json` only, so it cannot list itself; `wasm-bitmatch/build.rs` and `vector_runner.rs` classify it as an auxiliary), `vector-freeze.sh`'s frozen guard iterates `grep -v '^#'` and cannot see a directive, and the `#! kind` block has been appended to **three times post-freeze** (S4, R12, A22). Also corrected: `TODO.md:560`'s *"only `bundle` ×10 and `manifest` ×6 carry the field"* — 23 cases carry it, 16 dict-valued, the other 7 being `anchor`'s; and its *"Both now share one `_in_scope` helper"*, which is the claim this row exists to make true.

### Q131 — The registry's two prose surfaces are never compared
- Milestone: M2
- Size: S
- Deps: after Q129 (D108 §1.6)
- Discovered by: **the D108 lane** (2026-08-09).
- Problem: `docs/format/registry-v1.md` and `docs/format/registry-v1.json` state the same facts twice, and `crates/antseal-core/tests/format_registry_freeze.rs`'s D-family ("document ⟷ mirror") cross-checks only the *mechanical* half — keys, field names, presence, reserved slots, bands, enums, scalar lengths, tuple arities, caps. The `.md`'s `len/shape` cells and the `.json`'s `notes` fields are compared **to nothing, in either direction**: `the_document_map_tables_match_the_mirror` reads `cells[0]`, `cells[1]` and `cells[3]` and asserts `cells.len() == 5`, so `cells[4]` — the prose — is never read. §7.6 key 3 is the measured instance: the two copies of one claim differ in wording (the `.json` drops the emphasis and adds a clause). Both files are SHA-256-pinned, so a divergence is **frozen in place** rather than caught, and Q129 had to be resolved as an erratum against *two* sites precisely because nothing had noticed they were two.
- Do: add a **closed-vocabulary** cross-check to the D-family: a short list of load-bearing terms — `UNANCHORED`, `reserved`, `may be empty`, the tier letters `[P]`/`[X]`/`[R]` — which, if present in one surface's prose for a given key, must be present in the other's. Pin *terms*, not wording.
- Accept: the two prose surfaces cannot diverge on a load-bearing term without a named test going red, **or** the document records at the D-family why a prose cross-check must not exist, carrying `622f5fe` §1's argument so the next lane does not re-derive it.
- Notes: **the naive form is already ruled out and the ruling is right** — `622f5fe` §1: *"a substring match on the prose would pin editorial wording rather than format facts."* That is why this is scoped to a closed vocabulary rather than to equality. Explicitly **not Q129**, which is one word at one key; this is a structural gap spanning fourteen maps. It is also the reason Q129's site count was wrong in its own row: the second frozen site was invisible because nothing in the tree relates the two files' prose.

### Q132 — The freeze machinery cites a spec line that does not say what it is cited for
- Milestone: M2
- Size: XS
- Deps: after Q14, Q27 (D108 §1.7)
- Discovered by: **the D108 lane** (2026-08-09).
- Problem: `scripts/format-freeze.sh:179-181`, `docs/format/FROZEN.sha256:48-52` and `crates/antseal-core/tests/format_freeze.rs`'s failure text all cite `MVP-SPEC.md` line 123 as the authority for *"a byte change to a frozen entry is a format-version event"*. Line 123 says something else: *"every **released** manifest/bundle format version remains verifiable by all future CLI and page releases"* — a **compatibility** guarantee, conditioned on *released*, and D104 §1.5 measures with three independent confirmations that nothing has been released. The freeze antseal actually operates is a **stronger, self-imposed** discipline that line 123 does not require.
- Do: cite **Q14's own act** as the authority for byte-immutability, and name line 123 as the compatibility rule the freeze exists to protect. Three call sites, no behaviour change.
- Accept: every citation of the byte-immutability rule names Q14, and line 123 is cited only for compatibility — or the derivation from line 123 is written out where it is claimed.
- Notes: not pedantry. A reader who follows the citation finds a conditional whose condition is false today and may conclude the freeze is soft — which is exactly the inference D108 §3 spends a section refusing, and exactly the inference that would have made Q129's "just re-bless it" answer look cheap. A rule defended by a misquotation is defended by nothing.

### Q133 — The decision sweep cannot see `scripts/`, which is where A71 hid
- Milestone: M2
- Size: XS
- Deps: after Q57, Q85
- Discovered by: **D109** (2026-08-10) §8(i), from the surface comparison Q85 forced.
- Problem: `check_decisions()` sweeps `DECISION_SCAN = ["crates", "docs/format", "docs/testing"]` with `DECISION_SUFFIXES = {".rs", ".md", ".json", ".py"}` (`scripts/check-traceability.py:487-488`). The task half Q85 wrote sweeps `["crates", "docs/format", "docs/testing", "scripts"]` and adds `.sh` and `.mjs` — because that is exactly where **A71** was hiding: a task id named in `scripts/fuzz.sh:98-103`, in a committed gate script, with no row. The identical hole is still open one function away for `D<n>` citations. A decision cited in the gate is as normative as one cited in `crates/`, and nothing resolves it. Measured cost of closing it: **zero** new unresolved decisions (D109 §6.4, command 8).
- Do: make the two scan sets one constant, or two with a comment saying why they differ. The widening **must** carry `TASK_SCAN_SELF`'s self-exclusion in the same change: `check_decisions()` has no counterpart to `if relative == TASK_SCAN_SELF: continue` (`:789`), and this file contains the literal `D77000` at `:1229` as a self-test fixture whose entire purpose is to be an out-of-bound id. It is skipped today only because 77000 exceeds the ceiling — an accident of the fixture's value, not a rule, and it stops being true the moment `scripts/` enters the sweep.
- Accept: the two scan sets are one constant, or two with the reason written down; a planted `D<n>` citation in a `.sh` file under `scripts/` turns the lane red; and the checker's own fixtures are excluded by name rather than by luck.
- Notes: zero measured cost is what makes this XS; the self-exclusion is what stops it being trivial. It is also why the decision half has read green for weeks over a surface that omits the gate — the same blindness that made A71 invisible for the ten days between `fuzz.sh` naming it and D109 finding it.

### Q134 — Twelve open rows have no `tasks/*.md` detail entry
- Milestone: M2
- Size: M
- Deps: after Q85
- Discovered by: **D109** (2026-08-10) §8(iii), from the census `--task-entries` now makes machine-readable.
- Problem: twelve open `TODO.md` rows have no `### <ID> — ` entry: **A72, A74, A76, A105, A108, Q88, Q89, Q122, Q123, R60, R76, U39**. (Q130 was the thirteenth and closed at wave 12's bookkeeping, which is why the count here is twelve and D109's is thirteen.) This is the dangerous half of Q85: a lane that opens `tasks/A.md` to read its task's `Do`/`Accept` finds nothing and improvises — which is how A22 came to be executed against a superseded Accept for two days, and how A106 came to be defined second-hand in three places that disagreed about its scope. Each of the twelve carries a row of 386–2 193 characters holding its size and usually its deps, so for most of them the entry is substantially a reformat of text that already exists.
- Do: write the twelve entries in house style and delete each one's `ROWS_PENDING_ENTRY` line in the same change. **Two are not reformats**: **A105** and **A108** are described in the Current-focus block as reconstructed from disagreeing sources, so they need their domain owner to settle what the task *is*, not a lint lane to transcribe a row. Route those two separately rather than letting them hold the other ten.
- Accept: all twelve have `### <ID> — ` entries, their `ROWS_PENDING_ENTRY` lines are deleted **in the same commit**, and `check-traceability.py --task-entries` is green throughout — the register's staleness rule makes a written entry with a surviving register line a failure, so the two edits cannot be separated even by accident.
- Notes: the register is date-locked (`BACKLOG_FROZEN_ON = "2026-08-10"`) and may only shrink, so this row cannot be deferred by growing it: a row added since that date and lacking an entry is a defect to fix, not an exemption to grant. The eight **closed** rows are deliberately **Q135**, split so that archaeology cannot delay the live hazard.

### Q135 — Eight closed rows have no detail entry, and draining them retires the register
- Milestone: M3
- Size: S
- Deps: after Q85, Q134
- Discovered by: **D109** (2026-08-10) §8(iv).
- Problem: eight **closed** rows have no `tasks/*.md` entry: **Q124, S27, S29, S34, U36, U37, U38, U40**. Nobody will execute them, so this is archaeology rather than hazard — but their rows run 1 617–4 044 characters with the outcome written inline, so each entry is a transcription of text that is already in the tree, and the record is what entries are for.
- Do: write the eight entries and delete their register lines. When the last one goes `ROWS_PENDING_ENTRY` is empty, and at that point delete **the constant, `BACKLOG_FROZEN_ON`, the staleness rule and self-test case (k) together** rather than leaving an empty dict. The self-test already says so and will fail on the empty register precisely to force the choice: *"an empty register with a frozen date invites a future lane to reopen it."*
- Accept: eight entries, eight register lines deleted, and the register construct removed entirely rather than emptied; `check-traceability.py --self-test` still passes with case (k) gone, which requires deleting the case rather than letting its guard raise.
- Notes: deliberately split from **Q134** so eight closed rows cannot delay twelve open ones, and milestoned M3 for the same reason. The date lock is what makes the split safe — the register may only shrink, so nothing can accumulate in it while this waits.

### Q136 — Twelve self-reported counts in `TODO.md` are verified by nothing
- Milestone: M3
- Size: XS
- Deps: after Q85
- Discovered by: **D109** (2026-08-10) §8(v).
- Problem: `TODO.md` states twelve counts about itself — the header's task total, the decision triple, and the nine per-domain milestone-section cells — and every one is prose asserting a fact about the repository that nothing checks. All twelve are correct today, measured at this wave's bookkeeping. The class is the one this project keeps closing elsewhere: a number that reads like the checked ones and is not.
- Do: **the objection has to be answered before any check is written.** These counts drift *by design* during a wave — lanes mint rows, rows close, and the file is reconciled at bookkeeping — so a hard check in the required lane would be red for most of every wave and would train every contributor to ignore a red gate. The tractable forms are a separate flag bookkeeping runs deliberately (`--counts`, excluded from the no-flag run, in the idiom `--self-test` already establishes) or an advisory print that never sets the exit code. **Do not add it to the required lane.**
- Accept: either the twelve counts are checked under a flag the lane does not run and bookkeeping does, or the reason a count check must stay advisory is recorded where the counts are written, so the next lane does not re-derive it from scratch.
- Notes: filed at M3 because the cost of being wrong is a stale number in a planning document, not a wrong artifact — which is itself the argument for the flag being optional. `check-traceability.py` is the natural home: it already parses `TODO.md`'s rows and the decision register for four other checks, so the counts are a projection of data it holds rather than a new parser.

### Q137 — One field's tier sits in the neighbouring field, and both surfaces are frozen
- Milestone: M2
- Size: XS
- Deps: after Q131, Q129
- Discovered by: **the D111 lane** (2026-08-10).
- Problem: at registry **§7.3 key 6** (`file_entry`'s `units`) the mirror carries **no `tier` key at all**, while the same object's `rule` holds the literal `at least one kind=normal unit [P] (D77)` — the tier letter is present, one field over — and `docs/format/registry-v1.md` tags the key `[P]` in its `len/shape` cell. It is the **unique** such pair over the 68 documented §7.x rows: the only key where the document states a tier and the mirror declares none. (The one other object whose `rule` carries a bracketed letter, §7.14 key 2 `s_root`, also carries `tier: "R"` — there the letter is duplicated, not displaced.) D111 R5 records the instance in a comment and cannot catch it: its containment check runs only where both surfaces speak, and everything else falls to a catch-all `_ => skipped` arm that swallows **49** of the 68. What keeps R5 non-vacuous is not that arm's narrowness but the two counting assertions after the loop, `both_present == 19` and `both_present + skipped == 68`.
- Do: **not a byte edit.** Both surfaces are frozen, so this is an errata-shaped fix in the `docs/format/frozen-registry-errata.md` idiom D108 R4 established, on D108 §3.1's ladder: record that this key's tier is `P` and that it currently lives in `rule`, or record the finding as below the erratum bar with the reason.
- Accept: a reader of either surface can learn this key's tier without inferring it from a rule string, and the record says which of the two outcomes was chosen and why; no frozen byte moves.
- Notes: most of this row's value is what it proves about R5 — a check whose skip arm is a catch-all is sound for the rows where the mirror is genuinely silent and blind for the one where it is silently wrong, and the difference between those two is exactly what a `tier` field is for. Worth carrying into whatever a registry v2 does with that field, because an erratum records the defect and does not fix the shape.

### Q138 — The D-family drops three more document columns than Q131 counted
- Milestone: M2
- Size: S
- Deps: after Q131
- Discovered by: **the D111 lane** (2026-08-10).
- Problem: D111 landed four additive checks over the §7.x map tables — R2 `type`, R3 `presence`, R4 the may-be-empty rule, R5 tier containment — and touched no other table. The lane's own sweep found three columns elsewhere that the D-family reaches with nothing: registry **§6.2**'s `pubkey` and `signature` columns and its `notes`; **§2**'s `fields` and `source`; and **§11**'s `applies to`. The reads are narrow by index: `the_document_enum_tables_match_the_mirror` takes `cells[0]`/`cells[1]` and never `cells[2..4]`; `the_document_scalar_table_matches_the_mirror` takes `c[0]` only; `the_document_cap_table_matches_the_mirror` takes `c[0]`, `c[1]` and `c[3]`, stepping over index 2. §6.2's is the sharpest, because `pubkey`/`signature` are **byte lengths per signature algorithm** — a format fact of exactly the class R2 closed — and the values are pinned only by a different route, `scalars[]` against the code in `code_scalar_lengths_match_the_registry`, never against §6.2's own cells. **Named and unverified: none of the three was measured against a planted fault.**
- Do: for each column, either assert it in the D-family or name it as a recorded non-assertion in that family's header comment — D8 §14's discipline that a non-assertion must be named, which the D-family already holds itself to elsewhere.
- Accept: every column of §2, §6.2 and §11 is either read by a named test or listed as a deliberate non-assertion with its reason; a wrong byte length in §6.2's document copy turns a named test red.
- Notes: sized S rather than XS because it is three sections with three different shapes, not one more needle in an existing loop. **The columns are half-pinned already and it is worth knowing which half**: every one of these tables has its *header row* passed to `table_under` as a literal, so deleting, renaming or reordering a column reddens — it is the cell *contents* below the header that nothing reads. §2's narrowness is also deliberate and documented (the two surfaces group by byte length and by role, so their rows are not comparable and only the length *set* is asserted), which is the shape of answer this row should produce for the other two rather than a reflex assertion. The reason it is worth doing at all is Q131's own finding: both surfaces are SHA-256-pinned, so a divergence is *frozen in place* rather than caught, and Q129 had to be resolved as an erratum against two sites precisely because nothing in the tree related them.

### Q139 — The tag-bound format manifest's own bytes are pinned by nothing
- Milestone: M2
- Size: XS
- Deps: after Q14, Q27
- Discovered by: **the D111 lane** (2026-08-10), re-confirming D108 §1.4's table row by its own grep over `crates/`, `scripts/` and `.github/`.
- Problem: `docs/format/FROZEN.sha256` is the manifest that pins the wire registry's bytes, and **nothing pins the manifest**. A hand-edited digest line plus a matching document edit passes the whole suite, because the gate compares each listed file against the digest the manifest itself supplies — both halves of a forgery move together. It cannot even pin itself: `EntryPolicy`'s `excluded_paths` names `FROZEN.sha256` and `freeze_manifest`'s parser rejects a self-entry outright. Be precise about what *is* covered, because it is a weaker route rather than none: a *deleted* digest line reddens, through `format-freeze.sh`'s `registry_files()` re-discovery and `must_be_frozen()` — no `registry-v<n>.{md,json}` may sit outside the freeze — while the manifest's own bytes, prose, comments and digest *values* are covered by nothing. D108 recorded it in a table cell and it was never carried into a row.
- Do: pin it from outside itself — a signed tag, the `format-v1-freeze` tag's own content, or a CI assertion against a value the manifest cannot edit — or record the gap where a lane reaching for `--update` will meet it, which is the honest minimum.
- Accept: either the manifest's bytes are verified against something outside the manifest, or `scripts/format-freeze.sh` and the manifest's own header say plainly that they are not, and what that costs.
- Notes: **this is the tag-bound format manifest, not `testdata/vectors/v1/FROZEN.sha256`.** The two are easy to conflate and the vector manifest's unpinnedness is *deliberate and reasoned*: D112 §1.4 measures it — its `EntryPolicy` admits `.json` only so it cannot list itself, and `wasm-bitmatch/build.rs` and `vector_runner.rs` classify it as an auxiliary — and turns that into the argument that a `#! kind` edit is not a freeze event. None of that reasoning transfers to this file, which is under D108 §3's tag discipline.

### Q140 — Two rules in the tree disagree about whether `__pycache__/` is expected
- Milestone: M2
- Size: XS
- Deps: after Q4, Q5
- Discovered by: **the A112 lane** (2026-08-10), after it bricked that lane's `cargo clippy --workspace`.
- Problem: `crates/wasm-bitmatch/build.rs` walks `testdata/vectors/v1/` and **recurses into every directory it finds** (`:155-157`), then hard-fails on any file that is not a vector or a documented auxiliary (`:175-181`): *"unclassifiable file under vectors/v1/ — every file must be a vector (\*.json) or a documented auxiliary … nothing is silently skipped (Q4/Q5)"*. A `.pyc` does not satisfy `ends_with(".py")`, so one `testdata/vectors/v1/__pycache__/` directory fails the **entire workspace build** — not one crate, not one test. Meanwhile `.gitignore:44` lists `__pycache__/` as expected, with a comment citing the wave-7 incident. The directory returns whenever anyone runs or imports `crosscheck_cbor.py` without `-B`, which is the ordinary way to run it.
- Do: classify `__pycache__/` as ignorable in the walk rather than fatal. The Q4/Q5 intent — no vector silently skipped — is fully served by skipping a directory `.gitignore` already declares untracked, and is not served at all by failing a build over one.
- Accept: a stray `__pycache__/` under `testdata/vectors/v1/` does not fail the workspace build; an unclassifiable *file* still does, proven by planting one; and the two rules agree in the tree rather than in a contributor's head.
- Notes: the failure mode is why this earns a row at XS. It is a build failure, with a message about vectors, produced by running the vector checker, in a crate the contributor was not working on — three degrees from its cause, and it hit a lane that had touched neither Python nor `wasm-bitmatch`. The current mitigation is a convention (`python3 -B` everywhere) that nothing enforces and that every new contributor must be told.

### Q141 — The local gate runs the cross-check half that does not select
- Milestone: M2
- Size: XS
- Deps: after Q130, Q125
- Discovered by: **D112** §8.5 (2026-08-10), confirmed independently by the Q130 lane.
- Problem: `scripts/local-gate.sh:313` runs `scripts/cross-check.sh --check` and never `--self-test`. The omission is deliberate and stated at `:25-27` (*"the `--self-test` half and `cbor-drift-guard` are CI-only; the lane below runs `--check` only"*), but its cost has now been measured twice. The 2026-08-09 incident was **green under `--check` and red only under `--self-test`**, so the local gate structurally could not have caught it — only CI did, running the two as separate steps (`.github/workflows/ci.yml:382-385`). Q130's three new instruments are **all** invisible to `--check` and red only under `--self-test`, which is a second proof from an unrelated defect. And the gate's `cross-check` line does not say that the half it runs is the half that does not select a document.
- Do: run `--self-test` in the local gate, or say at the line that the half being run is the half that does not select — the omission is defensible, the silence about it is not. Measured cost of running it: **~4 s**.
- Accept: either the local gate runs both halves, or its `cross-check` line names what its half cannot catch and points at the CI step that can.
- Notes: this is **Q125**'s shape in a second lane — *"the gate compiles for wasm32 but never runs the wasm32 tests"* — and **Q128** is the same shape in a third. Three instances of one pattern is the argument for naming the pattern: a gate line is named for its subject, and a reader reads that as coverage.

### Q142 — `FROZEN.sha256` has an undeclared second parser
- Milestone: M2
- Size: XS
- Deps: after Q6
- Discovered by: **D112** §8.2 (2026-08-10).
- Problem: `crates/antseal-core/tests/vector_index.rs:151` defines `read_frozen`, which parses `testdata/vectors/v1/FROZEN.sha256` independently: it reads `#! pending ` lines and `<digest>  <path>` lines and **silently drops every other `#` line** (`:157-168`) — all `#! kind`, `#! status` and `#! manifest-version` directives included. The authoritative parser is `crates/antseal-core/tests/freeze_manifest/mod.rs:104`'s `parse_manifest`, whose module doc claims *"One directive vocabulary, one parser, two consumers"*. That claim is false: there are three readers, two of them go through the parser, and a malformed directive is invisible to the third while the authoritative one rejects it.
- Do: assert that the two readers agree on the digest set — the cheap form, and the one that catches divergence rather than trying to prevent it — or route `read_frozen` through `parse_manifest` if its independence is not the point.
- Accept: the digest sets the two readers produce are compared by a named test, or `read_frozen` is gone; and `freeze_manifest/mod.rs`'s *"parsed once"* claim is either true or corrected.
- Notes: D112 recorded this as a distinct defect rather than folding it into Q130 because it is a different crate and a different file, and R8 gave it only a comment. The independence may well be deliberate — a freeze test that re-implements its own reader is harder to fool — in which case the fix is the agreement assertion and not the merge. Harmless today; the cost is that a future directive change must remember a reader nothing names.

### Q143 — Thirteen task ids in the vector manifest are compared to nothing
- Milestone: M2
- Size: XS
- Deps: after Q85, F31
- Discovered by: **D112** §8.4 (2026-08-10).
- Problem: `testdata/vectors/v1/FROZEN.sha256` carries **13** `#! kind <name> <task>` directives. The task token is parsed, required non-empty (`crates/antseal-core/tests/freeze_manifest/mod.rs:155-159`) and stored as the map's value — then read by nothing. The only consumer, `vector_freeze_records_every_registered_kind` (`crates/antseal-core/tests/vector_freeze.rs:368-380`), compares `kinds.keys()` against `KNOWN_KINDS` and never touches `.values()`. So thirteen task ids sit inside a frozen manifest, checked against no task list. `testdata/vectors/v1/INDEX.json` separately carries a `task` per **vector** (14 entries), and nothing relates the two maps. Q85's `--task-citations` reaches neither: `.sha256` is not in `CITATION_SUFFIXES` and `testdata/` is not in `CITATION_SCAN`. (**Constant names corrected 2026-08-10 at wave 13's bookkeeping**: Q133 collapsed `DECISION_SCAN`/`DECISION_SUFFIXES`/`TASK_SCAN`/`TASK_SUFFIXES`/`TASK_SCAN_SELF` into `CITATION_SCAN`/`CITATION_SUFFIXES`/`CITATION_SCAN_SELF` and **deleted** the old names. The claim is unchanged under the new constants — `testdata/` and `.sha256` are still outside both. That a live row instructed a lane in names no longer defined is **Q151**.)
- Do: resolve the thirteen ids against `TODO.md`'s rows, and compare them against `INDEX.json`'s per-vector `task` where the two overlap. Decide whether the reach belongs in the Rust freeze test — which already parses the directive — or in `check-traceability.py`, which already resolves ids but would need `testdata/` and `.sha256` in scope. The first is cheaper and keeps the directive's parser count at one, which is the property Q142 shows is already strained.
- Accept: a `#! kind` line naming an id with no row turns a named test red; and the manifest's kind→task map and `INDEX.json`'s vector→task map are either asserted consistent or the reason they need not be is written down.
- Notes: **F31** is a dependency rather than a blocker — it is about to add a second attribute to this same directive line (`cbor=yes|no`) and Q54 extends it again, so a parser for the task token should land after the directive's shape settles, not before. Note also that this arm is lenient exactly here: D112 §1.3 measures `#! kind` as the **only** directive arm that swallows arbitrary trailing content, so `#! kind bundle F13 cbro` parses clean today.

### Q144 — `antseal-anchor` has no doc-pointer sweep, and it is the crate making cross-file claims
- Milestone: M2
- Size: S
- Deps: after Q70
- Discovered by: **the D110 lane** (2026-08-10), and confirmed in practice by the A114/A115 lane the same day.
- Problem: `doc_pointer_liveness` exists only at `crates/antseal-core/tests/doc_pointer_liveness.rs` and is crate-scoped by construction — it walks `env!("CARGO_MANIFEST_DIR")`'s `src/` and `tests/` (`:500-505`) and resolves every pointer relative to the same root. `crates/antseal-anchor` has no equivalent; its only integration test is `no_real_network.rs`. Two independent motives arrived in one wave. D110 §11 filed the general form. And the A115 lane's E5 edit existed **only** because a doc comment in `crates/antseal-anchor/src/testing/replay.rs` made a cross-file claim about the registry that another edit in the same lane was about to falsify — a claim nothing in the tree would have caught, found by a human reading a diff.
- Do: widen the sweep past `antseal-core`, which is **Q70**'s stated job: the rules and the walk are already crate-parameterised, and Q69 surveyed the other crates with zero findings, so this guards drift rather than clearing a backlog. Settle Q70's open question — a test target per crate, or one workspace-level sweep — with `antseal-anchor` as the worked case, since it is the crate that has now produced real instances.
- Accept: a doc comment in `antseal-anchor` citing a file or a test that does not exist turns a named test red; and `doc_pointer_liveness.rs`'s allowlist entry for the cross-crate `src/ots/mod.rs:126` pointer starts resolving, at which point `no_allowlist_entry_is_stale` requires it to be deleted — which is that entry's own recorded plan for this landing.
- Notes: Q70 was filed as *"guarding future drift, not clearing a backlog"* on the strength of a zero-finding survey, and that survey is now out of date: **A118**'s four stale `298x` doc comments and the E5 claim are both in `antseal-anchor` and both are what this sweep reads. **Q101** is adjacent — it records that the sweep covers `antseal-core`'s `src/` and `tests/` only.

### Q145 — A decision whose named owner closes without executing it is invisible
- Milestone: M2
- Size: S
- Deps: after Q85
- Discovered by: **the A114/A115 lane** (2026-08-10).
- Problem: a resolved decision record can name an owning task for a ruling, that task can close without discharging it, and nothing anywhere notices. The measured instance is **A121**: `docs/decisions/D104-max-ots-depth-lowering-window.md` §6 is titled *"RULING 5 — … and it is A106's"*, A106 closed 2026-08-10 having explicitly excluded that work, and no forwarding pointer was left. The consequence was not theoretical — A114's `tasks/A.md` entry, its `TODO.md` row **and** the brief that implemented it all described A114(b) as having no governing decision, while D104 §6 governed it and ruled more than any of the three said. The ruling was found by a lane reading the record for something else.
- Do: check the shape, not the instance. The machine-readable half is the first two clauses — a resolved decision naming an owner whose row is ticked — reported for a human to confirm the third. `check-traceability.py` already parses the decision register and `TODO.md`'s checkboxes, so this is a join over data it holds rather than a new surface.
- Accept: a decision record naming a closed owner for an undischarged ruling is reported by a named check; and the ruling's disposition — executed, re-owned, or declined — is recorded in the decision record, dated, per the amendment protocol.
- Notes: worth more than **A121** because the failure is silent in both directions: the decision record still reads as authoritative and the task still reads as closed, so the only way to find the gap is for someone to read the record for an unrelated reason. That is what happened here, in a wave where the ruling had already survived one lane scoped to execute part of it.

### Q146 — Four prose sites state a self-test fault count the checker no longer prints
- Milestone: M2
- Size: XS
- Deps: after Q130
- Discovered by: **the Q130 lane** (2026-08-10).
- Problem: Q130 made the CBOR self-test's summary computed from the run instead of hardcoded, so it now prints *"17 scenarios — 15 planted faults caught (7 vector mutation(s) × 2 in-scope document(s) + a wrong RFC expectation), 2 control(s) green"* where it used to print 8 planted and 1 control. Four sites still state the old count: `scripts/cross-check.sh:393` (*"8 planted faults"*), `CONTRIBUTING.md:66` (*"eight planted faults"*), `docs/ci-verification.md:1001` (*"8 planted faults and 1 control"*), and `docs/testing/cross-check.md:163` (*"8 planted faults + 1 control | red ×8, green ×1"*). **None is machine-asserted** — `cross-check.sh` reads only the checker's exit code — which is why they drifted and why they will drift again at the next mutation added.
- Do: correct the four, and prefer a form that cannot drift: the counts are computed and printed by the checker, so a site that must mention them can name where to read them instead. `scripts/cross-check.sh:393` is the one to fix first, because it misleads a reader of the lane itself.
- Accept: no committed prose states a planted-fault count that disagrees with what `--self-test` prints, and the counts are either asserted or replaced by a pointer to the run.
- Notes: recorded as an amendment to D112 (A2) because §5's edit set said `scripts/cross-check.sh` was *"not touched by either"* row, and all four sites are outside Q130's file scope — so they were correctly left alone rather than swept up, and this is where they land. The general shape is **Q136**'s: prose stating a number the tree can compute.

### Q147 — `scope_of` raises `KeyError` where it means `CheckFailure`
- Milestone: M2
- Size: XS
- Deps: after Q130
- Discovered by: **the Q130 lane** (2026-08-10).
- Problem: `testdata/vectors/v1/crosscheck_cbor.py`'s `scope_of` checks the envelope's `schema` and then indexes `vector['kind']` bare on both out-of-scope return paths (`:894`, `:902`); `run()` does the same at `:930` and `:933`. A document declaring `schema: antseal-golden-vector` and omitting `kind` therefore raises **`KeyError`, not `CheckFailure`** — it escapes `main`'s handler as a traceback instead of arriving as a diagnosis. This is the same escape class as the bare `StopIteration` Q130 closed, in the function Q130 rewrote. Pre-existing in `run`, and now also reachable from `--self-test`, which sweeps every in-scope document rather than the first.
- Do: make the lookup fall back rather than index. The same message already degrades gracefully for the neighbouring field (`sorted(expect) or 'nothing'`), so the treatment is established and costs nothing.
- Accept: a golden-vector envelope with no `kind` produces a `CheckFailure` naming the file, not a traceback; asserted by a planted document in the self-test, in the idiom the other planted faults already use.
- Notes: unreachable in the frozen tree — every committed vector has a `kind`, and `vector_freeze_records_every_registered_kind` would catch a new one that did not. Filed because the class is what matters: an exception the handler does not know about turns a diagnosable failure into a traceback, and this checker's entire value is that a human reads its output.

### Q148 — The line-123 misquotation is repository-wide; Q132 closed only the format freeze
- Milestone: M2
- Size: S
- Deps: after Q132
- Discovered by: **the Q132 lane** (2026-08-10).
- Problem: Q132 corrected three call sites in the *format* freeze — `scripts/format-freeze.sh`, `docs/format/FROZEN.sha256` and `crates/antseal-core/tests/format_freeze.rs` — so they now cite Q14's own act for byte-immutability and name `MVP-SPEC.md` line 123 as the compatibility rule the freeze exists to protect. **The same misquotation is live in at least six more places.** Line 123 still says what it said: *"every **released** manifest/bundle format version remains verifiable by all future CLI and page releases"* — a compatibility guarantee conditioned on *released*, and D104 §1.5 measures with three independent confirmations that nothing has been released. Live sites: `crates/antseal-core/tests/format_registry_freeze.rs:8` and `:1017`; `crates/antseal-core/src/manifest/registry.rs:17` and `src/bundle/registry.rs:18` (mildest — they name Q14 first); the **sibling Q6 vector freeze**, which repeats the defect exactly at `scripts/vector-freeze.sh:8`, `crates/antseal-core/tests/vector_freeze.rs:10` and `:437`, and whose authority is Q6's own act; and `crates/antseal-core/src/canon/unicode.rs:17` and `:63`, the same false-conditional shape applied to the Unicode tables.
- Do: apply Q132's correction at each — cite the freezing act as the authority for immutability (**Q14** for the wire registry, **Q6** for the vectors, and whatever act froze the Unicode tables), and cite line 123 only for compatibility. No behaviour changes at any site.
- Accept: no committed file cites `MVP-SPEC.md` line 123 as the authority for byte-immutability or for a *"format-version event"*; every such claim names the act that imposed it.
- Notes: Q132's own Notes state the stake and it transfers unchanged — *"a reader who follows the citation finds a conditional whose condition is false today and may conclude the freeze is soft"*, which is the inference D108 §3 spends a section refusing. The vector-freeze half is the worse of the two, because `vector-freeze.sh:8` cites line 167 **correctly** for the retention rule in the same breath as line 123 wrongly for immutability, so a reader sees two citations side by side with no signal that only one is right.

### Q149 — A red arm judged on exit status alone counts a crash as a proof
- Milestone: M2
- Size: S
- Deps: after Q134 (which fixed the first instance)
- Discovered by: **lane D** (2026-08-10), generalising the hole it had just closed in `scripts/check-traceability.py`.
- Problem: a self-test's *red arm* plants a fault and requires the checker to fail. If the arm judges that failure **on exit status alone**, then any nonzero exit satisfies it — a traceback, a missing file, an import error, a mutated fixture that will not parse — and the arm certifies a surface it never exercised. `check-traceability.py` was fixed this wave after a measured instance: deleting check 6's unregistered-owner branch left a case "going red" via a `KeyError` on the very lookup that branch guarded, so under exit-code-only validation that case pinned nothing. The fix is now in the tree and treats a traceback as a failure **in both directions** and additionally requires the check's own `::error::` annotation. **The fix was not propagated.** Measured over the five sibling instruments: `scripts/cross-check.sh` discards stdout *and* stderr and tests `rc` alone (`prove_can_fail`, the mutate arm and the restore arm both); `scripts/wasm-bitmatch.sh --self-test` prints `SELF-TEST PASSED: the injected divergence turned the lane red (exit ${status})` on any nonzero status, so a missing `.wasm` or a Node error passes; `scripts/vector-freeze.sh` has two red arms, both `if check_digests "${SCRATCH}" >/dev/null 2>&1`; `scripts/format-freeze.sh` has four of the same shape, of which the third **deletes** the frozen document, so any file-open error is indistinguishable from a working must-exist list.
- Do: give every red arm a second condition — the message the check prints when it finds the planted defect — in the shape `gate-features.sh` already uses (`if [ $? -eq 0 ] || ! grep -q '<expected>' <<<"$out"; then`), and make a traceback an explicit failure rather than a pass. Take the four confirmed instruments; do **not** take `check-ci-shell.py`, which is structurally immune (its arm reads a returned failure list in-process, so a crash propagates and fails the harness) and do not "fix" `gate-features.sh`, which is the in-repo template.
- Accept: each of `cross-check.sh`, `wasm-bitmatch.sh --self-test`, `vector-freeze.sh` and `format-freeze.sh` has every red arm matching on the expected message as well as the status; each is shown to **fail** when the planted fault is replaced by an unrelated crash — a deliberately broken helper, not a hypothetical — which is green today and must be red after; and the four together are recorded in one place so a fifth instrument added later inherits the rule rather than the defect.
