- Notes: **this is the third instance of one class.** Wave 12 found it in the CBOR self-test, where each fault was planted *inside the `try` that judged it*, so a mutation the harness could not plant printed *"RED as required"* and the run announced `self-test PASSED`, rc 0. Wave 13 found it in `check-traceability.py`. Five instruments were named on report and **two of the six are refuted** — the sweep's value is precisely that it measured which, rather than assuming all six. `check-ci-shell.py` has a lesser weakness worth one sentence when this is executed: its arm never checks *which* failure was raised, so an unrelated finding would satisfy a planted fault, partly compensated by its control run.
- **Row and entry authored 2026-08-10 by the wave-13 bookkeeping lane from lane D's report, verified against the tree before registering.** Verified here, not taken on report: all six named instruments were read, and the report's "almost certainly exists in six" is **corrected to four** — `gate-features.sh` already ANDs status with an expected-message grep at four arms, and `check-ci-shell.py` judges in-process on a returned list. Also verified: the `check-traceability.py` fix landed **this wave**, not in `d23dccc` (`git show d23dccc:scripts/check-traceability.py` contains zero occurrences of the traceback guard; the working tree contains one). **Authored here, not reported: the whole `Do`, the whole `Accept`, and the naming of `gate-features.sh` as the template** — the report named the class, its instances and its priority, and named no remedy.

### Q150 — `--self-test` copies the live tree and races any concurrent writer
- Milestone: M2
- Size: XS
- Deps: after Q85
- Discovered by: **lane C** (2026-08-10), when it went red in a required lane for a reason unrelated to traceability.
- Problem: `check-traceability.py --self-test` stages its mutations by `shutil.copytree`-ing the **live** repository, with `ignore=shutil.ignore_patterns(".git", "target", "node_modules")`. Another lane's atomic write created and renamed `TODO.md.tmp.<pid>.<hash>` between the copy's `scandir` and its read, and `copytree` raised `shutil.Error` naming a file that no longer existed, exit 1. The lane was green on an immediate re-run. In a parallel-lane wave this makes the `traceability` job — a required context — go red for a reason that has nothing to do with traceability, with a diagnostic naming a temp file a reader cannot find, which is the worst possible shape for a flaky failure.
- Do: add the project's atomic-write temp shape to the ignore list (`*.tmp.*` alongside `.git`, `target`, `node_modules`), or wrap the copy in a bounded retry, or both. The ignore glob is the smaller change and matches the existing mechanism; the retry additionally covers any other transient the copy can meet.
- Accept: a `copytree` run concurrent with a write to a file under the copied tree does not fail the self-test — asserted by staging a `*.tmp.*` file in the tree and running `--self-test` over it, which reproduces the failure today; and every other check stays green, so the ignore does not hide a real file from the mutation harness.
- Notes: the fix is small and the reason to file it rather than do it inline is that the self-test's whole value is that its own harness is trustworthy, so a change to what it copies wants its own before/after. Note the second-order hazard the `Do` must not create: an ignore glob wide enough to hide a real committed file would make the self-test's mutations land on a tree that is not the tree, which is a silent version of the defect it is fixing.
- **Row and entry authored 2026-08-10 by the wave-13 bookkeeping lane from lane C's report, verified against the tree before registering.** Verified here: `scripts/check-traceability.py` calls `shutil.copytree` with exactly `ignore=shutil.ignore_patterns(".git", "target", "node_modules")` — no temp-file pattern. **Not reproduced here: the race itself** — the `Accept` makes reproducing it the acceptance criterion rather than asserting it from the report. **Authored here, not reported: the `Do`'s two options, the whole `Accept`, and the second-order hazard in `Notes`.**

### Q151 — Prose describing a deleted identifier as live is caught by nothing
- Milestone: M2
- Size: XS
- Deps: after Q133
- Discovered by: **lane D** (2026-08-10), from the fallout of its own rename.
- Problem: Q133's fix collapsed five constants into three — `DECISION_SCAN`, `DECISION_SUFFIXES`, `TASK_SCAN`, `TASK_SUFFIXES` and `TASK_SCAN_SELF` were **deleted**, not aliased, in favour of `CITATION_SCAN`, `CITATION_SUFFIXES` and `CITATION_SCAN_SELF`. Prose across the tree still describes the deleted names as live constants — in live task rows and entries, and in a resolved decision's discovered-work list. Nothing catches it: `check-traceability.py` sweeps ids (`D<n>`, `<Domain><n>`) and registry line citations, and a Python identifier named in an English sentence is neither. **This is the same class as Q145 itself** — a pointer whose target nothing verifies — one namespace over, and the same class as Q148's line-123 citations a third time.
- Do: decide whether a check is warranted at all before writing one. The candidate is narrow and cheap: for a named set of load-bearing identifiers in `scripts/*.py`, assert that any prose naming them in backticks resolves to a name the module actually defines. The objection to answer first is the false-positive surface — prose legitimately names *deleted* identifiers when recording history, which is exactly what `check-traceability.py:662-663` does on purpose and correctly. So the rule cannot be "every backticked identifier must exist"; it has to distinguish a description of the present from a record of the past, and if it cannot, the answer is a convention rather than a check.
- Accept: either a check exists that is red on a prose reference to a deleted identifier and green on a historical record of one, shown on both arms; or the reason such a check cannot be written is recorded where the identifiers are defined, so the next rename does not re-derive it. The known instances are corrected either way.
- Notes: the instances found at this wave's bookkeeping were corrected in place rather than left for this row — `TODO.md`'s Q133 and Q143 rows, `tasks/Q.md`'s Q133 `Problem`/`Do` and Q143 `Problem`, and `tasks/A.md`'s A71 `Discovered by`. **Two more were found and deliberately left**: `scripts/check-traceability.py:662-663` names the deleted constants as *deleted*, which is correct and is the shape any check must not flag; and `docs/decisions/D114` §7's discovered-work bullet describes `DECISION_SCAN` and `TASK_SCAN` as live, which is a **resolved decision's body** and therefore blocked on **Q122** exactly as D115 §3.7's correction was. That D114 was falsified by a rename landing in the same wave is recorded as a dated amendment on the document instead.
- **Row and entry authored 2026-08-10 by the wave-13 bookkeeping lane from lane D's report, verified against the tree before registering.** Verified here, and the report's site list is **corrected**: it named `tasks/Q.md:1716` as Q143's `Problem` — line 1716 is **Q136's** `Problem`, about the twelve self-reported counts, and carries no such reference; Q143's `Problem` is at `tasks/Q.md:1782`. It also missed `tasks/Q.md:1682` and `:1683` (Q133's own `Problem` and `Do`). The corrected instance set is six sites in the tracker, not four. **Authored here, not reported: the whole `Do` — including its refusal to specify a check before the false-positive question is answered — the whole `Accept`, and the `Notes`.**

### Q152 — Nothing pins `MVP-SPEC.md`'s line numbering, and the citations are correct by accident
- Milestone: M2
- Size: S
- Deps: after Q148
- Discovered by: **D114** §7 (2026-08-10).
- Problem: the tree cites `MVP-SPEC.md` by line number in bulk, and the spec has **never been edited** — `git log -- MVP-SPEC.md` returns exactly one commit, `01cdc83`, the initial commit. So every `line N` citation in the repository is correct by historical accident rather than by construction, and a single inserted line above the cited one silently rots every citation below it with nothing going red. Line 123 alone is cited **189** times at `d23dccc` by D114's own regex; the wider `MVP-SPEC.md line N` class runs to about 1 500 sites. The instrument shape already exists and is proven: `crates/antseal-core/src/anchor/model.rs:1625`'s `spec_line_points_at_the_states_own_definition` reads the spec, indexes a line number and asserts its content, gated `#[cfg(not(target_arch = "wasm32"))]` — but it covers only the seven `AnchorState::ALL` taxonomy lines.
- Do: extend the existing test to a small register of load-bearing spec lines — the ones the tree cites most and relies on most heavily: 121, 123, 137, 153, 167, 168 — asserting for each that the line still contains the phrase the citations attribute to it. Keep it in `model.rs`'s shape and under its existing `cfg` gate rather than adding a new instrument; the register is a table, not a sweep, because a sweep over 1 500 sites would have to parse prose to know what each citation claims.
- Accept: the register exists and is green; mutating `MVP-SPEC.md` by inserting one line above the lowest registered line turns it **red**, naming which registered line moved — the mutation is the proof, and it must be run, because a green test over an unedited file is the definition of unfalsified; and the register states, at its definition, that it guards the recurrence vector and not the misquotation defect, which is Q148's and is closed.
- Notes: **deliberately not folded into Q148**, on D114's reasoning: it is green today, so it could not meet the *"red on a real site, green after"* bar Q148's own work was held to, and it deserves to be argued on its own terms rather than smuggled in as a rider. Note the interaction with **Q148**'s outcome: the tree now cites the *freezing act* rather than line 123 for immutability, so the number of citations depending on line 123's exact content is falling, which weakens the case for a register keyed on 123 and strengthens the case for one keyed on the lines that are genuinely load-bearing.
- **2026-08-10 — a confirmation was filed against this row by the wave-14 ledger, checked, and it belongs to a different class.** The report is that `docs/ci-verification.md` quotes the `wasm-bitmatch` self-test's old `SELF-TEST PASSED: the injected divergence turned the lane red (exit 1), as required.` under a dated **2026-07-28** section, while the script now prints a longer form that also names the message the comparator reported — the `AND the comparator reported it as "…"` half added by Q149's red-arm work. **Verified, and the disposition is right**: the quotation sits under a dated heading recording a past run, so under Q146's ruling it is *supposed* to disagree with today's output and rewriting it would claim that run printed something it did not. Deliberately not edited. **But the subject is not this row's**: this row is about `MVP-SPEC.md` line numbers being correct by historical accident, and the report is about a quoted *message* going stale — **Q146's** class, filed here by the ledger under the wrong id. Recorded rather than acted on, and **no action follows for this row**. One thing the check did surface and nobody has: unlike `ci-verification.md`'s other dated record of this kind, this section carries **no appended superseding note**, which is the discharge Q146 identified and demonstrated elsewhere in the same file.
- **Row and entry authored 2026-08-10 by the wave-13 bookkeeping lane from D114 §7's discovered-work list, verified against the tree before registering.** Verified here: the 189 figure reproduces exactly at `d23dccc` (`git grep -InE 'MVP-SPEC\.md line 123|MVP-SPEC\.md:123|spec line 123|line 123|line-123' HEAD | wc -l` → 189) and it is a **line-123-specific** count, not a count of all line citations — a reader of the ledger sentence *"189 citations depend on it"* would otherwise take it for the latter; `spec_line_points_at_the_states_own_definition` exists at `model.rs:1625`; `MVP-SPEC.md` has one commit. **Authored here, not transcribed: the specific line register in the `Do`, the whole `Accept`, and the second `Notes` sentence** — D114 §7 named the defect, the instrument shape and the candidate lines (123, 167, 121, 137, 168) and named no acceptance criterion.

### Q153 — Five gate lanes have never run on the remote
- Milestone: M2
- Size: M
- Deps: after Q125, Q141
- Discovered by: **D116** §1.4 (2026-08-10).
- Problem: `docs/ci-verification.md` states its own rule — *"**A lane that has never run on the remote is not evidence, no matter how long it has been committed.** Neither is a guard whose own command has never been executed."* Five lanes in `scripts/local-gate.sh` violate it, and nothing in the tree says so: `gate-features.sh --self-test`, `--check-partition` and `--heavy` (`grep -rn 'gate-features' .github/` returns **zero hits**, so the HEAVY features are compiled by no CI lane at all), `wasm-bitmatch.sh --trigger-self-test` (CI runs `--self-test` and the bare lane, neither of which is the trigger self-test), and `e2e-devnet.sh --self-test` (whose only remote venue runs the script bare — **Q154**). The gate header enumerates the opposite direction — CI steps the gate does not run — and is silent on this one.
- Do: move the three that are seconds and cargo-free onto the remote, on the existing `traceability` job so they add **zero** new required contexts: `gate-features.sh --self-test`, `gate-features.sh --check-partition`, and `wasm-bitmatch.sh --trigger-self-test`. Rule the other two separately and record the ruling: `--heavy` is a compile of the heavy tier and is a real cost, and `e2e-devnet.sh --self-test` belongs beside the cron lane Q154 fixes. Whatever is not moved gets a written reason at the gate header, so the asymmetry is declared rather than discovered a sixth time.
- Accept: the three cheap lanes run on the remote and are seen green there — a remote run id recorded in `docs/ci-verification.md`, per that document's own evidence rule, not a local run asserted to be equivalent; the required-context count is unchanged; and `scripts/local-gate.sh`'s header enumerates both directions, so the next reader learns of a never-remote lane from the gate rather than from a decision record.
- Notes: this is **Q125**'s shape a third and fourth time and in the direction nobody had looked — Q125, Q128 and Q141 all ran *"CI has it, the gate does not"*; this is *"the gate has it, CI does not"*, which is worse, because the gate is the surface a contributor trusts before pushing. D116 measured 35 comparison points and found **15 CI steps absent from the gate, 5 gate lanes absent from CI, 5 argument divergences and 3 undeclared classes**; four further argument divergences were measured **not** substantive (`fmt`, `clippy`, `test`, `wasm-bitmatch`) and must not be re-litigated.
- **Row and entry authored 2026-08-10 by the wave-13 bookkeeping lane from D116 §1.4's discovered-work list, verified against the tree before registering.** Verified here: all five lanes appear in `scripts/local-gate.sh` (`:256`, `:257`, `:316`, `:235`, `:336`) and none appears in `.github/workflows/`; the quoted rule is at `docs/ci-verification.md:1076-1082`; `local-gate.sh:45-60` already carries the gap in its own ledger. **Authored here, not transcribed: the split of the five into three-and-two, the whole `Accept`, and the requirement that the evidence be a remote run id** — D116 named the five, the violation and the "seconds and cargo-free" observation, and named no acceptance criterion.

### Q154 — The only venue that runs the devnet E2E lane runs it with its test-of-the-test off
- Milestone: M2
- Size: XS
- Deps: after Q141
- Discovered by: **D116** §1.4 (2026-08-10).
- Problem: `.github/workflows/devnet-e2e-cron.yml:119` is `run: ./scripts/e2e-devnet.sh` — **bare**. The step's own comment two lines above claims *"The same script bytes the local gate runs (the ci-lanes.sh rule)"*, which is true of the bytes and false of the invocation: `local-gate.sh:336` runs `e2e-devnet.sh --self-test` **unconditionally** and runs it bare only under `ANTSEAL_GATE_E2E=1`. So the only venue that runs the **lane** runs it without its test-of-the-test, while the gate runs the test-of-the-test and — in the ordinary case — not the lane. This is **Q141**'s shape exactly, inverted: Q141 was the gate running the half that does not select, and this is CI running the half that is not self-checked.
- Do: run `--self-test` in the cron workflow as a separate step before the bare lane, matching how `ci.yml` runs the cross-check's two halves as separate steps — the shape the 2026-08-09 incident already proved is the one that catches things; and correct the step comment so it describes the invocation rather than the file.
- Accept: the cron workflow runs `e2e-devnet.sh --self-test` and then the lane, as two steps; the comment claiming byte-identity with the local gate either becomes true of the invocation or stops claiming it; and this is confirmed by a remote run, not by reading the YAML, because `docs/ci-verification.md`'s rule applies to this change as much as to the lanes it fixes.
- Notes: cheap and mechanical, and filed separately from **Q153** because it is a workflow edit with a scheduled venue that already exists, where Q153's three lanes need a home found for them. Both are the same underlying fact — the gate and CI have never been compared in the CI-is-missing direction — and D116 is the record that compared them.
- **Row and entry authored 2026-08-10 by the wave-13 bookkeeping lane from D116 §1.4's discovered-work list, verified against the tree before registering.** Verified here: `devnet-e2e-cron.yml:119` is exactly `        run: ./scripts/e2e-devnet.sh`, the claim-comment is at `:110-112`, and `local-gate.sh:336`/`:338` are as described. **Authored here, not transcribed: the whole `Do`, the whole `Accept`, and the `Notes`.**

### Q155 — The gate runs proptest at a quarter of CI's case count, undeclared
- Milestone: M2
- Size: XS
- Deps: after Q141
- Discovered by: **D116** §1.5 (2026-08-10).
- Problem: `.github/workflows/ci.yml:152` sets `PROPTEST_CASES: "1024"` on the `test` job. `scripts/local-gate.sh` sets nothing, so the gate runs proptest at the library default of **256** — a quarter of the remote's coverage, with nothing announcing the difference at the point a contributor reads the gate's result. A gate-green, CI-red on a property that needs more than 256 cases to find is therefore expected behaviour that will read as flakiness.
- Do: decide whether an `ANTSEAL_GATE_PROPTEST` opt-in belongs beside the four that already exist (`ANTSEAL_GATE_WASM`, `ANTSEAL_GATE_BITMATCH`, `ANTSEAL_GATE_HEAVY`, `ANTSEAL_GATE_E2E`) — all four of which follow the same `auto`/`1`/`0` shape with a printed reason — and, whichever way that goes, **declare the divergence at the gate header**, which is the part that is not optional. The gate header already enumerates what it does not run; a case count it runs differently belongs in the same list.
- Accept: `scripts/local-gate.sh`'s header states the proptest case count it runs and the count CI runs; and either the opt-in exists and is exercised once at 1024, or the reason it does not exist is recorded beside the four that do.
- Notes: one of **three undeclared classes** D116 found in a 35-point comparison; the other two are the five never-remote lanes (**Q153**) and the bare cron invocation (**Q154**). `local-gate.sh:67` already mentions `PROPTEST_CASES` and `ci.yml:152` in a comment, so the fact is not unknown to the file — it is simply not in the header a reader consults, and not in the result a reader believes.
- **2026-08-10 — the *"already declared"* report is checked and does not discharge this row.** `scripts/local-gate.sh`'s top-of-file header comment block carries a `PROPTEST_CASES` entry stating that `ci.yml` sets 1024 on the `test` job, that this script sets nothing so the lane runs proptest's default of **256**, and that *"a green `test` here has explored a QUARTER of the cases CI will"*, citing `docs/testing/proptest-conventions.md` §4. Measured: that text is **byte-identical to the text at HEAD** and has only moved position as the header above it grew — it is not work done this wave. **This entry's `Problem` already considered that text and rejected it** as *"not in the header a reader consults, and not in the result a reader believes"*, so whether the non-optional half is discharged is a live disagreement between the report and this row's author, and is **left open rather than closed by note**. What is not in dispute: the `ANTSEAL_GATE_PROPTEST` opt-in decision is still owed.
- **Row and entry authored 2026-08-10 by the wave-13 bookkeeping lane from D116 §1.5's discovered-work list, verified against the tree before registering.** Verified here: `ci.yml:152` sets `PROPTEST_CASES: "1024"`; no `PROPTEST_CASES` assignment exists in `scripts/`; the four existing opt-ins are `ANTSEAL_GATE_WASM`, `ANTSEAL_GATE_BITMATCH`, `ANTSEAL_GATE_HEAVY` and `ANTSEAL_GATE_E2E` (`ANTSEAL_GATE_BASE` is a base-ref override, not a lane opt-in, and is not the fifth). **Authored here, not transcribed: the whole `Accept`, and the `Do`'s insistence that the declaration is mandatory whatever is decided about the opt-in** — D116 posed the opt-in as an open question and named no acceptance criterion.

### Q156 — `.github/` and `.yml` are outside the citation sweep, measured and deliberately not taken
- Milestone: M2
- Size: XS
- Deps: after Q133
- Discovered by: **D116** §1.6 (2026-08-10).
- Problem: `CITATION_SCAN = ["crates", "docs/format", "docs/testing", "scripts"]` and `CITATION_SUFFIXES` has no `.yml`, so the workflows — committed, normative, and the venue every required context runs in — are swept by neither the decision half nor the task half. D116 measured the widening: adding `.github/` and `.yml` is **green** and adds one citation. It was deliberately not taken, so that Q133's change was the single constant it claimed to be rather than a constant plus a scope decision.
- Do: rule whether the workflows are a citation surface, and if so widen both halves in **one** edit. The trap is specific and D116 named it: Q133 collapsed five constants into three precisely so the two sweeps could not disagree, and a widening applied to one half re-splits them — so the change is to `CITATION_SCAN`/`CITATION_SUFFIXES` themselves and nothing else, or it is not made.
- Accept: either the workflows are swept — with the measured +1 citation resolving, both halves moving in one commit, and a planted `D<n>`/`<Domain><n>` in a `.yml` turning the lane red — or the reason they are out is recorded at the constants, in the shape the `docs/decisions/` exclusion already has there.
- Notes: cheap, measured green, and filed rather than done for a reason worth keeping: a scope widening bundled into an unrelated change is how the two constants diverged in the first place. Note that widening to `.yml` does **not** reach root files, which is a separate hole (**Q176**) with a separate fix — the scan roots are subdirectory names and `rglob` from `ROOT/<sub>` never sees `ROOT/CHANGELOG.md`.
- **Row and entry authored 2026-08-10 by the wave-13 bookkeeping lane from D116 §1.6's discovered-work list, verified against the tree before registering.** Verified here: the constants at `scripts/check-traceability.py:506-507` contain neither `.github` nor `.yml`, and both sweeps (`:581-586` and `:815-820`) iterate the same pair, so the re-split hazard the `Do` names is real. **Not re-measured here: the "+1 citation" figure**, which is D116's and is taken on report; the `Accept` makes it a before/after for whoever executes. **Authored here, not transcribed: the whole `Accept` and the `Notes`.**

### Q157 — A third and a fourth directory walk, each with its own rule
- Milestone: M2
- Size: S
- Deps: after Q140
- Discovered by: **D116** §1.7 (2026-08-10), while measuring Q140's blast radius.
- Problem: Q140 found **two** directory walks over `testdata/vectors/` carrying the identical fatal classification rule — `crates/wasm-bitmatch/build.rs` and `crates/antseal-core/tests/vector_runner.rs` — and its fix put one list behind both with a byte-identity test. There are more walks. `crates/antseal-core/tests/vector_freeze.rs`'s `collect_json` recurses the same tree with a **third** rule, and `crates/antseal-core/tests/vector_index.rs` walks it twice more (`:111-114` over the version directories, `:124-130` recursively) with rules of their own. `collect_json` is immune to Q140's `__pycache__` fault **by accident, not by decision**: it filters *positively* for `.json` instead of asserting a closed classification, so an unknown file is skipped rather than fatal. The tolerant shape is already in the tree; nothing says it is the intended one, and nothing relates the walks.
- Do: bring the remaining walks under the same discipline Q140 established — one shared list of ignorable droppings, derived from `.gitignore` rather than restated — or record, at each walk, why its rule legitimately differs from the others. Do **not** convert `collect_json`'s positive filter into a closed classification: the positive filter is the shape that survived Q140 and the point is to make its tolerance deliberate, not to make it strict.
- Accept: every directory walk over `testdata/vectors/` either shares the one classification list or carries a written reason for its own; adding a git-ignored dropping to the tree leaves every walk green, asserted by planting one — which is what Q140 proved fatal for two of them; and the count of walks is stated somewhere, so a fifth is added knowingly.
- Notes: **the report's claim about `read_frozen` is refuted and this row is narrower than it was described.** `vector_index.rs:151`'s `read_frozen` is not a directory walk at all — it reads `FROZEN.sha256` and parses its lines — so it is a second *parser of the manifest*, which is exactly **Q142**'s subject and must not be re-registered here. The two genuine extra walks in that file are at `:111-114` and `:124-130`.
- **Row and entry authored 2026-08-10 by the wave-13 bookkeeping lane from D116 §1.7's discovered-work list, verified against the tree before registering.** Verified here: `collect_json` at `vector_freeze.rs:276` recurses on `path.is_dir()` and filters on `ends_with(".json")`; `vector_index.rs` calls `fs::read_dir` at `:111` and `:124` with an `is_dir()` recursion at `:130`; and `read_frozen` at `:151` calls `fs::read_to_string` on `FROZEN_NAME` and walks nothing. **Authored here, not transcribed: the whole `Do` — including its instruction *not* to make `collect_json` strict — the whole `Accept`, and the `Notes` correction.**

### Q158 — A cross-check that can only run when its subject has already succeeded
- Milestone: M3
- Size: S
- Deps: after Q140
- Discovered by: **D116** §1.7 (2026-08-10).
- Problem: `crates/wasm-bitmatch/tests/bitmatch.rs` holds a test asserting that the crate's two discovery mechanisms agree about what the vector set is. It **cannot see the divergence it exists to catch**, because `crates/wasm-bitmatch/build.rs` panics on an unclassifiable file and a `build.rs` panic precedes every test in that crate — so if the two rules disagree about a file, the build dies before the agreement test is compiled, let alone run. The instrument is downstream of the thing it checks. The general shape is what makes this a row rather than a fix: **a cross-check that only runs when its subject has already succeeded proves nothing about the case where its subject fails**, and that shape is not specific to this crate.
- Do: sweep for the shape and record what it finds before fixing anything. A candidate is any assertion whose precondition is the success of the mechanism it compares against — build-script-gated tests, self-tests that run after the lane they validate, and `--check`-then-`--self-test` orderings. For the measured instance, decide whether the agreement belongs somewhere that runs regardless of the build (a script, or a test in a crate the `build.rs` does not gate) or whether the build panic itself should carry the comparison.
- Accept: the sweep's result is recorded — the set of cross-checks whose precondition is their subject's success, with a judgement on each; and for `bitmatch.rs`'s instance specifically, a classification-rule divergence is **observed** turning something red, which is impossible today.
- Notes: filed at M3 because the cost of being wrong is a blind instrument rather than a wrong artifact, and because the sweep is the deliverable and the sweep is not urgent. Closely related to **Q149** in spirit — both are instruments that certify a surface they never exercised — but the mechanism is different: Q149's arms run and misread the result, and this one does not run at all.
- **Row and entry authored 2026-08-10 by the wave-13 bookkeeping lane from D116 §1.7's discovered-work list, verified against the tree before registering.** Verified here: `crates/wasm-bitmatch/build.rs` panics on `"unclassifiable file under vectors/{version}/"` at `:220` and on eight further conditions, and `bitmatch.rs:422` already records that *"if they disagree the stricter one fails the build (Q140). Edit both or neither."* **Not verified here: that the agreement test is the only such instance in the crate** — the sweep is the row's own work. **Authored here, not transcribed: the whole `Do`, the whole `Accept` and the `Notes`** — D116 named the instance and said the general shape *"is worth sweeping for"*.

### Q160 — A recorded cost that does not name what was timed
- Milestone: M2
- Size: XS
- Deps: after Q141
- Discovered by: **D116** §2 (2026-08-10), from a cost figure it had just measured to be wrong by ~12x.
- Problem: `TODO.md`'s Q141 row said *"Measured cost ~4 s"* and `docs/decisions/D112` §8.5 said *"The self-test costs ~4 s locally"*. Neither named the subject. The 4 s figure was `crosscheck_cbor.py --self-test`, measured at **0.10 s**; the thing Q141 actually proposed running was `cross-check.sh --self-test`, measured at **55.66 s then 46.41 s** against `--check`'s 24.63/22.48 s. So a 46-second lane was planned against a figure belonging to a different command, and the row's own cost argument was wrong by more than an order of magnitude in the direction that makes the change look free. The lane landed anyway, on a better argument — 46 s fires planted faults on six surfaces, five of which no local lane exercises — but the argument had to be rebuilt from scratch at planning time.
- Do: adopt a convention that a recorded cost names its subject — the exact command, the host, and the number of samples — and apply it where costs are already recorded: the gate's per-lane comments, decision records' cost sections, and task rows that argue from a measured cost. This is a writing rule, not a check; the `Do` is to write it down where cost figures are produced, which is `scripts/local-gate.sh`'s header and the decision-record template.
- Accept: the convention is stated in one normative place; the two known instances are corrected or superseded; and `scripts/local-gate.sh`'s existing cost comments — which already do this well, naming the command, the host and that a figure is a **single sample** — are cited as the model rather than a new form being invented.
- Notes: filed because the failure mode is specific and repeatable: a cost figure with no subject attaches itself to whatever the reader is thinking about, and the reader is thinking about the expensive thing. `local-gate.sh:221` already carries the antidote in its own voice — *"'32 s lane + 53 s self-test' is a SINGLE SAMPLE — one run, one host"* — so the house style exists and is simply not required anywhere.
- **Row and entry authored 2026-08-10 by the wave-13 bookkeeping lane from D116 §2's discovered-work list, verified against the tree before registering.** Verified here: `docs/decisions/D112-cbor-cross-check-scope-rule.md:955` reads *"The self-test costs ~4 s locally."*; `TODO.md`'s Q141 row reads *"Measured cost ~4 s"*; `local-gate.sh:359-364` now records the corrected figures with their subjects named. **Not re-measured here: the 55.66/46.41/24.63/22.48/0.10 s timings**, which are D116's, taken on report and now committed in `local-gate.sh`'s own comment. **Authored here, not transcribed: the whole `Do`, the whole `Accept`, and the `Notes`.**

### Q161 — No check that a resolved decision names an owner at all
- Milestone: M2
- Size: S
- Deps: after Q145
- Discovered by: **D113** §8 (2026-08-10), sharpened by lane D's re-measurement.
- Problem: `--decision-owners` landed under Q145 and checks that every `**Owner: <ID>**` line in a resolved decision names a registered task whose row names the decision back. It says nothing about decisions that name **no owner at all** — and a decision with no owner is the more common case and the one that produced Q145's motivating instance, since a ruling nobody owns cannot be orphaned by its owner closing because it never had one. A *presence* check on the front-matter field is a separate instrument with a separate domain. The domain is contested: two independent censuses disagreed with each other and both disagree with the tree, which is itself the argument for measuring it once, in code, where it stays measured.
- Do: settle the census first, then decide whether the check is worth building. The census has to be spelling-aware — see **Q162** — and its result determines everything: if the no-owner set is small it is a backlog to drain with a hard-fail check afterwards; if it is large the check has no green state and cannot be a required lane, and D113's own finding applies, that a monotonically non-decreasing report has no drain and D109's date-locked register cannot be transplanted onto it.
- Accept: the no-owner set is measured once, in a script rather than in prose, with the spellings it counts named; and either a presence check exists with a stated backlog mechanism and is green, or the reason a presence check cannot have a green state is recorded where `--decision-owners` is defined.
- Notes: **every figure this row was filed with is wrong, which is the point.** D113 §8 sized the domain at 27 of 94 and named D96 and D98 as carrying no owner field; lane D re-measured it at 43. Measured at this wave's bookkeeping: there are **99** `*.md` files in `docs/decisions/` (98 D-documents plus `README.md`), not 94; **46** carry neither `- **Owner:` nor `- **Owning tasks:`; and only **26** carry none of the **four** spellings that actually exist. **D96 and D98 both carry an owner field**, in the `- **Owner**:` spelling that a `- **Owner:` regex misses — which is almost certainly how they came to be named as counterexamples.
- **Row and entry authored 2026-08-10 by the wave-13 bookkeeping lane from D113 §8 and lane D's report, both of which it refutes.** Verified here: `ls -1 docs/decisions/*.md | wc -l` → 99; `grep -l -- '^- \*\*Owner:'` → 12; `grep -l -- '- \*\*Owning tasks:'` → 41; `grep -L` for both → 46; the four-spelling no-owner set → 26; `docs/decisions/D96-pending-case-fixtures-after-a21.md:4` and `D98-status-anchor-state-vocabulary.md:4` both read `- **Owner**:`. **Authored here, not transcribed: the whole `Do` — including its refusal to specify a check before the census is settled — and the whole `Accept`.** Neither source proposed an acceptance criterion.

### Q162 — Four owner-field spellings coexist with no rule about which is correct
- Milestone: M2
- Size: XS
- Deps: after Q145
- Discovered by: **D113** §1.2 (2026-08-10), whose own count of them was wrong.
- Problem: `docs/decisions/` uses four different spellings for the same field, and nothing says which is house style: `- **Owner:` (12 documents), `- **Owner**:` (2), `- **Owning task:` (18, singular), `- **Owning tasks:` (41). Every census of the corpus therefore depends on which spellings the counter happened to think of, and two censuses this wave disagreed with each other and with the tree for exactly that reason. It also has a functional cost: `--decision-owners` parses one anchored form, so a decision using another spelling is silently outside the check that exists to police ownership.
- Do: pick one spelling, record it where decision documents are written, and convert the corpus. Whether the two senses currently conflated — a single owning **task** for a ruling, versus a related-tasks **bibliography** — should share one field name or be split into two is the substantive question inside this one, and it must be answered before the conversion, because `- **Owning tasks:**` is used as a bibliography (D104's names six, including one that *"does not move"*) while `- **Owner:`'s anchored per-ruling form is what `--decision-owners` reads.
- Accept: one spelling per sense is stated normatively; the corpus uses only those spellings; and a census run after the conversion returns the same number whichever of the historical regexes is used, which is the property that does not hold today.
- Notes: the conversion touches **resolved** decision bodies, so it is gated on **Q122** exactly as D115 §3.7's correction was — unless the ruling there is that a purely mechanical field rename is not a "correction" in the sense Q122 is about, which is itself worth Q122 answering. Note the trap D8 already sets: `docs/decisions/D8-wire-registry-final.md`'s open item **C3** instructs a lane to *delete* the string `Owner:` from a tail, so a widened regex over that corpus will read an instruction to remove an owner as an owner assignment. `scripts/check-traceability.py` already plants that exact string as a **green** case for this reason.
- **Row and entry authored 2026-08-10 by the wave-13 bookkeeping lane from D113 §1.2, whose figures it refutes.** Verified here: `grep -rho -- '^- \*\*Own[a-z ]*[:*]*' docs/decisions/*.md | sort | uniq -c` returns the four spellings with counts 12 / 2 / 18 / 33+8 (41 combined). D113's *"`- **Owner:` in 12 documents, `- **Owning tasks:` in 55"* is right on the first and wrong on the second (41), and its *"two spellings"* is wrong outright. **Authored here, not transcribed: the whole `Do` — including the one-field-or-two question — the whole `Accept`, and both `Notes` paragraphs.**

### Q163 — Thirty-one open checkbox items live inside a RESOLVED decision
- Milestone: M2
- Size: XS
- Deps: after Q122
- Discovered by: **D113** §1.4 (2026-08-10), whose count of them was low by 24.
- Problem: `docs/decisions/D8-wire-registry-final.md` is marked `Status: RESOLVED` and carries **31** open `- [ ]` checkbox items — A=2, B=10, C=7, D=7, E=5 — which are follow-up work items written in imperative form inside a decision record. They are invisible to every tracker check: `check-traceability.py` reads `TODO.md`'s rows and the decision register, and `docs/decisions/` is deliberately outside the citation sweep, so a work item written as a checkbox in a decision body is registered nowhere and owned by nobody. Corpus-wide there are 36 checkbox lines in `docs/decisions/` (33 open, 3 checked), of which D8 holds 31.
- Do: decide what an open checkbox in a resolved decision means, and then either convert them to rows or mark them as historical. The three outcomes are all defensible and the row does not pick one: they are real outstanding work (rows), they are a record of what the decision considered (struck or annotated as such), or they are already done and the boxes were never ticked (ticked, with evidence). What is not defensible is leaving 31 imperatives in a document whose header says the question is closed.
- Accept: `docs/decisions/` holds no open checkbox in a document marked RESOLVED without a recorded disposition; each of D8's 31 is dispositioned; and if the outcome includes a check, it is red on a planted open checkbox in a resolved document and green on the corpus after.
- Notes: gated on **Q122** for the same reason as **Q162** — the disposition is an edit to a resolved decision's body. One of the 31 is a live trap for **Q161**/**Q162**: `D8:1492-1493` reads *"drop the 'Owner: D8 with F8/F9/R' tail"*, so any widened owner regex reads an instruction to delete an owner as an owner assignment.
- **Row and entry authored 2026-08-10 by the wave-13 bookkeeping lane from D113 §1.4, whose count it refutes.** Verified here: `grep -c -- '^- \[ \]' docs/decisions/D8-wire-registry-final.md` → **31**, not 7; the letter breakdown is A=2/B=10/C=7/D=7/E=5, so the report's "7" is the C group alone; `grep -rE -- '^- \[[ x]\]' docs/decisions/*.md | wc -l` → **36**, which confirms the corpus figure if it means open **and** checked (33 open alone); `D8:3` reads `- **Status: RESOLVED`; and `D8:1492-1493` carries the `Owner:` deletion instruction verbatim. **Authored here, not transcribed: the whole `Do` — including its refusal to pick among the three dispositions, per D115 §3.6 — and the whole `Accept`.**

### Q164 — Four resolved decisions are cited by no task row
- Milestone: M3
- Size: XS
- Deps: after Q145
- Discovered by: **D113** §8 (2026-08-10).
- Problem: `D2`, `D3`, `D4` and `D76` appear nowhere in `TODO.md` above the Decision register — not in any milestone task row, not in any `after:` clause. Their only appearances are their own register rows and the change log. Three of them are early infrastructure decisions whose consumers have long since shipped, so a missing back-citation costs nothing; **D76 is a format decision** (the file-salt split) and is the one worth a look, because a format decision that no row cites is a format rule with no task claiming to implement it.
- Do: check each of the four for a real consumer and, where one exists, add the back-citation to that row's `Deps` or entry. Where no consumer exists because the decision is spent, record that it is spent rather than adding a citation for symmetry — the goal is that a reader can find the work a decision governs, not that every decision has a citation.
- Accept: each of D2, D3, D4 and D76 either is cited by the row that implements it or carries a recorded note that its work is complete and no row remains; and D76 specifically is traced to whichever F-domain row implements the file-salt split, or to the fact that it has none.
- Notes: filed at M3 because three of the four are certainly harmless and the fourth is a one-look check, and because the general shape — *"a decision citation is a pointer with no reverse index"* — is what `--decision-owners` already began to address from the other end. Note the subtlety in "cited by no task row": all four **are** named in `tasks/*.md`, but D2/D3/D4 only inside `tasks/P.md`'s `## Open decisions (P)` appendix, which is a decision list and not a task entry, and D76 only as narrative prose inside Q53/Q58/Q65. Under a strict *"cited by an owning task"* reading the claim holds for all four; under a plain *"appears in tasks/\*.md"* reading it fails for all four, and the row means the strict one.
- **Row and entry authored 2026-08-10 by the wave-13 bookkeeping lane from D113 §8's discovered-work list, verified against the tree before registering.** Verified here: for each of D2, D3, D4, D76, `awk 'NR<655 && /^- \[[ x]\] \*\*/' TODO.md | grep -cE '\bD<n>\b'` → 0, and `awk 'NR<655' TODO.md | grep -cE` → 0, so none appears anywhere in `TODO.md` before the register at `:655`. Their `tasks/*.md` appearances were located and are as described in `Notes`. **Authored here, not transcribed: the whole `Do`, the whole `Accept`, and the `Notes` disambiguation of "cited by no task row"**, which the source did not draw and without which the row is ambiguous.

### Q165 — `--matrix --milestone M2` names eleven deferred rows and nobody has ever typed the flag
- Milestone: M2
- Size: S
- Deps: after Q85
- Discovered by: **D115** §8 (2026-08-10).
- Problem: `check-traceability.py --matrix --milestone M2` fails with **11** rows at or before M2 reading `deferred`: `V2.3`, `V3.5`, `V6.1`–`V6.6`, `V7.1`–`V7.3`. **Six of them are M1 rows** — `V6.1` through `V6.6` — so M1 shipped with either six uncovered verification bullets or six stale matrix rows, and nobody knows which. This is invisible in normal operation: `ACCEPTED_NON_COVERED` is empty, `CURRENT_MILESTONE` is `"M0"`, and every lane runs the checker with no flags, so `--milestone` defaults to M0 and the M0 rows all read `covered`. The failure only appears when someone types a flag no runbook tells them to type.
- Do: adjudicate the eleven — for each, is the work missing or is the row stale — and then close the invisibility, which is the durable half. `CURRENT_MILESTONE = "M0"` has been wrong since M1 shipped; moving it is what makes the eleven visible to the ordinary run, and it must not move until the eleven are dispositioned, because it turns a required lane red.
- Accept: every one of the eleven reads `covered`, or is registered in `ACCEPTED_NON_COVERED` with the written reason that register demands, or is struck as stale; `CURRENT_MILESTONE` names the milestone actually under review; and `python3 scripts/check-traceability.py` with **no flags** is green afterwards, which is the run every lane actually performs.
- Notes: the six M1 rows are the sharp end — M1's gate was declared passed, and six of its own matrix rows say `deferred`. `V7.2` is also **A105**'s row, so that one has an owner already; the other ten do not. `ACCEPTED_NON_COVERED` being empty means nothing is currently exempted, so the eleven are eleven live claims that work is missing.
- **Row and entry authored 2026-08-10 by the wave-13 bookkeeping lane from D115 §8's discovered-work list, verified against the tree before registering.** Verified here by running it: `python3 scripts/check-traceability.py --matrix --milestone M2` fails with exactly 11 problems naming exactly those row ids at `docs/testing/verification-matrix.md:67,77,102-107,116-118`; `ACCEPTED_NON_COVERED: dict[str, tuple[str, str]] = {}` at `:256`; `CURRENT_MILESTONE = "M0"` at `:244`. **Authored here, not transcribed: the whole `Do`, the whole `Accept`, and the ordering constraint that `CURRENT_MILESTONE` must not move first** — D115 §8 named the counts and the invisibility and named no remedy.

### Q166 — `Size`, `Milestone` and `Deps` live in a row and an entry with nothing comparing them
- Milestone: M3
- Size: S
- Deps: after Q134, Q135
- Discovered by: **D115** §8 and **lane C** (2026-08-10), independently and one field apart.
- Problem: three values are written twice by different hands. `Milestone` and `Size` appear in the `TODO.md` row and again in the `tasks/*.md` entry, and nothing checks that the two agree — they are the only scalars in the tracker with two homes and no comparison. `Deps` is the third and is worse: the row's `after:` clause and the entry's `Deps:` line state the same ordering in free-form prose, so equality cannot compare them even in principle. Twelve `Deps` lines were transcribed by hand at Q134 and nothing checked one of them. Two homes for one value is this project's most-found defect, and D115 §3.2 already wrote the rule that the entry carries the row's value verbatim — the rule exists and the enforcement does not.
- Do: check the two comparable fields and rule on the third. `Milestone` and `Size` are exact string equality between the row and the `### <ID> — ` entry and cost a parse the checker already performs. `Deps` cannot be compared by equality; the options are to make the entry's `Deps` a machine-readable list mirroring the row's `after:`, to declare the row authoritative and the entry's line advisory in prose, or to leave it and say so — the row does not pick, per D115 §3.6.
- Accept: `Milestone` and `Size` disagreeing between a row and its entry turns a named check red, shown by planting one disagreement of each; the check is green over the tree at the time it lands, or its backlog is registered in the shape `ROWS_PENDING_ENTRY` already uses; and the `Deps` question is ruled with its reason recorded where `Deps` is defined.
- Notes: this is the natural successor to Q134/Q135 and is filed at M3 for that reason — it wants the entry set complete before it is worth enforcing agreement across it. D115 §3.2's rule already anticipates the answer for a *re-estimate*: it goes in `Notes` as a dated observation and the field does not change, which is what A108's entry does (row says `S`, measured remainder is `XS`, field stays `S`) and which any equality check must therefore permit.
- **Row and entry authored 2026-08-10 by the wave-13 bookkeeping lane from D115 §8 and lane C's report, verified against the tree before registering.** Verified here: `scripts/check-traceability.py` contains no comparison of `Size` or `Milestone` between a row and an entry — the only occurrence of the word is inside a self-test fixture string; and the twelve entries lane C wrote all carry a `Deps:` line transcribed from a row `after:` clause in prose. **Authored here, not transcribed: the whole `Do`, the whole `Accept`, and the D115 §3.2 interaction in `Notes`** — both sources named the defect and named no remedy.

### Q167 — D115 contradicts itself on where the provenance bullet goes
- Milestone: M2
- Size: XS
- Deps: after Q134
- Discovered by: **lane C** (2026-08-10), which had to choose between the two and said so.
- Problem: **D115 §3.5** states the provenance line's *"exact form"* and places it **immediately after `Accept`**. **D115 §4**'s two reconstructed blocks — the worked examples the same decision provides — place it **last, after `Notes`**. A lane implementing the ruling therefore has two normative instructions from one document. Lane C followed §4 for all twelve entries. Twenty more entries will follow this convention when **Q135** drains the eight closed rows, so the convention is about to be set by precedent rather than by ruling, in a document that rules both ways.
- Do: settle it. The tree currently reads one way — all twelve entries put the bullet last — so the cheap resolution is to ratify §4's placement and correct §3.5, which makes the document agree with itself and with the tree in one edit. The alternative, ratifying §3.5, costs twelve edits and gains an argument about reading order that §3.5 does not actually make. Whichever is chosen, it is recorded before Q135 runs, not after.
- Accept: D115 states one placement; every entry in `tasks/*.md` carrying a provenance bullet uses it; and Q135's brief cites the settled placement rather than the section number, so its lane does not re-derive the contradiction.
- Notes: gated on **Q122** in the strict reading — correcting §3.5 is an edit to a resolved decision's body — which is why this row exists rather than the correction simply being made. If Q122 rules that a dated amendment is permitted, this is one amendment and one sentence; if it rules that only the register row moves, the ratification lands in the register row and the body's contradiction stands with a pointer to it.
- **Row and entry authored 2026-08-10 by the wave-13 bookkeeping lane from lane C's report, verified against the tree before registering.** Verified here: `docs/decisions/D115-entryless-row-reconstruction.md:385` (§3.5) reads *"a dated, bolded top-level bullet, immediately after `Accept`"*, while §4's blocks place it after `Notes`; and all twelve entries lane C wrote — A72, A74, A76, A105, A108, Q88, Q89, Q122, Q123, R60, R76, U39 — carry the bullet after `Notes`, measured by parsing each entry's field order. **Authored here, not transcribed: the whole `Do`, the whole `Accept`, and the Q122 gating in `Notes`** — lane C named the contradiction, its own choice, and the Q135 deadline.

### Q168 — The "D94 §2a" correction is the thing that is wrong
- Milestone: M2
- Size: XS
- Deps: after Q134
- Discovered by: **the wave-13 bookkeeping lane** (2026-08-10), while verifying a reported defect that turned out to be inverted.
- Problem: it was reported that `scripts/vector-freeze.sh`, `testdata/vectors/v1/FROZEN.sha256`, D101 RULING 3c and D115 §4.2 all cite **D94 §2a** for a three-class event table that is *"Ruling 4 in §2"*, making four wrong citations of the Q148 line-123 family. **The opposite is true.** In `docs/decisions/D94-anchor-verdict-vector-re-emit.md`, `## 2.` opens at `:41`, `### 2a.` opens at `:83`, Ruling 4 is at `:99` and its three-class table at `:102-106`, and the next `##` is at `:115` — so the table sits **inside** §2a. §2a is the containing subsection, not a neighbour, and all four citations are correct. D94 itself uses the bare `§2a` for the same content three times. What is wrong is the **correction**: `tasks/A.md:1278`, in A108's `Spec` line written this wave, asserts that the tree's citations *"spell it 'D94 §2a', which is the neighbouring subsection"*. That sentence is false, it is in a live entry, and it will send the next reader to change four correct citations.
- Do: correct `tasks/A.md:1278`'s parenthetical so it states that §2a contains Ruling 4 and that the tree's existing citations are right, or delete the parenthetical, which is the simpler option since the `Spec` line does not need it. Leave `scripts/vector-freeze.sh`, `FROZEN.sha256`, D101 RULING 3c and D115 §4.2 **untouched**.
- Accept: no document in the tree describes `D94 §2a` as a neighbour of the section holding Ruling 4; the four citation sites are byte-unchanged; and the reason the correction was wrong is recorded in one line, because this is the second time this wave a "citation is wrong" report survived to a document and needed refuting at the source.
- Notes: registered under the ID assigned to the reported defect rather than a new one, because the subject is the same question — what `D94 §2a` denotes — and the answer is the opposite of the one reported. This is exactly what a verification step is for: the report would have produced a row instructing a lane to break four correct citations. Note that bare `D94 §2` citations also exist (`verify/report.rs:391`, D105 `:18` and `:270`) and are also not wrong, since they point at the classification generally and §2a is inside §2.
- **Row and entry authored 2026-08-10 by the wave-13 bookkeeping lane, which refutes the report it was assigned.** Verified here: `docs/decisions/D94-anchor-verdict-vector-re-emit.md` heading and ruling offsets are `## 2.` `:41`, `### 2a.` `:83`, Ruling 4 `:99`, table `:102-106`, next `##` `:115`; the four cited sites are `scripts/vector-freeze.sh:117` (also `:284`, `:313`), `testdata/vectors/v1/FROZEN.sha256:83`, `docs/decisions/D101-anchor-golden-vector-home.md:507` and `docs/decisions/D115-entryless-row-reconstruction.md:617`; the false parenthetical is at `tasks/A.md:1278`. **Authored here in full** — there is no report to transcribe, because the report was wrong.

### Q169 — Two documents cite one check by line number and are wrong in different directions
- Milestone: M3
- Size: S
- Deps: after Q58
- Discovered by: **lane C** (2026-08-10).
- Problem: `verdict_event_ok`'s `/report/` guard in `scripts/vector-freeze.sh` is cited by line number in two decision records, and both citations miss. `docs/decisions/D101-anchor-golden-vector-home.md:482-483` says `vector-freeze.sh:99-101`; `docs/decisions/D115-entryless-row-reconstruction.md:619` says `:98-99`. In the current tree the function opens at `:103` and the guard is `:114` with its message spanning `:115-117`. At `d23dccc` the guard was at `:98` with the message at `:99-101` — so D115 had the right start line and a truncated span, D101 named only the message body and not the predicate, and **this wave's own edits to `vector-freeze.sh` moved everything +5 and made both wrong**. A third instance survives at `D101:784`, citing `vector-freeze.sh:257-278`. The tree already has the rule for one document — `check-traceability.py` hard-fails any `registry-v1.md:<n>` citation, because the registry is frozen and its line numbers are not — and the argument generalises to any file that moves, which is every file that is not frozen.
- Do: rule whether the no-line-number discipline extends beyond the frozen registry, and to what. The candidates in order of cost: names over numbers everywhere (cite the function, the test, the constant); the rule enforced only for decision records citing code, which is where the rot is measured; or the rule left as a convention with the known instances corrected. The Q58 check is the model for the enforceable version and its widening cost is known — see **Q177** for the diagnostic weakness it would inherit.
- Accept: the rule's scope is stated where the Q58 check is defined; the three known stale citations are corrected to names or to current lines; and if the rule is enforced, a planted line-number citation into a named non-frozen file turns the lane red while a legitimate one stays green.
- Notes: the two instances are worth reading together as evidence rather than as bugs — they were **both** correct-ish when written and were rotted by an unrelated edit five lines above, silently, in the same wave that wrote one of them. That is the whole argument for names over numbers, made by the tree rather than argued. Correcting D101 and D115's bodies is gated on **Q122**; correcting a citation elsewhere is not.
- **2026-08-10 — two further instances measured by the wave-14 bookkeeping lane, and they widen the row's domain in two directions.** **(1)** `D101` cites `testdata/vectors/README.md` by line number in **two** places — its Ruling 4 section heading and its edit-set table — and both name the same line. The cell they govern has since occupied **three** positions: the value cited, then a second position reached **in the commit that executed D101 itself**, then a third this wave. This is the row's strongest form of its own argument — a citation falsified by the act of executing the document that made it — and its subject is a `testdata/` document, not code, which the row's *"decisions-citing-code only"* option would not reach. **(2)** `tasks/R.md`'s `is_verified` correction carries the **right figure**, re-measured and confirmed at eight repo-wide Rust hits, and **five of its eight locators are stale after three days**; the three that still resolve are in files nothing touched. D117 §8 (vi) filed this second instance as *"the measured form of `Q169`'s argument"* and directed that it be carried here as an extension rather than as a new class, which is what this note does. **Per D117 RULING 6 no replacement line numbers are recorded here**, since a correction that cites a line number is the defect this row exists to rule on.
- **Row and entry authored 2026-08-10 by the wave-13 bookkeeping lane from lane C's report, verified against the tree before registering.** Verified here: both citations quoted at `D101:482-483` and `D115:619`; `scripts/vector-freeze.sh` currently opens `verdict_event_ok()` at `:103` with the guard at `:114-117`; `git show HEAD:scripts/vector-freeze.sh` puts the guard at `:98` with the message at `:99-101`, and `git diff --stat` shows the file gained 19 lines this wave. The report's *"actual `:103` and `:114-117`"* is confirmed. **Found here and not reported: the third instance at `D101:784`.** **Authored here, not transcribed: the whole `Do`, the whole `Accept`, and the `Notes`.**

### Q170 — A closed row whose entry carries no closing bullet
- Milestone: M3
- Size: S
- Deps: after Q135
- Discovered by: **lane C** (2026-08-10), while reading A114's entry for an unrelated reason.
- Problem: when a row closes, its `TODO.md` line takes a long dated outcome narrative and its `tasks/*.md` entry is supposed to take a dated closing bullet — `**DONE …**`, `**RULED …**` — recording what the execution actually found. Some entries do and some do not, and nothing distinguishes them. `A114`'s entry carries no execution record at all while its row closed with a full narrative; `A104`'s carries none either. The reader who opens the entry, which is where the `Do` and `Accept` live, cannot tell whether the task was executed as specified, executed differently, or ruled away — and D115 §3.5's provenance rule makes this sharper, since it requires the provenance bullet to be **deleted and replaced** by the executing lane's own dated correction, which is a rule with no enforcement and now twelve live subjects.
- Do: sweep the closed rows for entries with no dated closing bullet, and decide whether the bullet is required or conventional. If required, it wants a check — the parse is available, since `--task-entries` already pairs every row with its entry and the row's `- [x]` state is already read. If conventional, the reason is recorded where entries are written, so the next lane knows the entry is not the execution record and looks at the row instead.
- Accept: the set of closed rows whose entries carry no closing bullet is measured and recorded; the rule is stated; and if a check lands, it is red on a closed row with a bare entry and green after that entry takes its bullet, with the backlog registered rather than suppressed.
- Notes: **the wave that filed this row instantiated it nine more times.** Q134, Q145, Q148, Q133, Q141, Q140, Q146, A121 and A118 all closed at this wave's bookkeeping with their outcomes recorded in the row and nothing added to the entry, because the bookkeeping lane's scope was the register and the implementation lanes' scope was the code. That is the mechanism, not an oversight, and it is the argument for the rule being explicit: the closing narrative is written by whoever ticks the box, and whoever ticks the box is not editing `tasks/*.md`.
- **Row and entry authored 2026-08-10 by the wave-13 bookkeeping lane from lane C's report, whose supporting evidence it corrects.** Verified here by parsing every entry's top-level bolded bullets: `A114` has **zero**, which confirms the report; but the report offered `A104`, `A106` and `A107` as siblings that *"all carry a DONE/RULED bullet"* and **`A104` carries zero as well** — so A104 is a second instance of the defect rather than a counterexample, and the defect is wider than reported. A106 has one (`**DONE 2026-08-10 …**`) and A107 has four. **Authored here, not reported: the whole `Do`, the whole `Accept`, and the `Notes` observation that this wave's own nine closings instantiate it.**

### Q171 — A doc comment counts two errata entries where there are three
- Milestone: M2
- Size: XS
- Deps: after Q148
- Discovered by: **lane B** (2026-08-10), which added the third entry.
- Problem: `crates/antseal-core/tests/format_freeze.rs:827`'s doc comment states that the errata file carries *"its two entries"* under `## 1. Entries`. `docs/format/frozen-registry-errata.md` now carries **three** — the third landed this wave under Q148, as D114 §3.4's ruled erratum on registry §0's freeze blockquote. The sibling failure message at `:746` reads *"the errata file records D108's two frozen sentences; zero entries is the vacuous green this gate exists to refuse"*, which is now incomplete rather than wrong: it is the floor's diagnostic, and the floor is `count >= 1`. **Nothing is red**, because no assertion reads the count — the only assertion over `check_errata`'s return is that floor, which stays green at 1, 2, 3 or 300.
- Do: correct the doc comment to describe the entries by what they are rather than by how many there are, so the next erratum does not falsify it a second time; and decide whether the floor should become an exact count. The floor exists to refuse a vacuous green and does that job at `>= 1`; an exact count would additionally catch an entry silently deleted, at the cost of an edit every time an erratum lands. The row does not pick.
- Accept: no prose in `format_freeze.rs` states a count of errata entries that the file does not have; and the floor-versus-exact-count question is ruled with its reason recorded at the assertion, so the next reader does not re-derive it.
- Notes: prose-only and nothing is red, which is exactly why it is filed — the same class as **Q146**, closed this wave, where four prose sites stated a self-test fault count the checker had stopped printing. The cure Q146's lane landed applies here too: `cross-check.sh:393-399` now says *"do not restate them here. Restating them is exactly how this comment came to claim '8 planted faults' long after the run had stopped saying so"*. Note the neighbouring `count >= 2` at `format_freeze.rs:236-238` is a **different** function over `check_dir` with a different rationale and is unaffected.
- **Row and entry authored 2026-08-10 by the wave-13 bookkeeping lane from lane B's report, verified against the tree before registering.** Verified here: `format_freeze.rs:827` reads *"The real errata file carries its two entries under `## 1. Entries` and"*; `:744-747` carries the quoted floor and its message; `docs/format/frozen-registry-errata.md` has three `### ` entries under `## 1. Entries` at `:74`, `:115` and `:141`, the third added this wave; and the only assertion over the count is `count >= 1`. **Authored here, not reported: the floor-versus-exact-count question, the whole `Accept`, and the `Notes`** — the report named the stale count and that nothing was red.

### Q172 — A zero-byte editor-swap file appears in a tracked fixture directory from a script run
- Milestone: M2
- Size: XS
- Deps: after Q140
- Discovered by: **lane B** (2026-08-10).
- Problem: `testdata/vectors/v1/.crosscheck_cbor.py.swp` — zero bytes — and `testdata/vectors/v1/__pycache__/crosscheck_cbor.cpython-311.pyc` were both created by a run of `scripts/cross-check.sh`, the only thing in the tree that imports `crosscheck_cbor`. The `.pyc` is explained: Q140 established that importing the module writes it, and that a git-ignored dropping in that directory used to brick the workspace build. The `.swp` is **not** explained — nothing in `cross-check.sh` greps as creating one, no editor was running, and an editor-swap-shaped file appearing in a tracked fixture directory as a side effect of a gate script is the kind of thing that should have a reason before it is ignored. Both are gitignored and both are now correctly ignored by the runner, so nothing is red; the question is what wrote it.
- Do: find the producer. The candidates are the Python interpreter or a library writing a temp under a name that matches the swap pattern, the checker's own staging, or something outside the repository entirely. If it is the script, name it in a comment; if it is not, record that it is not, because *"we ignore it and do not know what makes it"* is a worse state than either answer.
- Accept: the producer of `.crosscheck_cbor.py.swp` is identified and recorded, or the file is shown not to be reproducible from a clean run of `scripts/cross-check.sh` — asserted by running the script on a clean tree and listing the directory before and after; and whichever it is, the `.gitignore` entry that covers it names the reason.
- Notes: filed `XS` and not urgent — both droppings are ignored and Q140's fix means neither is fatal any more. It is filed at all because Q140's whole lesson was that a git-ignored dropping in `testdata/vectors/` was simultaneously *"expected"* by `.gitignore` and *"unclassifiable"* by two walkers, and the way that was discovered was somebody noticing an unexplained file. A vim `.swp` bricks the build identically to a `.pyc`, which D116 measured, so this is the same class arriving by an unknown route.
- **Row and entry authored 2026-08-10 by the wave-13 bookkeeping lane from lane B's report, verified against the tree before registering.** Verified here: `testdata/vectors/v1/.crosscheck_cbor.py.swp` exists, is 0 bytes, and is timestamped 14:52 alongside `__pycache__/` at 14:52 and `crosscheck_cbor.py` at 14:50; `grep` over `scripts/cross-check.sh` finds no `.swp` literal. **Not verified here: the "0.23 s apart" figure**, which is lane B's and is below the resolution of the timestamps this lane can read. **Authored here, not reported: the whole `Do`, the whole `Accept`, and the `Notes`.**

### Q173 — Six orphaned table rows render as literal pipe-text inside a paragraph
- Milestone: M2
- Size: XS
- Deps: after Q146
- Discovered by: **lane E** (2026-08-10).
- Problem: `docs/ci-verification.md:1001-1006` is six Markdown table rows with **no blank line above them, no header row and no delimiter row**. They follow line `:1000`'s prose sentence directly, so a Markdown renderer treats them as continuation text and prints the pipes literally in the middle of a paragraph. Every other "Local verification" section in the file carries the `| Check | Command | Result |` header and its delimiter. The content is correct and is the wave's own verification evidence; only the rendering is broken.
- Do: repair the rendering under the file's own append-only convention, which is the constraint that makes this a row rather than an edit. `docs/ci-verification.md` records dated evidence and is not rewritten in place, so the fix is a **superseding note** carrying the six rows correctly formatted with a pointer to the original block, not a silent reformat of `:1001-1006`.
- Accept: the six rows render as a table; the original block is left in place or is annotated as superseded rather than deleted; and the section that supersedes it states which wave's evidence it carries, so the file's chronology stays readable.
- Notes: cosmetic in effect and not in kind — this file is the project's evidence of what has run on the remote, it is cited by `docs/ci-verification.md`'s own never-remote rule (**Q153**), and evidence a reader cannot read is evidence that will be re-gathered. The append-only convention is why this is `XS` rather than a one-minute fix: choosing to append rather than repair is the whole decision.
- **Row and entry authored 2026-08-10 by the wave-13 bookkeeping lane from lane E's report, verified against the tree before registering.** Verified here: `docs/ci-verification.md:1000` is prose ending *"**Runbook step 5 needs no re-run for this wave.**"*, `:1001` through `:1006` are six `| … |` rows with no intervening blank line and no header or delimiter, and `:1007` is blank. **Authored here, not reported: the whole `Accept` and the `Notes`** — the report named the defect and the append-only constraint, and the `Do` transcribes its conclusion.

### Q174 — The self-test's summaries are inconsistent, and only the one that scales stayed true
- Milestone: M2
- Size: XS
- Deps: after Q146
- Discovered by: **lane E** (2026-08-10).
- Problem: `scripts/cross-check.sh --self-test` reports its surfaces in several unrelated shapes: the four staged-copy surfaces each print a fixed per-surface line (`OK  ${label}: red with the fault, green without it`), the CBOR checker prints its own **computed** summary whose counts scale with the in-scope document set, the two repo checkers print their own, and the run ends with one fixed sentence (`self-test PASSED: every surface was observed failing on a planted fault`). Only the computed one carries counts, and it is the only one that drifted away from the prose describing it — which is **Q146**, closed this wave, where four sites still stated `8 planted faults / 1 control` against a run printing `15 / 2`. The lesson is already written into the script at `:393-399`: *"Q130 made the totals computed, so they scale with the in-scope set and are stated only by the checker's own printed summary — do not restate them here."* That comment governs one surface. Nothing governs a fifth surface added later.
- Do: state, once, what a self-test surface must print and where its counts may live — computed at the source and restated nowhere, which is the rule `:393-399` already applies to the CBOR checker. Apply it to the other surfaces or record why each legitimately differs, so a fifth surface inherits a rule rather than a precedent.
- Accept: `scripts/cross-check.sh` carries one stated convention for surface reporting; no count appears in prose that is not also computed by the code that prints it; and the existing summaries either follow the convention or carry a reason.
- Notes: **the report's own count did not verify and the row is filed on what did.** It described *"four self-test summary lines with three different shapes"*; `scripts/cross-check.sh` carries **two** `PASSED` summary lines (`:211` for `--check`, `:431` for `--self-test`) plus a per-surface `OK` line at `:338` and the checkers' own printed summaries. The substance — inconsistent shapes, one computed summary, and that the computed one is why only it drifted — is confirmed by the script's own Q146 comment; the arithmetic is not, and nobody should re-derive "four lines" from this row.
- **2026-08-10 — the quoted report line has changed, and this entry is now an instance of the class it was filed under.** The per-surface line quoted in `Problem` reads, in the working tree, `OK    ${label}: red with the fault (said "${expected}"), green without it` — the `(said …)` clause added by Q149's red-arm work, which is the same change that made the arms name the message they matched. The old form survives only inside a comment about the unsound shape, not as a report line. **Every other locator in this entry is also stale**: `scripts/cross-check.sh` grew +110/−17 this wave, moving both `PASSED` lines, the per-surface `OK` line and the Q146 comment block. That does not weaken the row — it is filed on the *shape* of the reporting, not on the arithmetic, as its own `Problem` says — but it does mean the entry demonstrates **Q169**'s defect while arguing **Q146**'s. **Per D117 RULING 6, no replacement line numbers are recorded here**; the surfaces are identified by their labels and by the functions that print them.
- **Row and entry authored 2026-08-10 by the wave-13 bookkeeping lane from lane E's report, which it partially refutes.** Verified here: `grep -n "PASSED" scripts/cross-check.sh` returns `:211` and `:431` and nothing else; `:338` is the per-surface line; `:393-399` carries the Q146 comment quoted in `Problem`. **Not confirmed: "four summary lines with three different shapes"** — reported and left unverified rather than restated as fact. **Authored here, not reported: the whole `Do`, the whole `Accept`, and the `Notes`.**

### Q175 — An entry authored from a row is indistinguishable from one written by its executing lane
- Milestone: M3
- Size: XS
- Deps: after Q134, Q135
- Discovered by: **D115** §8 (2026-08-10).
- Problem: D115 §3.5 requires an entry authored from a row alone to carry a dated provenance bullet saying so, precisely so that *"it must be possible to read the entry and know which sentences nobody verified"*. Nothing enforces it, and nothing distinguishes the two kinds of entry once written. An entry whose `Do` and `Accept` were invented by a drain lane reads exactly like one derived from executed work, unless its author chose to say otherwise — and the rule that would make the distinction permanent is the same rule that requires the bullet to be **deleted** at execution, so the marker is designed to disappear.
- Do: decide whether this needs a lasting instrument or a one-shot check at Q135's close. D115 §8's own reading is that it is probably the latter: the twenty entries Q134 and Q135 author are the population, they are authored in two known waves by two known lanes, and after that the population is closed. A one-shot check verifies the twenty carry the bullet; a lasting `CHECKS` entry would have to survive the bullets being deleted as each row executes, which means it is really a check about *execution*, not about authorship — and that is **Q170**.
- Accept: the question is ruled and the ruling recorded where entries are written; if the answer is a one-shot check, it is run at Q135's close over all twenty entries and its result recorded; and if it is a lasting check, it states how it behaves after a bullet is legitimately deleted.
- Notes: filed at M3 and deliberately small. The interesting content is the interaction it names: a provenance marker that must be deleted on execution cannot also be a permanent audit trail, so whoever wants a permanent record of *"this `Accept` was never derived from anything"* needs a different mechanism than the one D115 built. **Q170** is the row that would supply it from the other end, by requiring the closing bullet that replaces the provenance one.
- **Row and entry authored 2026-08-10 by the wave-13 bookkeeping lane from D115 §8's discovered-work list, verified against the decision text before registering.** Verified here: D115 §3.5 states both the requirement and the deletion rule as transcribed, and `scripts/check-traceability.py` contains no check over provenance bullets. **Authored here, not transcribed: the whole `Accept`, and the `Notes` observation that a deletable marker cannot be an audit trail** — D115 §8 named the gap and offered the one-shot-versus-lasting reading, which the `Do` transcribes.

### Q176 — No sweep reaches a root-level file, so the one externally-facing document is unswept
- Milestone: M2
- Size: S
- Deps: after Q133
- Discovered by: **D114** §7 (2026-08-10); re-verified against the post-Q133 tree.
- Problem: `CITATION_SCAN = ["crates", "docs/format", "docs/testing", "scripts"]` names **subdirectories**, and both sweeps walk `ROOT/<sub>` recursively — so no root-level file is ever read as a citation site. `CHANGELOG.md`, `README.md`, `CONTRIBUTING.md` and `MVP-SPEC.md` are all outside both halves. `CHANGELOG.md` is the only externally-facing document in the project and is what a third party reads first; it is swept by nothing anywhere in the tree — `grep -rn 'CHANGELOG' scripts/ .github/` returns zero hits — which is exactly why its line-123 misquotation drifted unobserved until D114's manual sweep found it as site S10, and would have kept drifting.
- Do: decide whether root-level files are a citation surface and, if so, add them in the shape the constants already support — a root-file list beside `CITATION_SCAN`, or a scan root of `"."` with an explicit exclusion set, the latter being the one that must be costed carefully since it reaches `target/`, `testdata/` and every dot-directory. Both halves move together or neither does, for the reason **Q156** states.
- Accept: either root files are swept, with `CHANGELOG.md`'s citations resolving and a planted unresolvable id in it turning the lane red, or the reason they are out is recorded at the constants beside the `docs/decisions/` exclusion, which is the precedent for a documented exclusion there.
- Notes: **Q133's widening did not close this**, and the ledger flagged it as possibly closed, so it was re-verified: Q133 added `scripts/` and `.sh`/`.mjs` to the decision half and collapsed the two constant sets into one, and added **no root path**. Note also that D114 §7's own statement of this finding is now stale in its first two clauses — it says *"`DECISION_SCAN` omits `scripts/`; `TASK_SCAN` includes it; neither reaches root files"*, and those two constants were deleted this wave. The third clause, which is the load-bearing one, is still true. That staleness is itself an instance of **Q151**.
- **Row and entry authored 2026-08-10 by the wave-13 bookkeeping lane from D114 §7's discovered-work list, re-verified against the post-Q133 tree as the ledger asked.** Verified here: `scripts/check-traceability.py:506-507` and `:527`; both sweeps iterate the same constants at `:581-586` and `:815-820`; `grep -rn 'CHANGELOG' scripts/ .github/` → zero hits; `TODO.md` is read at `:550`, `:555` and `:761` as the register, not as a citation surface. **Authored here, not transcribed: the two options in the `Do` and their relative cost, the whole `Accept`, and the `Notes` observation about D114's own staleness.**

### Q177 — Three identical failure messages from three occurrences in one file
- Milestone: M2
- Size: XS
- Deps: after Q133
