- Discovered by: **lane D** (2026-08-10), from the fallout of its own widening.
- Problem: the Q58 line-citation failure reports `f"{rel}: {hit}"` — the citing file's path and the registry citation it found — and **no line number within the citing file**. Three `registry-v1.md:<n>` citations in one file therefore produce three byte-identical messages, and a reader cannot tell them apart or find them. This was tolerable while the sweep covered documents; Q133 widened it to `scripts/*.sh`, where a single script can carry several, so the collision is now reachable for real. The check itself is correct and its rule is right — the point is that its diagnostic does not locate what it found.
- Do: carry the line number through to the message, in the `path:line` shape every other diagnostic in the file uses. The sweep already reads the file's text; it needs the enumeration rather than a bare `findall`, which is a small change to one loop.
- Accept: two Q58 citations in one file produce two distinguishable messages naming their own line numbers, asserted by a self-test case planting exactly that — which is green-and-useless today; and the existing self-test cases stay green, so the message change does not break the annotation matching the red arms now depend on.
- Notes: **the `Accept` interacts with Q134's fix and must not undo it.** `check-traceability.py --self-test` now requires a check's own `::error::check-traceability [<check>]` annotation in stderr before it will count a red arm as a proof, so any change to the message must keep that prefix intact. That is the one way this small edit could do damage, and it is the reason the second `Accept` clause exists.
- **Row and entry authored 2026-08-10 by the wave-13 bookkeeping lane from lane D's report, verified against the tree before registering.** Verified here: the Q58 loop collects `f"{rel}: {hit}"` from `re.findall(r"registry-v1\.(?:md|json):\d+", text)` with no enumeration, and the failure message is *"…cites the frozen wire registry by LINE NUMBER…"* with no citing-file line number; the annotation requirement the `Notes` guards is the `annotated = f"::error::check-traceability [{check}]" in result.stderr` guard added this wave. **Authored here, not reported: the whole `Accept` and the whole `Notes`** — the report named the collision and its new reachability.

### Q178 — Q6's row and its entry do not agree on who freezes
- Milestone: M3
- Size: XS
- Deps: after Q148
- Discovered by: **D114** §7 (2026-08-10), while establishing the authority for each vector-freeze citation.
- Problem: `Q6`'s entry splits the act in two — its `Do` imposes the append-only byte pin and its `Accept` defers enforcement to Q14 (*"Must-exist list … enforced before the M0 freeze tag (Q14)"*) — while `Q6`'s row reads as though Q6 did both, describing the parser that *"refuses `status frozen` while the pending set is non-empty, which makes Q14's gate executable rather than prose"*. D114 §1.7 had to resolve the split authority to decide what each of the vector-freeze citation sites should name, and it resolved it **for that wave only**. The consequence is concrete: three separate sites now restate the split because no single row states it, and Q148's corrections had to encode a distinction the tracker does not make.
- Do: state the split once, in Q6's row and entry, so they agree: Q6 imposes the byte pin, Q14 flips the status and is the act a citation should name as the authority for immutability. Correct whichever of the two is wrong rather than adding a third description, which is the failure mode D115 §1.4 and A106 both document.
- Accept: Q6's row and entry describe the same division of authority; the sites Q148 corrected cite it consistently; and a reader asking *"which act made the golden vectors immutable"* gets one answer from the tracker without opening D114.
- Notes: filed at M3 because both acts are long since executed and nothing is wrong in the artifacts — this is a record that disagrees with itself about history, and its cost is paid by the next lane that has to cite it. It is the same shape as A106's *"defined second-hand in three places that disagreed"*, one domain over, and the same shape as **Q166**'s two-homes-one-value in the narrative rather than the scalar fields.
- **Row and entry authored 2026-08-10 by the wave-13 bookkeeping lane from D114 §7's discovered-work list, verified against the tree before registering.** Verified here: Q6's entry `Accept` reads *"Must-exist list (empty-anchor, unbalanced-n) enforced before the M0 freeze tag (Q14)"* while its `Do` specifies the manifest and the CI job; Q6's row carries the `status frozen` sentence quoted above. **Authored here, not transcribed: the whole `Do`, the whole `Accept`, and the `Notes`** — D114 §7 named the disagreement and that §1.7 resolved it for one wave only.

### Q179 — Seven milestone-section task counts that nothing computes and nobody counted
- Milestone: M2
- Size: S
- Deps: after Q136
- Discovered by: **the wave-13 bookkeeping lane** (2026-08-10), while reconciling the counts it was asked to reconcile.
- Problem: `TODO.md`'s seven milestone-section headings each assert a task count — `## Pre-M0 … (8 tasks)`, `## M0 … (191 tasks)`, `## M1 … (70 tasks)`, `## M2 … (176 tasks)`, `## M3 … (21 tasks)`, `## M4 … (25 tasks)`, `## Continuous (6 tasks)`. They summed to **497** against **555** actual rows, and **four of the seven were already wrong before this wave**: M0 held 192 against a printed 191, M1 held 71 against 70, M4 held 19 against 25, and M2 held **203** against a printed **176** — a live milestone under-reporting itself by 27 rows while the Current-focus block one screen above quoted the right figure. **They are not among Q136's twelve.** Q136's set is the header line's three numbers plus the domain table's nine cells; all twelve were measured correct at this wave's bookkeeping, and the nine cells reconciled exactly. These seven are a thirteenth class that no row names, that nothing computes, and whose staleness is invisible precisely because the domain table beside them is right.
- Do: make the seven derived or asserted rather than hand-maintained. `check-traceability.py` already parses every row and already attributes each to its milestone section — `--task-entries` walks the file top to bottom — so the count is available to the process that would check it and no new parse is needed. The design question Q136 answered for its twelve applies here and its answer is **not** automatic: these counts also drift by design mid-wave, so a hard check in the required lane would be red for most of every wave. Rule which of the three shapes governs — a bookkeeping-only flag as Q136 concluded for its twelve, a generated heading, or a stated convention that the number is advisory — and record the reason where the headings are written.
- Accept: the seven headings are either computed, or checked under a flag bookkeeping runs and the required lane does not, or explicitly marked advisory with the reason recorded; a planted disagreement between a heading and the rows beneath it is caught by whichever mechanism is chosen; and the mechanism covers **all thirteen** counts in the file rather than adding a second, separate rule beside Q136's — one rule over one file, or the next reader has to learn which counts are governed by which.
- Notes: **a count typed by hand is a count that survives a raise**, which is D104 §6's phrase for exactly this and is why A121 exists. The specific harm here is not arithmetic: it is that the M2 heading and the Current-focus block state different sizes for the same milestone, and a reader deciding whether M2 is close to done gets a different answer depending on which line they read first. The seven were corrected to measured values at this wave's bookkeeping — Pre-M0 8, M0 **193**, M1 **71**, M2 **237 rows in the block (227 M2 + 9 M3-tagged + 1 struck)**, M3 21, M4 **19**, Continuous 6 — so the row is about the class, not the instances. M2's heading was deliberately changed from a bare count to a decomposition, because a single number cannot honestly describe a block holding M3-tagged and struck rows, and that is the reason its old figure was easiest to leave wrong.
- **Row and entry authored 2026-08-10 by the wave-13 bookkeeping lane, from its own measurement.** Verified here: every figure above was computed by parsing `TODO.md` row by row with section attribution, and the pre-wave values were recovered by subtracting this wave's own additions (M0 +1 for `F55`, M2 +33 for `Q149`–`Q178` and `A122`–`A125`) — which is how the four pre-existing errors were separated from the ones this wave would have introduced. Q136's scope was re-read before minting rather than folding: its twelve are the header line's three and the domain table's nine, and its `Problem` states all twelve are *correct today*, which is the premise these seven falsify. **Authored here, not measured: the whole `Do`, the whole `Accept`, and the `Notes`** — there is no source to transcribe, and the choice among the three shapes is deliberately left to whoever executes, per D115 §3.6.

### Q180 — The decision index is 29 records behind and read by nothing
- Milestone: M2
- Size: S
- Deps: after Q122
- Discovered by: **D117 §8 (i)** (2026-08-10), while measuring what *"the register row"* means for a resolved decision.
- Problem: `docs/decisions/README.md` opens with *"Numbered records of project-shaping decisions. One file per decision; statuses here mirror the files."* Measured 2026-08-10: the table carries **69 rows** against **98** decision files, and the 29 absent records fall in **two disjoint bands** — `D53`–`D61`, missing since 2026-08-02, and `D97`–`D116`. No row in the table lacks a file, so the drift is one-directional: the index only ever falls behind. `git grep "decisions/README" -- scripts/ .github/` returns **zero hits**, so nothing verifies the claim and nothing consumes the file. D117 §1.2 measured this while establishing that *"move the register row instead of the body"* is unimplementable: one of the three registers a decision has is 29 records behind and read by nothing, which is why moving a row there cannot discharge a body error.
- Do: Rule what this file is, and make it that. Either it is a register — the 29 rows land, and a check compares its id set against `TODO.md`'s Decision register, which `check-traceability.py` already parses in full — or `TODO.md`'s register supersedes it, in which case the opening paragraph says so and the table is marked as a curated selection rather than a mirror. Do not do both. The 29 rows are the smaller half of the work; the sentence that claims a property is the reason nobody noticed.
- Accept: The file either carries a row for every `D*.md` under `docs/decisions/`, or states in its opening that it does not and why; the claim *"statuses here mirror the files"* is true or gone; and if a check lands, it is red on a deliberately removed row and green with it restored.
- Notes: **This gates the `is_verified` follow-on D117 §4 scopes**, because three of that sweep's six stale statements are one register row living in three homes — this file, `TODO.md`'s register, and `D95`'s own body — so correcting them means knowing which of the three is authoritative. Sibling of **Q186**, which is the mechanism: 29 decisions carry an unexecuted `## Index row (orchestrator applies at merge)` section, so the rows the index is missing have mostly already been *written*, in the documents themselves, and merely never applied.
- **Row and entry authored 2026-08-10 by the wave-14 bookkeeping lane from D117 §8 (i), verified against the tree before registering.** Verified here, by counting rather than by reading the report: **69** `| [D<n>]` rows in `README.md`; **98** decision files at the time D117 measured (100 today, D117 and D118 having landed uncommitted since); the missing set is exactly `D53`–`D61` and `D97`–`D116`; no table row lacks a file; the opening sentence reads as quoted; and `git grep "decisions/README" -- scripts/ .github/` returns nothing. **Authored here, not transcribed: the `Do`'s do-not-do-both clause and the `Accept`'s red-then-green bullet** — D117 §8 (i) gives the problem, the disposition question and a two-clause Accept, and explicitly mints no id. **Taken on report: nothing** — every figure above was re-measured. This bullet's placement is D115 §3.5 as corrected 2026-08-10 by D117 §5.1 (Q167): last in the entry.

### Q181 — The nine `.md` files at the root of `docs/` are outside both sweeps with no recorded reason
- Milestone: M2
- Size: S
- Deps: after Q176
- Discovered by: **the Q176 lane** (2026-08-10), while widening `CITATION_SCAN` to reach root-level files.
- Problem: `CITATION_SCAN` lists `crates`, `docs/format`, `docs/testing`, `scripts` and — since Q176 — four root files. Directory entries are walked as `ROOT/<sub>`, so the nine `.md` files sitting directly in `docs/` are reached by **neither** the decision sweep nor the task sweep. The comment block above the constant records a reason for each of the two *deliberate* exclusions — `docs/decisions/` is out on D109 §3.3 because a record's job includes proposing work the orchestrator may decline, and sweeping it fails 13 rows; `.github/` is out on D116 §2 (c) and is called *"a close call"* — and says nothing at all about `docs/`. An exclusion with a reason is a decision; an exclusion with no reason is an accident that has not cost anything yet. Measured 2026-08-10 over the nine: **203** bare `D<n>` citations spanning **39** distinct decisions, and **24** citations in the marked form `check_task_citations` requires. All resolve today.
- Do: Decide whether the nine belong in the sweep, and write the reason at the constant either way. If they go in, the widening is the same one-line change Q176 made and the measured cost is the 227 citations above going from unchecked to checked — expected zero new failures, but *measure it*, because Q133's identical *"the widening is free"* was measured on one of two failure classes and turned the lane red immediately. If they stay out, the comment block gains a third entry naming the reason, in the shape the other two already use.
- Accept: `docs/`'s root `.md` files are either inside `CITATION_SCAN` with the widening's measured cost recorded, or outside it with a reason written at the constant beside D109 §3.3's and D116 §2 (c)'s; and a planted unresolvable id in one of the nine either turns the lane red or is explained by that reason.
- Notes: The direct consequence is already visible elsewhere: `docs/ci-verification.md` is one of the nine, and **Q152**'s confirmation this wave — a superseded `wasm-bitmatch` message quoted under a dated 2026-07-28 section — sits in it. That instance is *correct* under Q146's dated-record ruling, so the file being unswept has cost nothing yet; the row is about the reason, not the instance. Note the hazard the constant now states in its own voice after Q176: an entry is filtered by `CITATION_SUFFIXES` like any other path, so adding a scan root whose suffix is not in that set adds a root **that reads nothing at all** — a widening here must be checked for that, not just made.
- **Two measured instances added 2026-08-11 (wave 15), folded here rather than registered separately.** **(a) `docs/ci-verification.md` grew a normative surface while unswept.** It is one of the nine, it is now **2 251 lines**, and wave 15 appended a **new standing section** to it — the rule governing what a blast-radius measurement may count (`Q196`), which is cited by heading from `local-gate.sh`, `D116` §1.8 and `Q153`. So the ids in a live convention that three other places point at are checked by nothing, which moves this row from *"has cost nothing yet"* to *"is now the home of a cited rule"*. **(b) A second instance, in a second of the nine.** `docs/security-assumptions.md` cites the `docs/decisions/README.md` convention by name, and both ends of that citation are unswept — the citing file is one of these nine and the cited file is inside `docs/decisions/`, which is out on D109 §3.3 (**Q209**). Neither is a new row; both are measurements this row's `Do` should be executed against.
- **Row and entry authored 2026-08-10 by the wave-14 bookkeeping lane from the orchestrator's ledger, verified against the tree before registering.** Verified here: the nine files are exactly those named, counted directly in `docs/`; `CITATION_SCAN`'s membership and the shared file-or-directory branch used by both sweeps; the two recorded exclusion reasons, quoted from the comment block above the constant; and the citation census inside the nine (203 bare `D<n>` over 39 distinct ids, 24 marked-form task citations, 93 of the decision citations in `dependency-policy.md`). **Taken on report: nothing.** **Authored here, not reported: the whole `Do`, including the instruction to measure the widening rather than assume it — which is Q133's falsification applied to this row before it is executed — the whole `Accept`, and the `Notes`.** The ledger named no discovering lane; the attribution to the Q176 lane is this lane's inference from the subject matter and should be read as such.

### Q182 — Nothing asserts that a step added to the `traceability` job is cargo-free
- Milestone: M2
- Size: S
- Deps: after Q153
- Discovered by: **the Q153 lane** (2026-08-10), while placing three gate lanes into CI jobs chosen for what they do not need.
- Problem: Q153 placed `wasm-bitmatch.sh --trigger-self-test` on the `traceability` job and the two `gate-features.sh` steps on `core-dep-graph`, and the argument for the split is a property of the jobs: `traceability` has **no toolchain setup and no cache step**, so it is cheap precisely because nothing in it runs cargo, while `--check-partition` does need cargo and therefore rides the job that has it. **Nothing asserts that property.** It exists as prose in three places (`ci.yml` twice, `ci-lanes.sh` once), and `scripts/local-gate.sh`'s own header records the prose being wrong once already — *"the row calls these two 'seconds and cargo-free' … they are NOT cargo-free"*. A future step added to `traceability` that shells out to cargo will not fail fast; it will fail on a missing toolchain or run slowly against whatever is cached, in a job whose whole reason for existing is that it does neither.
- Do: Make the cargo-free property of the `traceability` job an assertion rather than a comment. The obvious home is `scripts/check-ci-shell.py`, which already reads every workflow's `run:` blocks — **but it reads them per file, not per job**, so this needs job attribution added to its parse first, and that is the larger half of the work. Rule whether the assertion is worth that parse; if it is, express the property as data (a per-job predicate list) rather than as a special case for one job name, since `core-dep-graph`'s converse property — cargo *is* available — is the same kind of fact.
- Accept: A step invoking `cargo` inside the `traceability` job turns a check red naming the job and the property; the property is stated once, where the check reads it, rather than in three prose sites; and if the parse is judged not worth it, that ruling is recorded at the `traceability` job with the three prose sites pointed at it.
- Notes: This is **Q153's own near-miss**, which is why it is filed rather than folded: the lane's first placement put `--check-partition` on `traceability`, where it would have been the first cargo user in a job with no toolchain. It was caught by reading, not by a check. The sibling property — that `core-dep-graph` *does* have cargo — is asserted by nothing either, but its failure mode is loud, which is why this row names only the silent direction.
- **Three supporting claims refuted 2026-08-11 (D124, executing this row; every figure measured at the working tree that day).** The **ruling stands and the row is closed** — the property is asserted now, by `scripts/cargo-free.sh` at the job rather than by a predicate over `run:` text — so what follows corrects the reasoning this row reached it by. **(a) *"three places"* is sixteen.** Sixteen prose statements of the property across six files, plus a seventeenth in `check-ci-shell.py`'s docstring; the largest share — six — is in `docs/ci-verification.md`, which this row does not count at all. **(b) The failure mode is wrong in both halves.** *"It will fail on a missing toolchain"* — no workflow installs rustup, so cargo comes from the runner image and is on `PATH`; nothing fails. *"Or run slowly against whatever is cached"* — the job has **no cache step**, so there is nothing cached. The real failure is **silent slowness**, and `docs/ci-verification.md` already had that mechanism right, so this row garbled its own source. **(c) The sibling's failure mode is not merely *"loud"*** — it is loud **and misdiagnosing**, sending the reader to the wrong file. Also corrected in the `Do`: the assertion was **not** worth the parse, and the parse is not the larger half of the work — a per-job predicate over `run:` text is **vacuous** against the very incident that produced this row (Q153's near-miss line contains no `cargo` token, and neither do any of the five `run:` lines the job carries), so D124 RULING 2 refused it and recorded the refusal in `check-ci-shell.py`'s docstring so the shape is not re-proposed. **What survives unchanged**: the property was unasserted, the three named prose sites exist, `local-gate.sh`'s record of the prose being wrong once, and the `Do`'s instruction to express the property as data rather than as a job-name special case.
- **Row and entry authored 2026-08-10 by the wave-14 bookkeeping lane from the orchestrator's ledger, whose supporting claim it corrects.** **Verified here, and it refutes the ledger**: the ledger states *"check-ci-shell.py already parses every `run:` block per job"*; it does not. `run_blocks(text)` is a hand-rolled line scanner returning `(1-based line number, body)` and `check(*targets)` flattens to `(workflow, lineno, body)` — **there is no job attribution anywhere in the file**, and the only occurrence of the word *job* is inside a prose reason string. Also verified: the `traceability` job's five run steps are all `./scripts/…` calls with no toolchain or cache step; the three prose statements of the property; and `local-gate.sh`'s record of the prose being wrong once. **Taken on report: nothing.** **Authored here, not reported: the whole `Accept`, the `Do`'s instruction to express the property as data rather than as a job-name special case, and the `Notes`** — the ledger reported the gap and the (incorrect) instrument.

### Q183 — Nothing makes a milestone review move `CURRENT_MILESTONE`
- Milestone: M2
- Size: XS
- Deps: after Q165
- Discovered by: **D118 §10 (i)** (2026-08-10), which calls it *"the defect that produced this whole decision"*.
- Problem: `CURRENT_MILESTONE` in `check-traceability.py` drives the verification matrix's cumulative status gate: every row at or before it must read `covered`. Two normative sites describe it as *"the line a milestone review bumps"* — the comment at the constant and `docs/testing/verification-matrix.md`'s *"How the gate uses this"* — and Q51's entry repeats it. **No procedure bumps it.** Grepping the three runbooks/checklists, `CONTRIBUTING.md`, `local-gate.sh` and `ci-lanes.sh` for "milestone review" returns nothing; the only files that mention it at all are the checker, the matrix, D118 and the Q14 freeze-gate plan. The consequence is measured: M1 shipped 2026-08-02 and the constant stayed at `M0` until 2026-08-10, so the gate covered sixteen rows where it should have covered twenty-two, and six M1 rows read `deferred` for eight days with nothing red.
- Do: Give the bump an owner and a trigger. Two shapes are available and the row does not pick: (a) a review-checklist item, in whichever document a milestone review is actually run from — which requires first establishing that such a document exists, since the sweep above suggests it does not; or (b) a check comparing `CURRENT_MILESTONE` against the milestone `TODO.md`'s Current-focus block declares, which is a register the project already maintains and which `check-traceability.py` already opens. Shape (b) closes the class rather than this instance, at the cost of making a narrative line load-bearing.
- Accept: Moving to a new milestone moves the constant by a mechanism that is not memory; a milestone review that leaves the constant behind is caught by a check or refused by a checklist item; and the reason for whichever shape is chosen is recorded at the constant, which currently asserts a procedure that does not exist.
- Notes: **A constant documented as *"the line a review bumps"* by a review procedure that does not exist is the same defect class as a count typed by hand** — D104 §6's *"a count typed by hand is a count that survives a raise"*, which is why A121 exists and why **Q179** exists one file over. Note shape (b)'s cost honestly: `TODO.md`'s Current-focus block is prose written by whoever runs the wave, and making it load-bearing gives it a checker, which is a change of status for that block and should be decided, not slid into.
- **Corrected and closed 2026-08-11 (ruled by D122 2026-08-11; executed by the wave-16 Q208 lane, which this note is from — D122 §7.5 orders it recorded here).** The `Problem`'s sweep claim — *"the only files that mention it at all are the checker, the matrix, D118 and the Q14 freeze-gate plan"* — is measured wrong in both directions (D122 §1 (d)): at `1c702d4` the same grep returns **five** files, D118 absent (it did not yet exist), with `TODO.md` and `tasks/Q.md` present and dropped as *trackers* — **and `TODO.md` rule 4 is the milestone-review checklist this entry's shape (a) supposed missing**. Neither offered shape was taken as offered: (b) is refused **on arithmetic** (as worded it demands the `M2` that D118 §5 refused with three measurements — red today against a correct constant), and (a)'s checklist item is enforced the way this project enforces every other obligation, as an `Accept` clause of a gate-execution row. The owners now exist — `Q14` (M0, done), **Q236** (M2), **Q237** (M3), `Q34` (M4) — and rule 4 names the bump as the second of three closing acts, citing them. The `Accept`'s *"recorded at the constant"* half had already landed in wave 15 (the constant's comment, the `--milestone` help text, the failure message); the checklist half closes here.
- **Row and entry authored 2026-08-10 by the wave-14 bookkeeping lane from D118 §10 (i), verified against the tree before registering.** Verified here: `CURRENT_MILESTONE` reads `M1` today with its own comment recording the 2026-08-10 move; the phrase *"the line a milestone review bumps"* occurs at the constant and in `docs/testing/verification-matrix.md`, with a third statement inside Q51's entry; "milestone review" appears in only four files, none of them a runbook, checklist, gate script or `CONTRIBUTING.md`; and the flagless run's status gate now reports 22 rows at or before M1 where it reported 16. **Taken on report: nothing.** **Authored here, not reported: shape (b)'s stated cost — that it promotes a narrative block to a checked one — and the `Accept`.** D118 §10 (i) supplies the problem, both shapes and the *"closes the class rather than this instance"* judgement.

### Q184 — Self-test fixtures pinned to values ordinary work can change
- Milestone: M2
- Size: S
- Deps: after Q165
- Discovered by: **D118 §10 (ii)** (2026-08-10), after two fixtures broke on a status the same decision was moving.
- Problem: `check-traceability.py --self-test` proves each check can go red by mutating a copy of the tree and requiring the named check to fail. A mutation is expressed as a string replacement, so every case embeds a literal drawn from the live tree — and a literal that ordinary project work may change is a fixture that will one day match nothing. D118 hit two at once: both status-gate cases pinned `V6.1`'s row cell, and moving `V6.1` from `deferred` to `covered` made both mutations no-ops. They were retargeted to `V9.2`, whose `deferred` status expires only at Q34's release gate, and the rule was written at the site: *"any status-gate fixture must be pinned to a row that outlives the constant it is testing against."* The general case is unswept. Measured 2026-08-10 across the 27 cases: roughly ten embed a live literal, including a prose fragment from `docs/format/anchor-artifact-limits.md`, two `tasks/Q.md` row **titles**, a decision id cited by one source file, a second matrix cell, and one built by concatenating an **unticked checkbox** with `**D18**`'s register row — which pins a decision row *and its unresolved state*, so resolving D18 silently disarms that case. **That case has a second failure mode, measured here rather than reasoned about**: the mutation is a `str.replace(…, 1)` over the whole of `TODO.md`, so *any* earlier occurrence of the same byte sequence absorbs it. Registering this row with the literal quoted verbatim did exactly that — the prose copy sat hundreds of lines above the register, took the replacement, and `--self-test` reported *\"decisions went green on the red-case mutation of `TODO.md`\"* while the register row it meant to flip was untouched. The repair was to spell the literal apart in the row and in this entry. Nine hard-coded target paths sit alongside them; a missing target is a hard failure rather than a skip, which is the right direction and is why the paths are the lesser half.
- Do: Sweep the 27 cases and classify each mutation literal: derived from a constant the script owns, drawn from a fixture the case itself creates, or borrowed from live tree content. For the third class, either retarget to something that outlives the case — as the `V9.2` retarget did — or record beside the case why the borrowed value is stable. Where the borrowed value is a *status* or a *checkbox state*, prefer retargeting: those are the values the project is in the business of changing.
- Accept: Every case's mutation literal is classified, and each borrowed-from-the-tree literal either moves to a stable target or carries a one-line reason at the case; the rule already written for status-gate fixtures is generalised to whatever the sweep finds; the D18 case in particular is dispositioned, since it will disarm itself the day D18 resolves; and a case whose mutation is a first-occurrence replacement over a whole file either anchors that replacement or is shown to be unique, so a second copy of the literal cannot absorb it.
- **The `Notes` below are half wrong, measured 2026-08-11 (the lane executing this row).** *"Self-announcing rather than silent"* holds for one of the two ways a pinned literal fails and not for the other, and the distinction is the whole safety argument. **Disappearance** — the literal no longer occurs in the target — is loud for a red case and a green case alike, because the no-op guard fires on `mutated == original`. **Absorption** — a second copy of the literal, elsewhere in the same file, takes the first-occurrence replacement — leaves the guard **silent**, because text did change. A red case then fails loudly, but a **green case passes vacuously and prints `ok`**, over a file whose intended target was never touched. For the green arms that is **A123's failure direction exactly**, not its opposite, so the `Notes`' contrast holds only for the red arms and for disappearance. This survives D120's delta arms: a mutation absorbed by a decoy changes no finding, which is what a green arm requires. Reproduced three times in an isolated copy against the pre-fix script — two decoys and one retargeted matrix row, one of which printed *"ok — matrix stays green where it must"* over an untouched table. **The row's conclusion still stands** — this remains a sweep worth doing and the guard is still the reason the red half costs a red run rather than a false green.
- Notes: **Self-announcing rather than silent**, which is why this is a sweep for confidence and not for safety: `--self-test`'s no-op guard reports *"matched nothing, so the case proves nothing"*, which is exactly how the `V6.1` pins were caught. That guard is the reason this class costs a red run rather than a false green — the opposite of **A123**, whose document-wide `REGISTRY.contains()` failed **green** under mutation and had to be found by mutating on purpose. Same shape, opposite failure direction, and the difference is entirely the guard.
- **Row and entry authored 2026-08-10 by the wave-14 bookkeeping lane from D118 §10 (ii), verified against the tree before registering.** Verified here, by reading the case list rather than the report: **27** cases; the `V9.2` retarget is in place in both status-gate cases and `V6.1`'s old cell string appears nowhere in the script; the rule sentence is written at the retarget; the D18 case's literal exists and pins the open checkbox; the other borrowed literals named above; the nine hard-coded target paths and the hard-failure-not-skip rule; and the no-op guard's wording. **Taken on report: nothing.** **One finding here is this lane's own, produced by registering the row**: writing the D18 literal verbatim into `TODO.md` above the register turned `--self-test` red with *\"decisions went green on the red-case mutation of `TODO.md`\"*, because the case's first-occurrence replacement took the prose copy — reproduced, diagnosed and repaired by spelling the literal apart, with no change to `scripts/`, which is outside this lane's surface. **Authored here, not reported: the three-way classification in the `Do`, the prefer-retargeting rule for statuses and checkbox states, the whole `Accept`, and the `Notes` contrast with A123's failure direction.** D118 §10 (ii) supplies the problem, the A123 analogy and the confidence-not-safety judgement.

### Q185 — The matrix's section preambles are unchecked prose asserting the state of the tree
- Milestone: M2
- Size: XS
- Deps: after Q165
- Discovered by: **D118 §10 (iii)** (2026-08-10), while correcting the two preambles it names.
- Problem: `docs/testing/verification-matrix.md` carries a machine-checked table per milestone and a prose preamble above it. `parse_matrix` recognises only pipe-delimited rows validated by a following separator; any non-table line resets the header, so **no preamble is read by anything**. Two were badly wrong and stayed wrong for eight days while the rows beside them were green. M1's asserted three things — `antseal-net` a doc-comment stub, no `StorageBackend`/`MockBackend`, no devnet script — all false from 2026-08-02. M2's asserted `antseal-anchor` was a doc-comment stub and `testdata/anchors/` held *"a README and nothing else"*, against four live protocol layers and 88 captured files. The reader's first impression of a milestone's verification state came from the one part of the document nothing checks, and it was the part that had rotted.
- Do: Decide what a preamble is for. Three shapes: delete them and let the rows speak; restrict them to statements about the *matrix* (what the section covers, how to read the status column) rather than about the tree, which is the class that rots; or keep tree assertions and give them the same treatment `Q146` gave four prose sites — state the structure and defer every measurement to the thing that measures it. Whichever is chosen, record the reason in the file, because the next preamble will be written by someone who did not read this row.
- Accept: No preamble in the matrix asserts a fact about the tree that nothing verifies; the chosen rule is stated in the file where preambles are written; and the two corrected preambles are consistent with it rather than being one-off repairs.
- Notes: This is **Q146's class in a second document** — prose stating a fact the tool no longer prints, drifting because nothing compares them — and Q146's ruling supplies the cheapest cure: remove the drift surface rather than build a checker for prose. Note that D118's own Finding 1 is the same shape one level up: a stale *paragraph* survived a wave after the *row* it described had been corrected.
- **Row and entry authored 2026-08-10 by the wave-14 bookkeeping lane from D118 §10 (iii), whose count it corrects.** **Verified here, and it corrects the source**: D118 §10 (iii) says *"two of the five"*; the file has five milestone sections but **M0 carries no preamble**, so the denominator is **four**. M1's old text is as quoted and carries exactly three false clauses; M2's old text is as quoted, and *"three clauses"* for it depends on whether *"holds a README and nothing else"* is read as one assertion or two — so the *"three clauses each"* in the source is exact for M1 only. Also verified: `parse_matrix` reads only validated table rows and no preamble prose; the corrections are present in the working tree. **Taken on report: the eight-day window**, which rests on the file's own prose plus the 2026-08-02 landing dates of the work the rows describe — the corrections are uncommitted, so there is no dated diff to confirm it. **Authored here, not reported: the three shapes in the `Do`, the whole `Accept`, and the Q146 comparison.**

### Q186 — Twenty-nine unexecuted `Index row` instructions inside resolved decision bodies
- Milestone: M2
- Size: S
- Deps: after Q180
- Discovered by: **D117 §8 (ii)** (2026-08-10), while measuring the three things *"the register row"* can mean.
- Problem: Thirty-five decision documents carry a section headed `## Index row (orchestrator applies at merge)` containing a fenced, ready-to-paste `| [D<n>](file.md) | summary | status | date |` row for `docs/decisions/README.md`. It is an **instruction**, and its heading looks identical whether it was executed or not. Measured 2026-08-10: **six** applied (D90–D95), **twenty-nine** not — `D53`–`D61`, `D97`–`D100`, `D102`–`D113`, `D115`–`D118`. Exactly one spelling, exactly one occurrence per file, so the count is not a parsing artefact. The mechanism last worked for a six-record block in August and has not worked since. A reader opening any of the twenty-nine cannot tell an unexecuted instruction from an executed one, and neither can a check.
- Do: Either apply them — which is **Q180**'s work and should not be done twice — or change the heading so it stops claiming to be an instruction. The second is the cheaper and more durable half: a section headed *"the row this document proposes for the index"* is a proposal and is true forever, where *"orchestrator applies at merge"* is a claim about an event that mostly did not happen. If the sections stay instructional, they need an executed marker, which is the same requirement **Q188** raises for *"orchestrator applies at source"* sections against other files.
- Accept: No section in `docs/decisions/` asserts that an edit was applied when it was not; either the twenty-nine rows are in the index, or the heading form states that the row is the document's proposal rather than a record of the register's state; and if a marker is chosen instead, an unmarked section is caught rather than assumed.
- Notes: This is **Q145's class** — a pointer whose target nothing verifies — and the direct sibling of **Q180**: the rows the index is missing have mostly already been *written*, inside the documents, and merely never applied, so Q180's 29-row backlog is largely a copy job whose source is sitting one heading away. Note the interaction with the count: applying rows moves Q180's arithmetic and this row's together, which is why neither should be executed without the other in view.
- **Row and entry authored 2026-08-10 by the wave-14 bookkeeping lane from D117 §8 (ii), whose figures it corrects in both terms.** **Verified here, and it refutes the source**: D117 §8 (ii) says *"27 of 33"*; measured over `docs/decisions/` there are **35** files carrying the heading and **29** whose row is absent from `README.md`'s table — one spelling, one occurrence per file, six applied (D90–D95). Also verified: the section holds a fenced ready-to-paste table row; D117 and D118 both carried one and neither was in the index. **Authored here, not reported: the whole `Do` including the change-the-heading option, the whole `Accept`, and the interaction note with Q180.** **A figure in this entry moved by this lane's own act**: applying D117's and D118's index rows at this bookkeeping takes the unapplied set from 29 to **27** of 35 — recorded here rather than left for a reader to rediscover, since a row whose number changes because of the act that registered it is exactly the kind of thing this project keeps finding.

### Q187 — An unregistered rule that a ruled record is immutable, which has suppressed two ordered corrections
- Milestone: M2
- Size: S
- Deps: after Q122
- Discovered by: **D117 §8 (iii)** (2026-08-10), which names it the highest-value item of its lane.
- Problem: `tasks/Q.md`'s **Q109** entry closes a bullet with *"`D94:554-555` still reads 'three of A22's four Accept rows' and is not corrected — **a ruled record is immutable**; the drift is recorded here."* That clause is a rule. It is registered in no decision, cites nothing, and contradicts the third sentence of `docs/decisions/README.md`, which forbids **silent** rewriting rather than change. Its cost is measured and already paid: **D101** ordered `D94:554-555` corrected (in §2.2, and again in §10's edit-set table), and **D103 §7.2 RULING 6a** ordered the same thing with a prescribed disposition — drop the count and cite by number. Neither ran, and `D94:554-555` still reads *"three of A22's four Accept rows"*. D117 RULING 1 now refuses the sentence explicitly. A second site applies the same rule to a different D94 citation, in `tasks/R.md`, in nearly the same words.
- Do: Execute the parked correction and remove the rule that parked it, in one act. `D94:554-555` is **struck** under D117 §2.3(b) — it is a description of already-executed work, not a rule a lane would act on — with D103 RULING 6a's prescribed disposition applied (drop the count) and a dated correction section appended in D117 §2.2's form. In the same change, correct **both** `tasks/Q.md`'s and `tasks/R.md`'s rule sentences: they are entry prose, not resolved decisions, so D117's correction mechanism does not gate them, but they must not be left asserting a rule the project has refused.
- Accept: `D94:554-555` carries its correction with the original quoted verbatim in an appended dated section; neither `tasks/Q.md` nor `tasks/R.md` asserts that a ruled record is immutable; and the two orders that were parked are recorded as executed, naming D101 and D103 RULING 6a, so a future reader does not re-issue them a third time.
- Notes: **The general lesson is not about D94.** An unregistered rule, written once inside a task entry, silently overrode two explicit rulings from two decisions for days — which is a stronger demonstration of Q122's premise than Q122's own forcing case, and is why D117 ranked it first. Note the shape: the rule was *stated in the same breath as the drift it justified*, so it read as an explanation rather than as a claim, which is how it survived review twice.
- **Row and entry authored 2026-08-10 by the wave-14 bookkeeping lane from D117 §8 (iii), whose supporting citations it corrects.** Verified here: the sentence in `tasks/Q.md`, quoted as above, sitting inside the **Q109** entry — a fact the source does not state and which decides where the correction goes; the near-identical second site in `tasks/R.md`, which the source does not mention at all; `README.md`'s third sentence; D101's order in **§2.2** and its repetition in **§10's edit-set table**; D103 §7.2 RULING 6a's order and its prescribed disposition; and that `D94:554-555` still reads as quoted. **This refutes the source in one detail**: D117 §8 (iii) and the ledger both cite *"D101 §2.2/§7"*; D101 §7 is *"Ruling 6 — the artifact bytes"* and contains no such order, so the second D101 site is §10. **Authored here, not reported: the whole `Accept`, the two-sentences-not-one scope, and the `Notes` observation about why the rule survived review.**

### Q188 — Three different acts share the words Amendment and Correction
- Milestone: M2
- Size: S
- Deps: after Q122
- Discovered by: **D117 §8 (iv)** (2026-08-10), while inventorying the in-place correction forms already in the tree.
- Problem: Three different acts are spelled with the same two words across `docs/decisions/`. **(α)** correcting the document you are reading. **(β)** instructing edits to *other* documents, conventionally headed *"orchestrator applies at source"* — **eight** documents carry one, and **six of the eight carry no execution marker at all**, with the remaining two carrying `[ ]`/`[x]` tokens inside quoted replacement blocks rather than as execution marks. **(γ)** correcting the Decision-register row that framed the question at authoring time — **five** documents, three of which use the identical heading *"The register's framing, corrected"*. Measured 2026-08-10 across the corpus: **95** headings carrying one of these keywords, in **12** distinct spellings, across **44** documents. A reader cannot tell (α) from (β) by the heading, and (β) sections are instructions that may or may not have run — which is **Q186**'s defect one section over, in a place where the instruction targets someone else's file.
- Do: Give the three acts three distinct heading forms, and give (β) an executed/not-executed marker. D117 RULING 2 already fixes (α)'s form from this decision forward — `## Correction — <clause>, <YYYY-MM-DD>`, with `## Amendment — …` reserved for a change that *adds* a ruling — and reserves the bare *"orchestrator applies at source"* spelling for (β). This row is the retrofit of what is already written. It must **not** renumber anything (D117 RULING 4) and must **not** unify the three acts under one word, which is the outcome the current vocabulary already produces by accident.
- Accept: The three acts are distinguishable by heading alone; every (β) section records whether its instructions ran; the retrofit changes no section, ruling or rider identifier; and the count of distinct spellings is stated in the file that fixes the form, so the next divergence is visible.
- Notes: This is **D113 §1.2's four-spellings finding in a second namespace** — that one was about `Owner:`, this one about corrections — and the pattern is the same: a convention adopted by imitation produces a family of spellings, and the family is invisible until someone counts it. D117 §7 lists non-retrofit as an explicit residual risk rather than an oversight, so this row is the discharge of a known debt, not a discovery of a new one. Size is **S** and transcribed from the source; the measured corpus is larger than the source assumed (44 documents, not 38), so an executor should re-estimate before starting rather than trusting this field — D115 §3.2's rule is that the re-estimate goes here and the field does not move.
- **Three measured additions 2026-08-11 (wave 15), folded here rather than registered separately.** **(a) Two more (β) instances, and they widen the vocabulary rather than the count.** `D101` §10 and `D103` §12 are edit-set tables carrying instructions against other documents with **zero** execution markers — and each is headed *"Consequences — the exact edit set"*, **not** the *"orchestrator applies at source"* spelling this row inventories. So the family is broader than the eight (β) sections named above, and the retrofit's domain is *edit-set tables*, not a phrase. Both carried a row unexecuted for two days, which is the forcing instance this row lacked. **(b) The inventory's own domain moved under it.** D119 RULING 4 deleted the 41 in-body `## Index row` sections on 2026-08-11, so any count of this family taken before that date over-counts by that block; what survives is **two numbered `Register row` sections** (`D86` §10 and `D87` §8, both marked *applied* and un-deletable because deleting them moves an identifier D117 RULING 4 forbids moving), **five `Index note` prose sites** (D27, D28, D29, D86, D87), and the (β) sections. Re-measure before executing; do not inherit a pre-D119 figure. **(c) The retrofit must run in both directions.** `D8` §E5 is an open `- [ ]` over work completed 2026-07-28 — an execution marker that is present and **false** — so a sweep that only adds markers to sections lacking them leaves the more misleading half untouched (**Q226**).
- **Row and entry authored 2026-08-10 by the wave-14 bookkeeping lane from D117 §8 (iv), whose figures it corrects in two of three terms.** **Verified here, and it refutes the source twice**: the source says *"thirteen spellings, across 38 documents"* — measured, **12** distinct heading spellings across **44** documents (95 headings), and no scoping of the corpus yields 38; and it says (β) is *"9 documents"* — measured, **8** (D53, D56, D91, D93, D94, D97, D100, D106), with a ninth file containing the phrase only as a prose quotation. Verified as reported: (γ) is **5** documents; three share the heading *"The register's framing, corrected"* verbatim; six of eight (β) sections carry no execution marker. Also verified: `Postscript` occurs **once** in the whole corpus, in D117's own prohibition list, never as a heading. **Authored here, not reported: the whole `Accept`, the do-not-unify constraint restated as a requirement rather than a risk, and the `Notes` re-estimate warning.**

### Q189 — A correction whose subject is a prediction has no defined disposition
- Milestone: M3
- Size: XS
- Deps: after Q122
- Discovered by: **D117 §8 (v)** (2026-08-10), found while measuring the domain of the `is_verified` correction.
- Problem: D117 rules how a *statement* in a resolved decision is corrected: replaced if a lane would act on it, struck and left standing if it is a measurement, census or argument. A **prediction** is neither. D98 rider 1b ends *"`is_verified` gains its first non-test caller"*, written when D98 measured three repo-wide hits, all definition-or-test. U23 made it true: `crates/antseal-cli/src/status.rs` calls `is_verified()` in two production paths — the `source_label` branch and the `--json` field — and the Rust-source count is now **8**. Striking the sentence would assert it was wrong; correcting it would assert it was a measurement; leaving it silent loses the one fact a later reader most wants, which is that the forecast was tested and held. Nothing in the tree records the discharge.
- Do: Define the disposition for a fulfilled or falsified prediction, and apply it to D98 rider 1b as the worked example. The shape is a **dated discharge note** — the prediction quoted, the event that settled it named by task id, the date, and whether it held — appended in D117 RULING 2's section form but with a heading that is not `Correction` or `Amendment`, because it is neither. Rule whether a *falsified* prediction takes the same note or becomes a correction; the two directions may not want the same treatment.
- Accept: D98 rider 1b carries a dated discharge naming U23 and the two production call sites; the disposition rule is written where D117's other rules live, so the next prediction is discharged rather than left ambiguous; and the rule states what happens when a prediction is falsified rather than fulfilled.
- Notes: Filed at **M3**, transcribed from D117 §8 (v)'s own milestone: nothing is wrong in any artifact, no lane is blocked, and the cost is paid entirely by a future reader of D98 who cannot tell a live forecast from a discharged one. **The general form matters more than the instance** — a decision that makes a prediction is doing something valuable and rare, and the reason to define the disposition is to keep predictions cheap to make.
- **Row and entry authored 2026-08-10 by the wave-14 bookkeeping lane from D117 §8 (v), verified against the tree before registering.** Verified here: D98 rider 1b's closing sentence, quoted as above; D98's own contemporaneous measurement of three repo-wide hits, all definition-or-test; that U23 is the task that produced `crates/antseal-cli/src/status.rs`, whose header names it; and the two production call sites plus a total of **8** `is_verified` occurrences in Rust source today. **Authored here, not reported: the discharge-note shape, the question of what a *falsified* prediction takes, the whole `Accept`, and the `Notes`** — D117 §8 (v) states the gap, names D98 §1b and U23, and stops there.

### Q190 — Three root files carry task citations the suffix filter can never read
- Milestone: M2
- Size: S
- Deps: after Q176
- Discovered by: **the Q176 lane** (2026-08-10), while writing the suffix hazard into the constant it had just widened.
- Problem: Q176 made root-level files scan roots, and the same change recorded the hazard it creates: a scan root is filtered by `CITATION_SUFFIXES` like any other path, so naming a file whose suffix is not in that set adds a root that reads **nothing at all**. Three root files are in exactly that state and carry real citations. `deny.toml` names **seven** distinct task ids — `P13`, `Q29` (six occurrences), `P12`, `Q10`, `P16` (three), `S20` and `P15`. `.gitattributes` names six — `Q1`, `G3`, `Q4`, `Q7`, `Q2`, `A6`. `.gitignore` names four — the `P16`/`P17` pair, the `R10`/`F17`/`A23` group, and `Q9`. `CITATION_SUFFIXES` is `{.rs, .md, .json, .py, .sh, .mjs}`: `.toml` is not in it, and a dotfile has an empty suffix. **Q176** covers only the four `.md` root files it named; **Q156** is about `.github/` and `.yml`. So seventeen citations in three files that govern licensing policy, line-ending discipline and what the repository ignores are checked by nothing.
- Do: Decide the suffix rule rather than adding files one at a time. Two questions: whether `.toml` joins `CITATION_SUFFIXES`, and how a suffixless dotfile is reached at all — the current filter cannot express it, so this needs either a per-root suffix override or an explicit allowlist of literal filenames. Measure the widening before taking it: adding `.toml` brings the six `Cargo.toml` files under `crates/` into both sweeps, and a manifest is a plausible home for a bare token that looks like a task id.
- Accept: The three files are either inside both sweeps with the measured cost of the suffix change recorded, or outside with a reason at the constant; a planted unresolvable id in `deny.toml` or `.gitattributes` turns the lane red or is explained; and the mechanism for a suffixless file is stated rather than left as an accident of `Path.suffix`.
- Notes: The hazard sentence at the constant is the reason this row exists in this shape: the widening was made and its limit was **written down in the same act**, so this row is a known remainder rather than a discovery. Note the direction of risk — these files are edited rarely and by hand, which is why nobody noticed, and is also why a stale id in one of them would survive a long time.
- **Three figures corrected 2026-08-11 (D123, ruling this row; enumerated file by file at the working tree that day).** The **ruling stands**: `CITATION_SUFFIXES` is not edited, and `CITATION_SCAN` gains a per-entry rule under which a literal file entry is read as itself, unfiltered. The counts this row reached it by are wrong in three places, all in the same direction — the surface is larger than stated. **(a) *"Three root files"* is five.** Root `Cargo.toml` and `requirements-crosscheck.txt` are in the same state and this row names neither; root `Cargo.toml` alone carries **57 distinct task ids in 100 occurrences and 19 distinct decision ids**, more than the row's three files combined. All five are now named literally in `CITATION_SCAN`. **(b) *"`.gitignore` names four"* is six** — `P16`, `P17`, `R10`, `F17`, `A23` and `Q9`, in **seven** occurrences (`P16` twice); the row's own enumeration lists all six and its numeral says four. **(c) *"Seventeen citations in three files"* inherits (b)**: the three files carry **19 (id, file) pairs, 18 distinct ids, 29 occurrences**. **And the row's direction-of-risk is refuted at the mechanism.** The row reasons that these files *"are edited rarely and by hand, which is why nobody noticed, and is also why a stale id in one of them would survive a long time"*. Ids in this project are **struck, never deleted**, so there is no decay path: the failure mode is *wrong when written*, and rarity **preserves** a correct citation rather than rotting it. Rarity therefore makes these the **cheapest** files to sweep — an argument *for* inclusion, which is where the ruling landed. The harms are also unequal: `deny.toml` carries RUSTSEC suppressions with a dated review obligation and is the real one; `.gitattributes` is navigational only. **What survives unchanged**: `CITATION_SUFFIXES`'s membership, the suffixless-dotfile question the `Do` raises — answered by the ruling — and the observation that a scan root whose suffix is outside the set reads nothing at all.
- **Row and entry authored 2026-08-10 by the wave-14 bookkeeping lane from the orchestrator's ledger, whose figures it corrects in two places.** **Verified here, and it corrects the ledger twice**: `deny.toml` cites **seven** distinct ids, not the six listed — **`P15`** is cited and is a registered task — and `.gitignore` carries a fourth `P16` occurrence the ledger's grouping omits. Also corrected: the ledger says widening to `.toml` *"also sweeps every `Cargo.toml`"*; measured, there are **nine** in the workspace and only the **six under `crates/`** are inside `CITATION_SCAN`, with `fuzz/` and `probes/` outside it and the root manifest reachable only if named. Verified as reported: `CITATION_SUFFIXES`'s membership, applied identically in both sweeps; Q176's four root files; Q156's scope; and the hazard sentence, quoted from the comment block above the constant. **Authored here, not reported: the suffixless-dotfile mechanism question, the whole `Accept`, and the `Notes`.** The ledger named no discovering lane; attribution to the Q176 lane is this lane's inference from the hazard sentence that lane wrote.

### Q191 — `--self-test` stages the live tree, and no ignore glob closes the class
- Milestone: M2
- Size: M
- Deps: after Q150
- Discovered by: **the Q150 lane** (2026-08-10), which wrote the limits of its own fix into the file.
- Problem: `check-traceability.py --self-test` stages a copy of the **live working tree** and mutates it. Q150 fixed the measured race with an `ignore=shutil.ignore_patterns(".git", "target", "node_modules", "*.tmp.*")` and a three-attempt retry with 0.25 s/0.50 s backoff, failing with the last exception quoted. The comment block above it concedes the residue: `__pycache__/*.pyc` and editor `.swp` files are not in the glob. Three further instances are outside any glob's reach. `fuzz/corpus` and `fuzz/artifacts` are git-ignored yet copied — **922 files, ~5.1 MB** staged on every run, because `"target"` is a basename match that catches `fuzz/target` and nothing catches these. A writer saturating the tree defeats the bounded retry, measured 3 of 3. And a sibling lane's half-finished edit reddened the lane 6 of 6 and then healed itself, which is worse than a hard failure: a red that goes away teaches everyone to re-run.
- Do: Rule whether `--self-test` should stage a **consistent snapshot** instead of a live copy. `git archive HEAD` is the obvious mechanism and appears nowhere in `scripts/` today. **It changes what the self-test tests**, and that is the decision, not the implementation: HEAD is not the tree the contributor is about to push, so a snapshot-based self-test proves the checks can go red against *committed* content while the working tree — the thing being checked by the same run's `--check` half — goes unproven. Evaluate at least: snapshot from HEAD; snapshot from the index; copy with a `.gitignore`-derived ignore set; and keeping the live copy with the retry widened. Each has a different answer to *"which tree did the red arm actually exercise?"*, and that question is the deliverable.
- Accept: The staging mechanism is chosen with its trade-off recorded at the code, naming which tree the self-test's arms exercise and why that is the right one; the git-ignored directories are either excluded or explicitly included with a reason; and a concurrent writer can no longer produce a red that is unrelated to traceability and disappears on re-run.
- Notes: **A red that self-heals is the expensive failure mode here**, not the hard one: the hard failure names a file and stops, while the healing one trains a reader to treat the required lane as flaky. That is the same cost D109 attaches to a monotonically non-decreasing report and the same reason Q150 was filed at all. Sized **M** rather than S because the deliverable is a ruling with measurements behind it, not an edit.
- **Row and entry authored 2026-08-10 by the wave-14 bookkeeping lane from the Q150 lane's report, verified against the tree where verification was possible.** Verified here: the copy is of `ROOT`, the live tree; the ignore list and the three-attempt retry with its backoff and its final quoted-exception message; the comment's own concession about `.pyc` and `.swp`; that `fuzz/corpus` and `fuzz/artifacts` exist, are git-ignored at two named `.gitignore` lines, and are **not** matched by the ignore list; the 922-file / ~5.1 MB measurement of what that stages; and that `git archive` appears nowhere under `scripts/`. **Taken on report, from the Q150 lane, and not reproducible from the tree: the saturated-writer 3/3 and the sibling-lane 6/6 self-healing red** — both are dynamic observations that leave no artifact. **Authored here, not reported: the four candidate mechanisms, the framing of the deliverable as *which tree did the red arm exercise*, the whole `Accept`, and the `Notes`.**

### Q192 — Q150's race one directory over, in shell, where an ignore glob cannot reach
- Milestone: M2
- Size: XS
- Deps: after Q150
- Discovered by: **a wave-14 lane, via the orchestrator's ledger** (2026-08-10) — the ledger names no lane, and this entry does not invent one.
- Problem: `scripts/format-freeze.sh` and `scripts/vector-freeze.sh` each stage a scratch directory for their `--self-test` arms with `cp -R "${DIR}/." "${SCRATCH}/"`, and each runs under `set -euo pipefail`. `cp` reads the directory and then opens each entry; a concurrent writer's temporary file that disappears between the two makes `cp` exit nonzero, and `set -e` aborts the whole script. This is exactly the failure Q150 measured and fixed for `check-traceability.py --self-test` — except the fix Q150 landed, an `ignore=` glob on the `shutil.copytree` call, **has no counterpart here**: these are POSIX `cp(1)` invocations in shell, with no ignore parameter and no retry. Measured 2026-08-10: **six** such copies, not the two the ledger reports. `format-freeze.sh` stages once and restores the scratch between each of its four red arms; `vector-freeze.sh` stages once and restores between its two. None is guarded by a retry, a `|| true`, or any handling at all.
- Do: Give the six copies the same treatment Q150 gave the Python one, in the form shell allows: a bounded retry around the stage-and-restore step, factored into one helper rather than repeated six times, failing with the underlying error quoted. Consider whether the restore-between-arms copies want the retry at all — a failure there is a self-test infrastructure failure and arguably should be loud — and if so, say which of the six get it and why, rather than applying it uniformly by habit.
- Accept: A concurrent writer during a `--self-test` stage no longer aborts either script with an unexplained `cp` error; the retry lives in one place; and the reason the restore copies are or are not covered is written where the helper is defined.
- Notes: The interest is in the **shape**, not the two scripts: Q150's fix was expressed in the vocabulary of the tool that had the bug, so it did not generalise to the two siblings doing the same thing with a different tool. That is the same failure Q133 found between `check_decisions()` and the task sweep — the identical hole one function away — and the same one **Q149** found across five red arms. Three instances now, in three vocabularies.
- **Row and entry authored 2026-08-10 by the wave-14 bookkeeping lane from the orchestrator's ledger, verified against the tree before registering.** **Verified here, and the count is larger than reported**: the ledger describes one `cp -R` per script; measured, there are **six** — four in `format-freeze.sh` (one stage, three restores) and two in `vector-freeze.sh` (one stage, one restore), all inside the `self-test)` arm, none guarded. Also verified: both scripts set `set -euo pipefail`; both copies are shell `cp(1)`, so no `ignore=` parameter exists to widen; and `grep` for `cp -R`/`cp -r` across `scripts/*.sh` returns only these six lines. **Taken on report: *"measured twice today"*** — the reproduction leaves no artifact in the tree and this lane did not re-run it. **Authored here, not reported: the one-helper instruction, the question of whether the restore copies want the retry, the whole `Accept`, and the `Notes` three-vocabularies observation.**

### Q193 — Four docstrings claim a helper is duplicated verbatim and it is not
- Milestone: M2
- Size: XS
- Deps: after Q149
- Discovered by: **the Q149 red-arm sweep** (2026-08-10), by a red arm returning code 2 rather than by reading.
- Problem: Four `testdata/vectors/v1/*/gen_vectors.py` docstrings state, verbatim, that *"the tiny `check_committed` helper below is duplicated verbatim in the sibling generators rather than shared through a common module"*, and give a good reason for the duplication — each generator is an independent cross-check vehicle (D31 tier T1) and a shared helper would be shared code between vehicles meant to stay apart. **The claim about the code is false.** The `crypto/`, `fine-tree/` and `hkdf/` helpers are byte-identical: `check_committed(path, produced) -> int`, failing to **stderr** with `FAIL  {name}: re-derived bytes differ from the committed vector …`, unified diff at n=2, capped at 60 lines. `storage/`'s is `check_committed(rendered, committed_path) -> int` — **arguments reversed and renamed** — failing to **stdout** with `MISMATCH  {name}: committed file differs from fresh derivation`, `keepends=True`, capped at 40, and with a different missing-file message. It carries the same docstring. `anchor/` has no such helper; its `--check` path is inline in `main()` with a third report shape. So the tree holds **two signatures, two messages, three reporters over five generators**, and four docstrings asserting one.
- Do: Correct the four docstrings to describe what is actually there, and decide whether the divergence is intended. The rationale for duplication is sound and should survive; what must go is the word *verbatim*, which is the part that is false and the part a reader relies on. If the four are meant to be identical, make `storage/` identical and say so; if `storage/`'s different shape is deliberate, the docstring says why it differs rather than claiming it does not.
- Accept: No generator docstring claims a duplication the tree does not have; the count of helpers, signatures and report shapes is stated once, wherever the rationale for duplication is written; and if the helpers are unified, `anchor/`'s absence of one is named rather than left as a silent fourth case.
- Notes: **The finding's provenance is the point.** The defect was surfaced by `scripts/cross-check.sh`'s red arm returning **2** — Q149's *"nonzero, but the check never printed what it prints when it finds the planted fault"* — which is the exact code that distinguishes a real detection from a crash. Under exit-status-only judging, the arm would have gone green and the docstrings would still say *verbatim*. `cross-check.sh` records this in its own voice: *"THREE reporters over five generators, not the two the tree describes … this arm is what found it."*
- **Row and entry authored 2026-08-10 by the wave-14 bookkeeping lane from the Q149 lane's landed artefact, verified against the tree before registering.** Verified here: the four `check_committed` definitions and their paths; the two signatures with their argument order and naming; the two failure messages with their streams, diff modes and line caps; the differing missing-file messages; that `anchor/gen_vectors.py` defines no such helper and reports inline from `main()`; the docstring text, quoted above, occurring in all four; and `red-arm.sh`'s definition of return code 2 together with `cross-check.sh`'s recorded account of the finding. **One correction to the ledger, in naming rather than in fact**: it lists the four sites as *"gen_vectors.py:58, crypto:47, hkdf:28, fine-tree:35"*, but **all five generators are named `gen_vectors.py`** and the unnamed first is `storage/` — which is precisely the one whose helper is *not* verbatim, so it is the load-bearing member of the set rather than an anonymous one. **Authored here, not reported: the whole `Do`, the whole `Accept`, and the `Notes`.**

### Q194 — A citation inside `tasks/` that has rotted four times, twice in one wave
- Milestone: M3
- Size: XS
- Deps: after Q169
- Discovered by: **a wave-14 lane, via the orchestrator's ledger** (2026-08-10) — the ledger names no lane, and this entry does not invent one.
- Problem: `tasks/F.md`'s **F35** entry reads *"The shell layer prints `::error::…: digest check FAILED — a frozen vector was modified or deleted` plus coreutils' own `report/verification-reports.json: FAILED` (`scripts/vector-freeze.sh:100-101`)."* The message now lives in `check_digests()`'s `else` arm, at `:209` in the working tree; `:100-101` is the `--verdict-event` argument parser. The citation names two lines because the prose describes two outputs, and it named them correctly when it was written: at `350948b` (2026-07-28) the message was at exactly `:100` and the coreutils passthrough at `:101`. It then moved to `:188` at `5f758de`, to `:204-205` at `1c702d4`, and to `:209` in this wave's edits — **four positions, two of the moves in this wave**. Nothing is red, nothing is wrong except the pointer, and a reader who follows it lands in an unrelated argument parser.
- Do: Re-point or de-number the citation. The tree's existing answer for this class is names over numbers — cite `check_digests`, which is stable and searchable, and quote the message rather than locating it — and that answer should be applied here rather than a fifth line number. Do it as part of **Q169**'s scope ruling rather than as a one-off, since the whole question Q169 asks is how wide the no-line-numbers convention should be, and this instance is the evidence that `tasks/` belongs inside it.
- Accept: F35's citation resolves to the message it describes, by a locator that survives an edit above it; and the scope ruling Q169 owes explicitly says whether `tasks/*.md` is inside the convention, with this instance named as the reason.
- Notes: **Filed at M3 because Q169 is at M3 and this is its evidence, not a separate task** — closing it separately would produce two rulings about one convention, which is the failure D115 §1.4 and A106 both document. The specific value here is scope: Q169's row is about *decision records* citing code, and its `Do` offers *"decisions-citing-code only"* as one of three options. This instance sits in `tasks/`, so it is the counterexample that option needs to answer.
- **Row and entry authored 2026-08-10 by the wave-14 bookkeeping lane from the orchestrator's ledger, whose premise it refutes.** **Verified here, and it refutes the ledger's central claim**: the ledger says of `:100-101` that *"those lines were never it"*. They were. `git show 350948b:scripts/vector-freeze.sh` — the commit that wrote F35 — puts the `digest check FAILED` message at line **100** and the coreutils passthrough at line **101**, exactly as the prose describes, and the same holds at `31d3a5b` and `9979dfe`. Also verified: the entry is **F35**, quoted above; the message is at `:209` today inside `check_digests()`; `:100-101` is now the `--verdict-event` parser; and the intermediate positions `:188` and `:204-205`. **The consequence of the refutation is the row's classification**: this is Q169's stale-citation class, not Q152's correct-by-accident class as the ledger files it, and the entry is written on the corrected finding. **Authored here, not reported: the whole `Do`, the whole `Accept`, and the `Notes` scope argument.**

### Q195 — A checker that prints the same word for a modified golden and a deleted one
- Milestone: M2
- Size: XS
- Deps: after Q149
- Discovered by: **the Q149 red-arm sweep** (2026-08-10), while auditing how instruments report what they find.
- Problem: `testdata/utf8-corpus/gen_corpus.py`'s `--check` arm compares each committed input and expected file against a fresh derivation. It reads the committed side as `got = path.read_bytes() if path.exists() else None` and then tests `if got != want:`. A deleted golden yields `None`, `None != want` is unconditionally true, and the deleted case falls into the same branch as the modified case — printing `MISMATCH <path>` for both, with no `else`, no `exists()` diagnostic and no distinct word. The same file gives the *opposite* asymmetry its own vocabulary: an unexpected extra file prints `STRAY <name>`. So of the three things `--check` can find, two have names and the third borrows one.
- Do: Separate the two branches and give the missing case its own word, in the shape the sibling generators already use — `testdata/vectors/v1/storage/gen_vectors.py` prints `MISSING   {name}: no committed file to check against` for exactly this condition, so the wording exists in the tree and does not need inventing. Keep both counted into the same failure total; the exit status is right and only the operator-facing word is wrong.
- Accept: A deleted golden and a modified golden produce different messages naming what happened; the missing case's word matches the one already used elsewhere in the tree for the same condition; and both still fail the check.
- Notes: **Deliberately filed as lesser than Q149's class**, and the distinction is the whole reason it is a separate row: in Q149's class an arm judged on exit status alone certifies a surface it never exercised, so the check is **vacuous**. Here the comparison genuinely runs, the deletion is genuinely detected, and the failure total is genuinely right — the operator is simply told the wrong story about why. A row that conflated the two would make Q149's class look cosmetic.
- **Row and entry authored 2026-08-10 by the wave-14 bookkeeping lane from the orchestrator's ledger, whose path it corrects.** **Verified here, and it corrects the ledger's locator**: the ledger says `gen_corpus.py` around `:501-503`; the file is `testdata/utf8-corpus/gen_corpus.py` — **not under `testdata/vectors/`** — and the arm spans `:499-504`, with the convergence in the ternary at `:501` and the print at `:504`. Also verified: the code as quoted; the absence of any `else` or `exists()` diagnostic; the `STRAY <name>` mirror case; that a missing file is counted into `bad` and does exit nonzero; and that `storage/gen_vectors.py` already prints a distinct `MISSING` message for the same condition. **Authored here, not reported: the reuse of the existing `MISSING` wording, the whole `Accept`, and the `Notes` distinction from Q149's class** — the ledger states the distinction in one clause and this entry gives its reason.

### Q196 — Blast radius measured under fail-fast is a lower bound reported as a radius
- Milestone: M2
- Size: XS
- Deps: after Q140
- Discovered by: **a wave-14 lane, via the orchestrator's ledger** (2026-08-10) — the ledger names no lane, and this entry does not invent one.
- Problem: When a lane asks *"how much does this break?"* it runs the suite and counts the reds. `cargo test` stops a target at its first failure and stops the run at its first failing target, so the answer is a **lower bound** whose tightness depends on test ordering. Measured in the repository: `--no-fail-fast` appears exactly **once**, in `docs/decisions/D95` §7 — *"`cargo test -p antseal-core --features test-util --no-fail-fast --tests`: **five** red, not D94 step 0's predicted two"* — which is this defect already found, already recorded, and never turned into a convention. No lane passes it: `local-gate.sh`'s `test` step, five `ci-lanes.sh` invocations and `ci.yml`'s `cargo test --workspace --locked` all run fail-fast. The reported instance this wave is 3 tests across 1 target rising to 8 across 4 with the flag — which was offered as a plausible mechanism for **D116 §1.5(a)** concluding Q140's radius was *"two walkers"* when Q157's later census found twelve walk sites and a planted `.vscode/settings.json` failing eight tests across four targets. **That mechanism is refuted — see the correction below.**
- Do: Make it a stated convention rather than a habit: any measurement of blast radius records the command that produced it, and that command carries `--no-fail-fast`. Write it where such measurements are written up — the decision-record conventions and whatever guidance a planning lane reads — rather than adding the flag to the gate, which is a different question with a different cost (a full run of a broken tree is slower and noisier, and the gate's job is to stop, not to survey).
- Accept: The convention is written where blast-radius measurements are made; the distinction between *surveying* (needs the flag) and *gating* (does not) is stated with its reason; and D116 §1.5(a)'s *"two walkers"* figure carries a dated note recording **how it was measured** — which, measured, is *not* fail-fast and *not* superseded, so the note records the method and the co-existence of the two figures rather than the supersession this bullet originally asked for.
- Notes: **The generalisation is Q160's**, one measurement over: a recorded figure that does not name what produced it attaches itself to whatever the reader is thinking about. Here the missing detail is not *what was timed* but *what was allowed to stop*. Note the honest limit of this row — a fail-fast count is not *wrong*, it is a lower bound, and a lower bound reported as a radius is the defect.
- **Corrected 2026-08-11 (the lane executing this row). The convention survives; the row's central hypothesis, its locator and two of its counts do not.** **(a) The hypothesis is refuted.** Fail-fast does **not** explain D116's *"two walkers"*. That finding was reached by **reading** `vector_runner.rs`'s classification and confirmed by two commands the flag cannot touch — a `cargo check`, which runs no test, and a single-named-target run whose own transcript reads `test result: FAILED. 7 passed; 1 failed`. **`--no-fail-fast` would have changed nothing there.** **(b) A fact this row states and gets wrong**: `cargo test` does **not** stop a target at its first failure. cargo 1.92.0, verbatim: *"The Rust test harness will run all tests within the executable to completion, this flag only applies to the executable as a whole."* The loss is at **target** granularity only, and the first failing target is counted **in full** — D116's own transcript above proves it. So *"tightness depends on test ordering"* is **target** ordering, which nothing in this repository pins. **(c) *"Two"* is confirmed, not superseded, by Q157's twelve** — twelve counts walk sites of every class, two counts walks carrying the identical `closed` rule, and Q157's own class column puts `closed` at exactly those two.
