Both figures are true at this epoch, which is why the `Accept` above now asks for a method note rather than a supersession note. **(d) The locator was wrong in three places in this entry** — the *"two walkers"* finding is at **D116 §1.5(a)**, not §1.7; §1.7 is Q141's cost figure. All three occurrences are replaced above and in this bullet; the original text read *"D116 §1.7"* at each. The mis-citation was inherited from D116's own Amendment, whose five section citations never resolved (**Q222**). **(e) *"`--no-fail-fast` appears exactly once"* was five when this entry was written** — D95 §7, `TODO.md`'s Q196 row, and **three lines of this entry itself**. The row falsified its own count by writing it down, which is **Q199**/**Q169**'s class. **The substantive claim survives**: no invocation in `scripts/`, `.github/` or `CONTRIBUTING.md` passes the flag. **(f) *"Twelve fail-fast invocations execute tests, not seven"*** — the seven named here plus `gate-features.sh`, `format-freeze.sh`, `vector-freeze.sh`, `wasm-tests.sh` and `e2e-devnet.sh`; all twelve are correct **as gates**, which is what this row's own survey-versus-gate distinction predicts.
- **Row and entry authored 2026-08-10 by the wave-14 bookkeeping lane from the orchestrator's ledger, verified against the tree where verification was possible.** Verified here, statically and without running cargo: `--no-fail-fast` occurs **once** in the whole repository, in D95 §7, quoted above — **refuted, see (e) above**; no `scripts/`, `.github/`, `tasks/` or `CONTRIBUTING.md` invocation passes it; `local-gate.sh`, five `ci-lanes.sh` invocations and `ci.yml` all run fail-fast; and D116 §1.5(a)'s finding heading reads *"There are two walkers with the identical fatal rule, not one"*. Corroborating and independently verified: `vector_walk/mod.rs` records a planted `.vscode/settings.json` failing **eight tests across four test targets**. **Taken on report: the 3 tests / 1 target → 8 tests / 4 targets measurement**, which this lane did not re-run — no cargo was invoked. **Authored here, not reported: the survey-versus-gate distinction, the instruction to annotate D116 §1.7's figure rather than restate it, the whole `Accept`, and the `Notes` link to Q160.**

### Q197 — An instrument that fires correctly and names the wrong cause
- Milestone: M2
- Size: XS
- Deps: after Q157
- Discovered by: **Q157's walk census** (2026-08-10), which records the finding in the census document itself.
- Problem: `bitmatch_embedded_table_equals_the_committed_tree` asserts that the vector table `crates/wasm-bitmatch/build.rs` embeds equals the tree on disk. The two sides are produced by **two independent walks**: `build.rs`'s `walk_root`/`walk_version_dir` at build time, and the test's own `walk_committed`, which applies its own prune. Its failure message says *"the embedded vector table is stale: rebuild `wasm-bitmatch` (build.rs watches testdata/vectors/)"* and lists the two set differences. Staleness is one cause; the other is that the two walks classify the tree differently, and **for that cause a rebuild regenerates exactly the same disagreeing set**. The message names only the cause that a rebuild fixes, so a reader who follows it does the one thing that cannot help and learns nothing from the result.
- Do: Change the message to state what was compared rather than to diagnose a cause it cannot determine: two walks, one at build time and one in this test, produced different sets, and here are the differences — with the rebuild suggested as one of two possibilities rather than as the finding. If the two walks are supposed to share a rule, say where that rule lives, which after Q157 is the shared ignorable-droppings list, so the reader is pointed at the thing that would actually differ.
- Accept: The failure message does not assert a cause the assertion cannot distinguish; the rebuild advice is presented as one candidate and the rule-divergence candidate is named beside it; and the message points at the shared classification list rather than at a build step.
- Notes: **Distinct from Q158, which is about the same test**, and the two must not be folded: **Q158** is that this instrument can never run at all, because a `build.rs` panic precedes every test in the crate, so it *"cannot see the divergence it exists to catch"*; **this row** is that when both walks succeed and return different sets — the case Q158 does not cover — the instrument fires and misnames the cause. Q158's own `Notes` concede its `Accept` is impossible today, and this row occupies exactly the window that concession leaves open.
- **Row and entry authored 2026-08-10 by the wave-14 bookkeeping lane from Q157's landed census, verified against the tree before registering.** Verified here: the test name and its assertion; the message text, quoted above; that `embedded` comes from `build.rs`'s own walk via `OUT_DIR` and `committed` from the test's independent `walk_committed`, so the comparison is genuinely between two walks and not between a build and a directory; the census's own sentence recording the defect; and Q158's subject and its `Notes` concession, which is what makes the two separable. **One correction to the ledger's locator**: it cites `bitmatch.rs:73`, which is where the `assert_eq!` sits **at HEAD**; in the working tree the assertion is at `:97` and the message at `:100` — the row is filed on the message, not on the position. **Authored here, not reported: the whole `Do`, the whole `Accept`, and the `Notes` articulation of the Q158 boundary.**

### Q198 — A root gate that is correctly fatal and reports the wrong category
- Milestone: M2
- Size: XS
- Deps: after Q157
- Discovered by: **Q157's walk census** (2026-08-10), while classifying the twelve walk sites by the rule each carries.
- Problem: `version_dirs_in` enumerates `testdata/vectors/` and keeps every entry for which `is_dir()` holds — **no `v<integer>` name filter, no prune**. `vector_index_exists_and_matches_the_committed_tree` then calls `load_index` on each, which reads `<dir>/INDEX.json` and panics on failure with *"every format-version directory must carry an {INDEX_NAME} (F10; testdata/vectors/README.md)"*. A stray `__pycache__/` or `.vscode/` directly under `vectors/` therefore fails the test with a sentence that misnames it: the offender is **not** a format-version directory, and that is exactly why it carries no `INDEX.json`. A reader takes the message at face value and goes looking for a missing index in a directory that should not be there at all.
- Do: Correct the diagnosis without touching the behaviour. `load_index`'s panic should distinguish *"this is a version directory and its index is missing"* from *"this is not a version directory and does not belong at the root"*, naming D116 R7 as the authority for the second being fatal. The root gate deliberately does not prune, so the second message is the common case for droppings and should read as the primary one.
- Accept: A stray directory at the vectors root fails with a message naming it as a stray rather than as an index-less version directory; a genuine version directory missing its `INDEX.json` still fails with the F10 message; and both messages cite the rule that makes them fatal, so neither reads as a bug in the gate.
- Notes: **Same shape as Q197, one instrument over, and both came out of the same census**: an instrument whose *verdict* is right and whose *explanation* is wrong. The distinction is worth keeping in view because the cheap fix for both is to make the message describe what was observed rather than infer a cause. Note that the behaviour here is doubly protected — D116 R7 rules it and `vector_index_root_gate_does_not_prune` now pins it — so an executor cannot accidentally *fix* the message by pruning the root.
- **Row and entry authored 2026-08-10 by the wave-14 bookkeeping lane from Q157's landed census, verified against the tree before registering.** Verified here: `version_dirs_in`'s `is_dir()`-only filter; the call path from `vector_index_exists_and_matches_the_committed_tree` through `load_index`; the panic message, quoted above; the function's own doc comment stating that root fatality arrives via `load_index`; D116 R7's text, quoted above; and that `vector_index_root_gate_does_not_prune` exists in the working tree, plants `__pycache__/` at a scratch root, asserts the stray survives, and is **new this wave** (absent from the file at HEAD). **Taken on report: nothing.** **Authored here, not reported: the two-message split in the `Do`, the whole `Accept`, and the `Notes` pairing with Q197 and the warning about accidentally pruning the root.**

### Q199 — The walk census's excluded set went stale on the day it landed
- Milestone: M2
- Size: XS
- Deps: after Q157
- Discovered by: **the wave-14 bookkeeping lane** (2026-08-10), while verifying a ledger item that turned out to describe HEAD rather than the tree.
- Problem: `crates/antseal-core/tests/vector_walk/mod.rs` states the census's counting rule and, inside it, an exclusion: *"A walk over a *copy* of the tree is not a site over the tree; **the two** in `scripts/vector-freeze.sh --self-test` are noted below and excluded."* Measured against the working tree the same day it landed: there are not two. `vector-freeze.sh`'s `--self-test` arm no longer walks the scratch copy for `*.json` — that `find` was deleted this wave and replaced by manifest-driven victim selection, with the reason written at the site (*"a stray `v1/.vscode/settings.json` … became the victim; mutating it changed nothing the freeze pins, `check_digests` stayed GREEN, and the arm reported 'the freeze guard is broken' about a guard that was working perfectly"*). The other `find` the count could refer to is in the `update)` arm and walks the **real** tree, not the copy. What remains reaching the scratch is `version_dirs`, whose filter is `-maxdepth 1 -type d -name 'v[0-9]*'` — it cannot see a dropping — and which is **already site 12** in the census.
- Do: Correct the exclusion note to match the tree: name the one surviving copy-walk, say that it is `version_dirs` and is already counted as a site over the real tree, and drop the claim of two. While there, make the rule's generality explicit — it excludes walks over a **copy**, in any language, and is not a rule about shell `find`, which the current phrasing invites a reader to assume because both examples were shell.
- Accept: The census's excluded set matches the scripts as they stand; no sentence in it states a count the tree does not have; and the counting rule's generic form is stated so that a future Python or Rust walk over a scratch copy is excluded by the same sentence rather than by analogy.
- Notes: **The census is one wave old and this is a note against it, not a defect in Q157's work** — the twelve sites, the shared list and the enforcement test are all correct. What went stale is a parenthetical about two *other* walks, made stale by an edit in the same wave, in a different lane's file. That is exactly **Q169**'s mechanism (*"self-falsified by the very commit executing it"*) expressed as a count rather than as a line number, which is worth recording because the no-line-numbers convention would not have prevented it.
- **Row and entry authored 2026-08-10 by the wave-14 bookkeeping lane from the orchestrator's ledger, whose claim it refutes and replaces.** **The ledger's version of this row is false against the working tree**: it says `vector-freeze.sh --self-test` *"walks a `cp -R` copy of the tree twice (a `find` at `:238` and the `:273` region), including git-ignored droppings"*. Verified: that was true at HEAD, where the scratch `find` sat at `:235` — the ledger's `:238` is off by three — but this wave **deleted** it, and the second `find` walks `${dir}`, the committed tree, never the copy. Registered here on the residue that is true: the census's *"the two"* is now wrong, verified by reading the counting rule and both scripts. **Verified here**: the counting-rule sentence quoted above; the three `find` invocations in `vector-freeze.sh` and what each walks; the deletion comment at the victim-selection site, quoted above; `version_dirs`' filter; and that it is registered as site 12. **Authored here, not reported: the whole `Do`, the whole `Accept`, and the `Notes` Q169 comparison.** The precedent for re-registering an assigned id on a corrected finding rather than dropping it is **Q168**, wave 13.

### Q200 — Where the provenance bullet sits beside a closed row's outcome bullet is undecided
- Milestone: M3
- Size: XS
- Deps: after Q167
- Discovered by: **the Q167 correction to D115 §3.5** (2026-08-10), which names the question and declines to answer it.
- Problem: D115 §3.5, as corrected under Q167 by D117 §5.1, requires the provenance bullet to sit **last in the entry**. A *closed* row's entry carries a second dated top-level bullet — the execution record, `**[YYYY-MM-DD DONE]**` or its variants — and nothing rules where the two stand relative to each other. Q135's eight closed-row entries answer it unanimously: outcome bullet first, provenance bullet last, 8 of 8, across `tasks/Q.md`, `tasks/S.md` and `tasks/U.md`. That unanimity is precedent, not a rule, and it is exactly the situation **Q167** was filed to prevent — a convention about entry structure being set by the tree while the governing document is silent. D115's correction section states the gap in its own words and explains why it cannot close it: a correction may not introduce a ruling.
- Do: Rule the order and write it into D115 §3.5 as a ruling, not a correction — which means it needs a decision or an executing task, not an appended note. Ratifying the tree costs one sentence and leaves eight entries untouched. Whatever is chosen, state it as a rule about *two* bullets rather than about the provenance bullet alone, because the next entry to acquire a third top-level bullet will otherwise re-open the same question.
- Accept: D115 §3.5 states where the provenance bullet sits when an entry also carries a dated outcome bullet; the eight existing closed-row entries either comply or are corrected; and the rule is expressed generally enough that a third top-level bullet does not reopen it.
- Notes: **Sits between Q170 and Q175 and belongs to whichever runs first.** **Q170** asks whether a closed row's entry must carry a closing bullet at all — if the answer is no, this row's subject partly evaporates. **Q175** asks how an authored entry is distinguished from an executed one, which is what the provenance bullet exists to do and therefore what its position should serve. Filed at **M3** to sit with both of them; nothing at M2 depends on it, and closing it in isolation risks a third rule about entry structure, which is the outcome all three rows exist to avoid.
- **Row and entry authored 2026-08-10 by the wave-14 bookkeeping lane from the orchestrator's ledger, verified against the tree before registering.** Verified here: D115 §3.5's corrected text reads *"last in the entry"* and its three binding rules are untouched; the correction section's refusal to rule, quoted above; that all **eight** Q135 entries put the dated outcome bullet first and the provenance bullet last, checked one by one across three task files; and that no rule anywhere in `docs/decisions/`, `tasks/` or `TODO.md` fixes the relative order. **Authored here, not reported: the *rule about two bullets rather than one* instruction, the whole `Accept`, and the `Notes` dependency argument.** The ledger's routing judgement — that it belongs to whichever of Q170 and Q175 runs first — is a reasonable inference stated in no artifact, and is recorded here as the ledger's, not as the tree's.

### Q201 — A deletion rule with no subject on an entry authored after its row closed
- Milestone: M3
- Size: XS
- Deps: after Q167
- Discovered by: **the Q167 correction to D115 §3.5** (2026-08-10), which restates the deletion rule without carving out the case Q135 had just created.
- Problem: D115 §3.5 binds the provenance bullet with three rules; the third is *"**It is deleted, not edited, when the task is executed** — at which point the executing lane's own dated correction replaces it, in the house form. A provenance note that outlives its uncertainty is the Q99 defect."* The rule assumes execution follows the entry. For the eight entries Q135 wrote, it does not: every one is a **closed** row whose execution is dated 2026-08-02 or 2026-08-09, recorded in a dated outcome bullet *above* a provenance bullet written 2026-08-10. There is no future execution event, therefore no deletion trigger, therefore the bullet is permanent — and the rule as written neither says so nor forbids it. The Q167 correction restates the three rules as still binding and does not carve out the case. **Q175** names the underlying tension in its own `Notes` — a marker that must be deleted on execution cannot also be a permanent audit trail — without noticing that eight entries are already on the permanent side.
- Do: State the disposition for an entry authored *after* its row closed. The candidate that keeps both intentions is that such a bullet is permanent **and says so**: it records provenance for an entry nobody will ever execute, which is precisely the reader's question, and it does not outlive an uncertainty because the uncertainty was never resolvable. Rule it, and if the answer is *permanent*, say what distinguishes such a bullet from one awaiting deletion, so a future lane sweeping for undeleted bullets does not delete these eight.
- Accept: The rule states what happens to a provenance bullet on an entry whose task was already executed; the eight Q135 entries are covered by it explicitly; and a sweep for undeleted provenance bullets can tell a permanent one from a stale one without reading the row's date.
- Notes: **Filed at M3 with Q170, Q175 and Q200**, which are the same neighbourhood: what an entry's top-level bullets mean, in what order they appear, and which of them are permanent. Closing any one of them alone risks a fourth rule about entry structure. The instance is harmless today — nothing is wrong in the eight entries — and the cost is paid by a future lane that reads the rule literally and deletes eight correct bullets.
- **Row and entry authored 2026-08-10 by the wave-14 bookkeeping lane from the orchestrator's ledger, verified against the tree before registering.** Verified here: D115 §3.5's third rule, quoted verbatim above, and that the Q167 correction leaves all three rules binding without a carve-out; that all **eight** Q135 entries carry a provenance bullet as their last bullet and a dated outcome bullet above it, with execution dates preceding the entry's authoring date; and Q175's `Notes` sentence, quoted above, which states the tension without applying it to these eight. **Authored here, not reported: the candidate disposition, the sweep-safety requirement in the `Accept`, and the `Notes`.**

### Q202 — A self-test precondition judged by an abort rather than by a reported case
- Milestone: M2
- Size: XS
- Deps: after Q135
- Discovered by: **the wave-14 bookkeeping lane** (2026-08-10), while verifying a ledger item whose account of the mechanism was inverted.
- Problem: Until Q135 drained and deleted it, `check-traceability.py --self-test` opened with an unguarded `raise AssertionError` asserting that `ROWS_PENDING_ENTRY` was non-empty — *"an empty register with a frozen date invites a future lane to reopen it"*. It sat in `self_test()`'s **setup** block, before the `(check, file, mutation, expect)` case tuples are built and long before the mutation loop runs them. On a drained register it therefore aborted the entire self-test: **not one case executed**, and the operator saw a traceback-shaped failure from the harness rather than a verdict about any check. That is **Q149's class one level up** — a nonzero exit standing in for a judgement — expressed in the harness that exists to prove red arms are real. It worked, in that it forced the deletion Q135 made; it worked the way a crash works.
- Do: Register the shape, since the instance is gone. Decide how a setup-time invariant in `self_test()` should fail: as a reported case with its own name and expected message, in the same vocabulary as the arms it guards, or as an abort with a message that distinguishes *"the self-test's own precondition is violated"* from *"the self-test is broken"*. Whichever is chosen, the distinction must be visible in the output, because those two conditions currently produce the same shape of failure and have opposite responses.
- Accept: A precondition failure inside `--self-test` is distinguishable in its output from a harness failure; the rule is written where such preconditions are added; and if any remain, each states which of the two it is.
- Notes: **Deliberately filed on the shape rather than the instance**, because the instance was deleted by Q135 in the same wave and a row about deleted code is archaeology. The residual risk is small and specific: the next setup-time invariant will be written by imitation, and the only example in the file's history is the one that aborted. Note that Q149's register in `scripts/lib/red-arm.sh` covers instruments with red arms; a harness's own preconditions are outside it, which is the gap this row names.
- **Row and entry authored 2026-08-10 by the wave-14 bookkeeping lane from the orchestrator's ledger, whose account of the mechanism it refutes.** **Verified here, and it inverts the ledger**: the ledger says *"the drain reddens the baseline first, so `--self-test` fails its two task-entries GREEN-arm cases and never reaches the empty-register assertion"*. Measured against HEAD, the assertion was an **unguarded `raise`** in the setup block, constructed **before** the case list, so it fired first and alone and cases (h) and (i) never ran — the reverse of the ledger's ordering. The file's own surviving comment agrees with the measurement and not with the ledger: *"`--self-test` refused to **run** against one"*. Also verified: the deleted assertion's text, recovered from HEAD; the two `task-entries` GREEN-arm cases, (h) the struck-row shape and (i) the `####` sub-heading; and the comment block recording the deletion of the constant, `BACKLOG_FROZEN_ON`, the staleness rule and case (k) together. **Authored here, not reported: the whole `Do`, the whole `Accept`, and the `Notes`** — nothing of this row's registered substance came from the ledger, which described a different mechanism. Re-registering an assigned id on a corrected finding rather than dropping it follows **Q168**, wave 13.

### Q203 — One decision's routing error was transcribed into a live entry and now blocks the wrong work
- Milestone: M2
- Size: XS
- Deps: after Q122 (which ruled how a resolved body is corrected)
- Discovered by: **D118** §10 (v) (2026-08-10), while adjudicating the eleven deferred matrix rows.
- Problem: `docs/decisions/D115-entryless-row-reconstruction.md` §4.1 ends its `V7.2` acceptance clause with *"Whether `V7.2`'s status may move `deferred` → `covered` depends on `A25`'s row-1 script, which is A25's, not this row's"*. **A25's row 1 is the OTS calendar cycle; `V7.2` restates A25's row _2_** (the two real TSA tokens reaching `proven` against the pinned store), which D118 adjudicated **SATISFIED on its own evidence**, with no dependence on the missing `scripts/anchor-smoke` at all. The routing error did not stay in the decision: it was transcribed **verbatim** into `tasks/A.md`'s **A105** entry, where it is the last bullet of the `Accept`. So the live instruction a future A105 executor reads tells them their row is blocked on work that does not block it — and the work it names is genuinely missing (**A127**), so the executor has every reason to believe it and stop. D115 §4.1 also cites `scripts/check-traceability.py:241` for `CURRENT_MILESTONE`; at `95fcee0` `:241` is a comment line and the constant is `:244` — the same subsection, wrong twice.
- Do: correct **both** sites, under the two different rules that now govern them. `tasks/A.md`'s A105 `Accept` bullet is a **live task entry**, so it is edited in place with a dated clause and no ceremony — D117 does not gate it. D115 §4.1 is a **resolved decision body**, so it takes D117's **STRIKE** arm: the sentence is a routing claim, not an instruction a lane executes verbatim, and the original must stay legible beside the correction. Do not renumber §4.1. Do not restate the `:241` line number when correcting it — D117 RULING 6 forbids replacing one stale locator with another.
- Accept: A105's `Accept` no longer conditions `V7.2` on A25's row-1 script and names D118's adjudication as the reason; D115 §4.1 carries a dated strike naming the correct row and pointing at its correction section; the `:241` citation is corrected without a replacement line number; and `python3 scripts/check-traceability.py` is green afterwards, since both files are swept surfaces.
- Notes: this is the **second** measured instance of one decision's error propagating into a live task entry that then outlives it — **Q151**'s deleted-constant prose was the first, one namespace over. It is also the first correction to be routed by D117's strike-vs-replace test in a wave other than D117's own, and the routing is not obvious: the sentence *looks* like an instruction (it tells a lane what to do about a status cell) but its defect is a **misattribution of fact**, which is what puts it on the strike side. If that reading is wrong, the correction lands as a replace and the ruling gains its first contested case — either outcome is worth more than leaving the sentence standing.
- **Row and entry authored 2026-08-10 by the orchestrator, from D118 §10 (v), verified against the tree before registering.** Verified here, not taken on report: D115 §4.1's sentence is at `docs/decisions/D115-entryless-row-reconstruction.md:542` and its verbatim transcription is at `tasks/A.md:1237`; `V7.2`'s matrix row is owned by `A25 + A7` and restates A25's Accept row 2; A25's Accept row 1 is the calendar cycle that `V7.1` covers. ~~**Found here, not reported: the transcription into A105's live entry** — D118 §10 (v) named only the decision, and the propagated copy is the half that actually misdirects a lane.~~ — **Corrected 2026-08-11 (the lane executing this row): this entry claims credit for a finding its own source reported.** D118 §10 (v) names the transcription in its own words — *"The practical consequence is that A105's entry tells its executor `V7.2`'s status is blocked on work that does not block it"* — so the propagated copy was reported, not found here, and *"named only the decision"* is false. **What was genuinely found here** is narrower and should be read as the whole of the claim: that the transcription is **verbatim**, that it sits in A105's `Accept` rather than anywhere else in the entry, and its exact location. **The rest of this bullet stands**, including the two verified line citations and the `V7.2`/A25-row-2 mapping. **Authored here, not transcribed: the whole `Do` — including its split of the two sites across D117's two arms — the whole `Accept`, and the `Notes`.**

### Q204 — The immutability rule Q187 withdrew is still asserted, twice applied, inside a resolved decision
- Milestone: M2
- Size: S
- Deps: after Q187
- Discovered by: **the Q187 lane** (2026-08-11), while executing Q187's own Accept and finding it satisfiable without reaching the rule's load-bearing home.
- Problem: `Q187` charged the removal of an unregistered rule — *"a ruled record is immutable"* — from the two live task entries that asserted it, and it did that: `tasks/Q.md`'s Q109 bullet and `tasks/R.md`'s R69 bullet both now carry a dated withdrawal citing D117 RULING 1. **The rule's third and fourth statements were outside that Accept and are still standing.** `docs/decisions/D105-post-freeze-report-value-additions.md` is `Status: RESOLVED`, and its §7 discovered-work list carries a subsection whose heading is *"Drift, reported not edited (records are immutable; these are not records)"* — the rule stated as the section's organising principle — and applies it twice by name below: *"D94 is a ruled record and must not be edited"* and *"D29 is immutable"*. So `Q187`'s premise that the sentence is *"registered in no decision"* is **false**, and R.md's copy was transcription rather than invention: R.md's own withdrawal names *"D105 §7 drift 1"* as the clause's source. D117 engages D105 once, in a bare id list, and never reaches §7. **A row closed, its defect did not**, and the surviving home is the more authoritative of the four. Two further facts bear on the disposition. D105 §7's second application names **D29's immutability**, and D119's edit set makes correcting D29's own record its **step 1** and **load-bearing** — so the example and the counter-example now sit in the same wave. And the two applications are *parked corrections* (measurements of drift, with a reason for not acting), while the heading is a *rule* — which puts them on opposite sides of D117 §2.3's fork.
- Do: Correct D105 §7 under D117, and route the heading and the two applications separately rather than as one act. The heading states a rule a lane would act on, which is §2.3(a) **REPLACE**; the two numbered drift items are dated measurements with their reasoning attached, which is §2.3(b) **STRIKE**, leaving the original legible. Do not renumber §7 or its subsection (D117 RULING 4). The correction section quotes each sentence verbatim, names D117 RULING 1 as the authority and `Q187` as the lane that measured it, and states which of D105's own rulings still stand — all of them, since none rests on the immutability claim. Say explicitly that drift item 1's *"`tasks/R.md` is R69's own entry and is corrected by §9"* survives untouched; only the clause about D94 changes.
- Accept: D105 §7 no longer asserts that a record is immutable, in its heading or in either application; the correction is one dated appended section carrying both arms with the routing argued rather than asserted; §7's identifiers do not move; the D29 application names D119 step 1 as the act that supersedes it, so a reader meeting *"D29 is immutable"* on 2026-08-11 is not sent to argue with the lane correcting D29 the same day; and a sweep of the tree for the rule's wording returns only withdrawn or corrected instances.
- Notes: Read this beside **Q186** and **Q200**, which are the same failure at other joints — an instruction or a rule that lives in a decision body and is discharged only where somebody happened to look. The general lesson is the one **Q187** paid for and did not bank: an Accept written from the two sites a row names closes the two sites a row names. The count matters here — the row said two sites, the tree held four — and the two it missed are in the more durable document. Note also that D105 §7's own framing is the defence a corrector must answer: the parenthetical *"these are not records"* is drawing a line between the decision (immutable) and the drift list (not), which is a coherent position and simply the one D117 RULING 1 refused.
- **Row and entry authored 2026-08-11 by the wave-15 Q-domain registering lane from the orchestrator's ledger.** **Verified here, not taken on report**: D105's `Status: RESOLVED`; §7's subsection heading, quoted verbatim; both applications, quoted verbatim from their numbered drift items; that `tasks/R.md`'s withdrawal names *"D105 §7 drift 1"* as its source, which is what makes the R.md copy transcription; that `tasks/Q.md`'s and `tasks/R.md`'s copies are both now struck and withdrawn under Q187; and that D119's edit set makes D29's status correction its step 1. **Taken on report: that D117 engages D105 only in a bare id list** — this lane did not read every D105 reference in D117. **Authored here, not reported: the split of the heading and the two applications across D117 §2.3's two arms, the instruction to name D119 step 1 at the D29 application, the whole `Accept`, and the `Notes`** — the ledger supplies the four-site measurement and the observation that the routing is not obvious.

### Q205 — Every line-number citation of the decision index inside D117 is stale, and one of them silently resolves to another decision's row
- Milestone: M2
- Size: S
- Deps: after Q180
- Discovered by: **the wave-15 orchestrator** (2026-08-11), while checking what D119's index backfill moved.
- Problem: `docs/decisions/D117-resolved-decision-corrections.md` is the record that ruled *"a correction never cites a line number"* (RULING 6), and it cites `docs/decisions/README.md` by line number **eight times** across **three** distinct locators — `README.md:81` six times, `README.md:1-5` once, `README.md:3-5` once. D119's index act rewrote the opening prose and backfilled the table from 71 rows to 106. **All three locators are now wrong, in two different ways.** `:1-5` and `:3-5` are quoted in D117 §1.2 and §1.5 as the file's opening and as the sentence its central ruling rests on; both quotations still match text in the file, but that text has moved — the *"never by silently rewriting history"* sentence now ends at `:11`, and lines 3-5 are the new register-versus-index paragraph, so a reader following the locator reads a different sentence and finds the quotation absent. `:81` is cited six times as *"D95's register row"*, the applied copy that D117 §1.3 and §4 build an argument on; it is now **D75's row**. That is the silent case rather than the dangling one: the reader lands on a well-formed index row for a real decision and has no signal that it is the wrong one.
- Do: Replace all three locators with symbol-or-heading citations, under D117's own RULING 6, in one dated correction section. `:81` becomes a citation of **D95's row in the index**, named by decision id — the index is keyed by id and ascending, so the id is a stable address in a way a line is not. `:1-5` and `:3-5` become citations of the file's **opening paragraph** and of the *"never by silently rewriting history"* **sentence**, quoted as they already are, with no positional locator at all. Do not substitute the current line numbers: RULING 6 forbids replacing one stale locator with another, and this document is the one that forbids it. Do not renumber §1.2, §1.5, §1.3 or §4.
- Accept: No line-number citation of `docs/decisions/README.md` survives anywhere in D117; each replacement is a symbol, heading or quoted sentence; the correction section states, per D117 §2.5, that the defect is *went stale* rather than *wrong when written* and names D119's backfill as the event; every ruling of D117 is stated as standing, because none of them depends on where in the file the sentence sits; and D117's own RULING 6 is cited as the authority for the form of the repair.
- Accept, second half: a reader arriving at what was `:81` reaches D95's row, and the record says so in words rather than by position.
- Notes: This is **Q194**'s class inside the document that ruled on the class, which is the reason to do it rather than to note it — a rule whose own record violates it is the strongest available argument for ignoring the rule. Note the asymmetry the six-times figure creates: five of the six `:81` citations are inside evidence tables and argument, so the repair is mechanical, but the sixth is inside §4's domain re-measurement where the row *count* matters. Note also that D117 RULING 6's justification was measured on `tasks/R.md`'s locators going stale in three days; this instance went stale in one, by an act D117's own discovered work asked for.
- **Row and entry authored 2026-08-11 by the wave-15 Q-domain registering lane from the orchestrator's brief.** **Verified here, and it corrects the brief's count**: the brief says *"four places"*; measured by grep over D117, there are **eight occurrences** of **three distinct locators** — `:81` at six sites, `:1-5` at one, `:3-5` at one. Verified directly: `README.md:81` today is **D75's** row, not D95's; `README.md:1-5` is the new register-versus-index paragraph and does not contain the quoted opening; the *"never by silently rewriting history"* sentence survives verbatim and now ends at line 11; and the index carries **106** rows. **Taken on report: nothing.** **Authored here, not reported: the id-keyed replacement for `:81`, the split between the dangling and the silent failure modes, the whole `Do`, the whole `Accept`, and the `Notes`.**

### Q206 — D117's placement rule anchors every correction section to a heading that now exists in zero documents
- Milestone: M2
- Size: XS
- Deps: after Q186
- Discovered by: **the D119 execution lane** (2026-08-11), immediately after deleting the sections the rule names.
- Problem: D117 §2.2 is the normative form of a correction section, and its first bullet reads *"**Placement**: after `## Outcome` and after `## Index row`, in date order."* D119 RULING 4 demoted every `## Index row (orchestrator applies at merge)` section into a single index-table line, and the executing lane deleted 41 of them and rewrote D86's and D87's numbered `Register row` sections in place. **`## Index row` now occurs in zero documents in the tree.** So half of a normative placement rule names an anchor that cannot be found, in the wave that obeyed the decision which removed it. Two correction sections written this wave — D94's *"§11's Q109 bullet miscounts A22's Accept rows"* and D116's *"the section numbers in the Amendment above"* — are already positioned by the surviving half of the rule, so the tree contains instances that satisfy a rule whose text no longer describes what they did.
- Do: Correct §2.2's placement bullet under D117's own machinery, and take the opportunity the deletion creates rather than merely patching around it. The anchor `## Index row` is gone permanently, so the bullet should name what a correction actually goes after in a post-D119 record — the end of the document, after `## Outcome` and after any earlier dated `## Amendment` or `## Correction`, in date order. State the date-order rule explicitly for the multi-correction case, which D116 now exercises and the current text only implies. Do not renumber §2.2 or any bullet inside it.
- Accept: §2.2's placement bullet names only anchors that exist; the rule covers the case of a second correction appended after a first, with date order stated rather than implied; D94's and D116's existing correction sections are consistent with the corrected rule rather than being retrospectively wrong; and the correction section records that the anchor was removed by D119 RULING 4 on 2026-08-11, so the change reads as a consequence rather than a re-decision (D117 §2.7 — a correction may not carry a new ruling, and this one must be careful to state a placement, not to re-rule placement).
- Notes: The interesting property is that this is a normative rule **invalidated by the act it authorised** — D119 could not have demoted the sections without D117's corrections machinery, and demoting them broke that machinery's own text. Worth stating in the correction, because the class recurs: **Q201** is a deletion rule with no subject, one namespace over. Do not merge this with **Q186**, which is about the *unexecuted instructions* inside those sections; that row's subject is gone and this one's is what the going created.
- **Row and entry authored 2026-08-11 by the wave-15 Q-domain registering lane from the orchestrator's ledger.** **Verified here, not taken on report**: §2.2's placement bullet, quoted verbatim; that `## Index row` and every other heading level of that name return **zero** matches across `docs/`; that D86 §10 and D87 §8 survive as numbered `Register row` sections marked *applied*; and that D94 and D116 each carry exactly one dated `## Correction` section written 2026-08-11, both positioned after `## Outcome`. **Taken on report: the figure of 41 deleted sections**, which this lane did not recount. **Authored here, not reported: the whole `Do`, including the date-order clause for a second correction and the warning that the repair must state a placement rather than re-rule it, the whole `Accept`, and the `Notes`.**

### Q207 — Nothing in the repository checks that a workflow file is parseable YAML
- Milestone: M2
- Size: S
- Deps: after Q182
- Discovered by: **the D124 execution lane** (2026-08-11), which wrote an unparseable `ci.yml` by following a decision's prescribed text and found `check-ci-shell.py` green over it.
- Problem: `scripts/check-ci-shell.py` is the only instrument that reads the workflows, and it is a **line scanner by ruling** — its docstring records that a YAML loader is refused because the `traceability` lane it rides must run with no third-party dependency. A line scanner cannot tell a parseable document from an unparseable one. Measured this wave: D124 §5.2(c) prescribed a step name containing a colon-space sequence, which makes `ci.yml` invalid YAML (`mapping values are not allowed here`), and `check-ci-shell.py` returned **rc 0** over the broken file. Nothing else in the tree parses a workflow. The failure would have been a red remote run on a file every lane depends on, caught here only because a human ran a loader by hand. **The venue is the whole difficulty and it is why this is a row rather than a fix**: D124 §3.2 refuses PyYAML *inside the traceability job* on the ground that a hash-pinned `pip install` there would consume the very property the job exists to have, and that refusal is correct. So the assertion needs a home that is neither `check-ci-shell.py` nor a new required context — the two obvious answers are the two that are ruled out.
- Do: Decide where a parseability assertion lives, treating the venue as the question. Three shapes worth pricing: put it in `scripts/local-gate.sh` only, so a contributor cannot push an unparseable workflow but CI does not assert it — cheap, and honest about being a local guard; put it in a job that already has a toolchain, where a loader costs nothing that job does not already pay; or assert it without a loader at all, by having the workflow-consuming step fail loudly rather than silently, which is the shape D124 used for the cargo-free property itself. Measure before choosing: the interpreter on the runner may already carry a loader, in which case the dependency argument does not apply and the first two shapes collapse into one. Record the decision at `check-ci-shell.py`, beside the docstring paragraph that already explains why *that* file will not do it, so the next reader does not re-propose the refused venue.
- Accept: A workflow file that does not parse turns something red before it reaches the remote; the instrument that does it is not `check-ci-shell.py` and does not add a required status context; the reason `check-ci-shell.py` is not the venue is recorded where a reader will meet it, citing D124 §3.2; and the guard is proven by a planted fault — a step name carrying the colon-space sequence, reproducing this wave's incident — judged by the message it prints and not by exit status alone, per `scripts/lib/red-arm.sh`.
- Notes: The near-miss is the argument. **The instruction that produced the break was in a decision document, not in a brief** — a lane following a ruling faithfully is exactly the reader least likely to second-guess it, which is why the class is not covered by review. Note the interaction with **Q182**: D124 was written to assert what a job may *not* do, and this row is what nobody asserts about the file that declares the jobs. Do not describe the fix as *"add PyYAML to the traceability lane"* — that is the shape D124 refused with a measured reason, and re-proposing it is the outcome this row's `Do` exists to prevent.
- **Row and entry authored 2026-08-11 by the wave-15 Q-domain registering lane from the orchestrator's ledger.** **Verified here, not taken on report**: `check-ci-shell.py` is a hand-rolled line scanner and its docstring's own paragraph records the no-third-party-dependency reason as the surviving one, with the superseded reason kept beside it; the repository contains **no** `import yaml`, `safe_load` or `yaml.load` anywhere outside a comment; `scripts/cargo-free.sh` reasons about the YAML in prose rather than parsing it; and `scripts/lib/red-arm.sh` states the judge-by-message rule this row's Accept cites. **Taken on report: the incident itself** — the prescribed step name, the loader's exact error text, and `check-ci-shell.py` returning rc 0 over the broken file — which this lane did not reproduce, because `ci.yml` is another lane's surface this wave. **Authored here, not reported: the three venue shapes and the instruction to measure the runner's interpreter before choosing, the whole `Accept`, and the `Notes`.**

### Q208 — Three milestones have a declared gate and no row that executes it
- Milestone: M2
- Size: S
- Deps: after Q183
- Discovered by: **the D122 lane** (2026-08-11), while ruling what moves `CURRENT_MILESTONE` and finding the answer has no owner for three milestones.
- Problem: `TODO.md` rule 4 makes the gate, not the task count, a milestone's exit criterion, and every milestone section opens with a `Gate:` clause list. **Only two of the six gates are task rows.** `Q14` executed M0's and is closed; `Q32` scripts M4's and `Q34` executes it. Pre-M0, M1, M2 and M3 have a declared gate and no row — and M1's, M2's and M3's are the ones that still matter, because M2's review is next and is owned by nobody. This is the structural cause behind **Q183**: the one milestone whose review was a task row is the one whose constant was correct at the end of it, and `CURRENT_MILESTONE` sat at `M0` through M1's shipping because no row said to move it. It also blocks D122's own edit set: adding the constant-bump to `TODO.md` rule 4 makes rule 4 a second normative statement of a procedure that does not exist, which is this row's defect reproduced by its own fix.
- Do: Register M2's and M3's gate-execution rows and give each the same shape `Q14` had — run the gate, record the evidence, and **move `CURRENT_MILESTONE`**, named as a step rather than left to the constant's comment. Take the `Gate:` clause list at the head of the milestone's section as the checklist rather than restating it, so the two cannot drift. Decide separately and record whether M1's missing row is worth a retrospective one: M1 has shipped, so its value is a record of what was verified rather than a gate, and inventing a closed row for it may be worse than a note saying it had none. Do not fold the constant-bump into rule 4 until at least M2's and M3's rows exist.
- Accept: M2 and M3 each have a task row whose `Do` runs that milestone's declared gate and whose `Accept` includes moving `CURRENT_MILESTONE` to that milestone; the rows are cited from `TODO.md` rule 4 rather than duplicating its text; D122's rule-4 edit lands only after those rows exist; and the disposition of M1's absence is recorded either as a row or as a stated reason, not left as a gap that looks like an oversight.
- Notes: The measured shape is the argument: two of six gates are rows, and *the two that are rows are the two whose milestones are the only ones with a correct constant at the review boundary*. That is close to a controlled experiment and should be quoted rather than paraphrased. Note the honest limit — Pre-M0 also has a declared gate and no row, so the figure is two-of-six rather than two-of-five, but Pre-M0's review is historical and registering it retrospectively buys nothing; this row deliberately scopes to M1, M2 and M3 and says why. Read beside **Q179**, which is the same absence one level down: milestone-section task counts nothing computes.
- **Row and entry authored 2026-08-11 by the wave-15 Q-domain registering lane from the orchestrator's ledger.** **Verified here, independently of D122's own census**: `grep -nE "Execute the M[0-4]|Script the M[0-4]" TODO.md` returns exactly three rows across two milestones — `Q14` (M0, closed), `Q32` and `Q34` (M4, both open); the six milestone sections are Pre-M0, M0, M1, M2, M3 and M4, and `Gate:` clause lists sit at Pre-M0, M1, M2, M3 and M4 with M0's expressed as the section heading's *"ends at the format-v1 freeze"*; and `CURRENT_MILESTONE` reads `"M1"`. **Verified and it refines the ledger**: the ledger says *"M1, M2 and M3 have none"*, which is true but is three of the four rowless gates — **Pre-M0 has none either**, so the ratio is two of six and this entry states it that way. **Taken on report: D122's ruling that the bump belongs to the gate row**, which this lane did not re-argue. **Authored here, not reported: the instruction to cite the section's `Gate:` list rather than restate it, the separate disposition for M1, the ordering constraint against D122's rule-4 edit, the whole `Accept`, and the `Notes`.**

### Q209 — The decision corpus is outside every sweep, so no citation inside a record is checked by anything
- Milestone: M2
- Size: M
- Deps: after Q181
- Discovered by: **the D119 execution lane** (2026-08-11), after writing 41 new decision-to-decision citations that nothing reads.
- Problem: `CITATION_SCAN` is `["crates", "docs/format", "docs/testing", "scripts"]` plus nine named root files, and `docs/decisions/` is deliberately excluded on **D109 §3.3** — a record's job includes proposing work the orchestrator may number, renumber or decline, so sweeping the corpus for task ids fails on ids that were only ever proposals. That reason is sound and this row does not reopen it. **It covers only one of the two citation kinds the corpus carries.** D117 §1.9 counted **2938** `D<n> §<n>` citations across 63 decisions, and every one of them is a pointer into a document whose sections nothing verifies; the corpus additionally carries file-and-line locators, which D117 RULING 6 forbids in corrections and permits everywhere else. Both kinds are checked by nothing, and the cost is now measured rather than hypothetical: **Q205** found all three of D117's `docs/decisions/README.md` locators stale, one resolving silently to a different decision's row; **Q222** found five section citations in D116's Amendment, none of which ever resolved; and the D119 act wrote 41 more citations into a file that has no checker at all. The exclusion's reason protects the task half; the section half inherited the exclusion without ever being argued.
- Do: Decide the section-citation half on its own merits rather than as a consequence of D109 §3.3. The cheap instrument is narrow and does not require sweeping the corpus for task ids: for every `D<n> §<n>` citation, assert that `D<n>` has a record and that a heading numbered `<n>` exists inside it. That is a heading match, not a semantic one, and it would have caught D116's Amendment and half of D117's problem while staying silent on proposed-but-unnumbered task ids — which is the whole of D109 §3.3's concern. Price it before building it: measure how many of the 2938 fail today, because a check that arrives red on hundreds of citations is a check nobody turns on. If the count is large, rule whether the check is introduced with a recorded baseline or whether the corpus is repaired first. Record the outcome at `CITATION_SCAN`, beside D109 §3.3's reason, so the next reader sees two decisions rather than one exclusion.
- Accept: The `D<n> §<n>` citation form inside `docs/decisions/` is either checked, with the measured count of current failures recorded at the point the check lands, or explicitly ruled out of scope with a reason written at the constant beside D109 §3.3's; the ruling distinguishes the *task-id* half (settled) from the *section-locator* half (this row); a planted citation of a section that does not exist either turns something red or is explained by the recorded reason; and the decision does not widen `CITATION_SCAN` to include `docs/decisions/`, since that would re-run D109 §3.3's failure.
- Notes: The reason to build the narrow check rather than the general one is that **the general one is already refused and the refusal is right**. Note the direction of harm measured this wave: a stale section citation does not dangle, it resolves — to a heading that exists and says something else — which is the failure mode a reader cannot detect. That is why heading existence, though weak, is worth more here than it looks: it catches the *renumber* and the *never-resolved* cases, which are the two this project has actually produced. Do not merge with **Q181** (the nine `docs/` root `.md` files, a different surface with a different question) or **Q210**.
- **Row and entry authored 2026-08-11 by the wave-15 Q-domain registering lane from the orchestrator's ledger.** **Verified here, not taken on report**: `CITATION_SCAN`'s exact membership and the comment block above it, including D109 §3.3's recorded reason quoted in the constant's own words; that `docs/decisions/` appears in neither sweep; that D117's three `README.md` locators and D116's Amendment's five section citations are all currently wrong, each measured separately for **Q205** and **Q222**; and that the `decision-index` check now in the script asserts row presence, order, status and date, and reads no citation. **Taken on report: D117 §1.9's figure of 2938 citations across 63 decisions**, which this lane did not recount, and the figure of 41 citations written by the D119 act. **Authored here, not reported: the split of the exclusion into a settled task-id half and an unargued section half, the heading-existence instrument, the instruction to price the current failure count before building, the whole `Accept`, and the `Notes`.**

### Q210 — Three directories carry task citations under no scan root, two of them with no recorded reason
- Milestone: M2
- Size: S
- Deps: after Q190
- Discovered by: **the D123 lane** (2026-08-11), while ruling that a literal `CITATION_SCAN` entry is read as itself.
- Problem: The comment block above `CITATION_SCAN` is unusually disciplined: it records a reason for every deliberate exclusion — `docs/decisions/` on D109 §3.3, `.github/` on D116 §2 (c) and called *"a close call"*, `MVP-SPEC.orig.md` and `SPEC-REVIEW.md` as preserved artifacts measured green before exclusion, `TODO.md` and `tasks/*.md` as the register and the working notes, and a scan root of `"."` refused with its consequences enumerated. **`fuzz/` and `probes/` appear nowhere in it.** They are outside `CITATION_SCAN` and no sentence says why, in a constant where every other exclusion is argued. `.cargo/` is a third case and a different one: it is a dot-*directory*, so the directory walk cannot reach it and the literal-file rule D123 added reaches dot-*files* only by being named — and `.cargo/config.toml` carries four task ids (`C5`, `C9`, `C11`, `P14`) and one decision id (`D18`) governing the wasm32 test runner, which is a live normative surface, not a dropping. In a comment block whose whole design is that an exclusion with a reason is a decision and an exclusion without one is an accident, three exclusions are currently accidents.
- Do: Give each of the three a reason or a root, and do not treat them as one question. For `fuzz/` and `probes/`, measure the citation content first — `scripts/fuzz.sh:98-103` hid `A71` from the task half until D116 §1.1 found it, and these are the same kind of surface one directory over. For `.cargo/config.toml`, the answer under D123's per-entry rule is a one-line addition to `CITATION_SCAN` naming the file literally, which costs one file and no collateral; the question to rule is whether the *dot-directory* case gets a general answer or whether naming files one at a time is the accepted cost. Whatever is decided, write it into the comment block in the shape the existing entries use, so the block stays a list of decisions.
- Accept: `fuzz/`, `probes/` and `.cargo/` are each either inside `CITATION_SCAN` with the widening's measured cost recorded, or outside it with a reason written at the constant in the same shape as D109 §3.3's and D116 §2 (c)'s; the dot-directory case is answered as a rule rather than as an instance; and a planted unresolvable id in `.cargo/config.toml` either turns the lane red or is explained by the recorded reason.
- Notes: This is **Q181**'s question on three more surfaces and it should be scheduled with it, not merged into it — Q181 is nine `.md` files inside a swept parent, this is three directories the walk never enters, and the two have different cheapest answers. Note that the D123 ruling makes the `.cargo/config.toml` half nearly free and that this is the argument *for* doing it: the file is small, hand-edited, rarely touched, and its ids point at the wasm32 runner wiring — the rarity that hid it is also what makes a stale id there survive.
- **Row and entry authored 2026-08-11 by the wave-15 Q-domain registering lane from the orchestrator's ledger.** **Verified here, not taken on report**: the comment block above `CITATION_SCAN`, read in full — it records reasons for `docs/decisions/`, `.github/`, `MVP-SPEC.orig.md`, `SPEC-REVIEW.md`, `TODO.md`/`tasks/*.md` and the refused `"."` root, and **contains no occurrence of `fuzz` or `probes` at all**; `CITATION_SCAN`'s exact membership; the two-branch literal-versus-directory loop D123 added, confirming that a named file is read unfiltered and that a dot-directory is unreachable by the walk; and `.cargo/config.toml`'s contents, which carry `C5`, `C9`, `C11`, `P14` and `D18`. **Verified and it refines the ledger**: the ledger reports *"4 task ids"* in `.cargo/config.toml`, which is exact, and does not mention the decision id, which is also there. **Authored here, not reported: the grouping of the three under one row, the distinction between a directory the walk skips and a dot-directory it cannot express, the instruction to measure `fuzz/` and `probes/` before ruling, the whole `Accept`, and the `Notes`.**

### Q211 — Inside the swept `crates/` tree, two suffixes carry citations the filter drops
- Milestone: M2
- Size: S
- Deps: after Q190
- Discovered by: **the D123 lane** (2026-08-11), which measured the widening and deliberately declined to answer this as a side effect.
- Problem: `crates` is a `CITATION_SCAN` directory entry, so it is walked recursively and filtered by `CITATION_SUFFIXES = {.rs, .md, .json, .py, .sh, .mjs}`. Two kinds of file inside it carry real citations and neither suffix is in the set. The six member `Cargo.toml` files carry **62 distinct task ids**, all allocated and none above ceiling — a larger citation surface than the three root files **Q190** was written about. Nine `crates/**/*.txt` files carry **19 decision-id tokens**, and seven of the nine are golden CLI snapshots, which makes the question there a different one: a decision id inside a committed snapshot of user-facing help text is a citation in *shipped copy*, and a sweep that requires it to resolve is asserting something about a frozen artifact rather than about a document. D123 ruled on the *root* manifest by naming it literally and recorded the resulting asymmetry in its own voice — the root `Cargo.toml` is swept because it is named, the six member manifests are not because `.toml` is not a directory-walk suffix — and stated that whether `crates/` should gain `.toml` is *"a separate question with its own measurement, deliberately not answered as a side effect of this one"*. This row is that question.
- Do: Answer the two suffixes separately, because they have different failure modes. For `.toml`: measure the widening (D123 measured it at 359 → 365 files, six manifests, zero collateral and zero new failures) and rule whether the six join the sweep — the measurement is already taken, so this is a decision rather than an investigation. For `.txt`: rule **snapshots yes or no**, not `.txt` yes or no. A golden snapshot's contents are pinned by a bless mechanism, so requiring its ids to resolve creates a second authority over frozen bytes; the two non-snapshot files are `proptest-regressions`, which are machine-written. Whichever way each goes, write the reason at `CITATION_SUFFIXES` rather than at `CITATION_SCAN`, since this is the directory-rule half of D123's distinction.
- Accept: The six member manifests are either swept, with the measured cost recorded, or excluded with a reason at the constant; the `.txt` decision is expressed as a rule about snapshots and states what it means for a citation to live inside blessed bytes; a planted unresolvable id in a member manifest either turns the lane red or is explained; and `CITATION_SUFFIXES` carries the reasoning for whatever it now excludes, since D123 made that constant the directory rule and left it otherwise unannotated.
- Notes: The strongest argument for the manifests is one D123 created rather than found: after its ruling, the root manifest **is** swept, so the six members are excluded on a distinction — being reached by a walk rather than by a name — that has nothing to do with what they contain. That is the shape **Q176**'s hazard sentence was written about, one directory in. Note the honest counterweight for the `.txt` half: seven of the nine are exactly where a citation is *supposed* to be user-visible, and a check that forces them to resolve makes every id retirement a snapshot re-bless.
- **Row and entry authored 2026-08-11 by the wave-15 Q-domain registering lane from the orchestrator's ledger.** **Verified here, not taken on report**: `CITATION_SUFFIXES`'s membership; that `crates` is a directory entry and is therefore filtered; the R7 paragraph at `CITATION_SCAN` recording the root-versus-member asymmetry and deferring the `.toml` question, quoted from the constant's own comment; and the nine `crates/**/*.txt` files, enumerated — two `proptest-regressions/*.txt` and **seven** `crates/antseal-cli/tests/snapshots/*.txt`, which matches the ledger's split exactly. **Taken on report: the 62-distinct-task-id figure across the six manifests and the 19 decision-id tokens in the nine `.txt` files**, and D123's 359 → 365 measurement; this lane re-measured neither. **Authored here, not reported: the framing of the `.txt` half as *snapshots yes or no*, the observation that requiring resolution inside blessed bytes creates a second authority over them, the instruction to write the reason at `CITATION_SUFFIXES` rather than `CITATION_SCAN`, the whole `Accept`, and the `Notes`.**

### Q212 — The citation sweep loop is written out three times and every widening has to be applied to all three by hand
- Milestone: M2
- Size: S
- Deps: after Q190
- Discovered by: **the D123 lane and the Q184 lane independently** (2026-08-11), the first predicting two copies and the second landing the third.
- Problem: The loop that turns `CITATION_SCAN` into a list of files — the `for sub in CITATION_SCAN` walk, the literal-versus-directory branch, the suffix filter, the `target`/`node_modules`/`__pycache__` prune, the self-exclusion and the decode guard — exists **three times** in `scripts/check-traceability.py`: in the decision sweep, in the task sweep, and now in the self-test's scratch-tree fixture helper. D116 R2 requires the first two to carry the branch *byte-identically*, and the only mechanism enforcing it is a comment telling a human to read the other copy. D123's edit set was written for two sites; the third landed from a different lane in the same wave and had to be patched by hand, and its docstring says it reads the constants *"so a narrowing of either moves this fixture with the check"* — an intent the copied filter defeats, because a narrowing of the *filter* does not move with it. Three copies of a rule held in step by prose is the exact defect **D112** spent a wave on and the reason D116 R1 refused a second constant.
- Do: Extract one `iter_citation_files()` generator yielding every path both sweeps read, and call it from all three sites. Keep the two constants as they are — this is not a constants change and must not become one. The self-test fixture helper is the site that most needs it: it exists to prove a narrowing moves the fixture, and it can only do that if it shares the code the narrowing changes. Preserve the self-exclusion and the decode guard inside the generator so no caller can forget them, and keep D116 R2's byte-identity requirement satisfied trivially rather than by inspection. Do not change what is swept; a refactor that moves the file count is a different act and must be measured as one.
- Accept: One generator is the single expression of the walk; all three call sites use it; the swept file count is unchanged before and after, measured and recorded; D116 R2's byte-identity comment is replaced by the fact rather than restated; and the self-test's fixture helper demonstrably follows a narrowing of `CITATION_SUFFIXES` as well as of `CITATION_SCAN`, which is what its docstring already claims and cannot currently do.
- Notes: This row's value is measurable and was measured: D123 was designed as a two-site edit and executed as a three-site one, and the only thing that stopped the third copy diverging silently was a docstring paragraph a human happened to read. That is the *near-miss* form of the class, and it is worth recording that the near-miss came from two lanes in the same wave rather than from six months of drift.
- **Row and entry authored 2026-08-11 by the wave-15 Q-domain registering lane, merging one item reported by the D123 lane and one by the Q184 lane.** **Verified here, and it confirms the orchestrator's own count**: `grep -c 'path.suffix not in CITATION_SUFFIXES' scripts/check-traceability.py` returns **3**, at the decision sweep, the task sweep and the self-test fixture helper; the two production copies carry the same two-branch comment; and the D116 R2 byte-identity instruction is present as a comment at both. **Taken on report: the fixture helper's docstring claim** about narrowing, quoted from the ledger rather than re-read in full. **Authored here, not reported: the generator's shape, the requirement to hold the file count constant across the refactor, the observation that the fixture helper is the site with the strongest need, the whole `Accept`, and the `Notes`.**

### Q213 — Both citation sweeps report a file count and nothing asserts it
- Milestone: M2
- Size: XS
- Deps: after Q190
- Discovered by: **the D123 lane** (2026-08-11), after the third partial widening in three waves.
- Problem: The green line of each citation check names how many files it read — *"105 distinct decisions cited across 365 files"* at the current tree. That number is the check's only statement about its own **domain**, and nothing compares it to anything. A widening that silently reaches nothing, an exclusion that silently reaches too much, or a typo in a `CITATION_SCAN` entry all produce a green line with a different number and no other signal. Three partial widenings have landed in three waves — Q176's root files, D123's literal-entry rule, and whatever Q210 and Q211 decide — each moving the count by a handful, and the record of what the count *should* be exists only in commit messages and decision bodies.
- Do: Assert a floor rather than an exact count. An exact number is a fixture that ordinary work breaks weekly and is the class **Q184** exists about; a floor — *"the sweep reads at least N files"* — goes red on the failure that matters (a scan root that stopped reaching anything) and stays silent on the growth that does not. Derive the floor from the constants where possible rather than pinning a literal, and if a literal is unavoidable, record beside it the measurement and date, in the form D123's own comment already uses. Consider asserting per-root rather than in total: a total floor cannot tell a root that went to zero from a root that grew.
- Accept: A `CITATION_SCAN` entry that reaches zero files turns something red; the assertion is a floor or a per-root non-emptiness rather than an exact count; if any literal is pinned, it carries the command that measured it and the date; and the check's green line states the assertion it satisfies rather than merely reporting a number.
- Notes: The cheapest correct version of this may be per-root non-emptiness, which needs no number at all — *"every entry in `CITATION_SCAN` reached at least one file"* — and that alone would have caught the pre-D123 state, where three named root files were scan roots that read nothing. Worth pricing that first, because a rule with no constant in it cannot rot. Read beside **Q174**, which is the same observation about the self-test's summaries: of two numbers a tool prints, the one that scales is the one that stays true.
- **Row and entry authored 2026-08-11 by the wave-15 Q-domain registering lane from the orchestrator's ledger.** **Verified here, not taken on report**: the green line's format string, which interpolates a live `len()` and is compared to nothing; that no assertion anywhere in the script reads that count; and that the pre-D123 hazard the `Notes` cites is recorded in the constant's own comment — naming a root file whose suffix was outside the set *"adds a scan root that reads nothing at all"*, measured at the time as moving the count 359 → 359. **Taken on report: the count of three partial widenings in three waves.** **Authored here, not reported: the floor-versus-exact-count argument, the per-root non-emptiness alternative and the recommendation to price it first, the whole `Do`, the whole `Accept`, and the `Notes`.**

### Q214 — The self-test's mutate-run-revert has no `try`/`finally`, so one exception corrupts every later case on that file
- Milestone: M2
- Size: XS
- Deps: after Q191
- Discovered by: **the Q184 lane** (2026-08-11), while rewriting every fixture in the case table.
- Problem: Each `--self-test` case writes a mutation into the staged tree, runs the check as a subprocess, and writes the original back — three bare statements with no `try`/`finally` between the first write and the third. If anything raises in between, the staged tree keeps the mutation. The harness is otherwise carefully defended in this exact direction: a mutation that matches nothing is a hard failure rather than a skip, a missing target is a hard failure rather than a skip, and a traceback from the checked subprocess is caught and reported as a distinct verdict — so the *checked* process falling over is handled and the *checking* process falling over is not. The window is narrow, and that is the point: the surviving mutation does not announce itself, it makes the next case on the same file run against a tree that is not the tree, and under D120's delta arms a leftover mutation shows up as a green arm reporting changed findings — a message naming the wrong case and the wrong mutation. That is **Q197**'s class inside the instrument whose whole job is to be trustworthy when everything else is not.
- Do: Wrap the mutate-run-revert in `try`/`finally` with the revert in the `finally`, and make the revert's own failure loud. Do not widen it into a general exception handler: the harness's design is that unexpected exceptions are *fatal*, and this change must preserve that — the `finally` restores the file and the exception still propagates. Consider asserting the revert took effect (re-read and compare) for the cases whose target is shared by several cases, which is where a silent leftover costs the most.
- Accept: An exception raised between the mutation write and the revert leaves the staged file byte-identical to its pre-mutation state; the exception still terminates the run rather than being swallowed; a planted fault demonstrates it — raise inside the subprocess call for one case and show the following case on the same file still behaving as it does today; and the revert's own failure produces a message rather than a bare traceback, in the shape the staging retry already uses.
- Notes: The scope is honestly small and the entry should not oversell it: `subprocess.run` without `check=True` does not raise on a non-zero exit, and Q184's fixture helpers raise *before* the first write rather than after it, so the reachable causes are an OS-level failure of the subprocess call itself and an interrupt. It is registered because the cost when it does happen is a **misattributed** failure in the one instrument every other verdict this wave was judged by, and because the fix is four lines. Read beside **Q202**, which is the same preference stated the other way: a precondition judged by an abort rather than by a reported case.
- **Row and entry authored 2026-08-11 by the wave-15 Q-domain registering lane from the orchestrator's ledger.** **Verified here, not taken on report**: the three bare statements in the case loop, read in the working tree, with no `try` between them; that the surrounding branches — no-op guard, missing target, subprocess traceback — are each hard failures with their own message, so the asymmetry described above is real; that `findings()` calls `subprocess.run` without `check=True` and returns `None` on a traceback rather than raising, which is what bounds the window; and that D120's delta arms are live, so a leftover mutation surfaces as a changed-findings message rather than as a bare non-zero exit. **Taken on report: nothing.** **Authored here, not reported: the requirement that the exception still propagate, the suggestion to assert the revert on shared targets, the honest bounding of the reachable causes, the whole `Accept`, and the `Notes`.**

### Q215 — No self-test case ever passes `--milestone`, so the status gate is only ever exercised at the constant
- Milestone: M2
- Size: XS
- Deps: after Q184
- Discovered by: **the Q184 lane** (2026-08-11), while sweeping the case table for borrowed literals.
- Problem: `--milestone` exists so that a lane can ask *"would M2 pass today?"* without moving `CURRENT_MILESTONE`, and D118 used it to turn an eleven-row failure into a one-row finding. **Every `--self-test` subprocess invokes the matrix check bare.** So the flag's override path — the code that takes the argument, prefers it over the constant, and gates the cumulative row set on it — is exercised by no case, in a harness whose stated purpose is that every check can be shown to go red. The gate is proven only at whatever `CURRENT_MILESTONE` happens to read, which is the one value the flag exists to bypass. The coupling got tighter this wave, not looser: the status-gate fixtures now read `gated_milestones(CURRENT_MILESTONE)` to derive their target, so the fixture and the thing it tests are computed from the same constant.
- Do: Add a case that passes `--milestone` with a value other than the constant's and requires the check to bite on a row the bare run does not gate. The natural shape is a red case at a later milestone over a row that is `deferred` there and ungated today, which is exactly the state D118 adjudicated and left in the tree. Keep the derivation Q184 established rather than pinning a status literal, and make the case's target selection independent of `CURRENT_MILESTONE` — otherwise the new case inherits the coupling it exists to break. Whoever implements D122's `under_review` rename must know this case exists, since it will be the first thing to read the renamed parameter.
- Accept: A self-test case invokes `--milestone` with a value that differs from `CURRENT_MILESTONE` and requires the named check to report a finding it does not report on the bare run; the case's target is derived, not pinned to a status literal; and the derivation does not read `CURRENT_MILESTONE`, so the case cannot go vacuous when the constant moves.
- Notes: This is the flag half of **Q183**'s finding and it should be read with it: Q51's cumulative gate was *correct and unarmed*, and the reason nobody noticed for eight days is that the only path anyone ever runs is the default one. An override no test takes is an override that will be wrong the first time somebody needs it — and D118 is the wave where somebody needed it. Note that the new case is a genuine widening of the harness's surface, so it should be counted in whatever asserts the case inventory (**Q217**).
- **Row and entry authored 2026-08-11 by the wave-15 Q-domain registering lane from the orchestrator's ledger.** **Verified here, not taken on report**: `--milestone` occurs in `scripts/check-traceability.py` only in the argparse declaration and in comments — **no self-test case passes it**, and every self-test subprocess invocation is built from a bare check name; and `CURRENT_MILESTONE` reads `"M1"`, so the gate is exercised at M1 alone. **Taken on report: the new coupling** — that the status fixtures read `gated_milestones(CURRENT_MILESTONE)` — which this lane confirmed exists as a call but did not trace through both fixtures. **Authored here, not reported: the requirement that the new case's target derivation not read the constant, the note to D122's implementer, the whole `Accept`, and the `Notes`.**

### Q216 — The self-test's closing assertion measures the live tree and judges the staged one
- Milestone: M2
- Size: XS
- Deps: after Q191
- Discovered by: **the D120 lane and the round-2 script lane independently** (2026-08-11), the second finding that git staging made it newly reachable.
- Problem: The floor/ceiling assertion at the end of `--self-test` is a two-part instrument: it first asserts that six non-citation tokens (`A0`, `C0`, `F0`, `P384`, `U256`, `F64`) are **present** in the swept tree, so that the second part cannot pass by absence, and then requires that a `--task-citations` run report none of them. **The presence half calls `sweep_task_surfaces()` in-process, which reads the live `ROOT`; the reporting half runs a subprocess against the staged copy.** The two halves therefore describe two different trees. Today that is harmless because the staged tree is a faithful copy of the live one. Under D120's git-list staging it stops being harmless in a specific, reachable way: a token that lives only in a git-ignored file is present in `ROOT` and absent from the staged tree, so the anti-vacuity half passes on a file the assertion half cannot see. It is one missing parameter, and the reason to fix it now rather than when it bites is that the assertion's whole design is to refuse to pass by absence.
- Do: Point the presence half at the staged tree, by parameterising the sweep's root or by running it as a subprocess against the staged copy the way the reporting half already does. Keep the traceback gate D120 installed in place of the exit-code gate — that is a separate and correct change and this row must not disturb it. State at the assertion which tree each half reads, since the two-tree structure is what made this invisible.
- Accept: Both halves of the closing assertion read the same tree; a token present in the live tree and absent from the staged one fails the presence half rather than passing it; the traceback gate is unchanged; and the assertion's comment names the tree it measures, so the next reader does not have to infer it from a call signature.
- Notes: Registered as XS and harmless-today deliberately — the entry should not claim a live defect it does not have. Its value is that D120's staging change moved this from *theoretically wrong* to *reachable*, and the reachable case is silent: the assertion still prints its ok line. Read beside **Q202** and **Q195**, which are the same preference for an instrument that cannot pass for the wrong reason.
