- **Row and entry authored 2026-08-11 by the wave-15 Q-domain registering lane, merging one item reported by the D120 lane and one by the round-2 script lane.** **Verified here, not taken on report**: the presence half calls `sweep_task_surfaces()` with no argument, and that function walks `ROOT` — the module-level live-tree constant — while the reporting half invokes `scripts/check-traceability.py` **inside the staged tree**; the six tokens and both failure messages, read in the working tree; and that D120's traceback gate has landed and carries its own explanatory comment. **Taken on report: nothing.** **Authored here, not reported: the git-ignored-file mechanism that makes the divergence reachable, the instruction to state which tree each half reads, the whole `Accept`, and the `Notes`.**

### Q217 — "Every check has at least one green arm" is load-bearing and asserted nowhere
- Milestone: M2
- Size: XS
- Deps: after Q191
- Discovered by: **the round-2 script lane** (2026-08-11), after adding two red cases without disturbing the property by luck rather than by mechanism.
- Problem: `--self-test`'s case table is a mix of red arms (a mutation the named check must bite) and green arms (a mutation it must **not** bite, pinning a tolerance). D120 made the green arms strictly stronger by turning them into deltas against a baseline: a green arm now asserts that the findings do not *change*, so a widening that makes a check report **fewer** findings fails it, where under exit-code validation it would not. That makes green-arm coverage a real property of the harness — **and nothing asserts that every check has one.** The current table happens to cover all six checks. D123 then added two red cases and left the property intact by not touching it; the round-2 lane recorded that as luck. A check that loses its last green arm loses its tolerance pin silently, and the loss is invisible because the run still prints an ok line per remaining case.
- Do: Assert the property in the harness itself, next to the case table, in the same place the case inventory is already known. The cheap form is a derived check over the table before the loop runs: every check name that appears with `expect == "red"` must also appear with `expect == "green"`, failing with a message naming the check that lost its arm. Do not express it as a count, which rots; express it as coverage, which does not. State at the assertion *why* the property matters — the delta semantics — so a later simplification of the arms does not quietly remove its reason.
- Accept: Deleting the last green case for any check turns `--self-test` red with a message naming that check and the property; the assertion is derived from the case table rather than from a pinned list of check names; and the reason the property is load-bearing — that a green arm under delta semantics pins a tolerance in both directions — is recorded where the assertion lives.
- Notes: Do not merge with **Q213**, which is the same shape (a property the tool relies on and does not assert) in the citation sweeps rather than in the harness. Note the honest framing: nothing is broken today, and the row exists because the property became load-bearing *this wave* — before D120, a green arm asserted only exit 0 and losing one cost much less. A property that quietly changed weight is exactly the kind that has no assertion, because the assertion would have been written when it was cheap.
- **Row and entry authored 2026-08-11 by the wave-15 Q-domain registering lane from the orchestrator's ledger.** **Verified here, not taken on report**: the green arm's delta semantics, read in the working tree — a green case fails on `added or removed`, and the comment above it states the *fewer-findings* consequence in the script's own words and warns against simplifying it back to a truthiness test; and that no assertion anywhere in `self_test()` reads the case table for per-check arm coverage. **Taken on report: that the current table covers all six checks with a green arm, and that D123's two additions preserved that by luck** — this lane did not enumerate the table's arms per check. **Authored here, not reported: the derived-coverage form of the assertion, the instruction to express it as coverage rather than as a count, the whole `Accept`, and the `Notes`.**

### Q218 — The gate lane asserts one cause for every non-zero self-test, and returns before the half that names the real one
- Milestone: M2
- Size: XS
- Deps: after Q191
- Discovered by: **the D120 lane** (2026-08-11), which reproduced the inverse annotation twice.
- Problem: `lane_traceability` in `scripts/ci-lanes.sh` runs `--self-test` first, and on failure prints `::error::traceability self-test FAILED — a check stayed green over corrupted input, so a green run below would prove nothing` and **returns**. Two things are wrong with that and they compound. The annotation asserts a *cause*, and it is one of at least seven distinct failure classes the self-test can now report — a check that added no finding on a red case, a check whose findings changed on a green case, a crash on a mutation, a crash on the unmutated baseline, a mutation that matched nothing, a missing target, and the closing assertion — plus three informational lines. For most of those the annotation states the inverse of what happened: a check that *crashed* did not stay green, and a check that went red where it should have stayed green is the opposite failure. And because the lane returns, the flagless half never runs, so the accurate finding — the one sentence naming the actual defect — is never printed at all. D120 measured this on a planted tree: the planted id appeared **zero** times in the whole lane output while the annotation confidently named a cause that did not apply.
- Do: Relay rather than assert. The annotation should reproduce the self-test's own message instead of stating a diagnosis, and the lane should run the flagless half regardless, reporting both results, so a reader gets the accurate finding even when the harness half fails. Keep the lane's exit status failing if either half fails — this is about what is printed, not about what is tolerated. Apply the same reading to `lane_ci_shell`, which carries the identical shape one function down.
- Accept: A non-zero `--self-test` produces an annotation containing the self-test's own message rather than a fixed diagnosis; the flagless half runs and its output appears even when the self-test half failed; the lane still exits non-zero if either half fails; and `lane_ci_shell`'s annotation is dispositioned in the same act, either corrected or explicitly left with a reason.
- Notes: This is **Q197** — an instrument that fires correctly and names the wrong cause — with an aggravating factor: the wrong name is printed *instead of* the right one rather than beside it. Note the direction the harness has moved: D120's delta arms made the self-test able to distinguish far more failure classes than the annotation was written for, so the annotation's accuracy has fallen as the instrument's has risen. Recording that is worth more than the fix, because the same shape will recur the next time an instrument gains resolution.
- **Row and entry authored 2026-08-11 by the wave-15 Q-domain registering lane, merging one item reported by the D120 lane and one by the round-2 script lane.** **Verified here, not taken on report**: `lane_traceability`'s body, read in `scripts/ci-lanes.sh` — the annotation's exact wording, the `return 1` before the flagless invocation, and that `lane_ci_shell` carries the same shape with its own fixed diagnosis; and the self-test's distinct failure messages, counted in the working tree, which is what makes the annotation wrong for most of them. **Taken on report: D120's planted-tree measurement** that the planted id appeared zero times in the lane output; this lane did not re-run it. **Authored here, not reported: the relay-rather-than-assert instruction, the requirement to run the flagless half regardless, the extension to `lane_ci_shell`, the whole `Accept`, and the `Notes`' observation about resolution outrunning the annotation.**

### Q219 — What the self-test stages depends on a machine-local file nobody reviews
- Milestone: M2
- Size: XS
- Deps: after Q191
- Discovered by: **the D120 lane** (2026-08-11), which found a lock file staged on every local run.
- Problem: D120 replaced `copytree` with a git-listed staging set — `git ls-files` unioned with `git ls-files --others --exclude-standard` — which is correct and closed the race. It also made the staged tree's contents depend on **`.git/info/exclude`**, because that is part of git's standard exclude set. That file is per-clone, untracked, unreviewed and absent from any other developer's machine. In this working tree it holds ten entries, all under `.claude/` — a scheduled-task lock, a state file, a routines directory, worktrees, checkpoints, a mailbox, an agent registry, agent-local memory, a first-run marker and a daemon state file — none of which is in `.gitignore`. So the harness's input set is currently governed by two ignore surfaces, one reviewed and one not, and only the reviewed one is visible to anyone reading the repository. The immediate consequence is benign — the lock file is now correctly excluded — and the durable one is not: a contributor whose `.git/info/exclude` differs stages a different tree, and a file ignored **only** locally is invisible to the check on one machine and swept on another.
- Do: Decide which ignore surface governs the harness, and make the divergence visible either way. Two shapes: move the entries that are genuinely project-wide into `.gitignore` so the reviewed file is complete, leaving `.git/info/exclude` for what is truly personal; or keep the split and have the staging **report** when `.git/info/exclude` contributed to the exclusion set, so a lane reading the output can see that its tree was shaped by an unreviewed file. Do not simply drop `--exclude-standard`: that would restage the lock file and reopen the race D120 closed.
- Accept: Either `.gitignore` names every exclusion the harness relies on, or the staging states when a local-only rule shaped the staged set; the reason for whichever split survives is recorded where the staging is built; and the same run on a clone with an empty `.git/info/exclude` either stages the same set or says that it did not.
- Notes: The general shape is **a reviewed rule and an unreviewed rule with the same effect**, which this project has met before in the `CITATION_SUFFIXES`-versus-`CITATION_SCAN` pair D116 R1 refused to duplicate. Note the honest limit: the entries currently in `.git/info/exclude` are all tooling droppings, so nothing is being hidden today; the row is about the input set of a verification instrument being determined by a file that is not in the repository. Read beside **Q192**, which is the same surface's other half — what an ignore glob can reach, rather than who wrote the rule.
- **Row and entry authored 2026-08-11 by the wave-15 Q-domain registering lane from the orchestrator's ledger.** **Verified here, not taken on report**: `.git/info/exclude` holds exactly ten non-comment entries, all under `.claude/`, enumerated above; none of them appears in `.gitignore`; the staging's own comment records that these are *"the entries in `.git/info/exclude` that a `.gitignore` parser cannot see"* and names the lock file as the instance; and the staging set is `git ls-files` unioned with `git ls-files --others --exclude-standard`, so the local file is in scope by construction. **Taken on report: nothing.** **Authored here, not reported: both `Do` shapes, the warning against dropping `--exclude-standard`, the whole `Accept`, and the `Notes`.**

### Q220 — `gate-features.sh` discards cargo's exit status and its stderr, then blames the extractor
- Milestone: M2
- Size: XS
- Deps: after Q153
- Discovered by: **the D124 lane** (2026-08-11), while measuring what a cargo invocation inside a gate script actually reports when it fails.
- Problem: `declared_features()` in `scripts/gate-features.sh` runs `cargo metadata --format-version 1 --no-deps --locked 2>/dev/null | python3 -c '…'`. Two losses in one line. `2>/dev/null` throws away cargo's own diagnosis — the lock file being stale, a manifest failing to parse, a member missing — which is the only text that says what went wrong. And because the invocation is the left side of a pipe, its exit status is discarded too: the pipeline reports python's. So a failing `cargo metadata` hands the reader an empty stdin, python raises an unhandled `JSONDecodeError`, and the `::error::` that follows names the **extractor** — the one component that is behaving correctly. The reader is sent to read a five-line python snippet to diagnose a cargo failure whose message was deleted three characters earlier.
- Do: Keep cargo's stderr and its status. Capture the metadata into a variable or a file, check the status explicitly, and on failure print cargo's own stderr with a message naming cargo as the failing component. Then feed the captured text to the extractor, so a `JSONDecodeError` means what it says — the metadata parsed as something other than JSON — rather than meaning *"the thing upstream died silently"*. Do not fix this by adding `set -o pipefail` alone: that recovers the status and still discards the message, which is the half that costs the reader the most.
- Accept: A failing `cargo metadata` produces an error naming cargo and carrying cargo's own stderr; the extractor's error is reachable only when the metadata is genuinely malformed; a planted fault demonstrates both directions, judged by message per `scripts/lib/red-arm.sh`; and no other invocation in the script discards a status through a pipe for the same reason.
- Notes: This is the misdiagnosis half of the class **Q197** and **Q198** name, in a gate script rather than in a checker, and it is worth registering separately because the cause here is a shell idiom rather than a wrong assertion — `2>/dev/null |` is the two-character version of *"suppress the evidence and keep the verdict"*. Note the standing project rule this instance argues for: a nonzero exit is not proof, and `$?` after a pipe reports the pipe. Note also the honest limit — the guard D124 built is unaffected; this is the behaviour for **every other** cause of a metadata failure.
- **Row and entry authored 2026-08-11 by the wave-15 Q-domain registering lane from the orchestrator's ledger.** **Verified here, not taken on report**: `declared_features()`'s body, read in the working tree — the `2>/dev/null`, the pipe into the inline python extractor, and the absence of any status check between them; and that the extractor is an unguarded `json.load(sys.stdin)`, so an empty stream raises rather than reporting. **Taken on report: the `::error::` text that names the extractor**, which this lane did not trigger. **Authored here, not reported: the capture-then-check instruction, the argument against fixing it with `pipefail` alone, the whole `Accept`, and the `Notes`.**

### Q221 — A parked citation whose target has moved onto a different symbol, so following it finds nothing wrong
- Milestone: M2
- Size: XS
- Deps: after Q187
- Discovered by: **the Q187 lane** (2026-08-11), while measuring what it was leaving behind.
- Problem: `docs/decisions/D94-anchor-verdict-vector-re-emit.md` §11's `R69` bullet cites a line range in `crates/antseal-core/src/verify/report.rs` for a `StorageLinkageResult` claim. `D105` §7 recorded in 2026-08-09 that the citation was stale and that the live line was `:299`. Three things have since moved and the correction has moved with them, in the worst available direction. The quoted phrase is **no longer a live claim anywhere** — the slot's doc now records only that it *said* that until D105 — so there is nothing to restate, only a withdrawal to record. `:299` is itself stale and points into a signature-verdict doc comment. And the original range has drifted onto a **different symbol**: at D94's own commit those lines were the receipt arm's doc, which is what the bullet is about; today they are `StorageLinkageResult`'s slot doc — so a reader following the citation arrives somewhere plausible, reads something coherent, and finds no error at all. That is the failure mode a dangling pointer does not have. `tasks/R.md` already records all three measurements and states that D117 RULING 6 forbids swapping one line locator for another, so the repair is a **symbol** citation — which is why it was left as measured work rather than done in passing. `Q187`'s Accept covered the A22 Accept-row count in the same record's `Q109` bullet and did not reach this one.
- Do: Correct D94 §11's `R69` bullet under D117, citing by symbol and quoted phrase rather than by line. State the withdrawal rather than a replacement claim: the sentence the bullet points at no longer exists as an assertion, so the correction records that the drift was resolved at D105 and that the citation now has no live target. Name D105 §7's drift item 1 as the prior measurement and `tasks/R.md`'s dated correction as the one that carries the current state, so the three records agree rather than each holding a different epoch. Do not renumber §11 or the `R69` bullet, and do not write a fresh line number anywhere (D117 RULING 6).
- Accept: D94 §11's `R69` bullet carries a dated correction citing `report.rs` by symbol; the correction states that the quoted claim is withdrawn rather than relocated; it names the three measured facts — the phrase is not live, `:299` is stale, the original range now covers a different symbol — with the dates they were measured; `tasks/R.md`'s correction and D105 §7's drift item 1 are cited so the chain is legible from any of the three; and no line number is introduced.
- Notes: This is the **sharpest measured instance of locator rot in the tree**, and its value is as evidence rather than as repair: it is the case where a stale locator is not merely useless but actively reassuring. D117 RULING 6 was justified on locators going stale in three days; this one went stale, was corrected, and the correction went stale onto a different symbol. Read beside **Q194** and **Q205**, which are the same rot in `tasks/` and in D117 itself. Note that whoever takes it must **re-measure first** — `tasks/R.md` says so explicitly, and three things had already moved between D105's measurement and this one.
- **Row and entry authored 2026-08-11 by the wave-15 Q-domain registering lane from the orchestrator's ledger.** **Verified here, not taken on report**: D105 §7's drift item 1, quoted verbatim, naming both the `tasks/R.md` and the `D94` citations and giving `report.rs:299` as the then-live line; `tasks/R.md`'s dated correction, which records all three of the measurements above, cites D117 RULING 6 for the symbol-citation repair, and states that the D94 half is outside Q187's scope; and that `Q187`'s own entry describes its executed scope as the `Q109` bullet's Accept-row count, not this citation. **Taken on report: the three measurements themselves** — that the phrase is not a live claim, that `:299` is a signature-verdict doc comment, and that the original range now covers `StorageLinkageResult` — which are `tasks/R.md`'s dated findings of 2026-08-11 and which this lane did not independently re-measure in `report.rs`. **Authored here, not reported: the instruction to record a withdrawal rather than a replacement, the requirement that the three records be cross-cited so the chain is legible from any of them, the whole `Accept`, and the `Notes`.**

### Q222 — Five section citations in one amendment, none of which ever resolved, live in three documents
- Milestone: M2
- Size: XS
- Deps: after Q122
- Discovered by: **the Q196 lane** (2026-08-11), while looking for the route by which its own row acquired a wrong section number.
- Problem: `docs/decisions/D116-gate-and-sweep-coverage.md`'s Amendment of 2026-08-10 closes by registering its discovered work against five section citations — §1.4 twice, §1.5, §1.7 and §2 — and **none of the five resolves**. §1.4 is the D-namespace density census, not the never-remote gate lanes; §1.5 is Q140 reproduced, not the proptest divergence; §1.7 is Q141's cost figure, not the extra directory walks; §2 is the options section, not the cost-figure lesson. The defect is **wrong when written**, not gone stale: the document has one commit, §2.4.1 forbids renumbering, and the same document's §9 cites `(§1.9)` and `(§1.7)` correctly for two of the same subjects — so the Amendment is the outlier and never resolved against any published state. The cost is measured rather than hypothetical: **`tasks/Q.md`'s Q196 entry and `TODO.md`'s Q196 row both carry `D116 §1.7`** for the *"two walkers"* figure, which actually lives at §1.5(a). D116's implementing lane recorded the finding in a dated correction section and deliberately declined to apply it at the source, on the ground that a destination signpost catches all three arrivals where a source fix catches one — and on the ground that D117 §2.2 governs where a correction section goes, not how one amendment corrects another, which it declined to improvise.
- Do: Two acts, and the second is the durable one. Apply the §2.3(a) REPLACE at the source — the Amendment's five citations become the sections that hold their subjects — now that the destination signpost exists to catch readers who arrived before the fix. Then rule the form the implementing lane declined to invent: **how a correction reaches text inside an appended amendment**, which D117 §2.2 does not contemplate. That is a small addition to D117's own machinery and it should be made as one, not improvised per instance. Correcting `tasks/Q.md`'s Q196 entry is a live-entry edit under **Q196**'s own correction and needs no ceremony; `TODO.md`'s row is the orchestrator's.
- Accept: The Amendment's five citations resolve; the correction section at the end of D116 records that the source was repaired and remains as the signpost for readers arriving from elsewhere; D117 carries a stated form for correcting text inside an appended amendment, or an explicit ruling that the appended-section-only form covers it; and no section identifier moves in either document.
- Notes: The reason this is a row rather than a repair is the second half. A record's *body* has a correction form and its *amendments* do not, which is a gap nobody would notice until an amendment is wrong — and this is the first time one has been. Note the transmission: five citations wrong in a decision produced one wrong citation in a task entry and one in the tracker, which is **Q203**'s propagation class with the arrow pointing the same way. Note also the honest limit — every one of the five is a navigation error, not a substantive one; the Amendment's findings and D116's rulings are unaffected, which is what makes the REPLACE arm safe.
- **Row and entry authored 2026-08-11 by the wave-15 Q-domain registering lane from the orchestrator's ledger.** **Verified here, not taken on report**: D116's Amendment, read in full, and its closing registration sentence quoted; the correction section appended 2026-08-11 with its five-row table of what each citation says versus where the subject is, and its two stated reasons for declining the source-side fix; D116's own heading list, confirming §1.4 is the density census, §1.5 is Q140 reproduced, §1.7 is Q141's cost figure and §2 is the options section; that the *"two walkers"* finding is at **§1.5(a)**; and that `tasks/Q.md`'s Q196 entry carries `D116 §1.7` — corrected by this lane in the same act as registering this row. **Taken on report: that `TODO.md`'s Q196 row carries the same mis-citation**, which this lane did not open. **Authored here, not reported: the two-act `Do`, the framing of the amendment-correction form as the durable half, the whole `Accept`, and the `Notes`.**

### Q223 — The census whose headline number is unreachable by the command anyone would use to reproduce it
- Milestone: M2
- Size: XS
- Deps: after Q196
- Discovered by: **the Q196 lane** (2026-08-11), while establishing the convention that would have prevented it.
- Problem: **Q157**'s entry reports a planted `.vscode/settings.json` failing *"eight tests across four test targets"*, and that figure is the row's corroborating measurement — it is what makes Q140's *"two walkers"* look like an undercount. The entry does not record the command that produced it, and **it cannot have come from one default `cargo test`**: four targets span two crates, and a fail-fast run stops at the first failing target. So the number is either the sum of several named-target runs, or a run carrying a flag the entry does not name. Both are legitimate; neither is recoverable from the record. **Q196** has since made *"a blast-radius measurement records the command that produced it"* a stated convention with a written home, so this row is the one document whose headline number is unreachable without it — the convention's first retrospective subject rather than a new discovery.
- Do: Re-measure and record. Run the plant again, with the command written down, and either confirm eight-across-four or replace it with what the recorded command produces; then add the command to Q157's entry as a dated in-place correction, per the convention. If the original figure cannot be reproduced, say so and record the figure that can be — a number with a command beats a larger number without one, and Q196's whole point is that the missing detail is *what was allowed to stop*.
- Accept: Q157's entry states the command that produced its walk-failure figure, with the flag that governs stopping; the figure is either confirmed or replaced, with the discrepancy stated rather than silently overwritten; and the correction is dated and cites Q196's convention as the authority, so the entry reads as the convention's first application rather than as a lone repair.
- Notes: Registered as the convention's forcing case and deliberately scoped to one entry — do not turn it into a sweep of every recorded count, which is a much larger act with a much weaker argument. The interesting property is that **the number is not suspected of being wrong**; it is suspected of being unreproducible, and those are different defects with different fixes. Read beside **Q160**, whose generalisation this is: a recorded figure that does not name what produced it attaches itself to whatever the reader is thinking about.
- **Row and entry authored 2026-08-11 by the wave-15 Q-domain registering lane from the orchestrator's ledger.** **Verified here, not taken on report**: **Q157**'s entry and its `Notes`, read in full — it records the third and fourth walks, the `read_frozen` refutation and the per-file line citations, and **names no command anywhere**; and that `Q196`'s entry carries the eight-across-four figure as corroboration, attributing it to `vector_walk/mod.rs` rather than to a command. **Taken on report: the arithmetic that four targets span two crates**, which this lane reasoned from the entry's own text rather than from the crate graph. **Authored here, not reported: the two candidate origins for the figure, the instruction to prefer a smaller reproducible number over a larger unreproducible one, the whole `Accept`, and the `Notes`.**

### Q224 — A reconstruction and the entry it produced have drifted apart in three clauses, in both directions
- Milestone: M2
- Size: XS
- Deps: after Q203
- Discovered by: **the Q203 lane** (2026-08-11), one bullet below the clause it was charged to correct.
- Problem: `docs/decisions/D115-entryless-row-reconstruction.md` §4.1 carries a fenced *"Proposed entry — paste into `tasks/A.md`"* block, and `tasks/A.md`'s **A105** is the entry that block produced. **Q203** corrected one `Accept` bullet in each. Three further clauses now disagree, and they disagree in different directions. **(i)** A105's live `Notes` bullet still reads *"`--milestone` defaults to `CURRENT_MILESTONE`, which is `"M0"`"*; the constant reads `"M1"` and has since D118 moved it — so the fix Q203 applied inside D115 has made the decision's copy correct and left the live entry, the one an executor actually reads, wrong. **(ii)** The eleven-row clause — *"`V7.2` is one of 11 rows `--matrix --milestone M2` names as `deferred`"* — is stale in **three** places: twice inside D115 §4.1 and once in A105's live `Notes`. D118 moved ten of the eleven to `covered` and one to `gap`; `V7.2` itself now reads `covered`, so the clause's own subject contradicts it. **(iii)** The two provenance bullets disagree about their own history: D115's fenced copy says *"authored 2026-08-11 … from `TODO.md`'s A105 row alone — no other source existed"* while the live entry says *"authored 2026-08-10 … from `TODO.md`'s A105 row, D115 §4.1's reconstruction, and the files both name"* — different dates, in the wrong order, and mutually exclusive claims about whether the reconstruction was a source.
- Do: Correct the three clauses under the two rules that already govern them, exactly as Q203 split its own work. A105's `Notes` bullet is a live task entry: edit it in place with a dated clause, cite `CURRENT_MILESTONE` **by symbol and never by line** (D117 RULING 6), and state the current eleven-row position rather than restating a count that will move again — `V7.2` is `covered`, `V7.1` is `gap`, and what the bullet was warning about is that a flagless green says nothing about the M2 review, which is still true and is the part worth keeping. D115 §4.1's two eleven-row clauses take a **dated strike** — they are measurements, not instructions (D117 §2.3(b)) — appended to the correction section Q203 already created rather than as a second one. The provenance mismatch is the one to think about rather than patch: decide which copy is the record, and say so.
- Accept: A105's `Notes` no longer states a stale constant value or a stale row count, and cites the constant by symbol; D115 §4.1's eleven-row clauses carry a dated strike naming D118 as the event; the provenance disagreement is resolved by a stated rule about which of a decision's fenced proposal and a live entry is authoritative for its own history, not by editing one to match the other; and the three sites agree afterwards, checked by reading all three rather than by fixing the one that was reported.
- Notes: This is **Q203**'s class one bullet down, and the sharpest thing in it is that Q203's own fix produced instance (i): correcting the decision's copy of a clause and not the live copy made the two halves disagree where they previously agreed in error. A propagated defect has two sites and a lane's charge usually names one. Note that the `Do` deliberately does not ask for the eleven-row clause to be *updated* — a count of deferred rows moves every wave, and restating it is how the clause got stale twice; the durable form is the claim about what a flagless green proves.
- **Row and entry authored 2026-08-11 by the wave-15 Q-domain registering lane, merging three items reported by the Q203 lane.** **Verified here, and it corrects the ledger twice.** Verified: `CURRENT_MILESTONE` reads `"M1"`; A105's live `Notes` bullet reads `"M0"`; `V7.2` reads `covered` and `V7.1` reads `gap` in the matrix; the eleven-row clause occurs twice in D115 §4.1 and once in A105's live `Notes`; and the two provenance bullets, quoted above, carry the dates and source lists shown. **Corrected**: the ledger reports A105's `Notes` as carrying *"stale line number AND stale `"M0"` value"* — measured, the live bullet carries **no line number at all**; the locator half was removed when the entry was written and only the value is stale. **Corrected**: the ledger reports *"A105 has two provenance bullets that disagree"* — A105 has **one**; the disagreement is between A105's single bullet and D115 §4.1's fenced copy, which is a different and more interesting fact. **Taken on report: nothing.** **Authored here, not reported: the split of the three clauses across D117's two arms and the live-entry rule, the instruction to replace the eleven-row count with the durable claim rather than update it, the framing of the provenance mismatch as a question about authority rather than a typo, the whole `Accept`, and the `Notes`.**

### Q225 — Q109's `Do` cites A22's Accept by numbers that predate the renumber, and contradicts its own correction bullet
- Milestone: M2
- Size: XS
- Deps: after Q187
- Discovered by: **the Q187 lane** (2026-08-11), while executing D103 §7.2 RULING 6a's parked correction.
- Problem: `tasks/Q.md`'s **Q109** entry says two things about the same subject and they do not agree. Its `Do` reads *"A22 Accept rows 1 and 2 depend on the distinction"*, which is RULING 6a's prescribed replacement text and is **pre-§7.1 numbering**. Its correction bullet, four lines below, records that against the numbered `Accept` the properties D94's sentence names are supplied by **rows 2 and 3**. A reader taking the `Do` as the instruction is pointed at row 1 (the home) and row 2 (freeze and retention); a reader taking the correction is pointed at row 2 and row 3 (byte-identical native/wasm output). D103 §7.1 replaced A22's Accept with four explicitly numbered rows precisely so that citation by number would be stable — and RULING 6a's prescribed text, written before that replacement, was applied afterwards. So one half of a correction shipped in its pre-renumber form into the very entry whose defect class is *counting*.
- Do: Re-derive the mapping against A22's four numbered Accept rows as they stand, and correct whichever of the two statements is wrong — not by preferring one, but by reading the rows. Then state the mapping once, in one place in the entry, and have the other reference it rather than restate it, because two independent statements of the same mapping is how this entry acquired the contradiction. Record the correction dated and in place, as a live task entry takes (D117 does not gate `tasks/*.md`). Check whether D103 §7.2 RULING 6a's prescribed text needs the same note at its source, since a future executor reading the ruling will re-derive the pre-renumber numbers.
- Accept: Q109's `Do` and its correction bullet agree about which of A22's numbered Accept rows the distinction reaches; the mapping is stated once; the correction is dated and names D103 §7.1's renumber as the event that invalidated the prescribed text; and RULING 6a's own text either carries a note that its row numbers predate §7.1 or is recorded as not needing one.
- Notes: The instructive part is the mechanism rather than the numbers: **a prescribed replacement text is a prediction about the document it will be applied to**, and this one was overtaken by a renumber inside the same decision that issued it. That is the same shape as **Q189** — a correction whose subject is a prediction — from the other end. Note the honest ambiguity a corrector must resolve rather than assume: the `Do`'s sentence and D94's sentence are about *related but different* claims, so *"rows 1 and 2"* may be right for the `Do` even though *"rows 2 and 3"* is right for D94; the defect is that the entry does not say which.
- **Row and entry authored 2026-08-11 by the wave-15 Q-domain registering lane from the orchestrator's ledger.** **Verified here, not taken on report**: Q109's `Do` sentence and its correction bullet, both quoted from `tasks/Q.md`, and the contradiction between them; A22's `Accept` as it now stands in `tasks/A.md` — four explicitly numbered rows, row 1 the `anchor`-kind home, row 2 the freeze-and-retention pair, row 3 native/wasm byte-identical `recomputed_digest`, row 4 the M2 exit checklist — together with the dated bullet recording that D103 §7.1 replaced the previous three; and that the correction bullet attributes *"rows 1 and 2"* to RULING 6a as pre-§7.1 numbering. **Refused to transcribe**: the ledger's proposed reading *"rows 1–3 hard + row 4 consequentially"* is a third figure this lane could not confirm from A22's Accept text, so the `Do` asks for a re-derivation rather than prescribing it. **Authored here, not reported: the state-it-once instruction, the check on RULING 6a's own text, the observation that the two sentences may have different correct answers, the whole `Accept`, and the `Notes`.**

### Q226 — An open checkbox ordering work that was completed two weeks ago, and this wave made it worse
- Milestone: M2
- Size: XS
- Deps: after Q186
- Discovered by: **the D119 lane** (2026-08-11), as the inverse of the defect Q186 was written about.
- Problem: `docs/decisions/D8`'s §E5 is an open `- [ ]` instructing that `docs/decisions/README.md` gain rows for **D8**, **D78** and **D79**. All three have been indexed since 2026-07-28/07-31. So the marker asserts outstanding work that was done a fortnight ago — **the inverse of Q186's complaint and the more misleading direction**: an unexecuted instruction with no marker costs a reader a check, while an executed instruction still marked open costs them the work. D119's execution made it worse rather than better: the three rows E5 names have now been joined by 35 more, the index is complete at 106 rows against 106 records, and a `decision-index` check holds it that way — and the open checkbox still says the opposite. It is now the only statement in the corpus claiming the index is incomplete.
- Do: Mark E5 executed with the date its subject was satisfied, not the date the marker is corrected, and name what satisfied it — the three original rows in 2026-07-28/07-31 and the D119 backfill for everything E5's class covers. Take the general case with it: **Q188** owns the retrofit of execution markers for edit-set sections, and this is the case that proves the retrofit must run in **both** directions rather than only adding markers to sections that have none. Do not delete the checkbox; the record of what was ordered is the part worth keeping.
- Accept: D8 §E5 records execution with a date and names the acts that satisfied it; nothing in the corpus asserts the index is incomplete; the correction is dated and cites D119 as the act that closed the class; and **Q188**'s scope explicitly includes falsely-open markers, not only missing ones.
- Notes: The value is in the direction, not the instance. A missing marker makes a reader verify; a false marker makes a reader work. This project has now measured both — Q186 counted 27 of 33 instructions never applied, and this is one that was applied and never marked — which means the retrofit cannot be a sweep for absent markers. Note the sharp version for whoever writes that sweep: **the two failures are indistinguishable from the marker alone**, so the retrofit's real cost is re-measuring each section's subject, not editing each section.
- **Row and entry authored 2026-08-11 by the wave-15 Q-domain registering lane from the orchestrator's ledger.** **Verified here, not taken on report**: D8 §E5's text and its open `- [ ]` marker, read in the working tree; that `docs/decisions/README.md` carries index rows for **D8**, **D78** and **D79** — all three present; that the index now holds **106** rows and that a `decision-index` check exists in `scripts/check-traceability.py` with self-test cases; and that Q186's own entry is scoped to *unexecuted* instructions, which is what makes this the inverse case rather than a duplicate. **Taken on report: the 2026-07-28/07-31 indexing dates**, which this lane read from the ledger rather than from git history. **Authored here, not reported: the both-directions requirement for Q188's retrofit, the observation that the two failures are indistinguishable from the marker alone, the whole `Accept`, and the `Notes`.**

### Q227 — Nothing in the tree links to the decision index, which is why fifteen days of drift cost nothing
- Milestone: M2
- Size: XS
- Deps: after Q180
- Discovered by: **the D119 lane** (2026-08-11), while explaining why a 29-record gap had no observable consequence.
- Problem: `docs/decisions/README.md` is now correct — 106 rows against 106 records, machine-checked for presence, order, status and date. **Nothing points at it.** The root `README.md`, `CONTRIBUTING.md` and `docs/threat-model.md` each reference the decision *directory* or an individual record, never the index. That is the mechanism behind D119's most useful observation: the index was 29 records behind for fifteen days and the drift cost nothing, because no reader was ever routed through it. The consequence now runs the other way. A newly-correct, machine-held index that no document links to is an artifact whose accuracy nobody will consume — and whose next drift will again be free, because the check is the only reader. The work D119 did is worth linking to; the work of keeping it correct is only worth it if someone arrives.
- Do: Give the index at least one entry point from a document people actually open. The candidates are the root `README.md`'s project-structure section and `CONTRIBUTING.md`'s conventions, and the right choice is wherever a contributor is told how decisions are recorded — which is also where D119's ruling that `TODO.md` holds the id space and the index holds the files should be restated in one sentence, since a reader arriving at the index without that distinction will read it as a register. Prefer one good link to three; a convention cited from three places is a convention that will be corrected in one.
- Accept: At least one document a contributor reads on arrival links to `docs/decisions/README.md` by path, describing it as the index of records that exist; the register-versus-index distinction is stated at that link in one sentence citing D119; and the link's target is checked by the same mechanism that checks the rest of that file's links, or its absence from any check is recorded.
- Notes: Registered because it is the honest completion of D119 rather than a new defect: the decision made the index true, and this row makes it read. Note the small paradox worth stating in the change — a file that nothing links to is also a file whose links nothing checks, so adding the link adds a checked pointer in a direction that currently has none. Read beside **Q176**, which found `CHANGELOG.md` — the one externally-facing document — swept by nothing, for the same reason: an artifact nobody arrives at is an artifact nobody maintains.
- **Row and entry authored 2026-08-11 by the wave-15 Q-domain registering lane from the orchestrator's ledger.** **Verified here, not taken on report**: `docs/decisions/README.md`'s current state — 106 index rows, the rewritten opening naming `TODO.md`'s register as the id space and this file as the index of records that exist, and the sentence naming the `decision-index` check as what holds it true. **Taken on report: that the root `README.md`, `CONTRIBUTING.md` and `docs/threat-model.md` point at the directory or at records and never at the index**, and the fifteen-day drift window; this lane did not sweep the three files for links. **Authored here, not reported: the whole `Do` including the prefer-one-link instruction and the placement argument, the observation that an unlinked file's links are also unchecked, the whole `Accept`, and the `Notes`.**

### Q228 — A green index check will be read as vouching for 48 KB of prose it does not read
- Milestone: M2
- Size: XS
- Deps: after Q180
- Discovered by: **the D119 execution lane** (2026-08-11), while writing 35 index rows from planner self-descriptions.
- Problem: `docs/decisions/README.md`'s rows carry a decision id, a link, a **summary**, a status and a date. The new `decision-index` check verifies presence, ordering, and that each row's status and date match the record's own `- **Status`/`- **Date` lines. **It reads no summary.** Of the 35 rows backfilled this wave, 32 are verbatim planner self-descriptions harvested from the records' own pre-written sections and 3 were composed from scratch — so the largest single body of prose in the decision corpus outside the records themselves is unverified, self-attributed, and now sits under a green machine check. The hazard is not that a summary is wrong; it is that *"the index check is green"* will be quoted as though it meant the index is accurate, when what it means is that the index is **complete, ordered, and correctly statused**. That is a valuable property and a much narrower one than the green line implies.
- Do: Say what the check covers where the check reports. The green line and the file's own opening should each state the three properties held and name the summaries as unverified prose — one clause, not a section. Then rule whether that is acceptable permanently or whether summaries need any weaker discipline (a length bound, a required first sentence naming the question, a rule that a summary is a quotation from the record rather than a paraphrase of it), and record the answer at the index. Do not build a prose checker: this project has refused that shape twice, in **Q146** and **Q185**, both times for the same reason.
- Accept: The `decision-index` check's green line names the properties it holds and does not imply more; `docs/decisions/README.md`'s opening states that summaries are unverified; the disposition of summary discipline is recorded as a decision rather than left implicit; and nothing anywhere describes the index as verified without that qualification.
- Notes: This is the *"coverage without a claim"* shape inverted — usually a test asserts something nobody claimed; here a check will be read as claiming something it does not assert. Both are failures of the report rather than of the instrument, which is why the fix is a sentence rather than code. Note the specific risk in the numbers: 32 of 35 summaries are **self-descriptions**, so the index inherits each planner's own account of its record, and a decision whose planner overstated its ruling now has that overstatement in the project's front page for decisions.
- **Row and entry authored 2026-08-11 by the wave-15 Q-domain registering lane from the orchestrator's ledger.** **Verified here, not taken on report**: the index's opening, which states that each row's status and date are *"taken from that record's own `- **Status` and `- **Date` lines"* and names the `decision-index` check as what holds all three true — three properties, none of them about the summary; that the check exists in `scripts/check-traceability.py` with three self-test cases; and that the index carries 106 rows whose summary cells are multi-hundred-word paragraphs. **Taken on report: the 48 KB figure and the 32-harvested/3-composed split**, which this lane did not recount. **Authored here, not reported: the framing as a reporting defect rather than a coverage gap, the three candidate disciplines, the explicit instruction not to build a prose checker with its two precedents, the whole `Accept`, and the `Notes`.**

### Q229 — A ruling that demotes a section "into that document's existing edit set" has no referent in four documents
- Milestone: M2
- Size: XS
- Deps: after Q186
- Discovered by: **the D119 execution lane** (2026-08-11), while applying the ruling to 43 documents.
- Problem: D119 RULING 4 demotes each in-body index-row section into *"that document's existing edit set"* — a one-line entry in the table of files the decision's execution touches. The premise is that a decision has one. **Four of the 43 documents do not**: `D57`, `D95`, `D116` and `D117` contain no edit-set section at all, so the ruling's phrase has nothing to name and the executing lane had to improvise for each. That is a rule with a silent gap rather than a wrong rule — the demotion still happened, and what is missing is the recorded form for the case the ruling did not anticipate. The gap will recur: nothing requires a decision to carry an edit set, and the next ruling that says *"record it in the edit set"* meets the same four documents plus whatever has been written since.
- Do: Record what the four got, and rule the general case. Two shapes: require every decision record to carry an edit-set section, even if the section reads *"none — this decision changes no file"*, which makes the referent always exist and costs one line per record; or state, in D119 RULING 4, what happens when there is no edit set — most plausibly that the row is recorded in the `Outcome` section, which every record has. Prefer whichever the four documents already ended up with, so the ruling describes the tree rather than obliging a second pass over it. Record the choice at RULING 4 as a dated correction, since the ruling is in a resolved decision.
- Accept: D119 RULING 4 names a referent that exists for every record, including the four; the four are consistent with whatever is ruled rather than being four improvisations; and if the answer is *"every record carries an edit set"*, the four gain one and the requirement is stated where records are written rather than only in D119.
- Notes: Small, and worth registering because it is the second time this wave that a rule was invalidated by the act it authorised — **Q206** is the first. The pattern is specific enough to name: a ruling that quantifies over documents (*"every decision's X"*) is a claim about the corpus, and this corpus has no schema, so every such claim is an unmeasured universal. Whoever takes this should consider whether that observation belongs somewhere more durable than one row's `Notes`.
- **Row and entry authored 2026-08-11 by the wave-15 Q-domain registering lane from the orchestrator's ledger.** **Verified here, and it confirms the report by direct count**: `D57`, `D95`, `D116` and `D117` each contain **zero** occurrences of the phrase *"edit set"*, in a heading or anywhere else — measured individually, not sampled. **Taken on report: that all four nevertheless received their demotion**, and the figure of 43 documents; this lane did not read the four demotions. **Authored here, not reported: both `Do` shapes and the instruction to prefer whichever the four already got, the whole `Accept`, and the `Notes`' observation about unmeasured universals.**

### Q230 — The fix that corrected the gate constant created a second, unchecked copy of its value
- Milestone: M2
- Size: XS
- Deps: after Q183
- Discovered by: **the D122 lane** (2026-08-11), while ruling what moves the constant.
- Problem: D118 moved `CURRENT_MILESTONE` from `"M0"` to `"M1"` and, in the same act, wrote M1's gating position into `docs/testing/verification-matrix.md` as prose: the M2 section now opens *"Checked at the M2 review, which has not happened. `CURRENT_MILESTONE` is `M1`, so these rows are not gated by the ordinary run"*. That paragraph is correct today and **nothing compares it to the constant**. `parse_matrix` reads only pipe-delimited rows validated by a following separator, so every preamble is invisible to the checker — the defect **Q185** was registered for — and this is a fresh instance of that class, introduced by the change that closed the row about the constant being wrong. It rots at the exact moment the constant next moves, which is M2's review, which is the event the paragraph is about. The round-2 lane then added a third dated statement of the milestone position in the constant's own comment, so there are now three prose statements of one value and one machine-readable source.
- Do: Reduce the prose to something that cannot rot, rather than adding a checker for it. The durable form states the *rule* — that rows at or before the milestone under review must read `covered`, and that the ordinary run gates at whatever `CURRENT_MILESTONE` names — and defers the value to the constant, which is exactly what **Q146** ruled and **Q185** proposes for this file's preambles. Sweep for the other two statements in the same act and give them the same treatment; do not correct the value in three places, which is how it got to three. If a value must appear, it belongs in the checker's output, not in a document.
- Accept: No preamble in `docs/testing/verification-matrix.md` states the constant's value; the rule is stated instead, with the constant named by symbol; the constant's comment carries one statement of its position rather than three; and the sweep that found them is recorded so the count is known rather than assumed.
- Notes: The reason this is registered separately from **Q185** is the causation, and it should be stated in whichever row executes first: **Q185** is a sweep of preambles that had gone stale; this is a preamble that was *written* this wave, by the decision closing the row about the value being wrong. A fix that reproduces its own defect class is worth a named instance, because the next person to correct a constant will reach for the same prose. Read beside **Q160** and **Q231**.
- **Row and entry authored 2026-08-11 by the wave-15 Q-domain registering lane from the orchestrator's ledger.** **Verified here, not taken on report**: the M2 section's preamble in `docs/testing/verification-matrix.md`, quoted above, stating `CURRENT_MILESTONE` is `M1`; that the constant reads `"M1"`; and that **Q185**'s entry records `parse_matrix` as reading only validated table rows, so no preamble is read by anything. **Taken on report: that the constant's comment now carries a third dated statement of the milestone position**, added by the round-2 script lane, which this lane did not read in full. **Authored here, not reported: the state-the-rule-defer-the-value instruction with its two precedents, the requirement to sweep rather than patch, the whole `Accept`, and the `Notes`' causation argument.**

### Q231 — "Under review" names the wrong milestone in nine places, including a message users see
- Milestone: M2
- Size: S
- Deps: after Q183
- Discovered by: **the D122 lane** (2026-08-11), which traced Q165's misreading to this vocabulary.
- Problem: `CURRENT_MILESTONE` names **the last milestone review that passed**. It is described throughout as *"the milestone under review"*, which names the next one — the opposite. The two readings differ by exactly one milestone, and the difference is the whole of D118's ruling: M1's review has finished, M2's has not, and the constant reads `M1`. The wording is not confined to comments. It reaches `--milestone`'s argparse **help text**, which prints the fiction to anyone who types `--help`; the status-gate's failure message, which tells a reader that a milestone *"is under review"* when that milestone's review finished on 2026-08-02; and an optional parameter name carried across several call sites. D122 measured nine sites and identified this vocabulary as **provably where Q165's `M2` misreading came from** — a row that reasoned correctly from a wrong word and reached a conclusion D118 had to refuse with three measurements.
- Do: Rename the concept, not just the comments, and treat the rename as **all-or-nothing**: the round-2 lane measured the parameter's occurrences at ten over nine lines rather than the six D122 lists, because the census omitted the docstring and three interpolating f-strings, so a partial rename yields a `NameError`. Choose wording that cannot be read either way — *"the last milestone whose review passed"* or *"the gated-through milestone"* — and apply it to the comment, the help text, the failure message and the parameter together. Correct the failure message's claim as well as its wording: it currently asserts a review is happening. Q51's entry states the same thing a third time and takes a dated in-place correction, per D117's treatment of live entries.
- Accept: No comment, help string, failure message or identifier describes `CURRENT_MILESTONE` as the milestone under review; `--help` describes what the flag actually overrides; the status-gate failure message names the milestone it gated at without asserting a review; the parameter rename is complete, proven by the module importing and the self-test passing; and Q51's entry carries a dated correction rather than being left as the fourth statement.
- Notes: This is a **defect vector rather than a wording preference**, and the entry should keep that framing because the cheap reading of this row is *"tidy some comments"*. The evidence is that a competent row (**Q165**) reasoned from the word, wrote `M2` into its own Accept, and would have forced `V7.1` into the register whose comment reads *"a milestone shipping with a known hole"* — muting the finding at the review meant to read it. One word cost a decision. Note the merge hazard the rename carries: the parameter appears in interpolating f-strings, which a grep for the bare identifier misses.
- **Row and entry authored 2026-08-11 by the wave-15 Q-domain registering lane from the orchestrator's ledger.** **Verified here, not taken on report**: `CURRENT_MILESTONE` reads `"M1"`; the phrase *"the milestone under review"* occurs in `scripts/check-traceability.py`'s own gate comment — including the line recording that the correct reading is *not* that phrase, *"which is the reading that sent Q165 to M2"* — and in `docs/testing/verification-matrix.md`'s header rule, `tasks/Q.md`'s Q51 entry and `TODO.md`'s Q51 row. **Taken on report: the count of nine sites, the help-text and failure-message instances, and the ten-occurrences-over-nine-lines measurement of the parameter**; this lane confirmed the class and did not enumerate every site. **Authored here, not reported: the all-or-nothing rename constraint restated as an Accept criterion, the two candidate wordings, the instruction to correct the failure message's claim and not only its wording, the whole `Accept`, and the `Notes`.**

### Q232 — A second gate step's freedom from a tool is asserted only by a comment
- Milestone: M2
- Size: XS
- Deps: after Q182
- Discovered by: **the D124 lane** (2026-08-11), while building the guard for the sibling property.
- Problem: D124 built `scripts/cargo-free.sh` because *"this job runs no cargo"* was prose in seventeen places and an assertion in none. The same shape survives one property over: `scripts/wasm-bitmatch.sh`'s trigger arm records *"No cargo, no git, no…"* as a **comment**, and nothing asserts the git half. The cargo half is now covered — the guard's shims mask `cargo`, `rustc` and `rustup` across the whole armed job, so a step that shells out to cargo fails loudly wherever it is added. Git is not masked, and the trigger arm's git-freeness is the property that makes it cheap enough to run in the job it runs in. The extension is small and the trap is specific: **the other four steps in that job were not measured git-free** and must not be assumed so by a lane extending the mask, because a masked `git` that some step legitimately calls turns a working job red for a reason that is not a defect.
- Do: Extend the guard's mechanism rather than writing a second one — a `--arm` variant that installs a failing `git` shim, applied to the step whose comment claims the property, with the same `$GITHUB_PATH` mechanism and the same marker-file verdict. Measure the other steps first, exactly as D124 measured all five byte-identical under the cargo mask: a step that legitimately calls git is a finding about the comment, not about the step. If the mask cannot be scoped to one step, record that and leave the comment with the measurement attached, which is strictly better than the comment alone.
- Accept: `wasm-bitmatch.sh --trigger-self-test`'s git-freeness is either asserted by the guard or carries a dated measurement rather than a bare claim; every other step in the job is measured for git use before any mask is applied, with the result recorded; and a planted `git` call in the covered step turns the guard red by message, per `scripts/lib/red-arm.sh`.
- Notes: Registered as the honest residue of D124 rather than as a new discovery — the decision closed the cargo property and named this one. The general rule worth extracting is in the `Accept`'s second clause: **a mask is an assertion about every step it covers**, so widening one is a measurement task before it is an implementation task. That is the direction D124's own double-control method points, and it is the part most likely to be skipped.
- **Row and entry authored 2026-08-11 by the wave-15 Q-domain registering lane from the orchestrator's ledger.** **Verified here, not taken on report**: `scripts/wasm-bitmatch.sh` carries the *"No cargo, no git"* claim as a comment above its trigger arms, and nothing in the file or in `scripts/cargo-free.sh` asserts the git half; `scripts/cargo-free.sh` exists with `--arm`/`--verdict`/`--require`/`--self-test` and masks cargo, rustc and rustup; and `scripts/lib/red-arm.sh` states the judge-by-message rule. **Taken on report: that the other four steps in the job were not measured git-free**, and D124's five-steps-byte-identical measurement under the cargo mask. **Authored here, not reported: the measure-before-masking requirement, the fallback of a dated measurement beside the comment, the whole `Accept`, and the `Notes`' generalisation that a mask is an assertion about every step it covers.**

### Q233 — A red arm whose plant is an absent environment variable can be satisfied by the caller
- Milestone: M2
- Size: XS
- Deps: after Q149
- Discovered by: **the D124 execution lane** (2026-08-11), after building a red arm whose plant was an unset variable.
- Problem: `scripts/lib/red-arm.sh` states the project's rule — *"a red arm must match on the message the check prints when it finds the planted fault, not on the exit status alone"* — and carries a register of every instrument with a red arm and how each judges. It was written from three measured instances, all of which are the same shape: a fault the harness could not plant, or a crash, satisfying an arm that judged on exit status. **It does not name a fourth direction**: an arm whose planted fault is the *absence* of something in the environment — an unset variable, a missing directory, an empty `PATH` entry — is satisfied whenever the caller's environment already supplies that absence. Such an arm passes without the plant ever being applied, and it passes *by message*, so the rule as written does not catch it. `cargo-free.sh` has one such arm by construction (`--arm` must exit non-zero with `$GITHUB_PATH` unset), which is what surfaced the class; the D124 lane also had to make its own self-test hermetic with a symlink directory and `env -u` after finding that `env VAR=…` inherits the ambient environment.
- Do: Add the direction to the rule and sweep the register for arms that carry it. The rule gains a clause: **an arm whose plant is an absence must establish that absence itself and assert the pre-image** — that the thing really was present before the plant removed it — which is the same pre-image discipline the path-targeted self-test mutations already use. Then read each instrument in `red-arm.sh`'s register and mark which arms plant a presence and which plant an absence; the second kind needs the new clause and the first does not. Prefer a hermetic environment over an assertion where it is cheap, as `cargo-free.sh --self-test` now does.
- Accept: `scripts/lib/red-arm.sh` names the absence-plant direction with its mechanism and at least one measured instance; every arm in its register is classified as planting a presence or an absence; each absence-planting arm either establishes the absence hermetically or asserts the pre-image; and running any such self-test under a hostile ambient environment produces the same verdict as running it clean, demonstrated rather than argued.
- Notes: The rule in `red-arm.sh` is one of this project's better instruments and this row is a widening of it, not a criticism — its three recorded instances are all *"the fault was never planted and something else went wrong"*, and this is *"the fault was never planted because it was already true"*. Same family, opposite cause. Note the sharpest measured version of the class from this wave, one level out: a planted fault applied by pattern matched more than its target, so the run was green **for a reason that was not the fault** — caught only because the lane re-planted against a unique anchor. Pre-image assertion is the common cure.
- **Row and entry authored 2026-08-11 by the wave-15 Q-domain registering lane from the orchestrator's ledger.** **Verified here, not taken on report**: `scripts/lib/red-arm.sh` exists as a sourced library stating the judge-by-message rule, records three measured instances from waves 12, 13 and 14, and carries a register of instruments and how each judges — and **names no direction in which an arm can be satisfied by the caller's environment**; and that `scripts/cargo-free.sh` exists with a `--self-test` arm. **Taken on report: that `cargo-free.sh`'s `--arm` red arm is an absence plant, that its self-test was made hermetic with a symlink directory and `env -u`, and the re-planting incident**; this lane did not read `cargo-free.sh` in full. **Authored here, not reported: the pre-image clause and its link to the path-targeted mutation discipline, the presence-versus-absence classification of the register, the whole `Accept`, and the `Notes`.**

### Q234 — The self-test mints its fixture id from rows alone, so registering an entry disarms the case that guards entries
- Milestone: M2
- Size: XS
- Deps: after Q184
- Discovered by: **the wave-15 Q-domain registering lane** (2026-08-11), which disarmed the case by doing its own job and read the failure rather than the exit code.
- Problem: `--self-test`'s `task-entries` red case (g) is *"a NEW row without an entry fails"* — the only case standing between a row and a missing entry. It appends `- [ ] **<minted_q>** (S) fixture` to `TODO.md` and requires the check to report it. `minted_q` comes from `above_ceiling("Q")`, which is *"the highest **allocated** number + 1"* — and `scratch_rows()` reads **`TODO.md` rows only**. The finding the case must provoke is *"a row with no entry"*, so the mutation is sound **only if the minted id has no entry**, and the derivation never looks at the entry half. `[task-entries]` is bidirectional by design, and this project's registration workflow splits it across two acts on purpose: a registering lane writes entries into `tasks/*.md`, then the orchestrator writes the matching rows into `TODO.md`, which it alone owns. **During that window the highest row is `Q<n>` and the entries run to `Q<n+k>`, so `minted_q` is `Q<n+1>` — an id that already has an entry.** Appending its row then *pairs* them: the mutation **removes** the entry-with-no-row finding instead of adding a row-with-no-entry one, so `added` is empty and the red arm reports *"task-entries added no finding on the red-case mutation of `TODO.md`"* — measured here, at exactly the moment 30 entries existed and none of their rows did. This is the **third** time a registering act has disarmed a self-test case, and the first where the fixture was **derived** rather than pinned: Q184's cure for pinned literals does not reach it, because the derivation reads the half the case is not about.
- Do: Mint above **both** halves — `max(rows ∪ entries) + 1` — so the fixture id cannot collide with anything either surface holds. `above_ceiling` is shared with the `task-citations` cases, where the row ceiling is the correct domain, so widen it at the call site or give the entry-sensitive case its own minting helper rather than changing the ceiling for every caller. Assert the pre-image the way the path-targeted mutations do: the case should establish that the minted id has **no entry and no row** before mutating, and fail loudly if it does, so a future collision is reported as itself rather than as *"the check no longer bites this defect"*. State at the case that its id must be above both halves and why, because the reason is not visible from the mutation.
- Accept: Case (g)'s minted id is above the union of allocated rows and allocated entries; a tree carrying entries whose rows have not yet landed still turns the case red; the case asserts its minted id is unallocated on both surfaces before mutating and fails with a message naming the collision if it is not; and the fix is demonstrated on a tree in exactly this wave's state — entries present, rows absent — rather than on a clean one, since a clean tree cannot exercise it.
- Notes: The instructive part is that **the failure message is accurate and its diagnosis is not** — it says the check may no longer bite this defect, and the check bites it perfectly well; what changed is that the mutation stopped expressing the defect. That is **Q197**'s class inside the harness's own verdict text, and it is why this was diagnosed rather than dismissed. Note the trap for whoever fixes it: this row's own entry makes `Q234` the next colliding id if its row is not registered in the same act as the rest, so the fix must not be tested against a tree where the two halves are in step. Note also the general form, worth carrying beyond this case: **a derived fixture is only as safe as the surface it derives from**, and a case that guards a two-sided invariant must derive from both sides.
- **Row and entry authored 2026-08-11 by the wave-15 Q-domain registering lane, from its own measurement rather than from the orchestrator's ledger — this item is not in it.** **Verified here, by reading the code and the failure rather than by inference**: `above_ceiling`'s body and its docstring, which say *"read from the scratch tree"* and read `scratch_rows()`; case (g)'s mutation text and its `"red"` expectation; that `TODO.md`'s highest `Q` row is **203**, so `minted_q` is **Q204**; that `tasks/Q.md` now defines `### Q204` and that `--task-entries` reports it as an entry with no row, which is what the appended row cancels; and that `minted_a` is used only by the two `task-citations` cases, where the row ceiling is the right domain — which is why exactly one case failed and not two. **Taken on report: that this is the third such incident**, from the wave brief's account of the two earlier ones. **Authored here, not reported: the whole `Do`, including the mint-above-both-halves rule, the pre-image assertion and the instruction not to change the ceiling for every caller; the whole `Accept`, including the requirement to demonstrate on a split-halves tree; and the `Notes`.**

### Q235 — Every check's green line is suppressed by any earlier check's failure, so a red run cannot say what ran
- Milestone: M2
- Size: XS
- Deps: after Q174
- Discovered by: **the wave-15 Q-domain registering lane** (2026-08-11), reading its own red flagless run and finding two checks had said nothing at all.
- Problem: Each check in `scripts/check-traceability.py` prints its own green line — the line that says how much it read, which D116 R3 made mandatory precisely so that *"a check that silently narrowed its glob would not be invisible in a green log"*. Every one of those prints is guarded by `if not failures:`, and `failures` is the **shared** accumulator passed to all seven checks in turn, not that check's own findings. So the guard means *"nothing has failed anywhere yet"*, and the moment any check records a finding, **every check after it in `CHECKS` order goes silent**. Measured on the flagless run of this working tree: `task-entries` reported 38 findings, and the four checks that run before it printed their green lines while `decision-owners` and `decision-index` — which both **passed**, confirmed by running them individually — printed nothing. A reader of that log cannot distinguish *passed*, *crashed*, *skipped* and *not implemented* for the last two, and which two they are is decided by dictionary order rather than by anything meaningful. It bites hardest exactly where D116 R3 aimed: `decision-index` is new this wave, its green line carries **both** counts on purpose, and its first appearance in a red run is silence.
- Do: Scope each green line to its own check. The smallest correct change is to let `Failures` report per-check — a count or a membership test keyed on the check name, which the class already has the data for, since every message is stored prefixed with its check. Then each guard becomes *"this check found nothing"*. Do not delete the guards and print unconditionally: a check that failed must not also print a green line, which is the confusion the current shape avoids and the reason it was written this way. Consider printing a one-line summary of which checks ran and how each ended, since the per-check lines are the only record of that today and a red run currently has no roll call at all.
- Accept: A check that finds nothing prints its green line regardless of what any other check found; a check that finds something prints no green line; the flagless run in a state where exactly one check fails still shows a line for each of the other six; and a reader of a red log can tell which checks ran, without inferring it from the order of a dictionary.
- Notes: This is the report half of a class this project keeps meeting on the instrument side — **Q174** on the self-test's summaries, **Q197** and **Q218** on annotations that name the wrong cause. Here nothing is measured wrongly and nothing is asserted wrongly; the run simply stops saying what it did, and it stops in an order nobody chose. Note the sharp consequence for this wave in particular: the standard verdict people quote for this script is *"six of six ok"*, and in a red run that phrase is unavailable — not because six checks did not pass, but because four of them were the only ones allowed to say so. Note also that the split-halves state this row was found in is routine, not exotic: `[task-entries]` is red by design between a registering lane's write and the orchestrator's, every wave.
- **Row and entry authored 2026-08-11 by the wave-15 Q-domain registering lane, from its own measurement rather than from the orchestrator's ledger — this item is not in it.** **Verified here, by observation and then by code**: the flagless run over this tree printed green lines for `freeze-boundary`, `matrix`, `decisions` and `task-citations`, failed on `task-entries` with 38 findings, and printed **nothing** for `decision-owners` or `decision-index`; running `--decision-index` alone exits 0 and prints its green line, so it passed rather than crashed; `CHECKS` is an ordered mapping whose sequence is exactly the four that printed, then the one that failed, then the two that went silent; every green line in the file is guarded by `if not failures:` against the shared accumulator — **seven such guards**, counted; and `Failures.add` already stores each message prefixed with its check name, which is what makes the per-check fix cheap. **Taken on report: nothing.** **Authored here, not reported: the whole `Do`, including the warning against printing unconditionally and the roll-call suggestion; the whole `Accept`; and the `Notes`.**

### Q238 — Promote the two-environment reproducibility comparison to a required push context ~~before the repository goes public~~, and arm it when a hosted run has reported it green on `main`

**[D164 §2 R1-R3, 2026-08-27 — the ordering term named the WRONG EVENT, and the row is SPLIT rather than reworded.]** The struck words are kept because four surfaces quote them. `before the repository goes public` was neither a precondition of the flip nor co-timed with it: **co-timed fails the runbook's own AT-THE-FLIP definition** (*"cannot be done earlier — the API refuses it while the repository is private"*) because **no refusal has ever been measured** — `rulesets` returns `200 []` and `branches/main/protection` returns `404 "Branch not protected"`, an absence — and **precondition fails the BEFORE row's own failure definition**, because nothing this row guards becomes exposed at the instant of the flip: the verifier page *"is already live and public at `https://antseal.org/` while this repository is private"* (runbook A6), and D135 §3 R5 already ruled the claim comes into existence **at deploy time and at no other moment**. The flip does not create the claim; it creates the ability to check it. This row keeps the **lane half**, available today. **`Q265`** carries the **arming half**, bound to *a hosted run reporting the context green on `main`* — the event that actually gates it.
- Milestone: M4
- Size: M
- Deps: after R86 (the comparison this promotes, built 2026-08-16 as a step inside `pages.yml`'s `publish` job), D135 §3 R1 (the deploy-gated tier this raises), D135 §3 R6 (raise-only-by-decision, and the measured precondition), D135 §10 R2 (the pre-priced arm to promote to), Q1 (the CI skeleton and lane conventions), Q65 (the publish-scope decision and the pre-public scrub, which this is a sibling obligation of), Q153 (the local-only precedent this argues against for this one lane). **Blocks nothing at M3.** **[D164 §2 R2/R3, 2026-08-27.]** ~~it is a precondition of making the repository public~~ — **struck**: measured, it is not. The ordering run is now ***after R86, D135 §3 R6 + §10 R2, Q1; before Q65*** — one run carrying both words, on the U78/D163 §2 R6 precedent — and `before Q65` is a **DEADLINE on the maintainer's own stated wish**, not a gate on starting. **Why a struck ordering EVENT can still carry a DEADLINE, said here because a reader who meets §2 R2 first will read it as a contradiction and try to repair it.** §2 R2 struck the flip as the event this obligation is **ordered against** — that is a claim about *causation*: nothing this row guards becomes exposed at the flip, so the flip cannot be what makes the work due. `before Q65` survives as a claim about *scheduling*: the maintainer asked for it before going public, the work is available today, and a deadline that is met early costs nothing. **The test that separates them is the failure mode.** Miss a precondition and the later act is unsafe; miss a deadline and the later act merely happens with the earlier one still open — which is exactly what `Q265`'s Branch 2 describes and prices. Do not re-derive this as a typo: two waves running have now found an ordering word that was deliberate. It is M4 and not Continuous because it is bounded and tickable, not because the flip gates it.
- Spec: Page provenance — reproducible build and published hash (MVP-SPEC.md line 139); Risks — malicious verifier host (line 186); release (line 156)
- Discovered by: **the maintainer** (2026-08-16), in session, on being told R86's tier: *"can we deploy-gated only for now but add a TODO.md item to require context on every push before we go public with the project on github"*. Registered by the registrar in the wave-21 close.
- Problem: **the residue D135 accepted is acceptable only while nobody has been invited to reproduce anything.** D135 §3 R1 ruled the comparison **deploy-gated** on a measured budget — §1.1 counts ~3 154 weighted minutes burned 2026-08-01→15 against a 3 000-minute allowance, independently re-measured at **3 324** by the same method, with 45 jobs in three runs having executed zero steps for an exhausted allowance. §3 R4.1 names the residue in the record's own words: *"Nothing between deploys. A commit that breaks reproducibility is detected at the next deploy, not at the push that broke it"*, with measured exposure of **days to weeks** given that the whole 19-day history contains two deploys.
  That trade is right today and stops being right at publication. **The invitation is what changes it**: a private repository's footer digest is a claim the maintainer makes to themselves, and a public one is a claim to strangers that the page they are verifying with can be rebuilt from source. A break sitting on `main` for days, between deploys, is a claim about the world that nobody is checking — and the whole point of MVP-SPEC.md line 139's provenance story is that a reader who rebuilds and compares gets a match.
