- Do: promote the comparison to a **required push context**, per **D135 §3 R6**, using the arm **D135 §10 R2** already pre-priced — a **separate workflow** with a workflow-level `on: push: paths:` filter, which GitHub evaluates *before any runner is assigned*, so a push touching no build input costs zero minutes. Recorded measurements to build against, not to re-derive: hit rate **52 %** narrow / **61 %** broad over 33 real push ranges, price **~81 weighted min/month**. Two riders D135 §10 R2 states and this row inherits: the filter needs a **self-test** (a filter that silently matches nothing is a lane that never runs and always looks green — the [[assertions-that-cannot-fail]] family at the workflow level), and its path set must include `scripts/wasm-pack-build.sh` and the files the tool-pin greps read, **because a pin bump moves the bytes without touching a crate**. The promotion is itself a **decision act**: D135 §3 R6 forbids an implementer raising the tier to make a build go green, on the same terms as `FUZZ_BUDGET_CEILING_MINUTES`.
- Accept:
  - **The bill is re-measured and recorded before the promotion, not asserted** — taken the way D135 §1.1 takes it (every run since the window start, every job rounded up to the whole minute, platform factors ×1/×2/×10, read-only `gh` queries), with the figure, the window and the command written into this row.
  - The measured monthly bill is **under the allowance** at the moment of promotion. If it is not, the row does not close: it records the number and stays open, because D135 §3 R6's precondition is a measurement and not an intention.
  - The promoted job is a **required status context** on `main`, and the required-context count in `docs/ci-verification.md` moves in the same act with its new total stated. **[Parenthetical REPLACED 2026-08-22 by D163 §2 R9 — the *plan-blocked* premise is stale.]** D141 §2 R8 measured `gh api repos/aed900/antseal/rulesets` → **`200 []`** (it was a 403) and `branches/main/protection` → **404 "Branch not protected"** — an absence, not a plan refusal — and the account is on **Pro**. The measured sequence (D163 §1.6) is: flip → free runners → **one green run on `main`** → required contexts → ship. So the joint dependence on `Q65` is real but for a different reason than recorded: not the plan, but that no context has a recent green and `docs/ci-verification.md` C2 warns a never-green required context *blocks the next push*. **This row's `after Q1` term is LIVE** (D163 §2 R3) and this is the only open row whose `Accept` consumes `Q1`'s branch-protection clause. **[AMENDED 2026-08-27 by D164 §2 R6/R7/R9 — three changes, and the first is not about ordering at all.]** **(i) The arming clause MOVES OUT of this row to `Q265`.** What stays here is building the filtered lane; arming the context is a GitHub write no agent may take, and it is the *same* write as `Q1`'s residue and Maintainer-actions entry **(8)** — no second maintainer act is minted. **(ii) `Do` and this clause DID NOT COMPOSE, which is a bigger defect than the ordering.** `Do` mandates a workflow-level `on: push: paths:` filter while this clause mandates a required status context — and a filtered-out workflow reports **nothing**, so a never-reported required context holds the gate pending forever. Three shapes were measured: filtering **both** `push` and `pull_request` is refused by the tree (`check-ci-paths.py` asserts every required context is produced exactly once on `pull_request`); **twin workflows with complementary filters** — GitHub's own documented workaround — is refused in-repo, because `ci-always.yml`'s header records that both filters *"quantify EXISTENTIALLY"*, so a mixed push fires both and every shared context is produced **twice**, which reds R3; **filtering `push` only, leaving `pull_request` unfiltered, and registering the name in `REQUIRED_CONTEXTS` *and* `PUSH_EXEMPT`** passes, on the `cross-os-macos`/`cross-os-windows` precedent whose stated safety condition is *"still produced on `pull_request`, which is what keeps them safe to require"*. The third shape is what this row builds. **The arming MECHANISM — classic protection with `enforce_admins:false` versus a ruleset — is NOT decided**, they differ on exactly the case this shape creates, and the lane is **explicitly permitted to return the question rather than build an unarmable lane**. **(iii) *"the count moves in the same act"* is amended to REGENERATE, NEVER INCREMENT.** `REQUIRED_CONTEXTS` in `scripts/check-ci-paths.py` is the CI-mounted authority and carries *"NEVER shrink this list"*; the runbook's prose says 19 contexts from 17 jobs while `ci.yml` declares **15** job ids — stale, owned by `Q56`, and C2 already rules *"regenerate the payload rather than copying one"*. **(iv) One new `Accept` clause, minted in place of a refused checker (D164 §2 R9):** `check-ci-paths.py` gains **R8** — *a workflow carrying a `paths:` filter on `push` may not produce a `REQUIRED_CONTEXTS` name unless it is also in `PUSH_EXEMPT`* — with a planted-fault arm in the existing `--self-test`, red **by its own message** — and the arm must be planted on a **CONSTRUCTED subject, never on the live tree**. Measured 2026-08-27: **no workflow in this repository carries a `paths:` filter on `push`**, so R8's domain is size **0** until this row builds the first one. An arm whose subject is empty is green because nothing reachable could redden it, which is this project's dominant defect class — and minting one inside a ruling that refused a checker for weak yield would be the worst place to install it. `check-ci-paths.py` already has the mechanism: `check()` takes `extra_workflows` as **text**, so a fixture is injected without ever writing a workflow into `.github/workflows/` that GitHub would really run. Use it, and assert the finding set equals `{"R8"}` rather than asserting `"R8" not in got`. The standalone checker that was considered instead scored **4 occurrences, 3 false positives, yield 1, FP rate 75 %** and was **REFUSED on that count**.
  - ~~The `paths:` filter has a self-test that goes **red** when the filter matches nothing, verified by its message and not by exit code alone.~~ **[STRUCK 2026-09-13 by D168 §2 R4 — moot under an unfiltered lane; the decay concern moves to D168 §2 R5's arms, which red when a `paths-ignore` appears or the push trigger is removed.]**
  - ~~The filter set includes every input that can move the module's bytes without touching a crate — at minimum `scripts/wasm-pack-build.sh`, `rust-toolchain.toml`, `.cargo/config.toml`, `Cargo.lock` and the tool-pin declaration sites — and a test names them against the script that reads them.~~ **[STRUCK 2026-09-13 by D168 §2 R4 — there is no filter set under an unfiltered lane; the inputs that move the bytes without touching a crate (including `HEAD` itself, which no path filter can express) are all covered by running on every push.]**
  - The raise is recorded as a **decision act** with its date, its measured basis and its authority, cited from D135 §3 R6 and from R86's row.
  - R25's Accept row 1 is re-cited at the new tier in the same act, so the clause *"a CI job enforces this"* names the enforcement that actually runs on a push rather than the one that runs on a deploy.
- Notes: ~~**the trigger is publication, not a date** — this row is a gate on making `aed900/antseal` public, and **Q34** (the M4 gate) and **Q65** (publish scope and the pre-public scrub) are where it must be checked, which is why it is filed at M4 rather than Continuous.~~ **[STRUCK 2026-08-27 by D164 §2 R8 — neither named checkpoint carries this row, and the sentence was an assertion nothing could redden.]** Measured at the ruling: `Q34`'s ordering run is `after Q13,Q21,Q28,Q31–Q33,Q65` — **no `Q238`**; `Q65`'s row names no `Q238`; and the M4 `Gate =` line names five clauses, **none of them this row**. So *"where it must be checked"* named two places that do not check it, and under rule 4 the gate is the exit criterion — M4 could pass with this row open and nothing would notice. **The gate is deliberately NOT widened.** The check lands instead as runbook step **A9** (`docs/ci-verification.md`), which the flip operator reads in the sitting — a human checkpoint, named, rather than a machine one that does not exist. This row is filed at M4 because it is bounded and tickable. **What it does not do**: it does not re-open D135. The deploy-gated tier was correct on the measurement available and stays correct until the bill allows otherwise; this row is the raise D135 itself pre-authorised and pre-priced, executed under its own precondition. **Read D135 §10 R2 before designing anything** — it already refuses the three arms that look cheaper (a job-level `if:`, which bills its runner anyway; a required context on every push, which pays a wasm32 release build twice; a dispatch-only lane, which is enforcement in name and a human's memory in fact) and the reasoning is measured rather than argued.
- Notes: **[D168, 2026-09-13, wave 35 — the raise is RULED; the lane is BUILT LOCALLY by the CI lane; the row stays open for its hosted witness.]** **`Do`'s filtered shape is SUPERSEDED**: D135 §10 R2's `on: push: paths:` workflow, constructed and run through `check-ci-paths.py`'s own `check()`, goes red on R1, R2 and R3 by message, and GitHub advises against requiring workflows that can be skipped; D168 §2 R1 rules an **unfiltered** `.github/workflows/reproducible-build.yml` on push to `main` and every pull request, context `reproducible-build`, required contexts **19 → 20**, with an explicit `wasm-pack-build.sh --build-only` before the comparison. **`Accept` row 1 (bill re-measured and recorded) — MET**: D135 §1.1's method over 2026-09-01T00:00Z → the flip (private) gives **64 jobs, 596 weighted minutes**; the flip → 2026-09-13T00:25Z (public) gives **32 jobs, 111** (CodeQL 50, `ci` 55, `ci-always` 6); command and window in D168 §1.1. The timing endpoint's `billable` field reads 0 even on an executed private run and was **not** used as evidence. **`Accept` row 2 (under the allowance) — MET BY RESTATEMENT**: D168 §2 R2 replaces a precondition that had become trivially true with three conjuncts that can fail — visibility `public`, every `runs-on` label standard (census: 22 `ubuntu-latest`, 1 `macos-latest`, 1 `windows-latest`), proxy figure recorded — with reopen triggers. **`Accept` rows 4 and 5 — STRUCK** as moot (above). **The `R8` this row's `Accept` names via D164 §2 R9 is RESTATED**: tag `R8` belongs to D165's depth rule, so the clause now reads as the `MUST_RUN_ON_EVERY_PUSH` registration plus D168 §2 R5's decay arms. **`Accept` row 3 is met LOCALLY and WITNESSED ONLY by a hosted run**: the comparator has **never executed on a hosted runner** (both `pages` runs predate it), so this row ticks on the first push that carries the lane — a consented act — with the run log's stamped-commit, `build B`, reproducible-digest, both environment-path and comparison-seconds lines copied here verbatim (a `build A: …` line exists only on a red run — corrected 2026-09-13), because run records past retention are deletable from 2026-10-01.
- Notes: **[2026-09-13, wave 35 — lane W1: the D168 lane is BUILT LOCALLY; hosted witness OWED.]** Landed: `.github/workflows/reproducible-build.yml` (push to `main` + `pull_request`, no filter, `permissions: contents: read`, workflow-level anchor-network key, job and context `reproducible-build`, an explicit `wasm-pack-build.sh --build-only` before `reproducible-build.sh --compare`); `scripts/check-ci-paths.py` — `REQUIRED_CONTEXTS` **19 → 20**, `MUST_RUN_ON_EVERY_PUSH` now a mapping from each context to its own reason, R3 naming a `PUSH_EXEMPT` mis-registration, four whole-finding-set arms all exact (arm (iii) `[]`; `+ paths-ignore` `['R1','R6']`; push removed `['R3','R6']`; exempted `['R3']`) and six plants caught by message; `reproducible-build.sh`'s tier header and `REPRO_TIER`; the runbook's A9 re-observed and C2's payload **regenerated** at 20 contexts from `REQUIRED_CONTEXTS` (sha256 `2e98a94c…a495c`). **The act broke three self-test arms of a different checker, and the lane caught it**: the new workflow moved `.github/action-pins.tsv`'s invocation counts (checkout 23 → 24, rust-cache 19 → 20), so `check-action-pins.py --self-test` printed P5 `NO MATCH` and both P6 arms `NOT APPLIED` — which hosted `ci-always` would have turned red on the push; the arms now key their plants by action name, derive dates that cannot collide with a row's real review date, and read expected counts from the validated ledger, proven on today, on the 2026-11-16 renewal day and on two collision days. **Owed: one consented push of `main`**, whose `reproducible-build` check run is the witness, with its log lines copied here verbatim.
- Notes: **[2026-09-13, wave 35 — TICKED on its hosted witness.]** Pushed under the maintainer's express consent given 2026-09-13 in direct reply to a request naming each action, the destination `github.com/aed900/antseal` and the account `aed900` (`ef5f6d9..bfdc93e`, `main` by name, 0 stray refs, read back from `git ls-remote` and the API). **Run `34781450856`** (`reproducible-build`, event `push`, head `bfdc93e`): one job, `success`, 10 steps, runner assigned, 2026-09-13T20:39:36Z → 20:43:08Z; tool provisioning 2 m 10 s, build A (its own step) 32 s. Log lines, verbatim: `the commit both builds stamp: bfdc93eba79883c79f79fe663cb4a24d024f0d03` · `build B: 29 s` · `reproducible across environments: antseal_wasm_bg.wasm edd62f059c25c48674f47274fb8cb713fb4a2f83e3bf3d3fcbd54241040da2f3 (1867870 B), antseal_wasm.js 3249ed0267843953f3e4a1a9c5748fa7b6a178f5f6289e74262681d295b4cf06` · `A path=/home/runner/work/antseal/antseal CARGO_HOME=/home/runner/.cargo` · `B path=/tmp/antseal-repro.HubdI9zh/checkout CARGO_HOME=/tmp/antseal-repro.HubdI9zh/cargo-home (lengths differ by 3 and 19 characters)` · `two-environment comparison: 29 s`. **`Accept` row 3 — MET**: the promoted job reported `success` on `main` at the first push that carried it; arming it in branch protection is `Q265`'s, which the maintainer deferred on 2026-09-13. The same push's `ci` went 15/15 jobs green. **What it does not cover**: build B copies the registry `cache` and `index` from build A's `CARGO_HOME` (only `src` is re-extracted), so the comparison varies the path and the home directory but does **not** independently re-fetch crates — see `Q255`'s held re-decision.

### Q239 — Stop paying a full 19-job matrix for pushes that change no code, and re-cadence the billed cross-OS arms
- Milestone: M4
- Size: M
- Deps: after **D138** (ruled in the same act); **Q43** (no logic inline in CI shell — this row's checker exists because of it); **Q153** (the cheap-lane precedent and the required-context count); **R86/D135 §1.1** (the bill this row spends against); **Q20** (whose `copy-style` steps ride the job that moves). **Q238** is the sibling raise, not a dep. **Q78** still owns whole-allowance accounting.
- Spec: CI policy and the no-real-network rule (MVP-SPEC.md line 189); `docs/ci-verification.md`'s own evidence rules
- Milestone note (rule 6): the milestone is **inferred, not spec-assigned**, and is recorded as M4 rather than M3 deliberately — the work is CI-cost work minted and closed *after* M3's gate passed, it belongs to no M3 `Gate:` clause, and it sits beside **Q238**, the raise it will one day be reversed by. The D138 lane proposed M3; the reason for M4 is recorded here rather than silently applied.
- Discovered by: **the maintainer, 2026-08-16**, in direct reply to the measured Actions bill — *"Path-filter docs-only pushes and move cross-OS to weekly"* — after the allowance was observed exhausted in production.
- Problem: **3 324 weighted Actions minutes were spent 2026-08-01→15 against a 3 000 allowance, and it is now exhausted** — the wave-21 push produced `ci` `31943527193` with 19/19 jobs, `steps == []`, `runner_name: ""`, 7 s wall, and the Q19 dispatch `31943600047` was refused identically. Two draws are pure waste: **29 % of push runs (10 of 35 consecutive ranges) touched no code**, and **two cross-OS arms bill 22.9 weighted minutes per push** at ×10 and ×2 multipliers while being 88 % of that lane's cost for 9 % of its wall-clock. Nothing in the tree priced either. **The obvious fix is wrong twice over**: a blanket `paths-ignore` would skip `traceability`, whose inputs *are* the docs; and a path filter has **no failure mode at all** — when it is wrong the job simply does not run, and the absence is indistinguishable from a pass.
- Do: implement **D138**. Filter `ci.yml`'s **push** only and never `pull_request`. Move `traceability` + `secret-guard` byte-identically into a new unfiltered `ci-always.yml`, and the macOS/Windows arms into a weekly `cross-os-extended.yml` that keeps `pull_request`, with every job id and `name:` unchanged so no context is renamed. Build `scripts/check-ci-paths.py`: one definition of the exclusion mirrored into the YAML and held equal; no `pull_request` path filter anywhere; both per-event context sets frozen with no duplicate producers; and — the load-bearing rule — **no excluded path read by anything `ci.yml` runs**, proven over string literals, `include_str!` resolved against **both** `CARGO_MANIFEST_DIR` and the source file's directory, and registered `grep -r` surfaces re-run against excluded files' *content*. Close the script-edge set by **classification with reasons**, never blindly. Give it `--self-test` with an arm per rule, each red **by its own rule tag**, and wire both modes into `scripts/local-gate.sh` and into `ci-always.yml`'s existing job — zero new contexts.
- Accept:
  - 19 contexts on `pull_request` and 17 on `push`, each produced exactly once, verified against a frozen constant rather than counted by eye.
  - `check-ci-paths.py --self-test` passes with at least one arm per rule and a green control; planting `docs/decisions/**` into the exclusion reds with the R4c message **naming D18** as the reader.
  - `check-ci-shell.py`, `check-copy-style.py`, `ci-lanes.sh anchor-net-policy` and all seven `check-traceability.py` checks green; every workflow parses as YAML.
  - The saving is stated **with its arithmetic and its cadence assumption**, not as a headline.
  - **The remote witness is named and owed**: the first push touching only `TODO.md` must produce exactly two check runs and **no `ci` run at all**. Until that is observed, this row's mechanism has never run on the remote (Q43's rule) and the row says so.
- Notes: measured 2026-08-16 — 9 of 35 ranges skipped under the ruled exclusion; per-run **83.8 → 61.0** weighted (code push) and **→ 0.54** (docs push); **~2 450 weighted min/month** at the measured cadence of 64 pushes/month, ~3 000 on GitHub's billed per-job-round-up basis. The maintainer's own estimate of ~1 375 assumed ~25 pushes/month and is superseded by measurement, not by opinion. `concurrency` was measured at **1 superseded run in 35** and **refused** — it would have been the instinctive addition and it buys nothing here.
- Notes: **two things this act discovered rather than assumed.** (1) Moving `secret-guard` emptied the anchor strings `scripts/check-ci-shell.py`'s *own* self-test plants against, so it went red on the next gate run and was re-anchored on `golden-vectors` — the [[gate-finds-what-analysis-misses]] class again, and something no path checker could have seen. (2) The new checker's first complete `--self-test` caught **itself** reporting GREEN over a genuine read of `MVP-SPEC.md`, because both readers spell the path as an offset from `CARGO_MANIFEST_DIR` while the resolver anchored on the source file's directory — an assertion that could not fail, caught by the one instrument built to catch that class.
- Notes: **[Partial remote witness, 2026-08-16, and it arrived through a refusal.]** The push that landed this row (`aa9553e`) was refused for minutes like everything else this month, but **a refusal still proves the job graph**, because GitHub enumerates jobs and evaluates path filters *before* assigning a runner (D135 §10 R2 measured that ordering). Observed: all three workflows read back `state=active` from `GET /actions/workflows`, and the push created **`ci` with 15 jobs and `ci-always` with 2** — 17 on push, exactly as ruled, with `cross-os-linux` present and no macOS or Windows arm. So the **split, the context count and the cross-OS re-cadence are witnessed remotely**; what is still unwitnessed is the `paths-ignore` filter itself, which needs a push touching only excluded paths. **This row's own registration is that push** — see the next note.
- Notes: **[Witness COMPLETE, 2026-08-16 — and it cost nothing.]** Commit `8f3ed6e` changed only `TODO.md` and `tasks/Q.md`, both excluded paths, with the prediction written into the commit message *before* the push. Observed: **`ci-always` fired alone, run `31950452965`; no `ci` run was created for that SHA at all.** The identical push before this change would have enumerated 19 jobs. **A refused run is a sufficient witness for this property** — GitHub evaluates `paths-ignore` before assigning a runner, so an exhausted allowance suppresses the *execution* and not the *decision*, which is the whole of what Accept row 5 asks. That is the rare case where the thing being tested is observable precisely because everything else is broken; `docs/ci-verification.md`'s *"a lane that has never run on the remote is not evidence"* is satisfied here at the dispatch layer rather than the execution layer, and this note says so rather than letting a later reader assume the lane ran green.
- Notes: **owed, and outside D138's write scope** — `docs/ci-verification.md` and `CONTRIBUTING.md:38` need a provenance update, since four contexts changed which workflow file produces them while the payload itself is unchanged. `scripts/ci-lanes.sh`'s `fuzz-budget` lane does not see `cross-os-extended.yml`, so that workflow's cadence is not budget-guarded.
- Notes: **[Registrar, 2026-08-19 — wave 26 execution. Three statements of the 2026-08-17 amendment are superseded by the act that closed this row.]** **(a)** *"Nothing pins the behaviour … four hits, all inside `ant_backend.rs`, zero test assertions"* — **no longer true, which is the point**: the amount is now decided in one place, `allowance_to_approve` (`crates/antseal-net/src/backend.rs:133-141`, default feature set), and `crates/antseal-net/tests/allowance_policy.rs` holds it with five rule tests plus two call-site tests. **(b)** *"upstream carries the identical guard"* is right and **understated**: it is at **two** sites, `evmlib-0.9.0/src/wallet.rs:185-190` (merkle) as well as `:415-426` (single-node). **(c)** §2.3's locators moved — it ran `:692-876` and now runs **`:692-1004`**; the `Problem`'s `ant_backend.rs:437-455`/`:446-448` are superseded by `ensure_allowance` at `:433-500`, the rule at `backend.rs:133-141`, the policy call at `:479` and the approve at `:484`. **The rounded arm is priced for the first time and DECLINED**: it is a dial between the other two, not a third point, and it does not buy the anonymity because the discriminator is emitting a non-`U256::MAX` approval **at all** — every upstream approve in the pinned graph is `U256::MAX` at all four call sites. **The decider is an affordance antseal lacks**: no subcommand emits an `approve`, so any standing allowance it creates it cannot revoke — a **contract** risk, not a **key** risk.

### Q240 — antseal's exact-allowance approve is a public, permanent on-chain fingerprint, and nothing pins the behaviour that creates it
- Milestone: M4
- Size: S
- Deps: after Q21 (the threat model, whose §2.3 "Wallet linkability" is the section this belongs in and is `not written`), Q25 (wallet-hygiene territory), S: the `ant-backend` payment path; D73 §3.5.4 and §7 item 3 (the finding, explicitly *"a finding and a proposed row, not a ruling"*)
- Spec: Risks — wallet linkability and payment privacy (MVP-SPEC.md line 189); Storage — payments and receipts (line 105); Milestones M4 (line 157)
- Discovered by: **D73's lane** (2026-08-16), which measured it while ruling the zero-telemetry success metric and **deliberately refused to rule it** (§6 item 6: *"a wallet-hygiene and threat-model judgment with its own arms … it belongs to Q21/Q25, and a metric record has no business settling it in passing"*).
- Problem: **a defensible choice with an undocumented cost, held in place by nothing.** `crates/antseal-net/src/ant_backend.rs:437-455` (`ensure_allowance`) approves the **exact quoted total** — never `U256::MAX` — with a `if current >= total { return Ok(0) }` short-circuit at `:446-448`, and the module says so at `:49-51`. Upstream does the opposite: `evmlib-0.9.0/src/wallet.rs:415-421` approves `U256::MAX`, and `ant-core-0.5.0/src/data/client/payment.rs:130` documents *"Approves `U256::MAX` (unlimited) spending."* **Because an exact allowance is consumed by the very payment it was raised for, the short-circuit almost never fires**: an antseal user emits a fresh `approve(vault, <non-round exact amount>)` before essentially **every seal**, while a stock ant-core user emits one `approve(vault, 2^256-1)` once per address, ever. Calldata and the `Approval` log are both public and permanent, so **antseal payers are separable from the general Autonomi payer population** by a pattern nobody chose to publish. The choice is right on wallet-hygiene grounds — a standing unlimited allowance to a contract is its own risk — and this row is not an argument to reverse it. **Two things are wrong.** (1) `docs/threat-model.md` §2.3 opens at `:443` and its own scope line at `:448-450` says it must cover *"what an observer learns from the chain without any bundle at all"*; its status at `:452` is `not written — M4 (Q21)`, so the product's threat model does not contain the one linkability fact the product actively creates. (2) **Nothing pins the behaviour.** No test asserts the allowance is exact rather than unlimited, so a refactor toward upstream's shape — the obvious "simplification" — would silently destroy a deliberate privacy/hygiene trade and no lane would notice.
- Do: **weigh it, write it, and pin it.** Weigh the three arms D73 enumerates and does not choose between: unlimited-approve costs a standing allowance; exact-approve costs anonymity; a **rounded-up** approve might cost neither, and is the arm nobody has priced. Then land the conclusion in **two** places: the trade in `docs/threat-model.md` §2.3 (and Q25's wallet-hygiene guidance, if the answer changes user advice), and a **test** that reddens if the approve amount stops being what the ruling says it should be. Whichever arm wins, the observable is the same and the test is the same shape: assert the value passed to `approve_to_spend_tokens` against the rule, on the `MockBackend`/devnet path, with a planted fault.
- Accept:
  - The arm is chosen with its reason, in the threat model rather than in a code comment, and the anonymity cost is stated in the user-facing terms an observer would use — not as an implementation note.
  - A test asserts the approved amount matches the chosen rule and fails if it is changed to `U256::MAX`, to a rounded value, or to anything else; the fault is planted and watched red by message.
  - The `if current >= total` short-circuit's near-never-firing behaviour is stated where the code is, so the next reader does not mistake it for an optimisation that works.
  - Runs against `MockBackend` without a live network (project rule 1); no mainnet spend is required to prove any of it.
- **[AMENDMENT 2026-08-17, wave 23 — this row's premises have SPLIT, and three of its statements are now wrong. Re-measured centrally at registration.]** **(1) One premise is DISCHARGED.** *"The threat model does not contain the one linkability fact the product actively creates"* is no longer true: Q21 wrote `docs/threat-model.md` §2.3 in full, it records the exact-allowance approve as an observability property, it names this row as the owner, and it **rules nothing** — which is what leaves the rest of this row standing. **(2) The other premise STANDS, re-measured**: `grep -rn "approve_to_spend_tokens\|token_allowance\|U256::MAX"` over `crates/` returns **four hits, all inside `crates/antseal-net/src/ant_backend.rs`** — two prose (`:50`, `:435`) and two calls (`:441`, `:451`) — and **zero test assertions anywhere**, `crates/*/tests/` included. Nothing pins the behaviour. **(3) The `Problem`'s *"upstream does the opposite"* is HALF RIGHT and the half that is wrong matters.** Upstream carries the **identical** short-circuit guard — `evmlib-0.9.0/src/wallet.rs:415`, `if allowance < total_amount_to_be_paid` — so the two clients are the same algorithm (query, compare, conditionally approve) and differ **only in the amount**: upstream passes `U256::MAX` at `:421`, antseal passes `total` at `ant_backend.rs:451`. The frequency difference this row is about is a *consequence* of the amount, not evidence of a different code shape, and describing it as *the opposite* invites a fix that changes the shape. §2.3 states this correctly; this row's text did not. (The row's cited range `:415-421` is correct **as a range** — it spans guard through argument.) **(4) All three threat-model locators are STALE.** The row cites `docs/threat-model.md:443`, `:448-450` and `:452`; §2.3 now opens at **`:692`** and runs to `:876`, its scope line is `:694-700` with the quoted promise at `:697-698`, `:443` is **§2.1's** scope line, `:452` is a blank line, and the `not written — M4 (Q21)` marker the row quotes **no longer appears anywhere in the file** (`grep -c` → 0). **What is still owed is exactly the pin**, plus the choice between the three arms — and the third arm is still unpriced.
- Notes: **The framing this row was nearly minted with is wrong and is corrected here.** D73 §3.5.4 says of the finding *"It is documented nowhere as an observability property"* — that sentence is now **stale about itself**, because D73 §3.5.4 **is** the documentation, at length. What remains true, and is the whole of this row, is that **nothing pins it** and the threat model does not carry it. Registering the row against the accurate claim matters: a lane briefed to "document it" would find it already documented and close the row without writing the test, which is the only half that has teeth.

### Q241 — Every publishable antseal crate is unpackageable, and the obvious fix collides with the wildcard ban
- Milestone: M4
- Size: XS — **resized 2026-08-22 (D158 §2 R8)** from `M`; D72 §6 item 1 had registered it `S`. The `M` was sized against building the fix *and* the CI lane; with the fix deferred by D158 §2 R4 and the lane refused by D158 §1.11, the residue is one comment block, this entry's text, and a ledger line.
- Deps: after ~~P13/D19~~ (**STRUCK 2026-08-22, D158 §2 R5** — rule 7's test asks which `Accept` clause cannot be written until the term is `[x]`; the clause it would serve is row 3, and `deny.toml` is landed and green, while P13's open residue — *first scheduled-run evidence + D19 red-lane demo* — touches nothing this row measures. Retained as an **annotation, not an edge**: `P13/D19 (the rule this row measures against — consumer, not a dep)`), P2 (the crates.io name reservations whose `0.0.0` placeholders are what the resolver finds — **KEPT**: a true predecessor, because without them the failure has a different shape), ~~Q31~~ (**STRUCK 2026-08-22, D158 §2 R5** — it served `Accept` row 2 only, and that row was the registrar's rewording of D72 §6 item 1, which asked for *"a `cargo package --no-verify` smoke check"*, an **instrument** naming no lane and no release workflow; the rewording is what manufactured this edge. With row 2 restored to D72's scope (D158 §2 R7) no clause needs `Q31` `[x]`, and D141 §2 R1 records that `Q31` performs no release execution in any case), D72 §6 (the record that registers this as discovered work). **The real ordering is not a row and must not be written as one** (D158 §2 R5): this row's work is due **before the first crates.io publish** — D72 §2 R8's review date, *"the first release after M4"* — which under rule 7 is an `Accept`/venue condition (D154 §1.8's fourth surface), recorded here in Notes and never as an `after`, never as an inverted edge. **No cycle is created**: nothing in the tracker is `after Q241`, so striking two terms from a leaf changes no other row's availability.
- Spec: Milestones M4 — release (MVP-SPEC.md line 157); distribution channel (line 157)
- Discovered by: **D72's lane** (2026-08-16), registered at `docs/decisions/D72-…:730-737,757-762` with its reproducer; re-measured by the wave-22 registrar 2026-08-17.
- Problem: **a self-referential dev-dependency that cargo resolves against the registry rather than the workspace.** Four crates carry a `[dev-dependencies]` edge on an antseal crate with `features = ["test-util"]`, including two **self**-edges: `antseal-core/Cargo.toml:135`, `antseal-net/Cargo.toml:117` (self), plus `antseal-net:122`, `antseal-anchor:64`, `antseal-cli:126,132,139`. Each is written `{ workspace = true, features = ["test-util"] }`, so the version requirement arrives by inheritance from `Cargo.toml:64,70,76` (`version = "=0.0.0"`). At package time cargo resolves that requirement **against crates.io**, finds the published `0.0.0` placeholder P2 reserved, and that placeholder has no `test-util` feature — so `cargo publish --dry-run -p antseal-core` exits **101** with *"depends on `antseal-core` with feature `test-util` but `antseal-core` does not have that feature"*. `--no-verify` fails identically, which is what proves it is **packaging and not verification**. **The obvious fix collides with a rule that has its own record**: dropping the `version` makes the dev-dep a bare path dep, which cargo strips at package time and which unblocks the publish — and trips `deny.toml:80`'s `wildcards = "deny"`, whose reason is written on the field itself at `Cargo.toml:60-63` ~~and in `docs/decisions/D19-advisory-lane.md:51-53`~~ (**corrected 2026-08-22, D158 §2 R9 item 2**: `D19:51-53` is cited here as co-author of the collision, and that passage in fact records `allow-wildcard-paths = true` — it is evidence **against** a collision, not for one). **It is invisible to every lane**: `grep` over `.github/workflows/` finds no `cargo package` and no `cargo publish` anywhere, so no CI job can ever see it. ~~The only route that would is `scripts/reserve-crates.sh:191`'s `cargo publish --dry-run`, run by hand.~~ **Corrected 2026-08-22 (D158 §2 R7)**: `scripts/reserve-crates.sh:191` is **not** a route that could see it — it packages a *synthesized placeholder with no dependencies* (`make_placeholder()`, `:92-110`), which is why `docs/naming/P2-crates-reservation-runbook.md:109` records it **green for all five** on the same day `-p antseal-core` exits 101. **Nothing in this tree can see the failure, in CI or by hand.** *[Registrar locator note, 2026-08-22 (D162 §2 R5): the `Cargo.toml:60-63` and `Cargo.toml:64,70,76` citations above were exact when written and are now `:60-69` and `:70,76,82` — D162 §4 item 1 grew the comment block from 4 lines to 10. Trust the quoted string, not the line number.]*
- Do: **decide how a publishable crate carries its own test-only features, then prove it against a real package run.** Candidate arms, none free: (a) move `test-util` out of the crate it tests into a separate unpublished helper crate, removing the self-edge entirely; (b) keep the self-edge and set `allow-wildcard-paths`-compatible shapes — note `deny.toml:81` **already** sets `allow-wildcard-paths = true`, so the collision is narrower than D19's prose implies and this arm deserves re-measuring rather than assuming; (c) publish a real `0.1.0` carrying the feature before anything depends on it, which changes the ordering of P2's reservations; (d) `publish = false` on the affected crates, which is a decision about crates.io scope and belongs with D72 ~~§2 R5~~ **§2 R8**'s (**citation corrected 2026-08-22, D158 §2 R7** — §2 R5 is the GitHub-Releases channel ruling and defers to §2 R8 on crates.io) *"no `antseal*` crate is published to crates.io as part of the M4 release"* rather than being taken here by default. **Settle explicitly whether the failure survives a version bump** — it was measured only against the published `0.0.0`, and *"a real version would fix it"* is a plausible-sounding guess that nobody has tested.
- Accept:
  - `cargo package` (not merely `--dry-run`) succeeds for every crate the release intends to publish, or the intent is recorded as "publish nothing" with D72 §2 R5 cited and the dev-dep left as-is with its reason. — **Accept row 1 is MET via its second branch, 2026-08-22 (D158 §2 R1).** The intent is recorded: **no `antseal*` crate is published to crates.io as part of the M4 release**, ruled at **D72 §2 R8** — *not* §2 R5, which is the GitHub-Releases channel ruling and defers to §2 R8 on crates.io. The nine `[dev-dependencies]` edges are left in place; their reason is recorded at `Cargo.toml:60-63` as corrected by D158 §2 R6, and the fix that discharges them is written out at D158 §2 R4. **The row stays open** as the entry cost of §2 R8's own review date (*"the first release after M4"*, D72 §2 R8), which D72 §5.1 requires be paid *"first"*. *[Registrar, 2026-08-22: spliced verbatim per D158 §4 item 1; D162 §4 item 4 says "replace" where D158 says "splice onto", and the appending reading is taken because D156 §2 R1–R3 make the criterion and its annotation two axes — deleting the criterion would lose the branch that is MET. The block's own `Cargo.toml:60-63` locator now reads `:60-69` (D162 §2 R5).]*
  - ~~Whatever CI lane owns the release runs the packaging check, so this class cannot be invisible again.~~ **AMENDED 2026-08-22 (D158 §2 R7).** The reproducer and the exact nine-line fix are recorded where the next lane will find them (D158 §1.8, §2 R4), and the smoke check D72 §6 item 1 asked for lands **with** the fix, never before it: a check that is red on arrival cannot join a gate. Until then the class is invisible **by ruling, with the reason written down**, which is a different state from the invisible-by-accident this row was minted for. **Correction carried in the same act**: `scripts/reserve-crates.sh:191` is *not* a route that could see it — it packages a synthesized placeholder with no dependencies (`make_placeholder()`, `:92-110`), which is why `docs/naming/P2-crates-reservation-runbook.md:109` records it **green for all five** on the same day `-p antseal-core` exits 101. **Nothing in this tree can see the failure, in CI or by hand.**
  - `deny.toml`'s wildcard rule is either unmoved or moved with a recorded reason; it is not weakened silently to make a publish work. **MET 2026-08-22 (D158 §2 R7)**: unmoved; the recorded reason is `Cargo.toml:60-63` as corrected by D158 §2 R6. It was never in tension with the fix (D158 §1.3, §2 R2).
  - The version-bump question is answered by measurement, not by inference, and the answer is written down. **MET 2026-08-22 (D158 §2 R7), and the answer is NO.** D158 §1.8: for the two **self**-edges the failure is **unconditional and circular** — the requirement is forced to `=<the crate's own version>`, which is by definition not on the registry when the crate is packaged, so **no bump ever fixes it**; for the six cross-edges it is fixable only by publish **ordering**, which the two self-edges gate. **This is deduction from the measured index at D158 §1.1 plus the pinned toolchain's documented rules, not an execution** — D158 §3 says so plainly, and D158 §2 R3's multi-package run is what would convert it.
  - **[Fifth `Accept` row, added 2026-08-22 by D158 §2 R7 — D158 §1.9's blockers otherwise have no home.]** The three publish blockers that are **not** the dev-dep are named on this row so the first publish does not rediscover them one at a time: `0.0.0` is burned (D72 §1.6 blocker 1); **no `description` exists on any crate or in `[workspace.package]`**, a hard `cargo publish` error that D72 §1.6 blocker 2 expected *"at M4/Q29"* and that **Q29 ticked without** (D158 §1.9); and the multi-package `cargo package` mode has never been run here (D158 §1.8.3, §2 R3).
- Notes: **Blocks any publish, and therefore blocks Q31's execution if the release ships crates as well as binaries.** **D72's own citations into the manifests are stale by roughly eighty lines** (`D72:347` cites `antseal-core/Cargo.toml:133-134` for what is at `:134-135`; `:353` cites `Cargo.toml:53` for `:64`; `:359` cites `antseal-net/Cargo.toml:55-59` for `:113-117`; `:761` cites `Cargo.toml:49-52` for `:60-64`) — instrument-class, in the ledger, and noted here so a lane does not follow them. **One correction to the finding as it was handed over**: no dev-dep line carries a literal `version =`; every one uses `workspace = true`, so the fix has to be made at the **workspace** table and not on the per-crate line, which is precisely what makes the P13 collision unavoidable rather than dodgeable.

### Q242 — A cryptographic verifier invites audit with a public issue tracker as its only disclosure route
- Milestone: M4
- Size: S
- Deps: **with/before Q65** — *not* `after` *(corrected 2026-08-18 by D141 §2 R5(b): this row is **CO-TIMED**, and the `after` was the opposite of its own prose)*. It cannot be inverted outright either: Accept row 2 demands private vulnerability reporting read *enabled* **in the same window as the flip**, and the endpoint 404s on a private repository, so the policy must ship before the flip while the mechanism can only be switched on after it. Splitting the row would publish a `SECURITY.md` naming a route that 404s — the D140 103-minute false-window class. The ordering vocabulary `with`/`before` already exists in this tracker (`TODO.md:433`, `:482`). Q21/Q23 (threat model and security posture), Q22 (README, which must point at the policy)
- Spec: Risks — malicious verifier host (MVP-SPEC.md line **186**; *corrected 2026-08-18 — this entry read `line 189`, which is Free-TSA terms and rate limits; `docs/threat-model.md:2040-2060`'s risk-coverage map already had 186 → §2.4 and 189 → `docs/user/timestamp-authorities.md`*); Positioning (line 28); Milestones M4 (line 157)
- Discovered by: **the pre-public scrub's GitHub-side lane** (2026-08-17, `docs/reviews/pre-public-scrub-github-side.md`), and confirmed by the wave-22 registrar against the GitHub API.
- Problem: **the repository has no way to receive a vulnerability report privately, and is about to acquire a public one.** Measured 2026-08-17: Issues are **enabled**; `securityPolicyUrl` is empty and **no `SECURITY.md` exists anywhere in the tree**; `.github/` contains **only** `workflows/` and eight workflow files — no `ISSUE_TEMPLATE/`, no `CODE_OF_CONDUCT.md`; the only community-health file is `CONTRIBUTING.md` at the repo root. GitHub's private vulnerability reporting endpoint returns **404** because the feature is public-repository-only, so the one mechanism that would fix this **cannot be enabled until the flip and must be enabled as part of it**. For a proof-of-existence tool whose entire value rests on a verifier third parties are invited to audit, the moment the repo goes public the default disclosure route becomes *"open a public issue describing the flaw"* — which is the wrong shape for the one class of report this project most wants to receive.
- Do: **write the policy and turn the mechanism on in the same act as the flip.** A `SECURITY.md` naming the supported versions, the reporting route, the expected response window, and — specifically for this product — **what is and is not in scope**: the verifier and the formats are in scope; the Autonomi network's availability is not, because MVP-SPEC.md's own promise is that evidence validity never depends on it. Enable GitHub private vulnerability reporting the moment visibility allows. Add an `.github/ISSUE_TEMPLATE/` that routes a security report away from the public tracker rather than relying on a reporter reading `SECURITY.md` first. Point `README.md` at it (Q22). **Positioning discipline applies to this document too**: it describes proof of existence, integrity and priority, and must not drift into notary language.
- Accept:
  - `SECURITY.md` exists, is reachable from GitHub's security tab (`securityPolicyUrl` non-empty) and from the README, and states scope, route and response expectation.
  - Private vulnerability reporting is **enabled**, verified by the API returning enabled rather than 404, in the same window as the visibility change.
  - An issue template exists that offers a non-public route before a text box.
  - No claim in the policy exceeds what the product does — reviewed against Q20's positioning lint if that lint covers `SECURITY.md`, and added to its scan if it does not.
- Notes: **Ordering is the whole row**: landing this *after* the flip means a window in which the only disclosure route is public, and windows like that are measured in the time it takes one person to read the code. Registered as a product row rather than an instrument finding because it is a shipped artifact and a live disclosure channel, not a checker.

### Q243 — The job that publishes the devnet evidence artifact never runs the redactor's self-test
- Milestone: M4
- Size: S
- Deps: **before Q65** — *inverted 2026-08-18 by D141 §2 R5(a); the `after` was backwards and this row's own entry already said so* (*"which is why Q243 must land first"*, `tasks/Q.md` Q65 finding 4 — the contradiction sat between two entries in **one file**). The artifact becomes readable the instant visibility changes, so the gate must exist first. Note D145 §2 R2: the **existing** artifact `9132238802` predates this gate by five days and is therefore *not* covered by it retroactively — it was scanned by hand instead (`files=8 findings=0 verdict=CLEAN`) and is **kept**, not deleted. Q2 (`secret-guard`'s lane), S: the devnet E2E harness
- Spec: Risks — key/secret handling (MVP-SPEC.md line 189); project rule 6 (secret material lives only in the vault)
- Discovered by: **the pre-public scrub's GitHub-side lane** (2026-08-17); the gap between the local self-test and the publishing job was found by the wave-22 registrar re-measuring it.
- Problem: **the redaction is well built, well tested, and untested where it runs.** `scripts/e2e-devnet.sh:105-106` defines `redact()` as a key-name-scoped `sed` over `wallet_private_key`, `*PRIVATE_KEY` and `*SECRET_KEY`, applied at capture time (`:254-255`), and it has a genuinely good **two-sided** self-test at `:380-395`: one arm fails if key material survives, the other fails if a contract address is over-redacted — so it catches both directions rather than only the loud one. That self-test runs from `scripts/local-gate.sh:548`. **The CI job that actually produces and uploads the artifact does not run it**: `.github/workflows/devnet-e2e-cron.yml:119` invokes `./scripts/e2e-devnet.sh` **bare**, and `:127-130` then uploads `target/e2e-devnet/` as `devnet-e2e-evidence`. **No Rust test anywhere asserts the redaction** — every `wallet_private_key` hit in `crates/` is the `DevnetEnv` accessor and its consumers, never the redactor. The live artifact today is 52 269 B, retained to **2026-11-10**, and it is behind repo auth **only because the repository is private** — which Q65 is about to change. So the guard protecting a funded Anvil key in a published artifact is proven on a machine that never publishes it, by a lane the publishing job does not invoke.
- Do: **make the publishing path prove its own redaction.** Two independent fixes, and they are complementary rather than alternatives: (a) have `devnet-e2e-cron.yml` run `scripts/e2e-devnet.sh --self-test` before the capture step, so the job that uploads cannot upload without the filter having been proven in that environment; and (b) assert the **artifact itself** — after capture and before upload, scan the evidence directory for key-shaped material and fail the job if any is found, which is a check on the output rather than on the function. (b) is the one that survives a future refactor that stops calling `redact()` at all. Consider whether the evidence artifact needs a shorter retention than 90 days regardless.
- Accept:
  - The uploading job runs the self-test, and a planted fault in `redact()` turns that job red — proven by message, not by exit code.
  - A post-capture scan of the evidence directory fails on key-shaped material, and is itself proven by planting a fake key into a captured file.
  - The retention and visibility consequences are stated on the workflow: what this artifact contains, who can read it once the repo is public, and for how long.
  - No real key material is ever committed to prove any of this — the planted fakes stay in scratch space, per the canary discipline the history scrub used.
- Notes: **Not an instrument finding**, though it is one step from being mistaken for one: the subject is a **published artifact** containing a funded key's neighbourhood, and the fix changes what leaves the machine, not what a checker says. The redaction has never failed and there is no evidence it has ever leaked; this row exists because the *proof* is in the wrong place, and because Q65 is about to widen the audience from "the maintainer" to "anyone".

### Q244 — Nine third-party actions, fifty-seven invocations, zero pinned to a SHA
- Milestone: M4
- Size: S
- Deps: after Q1 (the CI skeleton); **before Q65** — *inverted 2026-08-18 by D141 §2 R5(c): the mitigations that make unpinned actions tolerable (private repo, no fork PRs, read-only token) are exactly what the flip removes, so the pins must precede it.* ~~Q31~~ — **the Q31 edge is RE-HOMED onto Q31's own Accept** rather than kept here, because what Q31 owes is the *arming* of `sha_pinning_required`, not this row's pinning work; keeping it here would have created the cycle `Q31→Q65→Q244→Q31`
- Spec: Risks — supply chain (MVP-SPEC.md line 189); Milestones M4 (line 157)
- Discovered by: **the pre-public scrub's GitHub-side lane** (2026-08-17); counts re-measured by the wave-22 registrar.
- Problem: **every step this project's CI runs is fetched from a mutable tag.** Across the eight workflow files there are **9 distinct actions and 57 `uses:` invocations, and not one is pinned to a commit SHA** — `actions/checkout@v4` (23), `Swatinem/rust-cache@v2` (**19**), `actions/upload-artifact@v4` (5), `actions/cache@v4` (5), and one each of `actions/setup-python@v5`, `actions/configure-pages@v5`, `actions/upload-pages-artifact@v3`, `actions/deploy-pages@v4`, `foundry-rs/foundry-toolchain@v1`. The repository setting agrees: `actions/permissions` reads `{"allowed_actions":"all","sha_pinning_required":false}`, the loosest position on that axis. A major tag is a moving pointer its owner can re-point at any commit, so the trust boundary of every CI lane — including the ones that produce evidence this project cites in gate rows — is "whatever that tag points at today". **Two invocations matter more than the other fifty-five**: `pages.yml:85`'s `actions/deploy-pages@v4` runs with GitHub Pages deploy credentials against the live `antseal.org` artifact, and `Swatinem/rust-cache@v2`, at 19 call sites, is the only non-`actions/`-owned action in the ordinary build path. **The mitigating facts, so the row is not over-priced**: `default_workflow_permissions` is `read`, `can_approve_pull_request_reviews` is `false`, `actions/permissions/access` is `none`, and the repository is private with no fork PRs possible — which is exactly the set of mitigations Q65's flip removes.
- Do: **pin by SHA with the version in a trailing comment**, the conventional form (`uses: owner/action@<40-hex> # v4`), and decide the renewal mechanism in the same act — an unmaintained pin is its own hazard, so either adopt a bot or record a review cadence with an owner. Set `sha_pinning_required: true` once the pins land, so the setting enforces what the files do. Order the work by blast radius: `deploy-pages` and the release workflow's actions first, `rust-cache` next, the rest in bulk.
- Accept:
  - Every `uses:` in `.github/workflows/` names a 40-character commit SHA with a version comment, verified by a check that fails on a tag reference — a grep-shaped lane, planted-fault proven.
  - `sha_pinning_required` is `true` at the repository, verified by API read-back rather than by having set it.
  - The renewal mechanism is recorded with an owner and a cadence; "we will update them when we notice" is not one.
  - No workflow's behaviour changes: the pinned SHA is the commit the tag pointed at when it was pinned, recorded so the equivalence is checkable.
- Notes: **Sharper once public** — a private repository with no fork PRs and read-only default token is a small target; the flip changes that, and this row is cheap enough that it should not wait for the incident that prices it. Registered as a product row rather than an instrument finding because the supply chain of the release artifacts is a property of what ships, not of what a checker says.
- Notes: **[Registrar, 2026-09-13, wave 35 — two things this entry did not carry.]** **(1) `Accept` row 2's amendment lived only on this row's `TODO.md` line** and is carried here verbatim in substance: *`Accept` row 2 is AMENDED, not met — `sha_pinning_required` stays `false` by D143 §2 R5, because arming it today refuses `pages.yml` outright; the blocker is `Q255`.* **(2) D169 §2 R4 — the pin rule's scope, and an exposure accepted rather than denied.** This row's rule is *every `uses:` in `.github/workflows/`*; nothing in D141, D143 or this row extends it to every action the repository runs. CodeQL default setup and the dependency-graph job are GitHub-managed dynamic workflows that resolve `actions/checkout@v6`, `github/codeql-action/*@v4` and `github/dependabot-action@main` **by tag at run time**, and `scripts/check-action-pins.py` cannot see them. That is D143's adversary **A2**, and it is **accepted**, not absent: default setup's token holds `SecurityEvents: write` and cache write on `main`. Reopen triggers are D169 §2 R4's four, the first carried as a term on `Q255`'s `Accept`.
- Notes: **[2026-09-13, wave 35 — lane W1.]** D169 §2 R4's scope sentence landed in `.github/action-pins.tsv`'s header and in `scripts/check-action-pins.py`'s docstring: the pin rule and its checker cover the `uses:` lines of workflow files in `.github/workflows/` and nothing else, and GitHub-managed dynamic workflows' A2 exposure is recorded as accepted, not absent. The checker's self-test had been tied to live invocation counts and live dates — a new workflow moved the counts on the day it landed and every 90-day renewal moves the dates; both are repaired and proven (see `Q238`'s note). Counts now: checkout **24**, rust-cache **20**; ledger rows moved down four lines (the Pages rows are `:15-17`, the `# RESIDUAL` header `:19`).

### Q245 — `secret-guard` cannot see the one form of the signing key that is catastrophic to commit, and Q30 is about to create it
- Milestone: M4
- Size: S
- Deps: after Q2 (the `secret-guard` lane), **before Q30** (which generates the minisign key), D71 (the signing ruling that names this gap at `:866-870`)
- Spec: Risks — key handling (MVP-SPEC.md line 189); project rule 6; Milestones M4 signing (line 157)
- Discovered by: **the pre-public scrub's history lane and D71's lane independently** (2026-08-16/17), each reproducing it against the guard's own patterns; **promoted from `docs/instrument-ledger.md` to a row by the wave-22 registrar** under protocol rule 8, because it blocks an acceptance on the ship path.
- **[CORRECTION 2026-08-17, wave 23 — the `Problem` below states the wrong mechanism, and it is left standing rather than rewritten because three other records repeat it.** Read off upstream source, **minisign writes `minisign encrypted secret key` unconditionally** — `generate()` emits the default comment on both paths, with no reference to whether the key is passphrase-wrapped — so a `-W` key **was** already caught by the shipped literal, and the paragraph's central claim is false. **The real gap is bigger and differently shaped.** `rsign2`, the pure-Rust signer D71 `:207`/`:664` sanctions, writes **`rsign encrypted secret key`**, so the shipped rule missed **every rsign2 key, including the passphrase-protected one D71 §2 R7.1 mandates** — and a key made with `-c` carries an arbitrary comment and matches no literal at all. D71 §1.3 row 4 had already **measured** the comment as unauthenticated and fully rewritable, which is why no header rule can be the answer and why the landed fix keys on the **key body** instead. The same false premise is recorded at `docs/reviews/pre-public-scrub-history.md:399-406`, `D71:866-870` and `D71 §2 R7.3 :557-562`; D71 owes a dated addendum. The *self-test* half of the paragraph below — that `planted=5` balanced while rule 4b had no fake — was **confirmed** and is what the fix's fixture-per-arm shape answers.]**
- Problem: **the guard's only minisign rule is keyed on the word `encrypted`, and the dangerous file is the one that lacks it.** `secret-guard` is `lane_secret_guard()` in `scripts/ci-lanes.sh:1055-1144` (dispatched `:1451`, run in CI by `.github/workflows/ci-always.yml:78-84`), and its minisign pattern at `:1093` is the literal `minisign encrypted secret key`. A key generated with `minisign -W` has **no passphrase**: the KDF field is zeroed, **no XOR mask is applied, and the raw Ed25519 secret key sits in cleartext base64** — and minisign's comment line for that file omits the word `encrypted`. Every one of the guard's six patterns is keyed on a literal a `-W` file does not contain, so **nothing catches it**. Reproduced in-repo at `docs/reviews/pre-public-scrub-history.md:399-406`: the shipped pattern reports `*** NO MATCH (blind) ***` where `minisign( encrypted)? secret key` matches. **D71 states the consequence in its own words** (`:866-870`): *"the one form of the key that is catastrophic to commit is the one form the guard cannot see."* **The self-test cannot find this**, which is why it survived: `ci-lanes.sh:1109-1128` plants five fakes and balances at `planted=5`, but **pattern 4b has no planted fake at all** — the age fake at `:1117` fills the "(4)" slot, so the count balances while one rule is never exercised. That is an assertion that cannot fail, sitting inside the guard that exists to make failure possible.
- Do: **widen the pattern and prove it, before Q30 runs.** The minimal fix is `minisign( encrypted)? secret key`, and it must come with a planted fake for **that rule specifically**, so `planted` counts rules rather than filling slots. **Do not take the naive companion fix in the same breath**: the guard also runs with `--exclude='*.md'` (`ci-lanes.sh:1072-1073`), and simply dropping that exclusion **reds the lane on twelve files** — measured 2026-08-17: ten on the vault-export magic, four on PEM key headers, four on the age literal, four on minisign, two on the EVM-keystore conjunction, union twelve — including all three pre-public scrub reports, `D71`, `D47`, `CONTRIBUTING.md`, `TODO.md`, `tasks/U.md`, `testdata/README.md` and `docs/ci-verification.md`. The workable shape is the one the guard **already uses**: an exact-path exclusion, as at `:1089-1090` for `crates/antseal-cli/src/vault/export.rs`, chosen by exact path and not by basename so a stray file elsewhere still trips the scan; or patterns keyed on a key's **body** rather than its header comment.
- Accept:
  - A `-W`-generated minisign secret key is detected by the guard, proven with a planted fake in the self-test — and the self-test's `planted` count is derived so that **every rule** has a fake, not so that the total happens to balance.
  - Landed **before** Q30 generates the real key, and Q30's entry cites this row as a precondition.
  - Any change to the `*.md` exclusion is exact-path shaped and the lane is green after it, or the exclusion is left alone with the reason recorded — the naive removal is not attempted and abandoned silently.
  - No real key material enters the repository to prove any of it; fakes stay in the temp directory the self-test already uses.
- Notes: **This is a promotion, and the promotion test is what makes it a row.** Its subject is a checker, so protocol rule 8 sends it to `docs/instrument-ledger.md` — and it is there, with its two siblings. It is promoted because **Q30 is on the M4 ship path and is about to generate exactly the artifact the guard cannot see**, which is the rule's *"blocks an acceptance on the ship path"* condition met literally rather than by analogy. **The two sibling blind spots stay in the ledger and are deliberately not in this row**: the PEM rule's failure on `-----BEGIN PGP PRIVATE KEY BLOCK-----` (the trailing `[-]{5}` is anchored immediately after `PRIVATE KEY`, and OpenPGP interposes ` BLOCK`), and the `*.md` exclusion's coverage hole. Neither blocks a ship-path acceptance; both are one line from being fixed by whoever takes this row, and taking them is welcome — but they are not what licences the ID.

### Q246 — The scheduled devnet lane ran below `CLOSE_GROUP_SIZE` and could never pass; the fix is landed and unwitnessed

- Milestone: M4
- Size: S
- Deps: Q15, D52 (2026-08-17 addendum); P16 (node-count evidence)
- Spec: Milestones M4 (line 157); the devnet E2E venue (D52)
- Do: **The defect is already corrected in the tree; what this row owns is the
  witness.** `.github/workflows/devnet-e2e-cron.yml` set
  `ANTSEAL_DEVNET_NODES: 5`, and `ant-protocol-2.3.0/src/chunk.rs:35` sets
  `CLOSE_GROUP_SIZE = 7`, so no quote could be obtained and every paying test
  failed. Run `31571938292` (`event: schedule`, `conclusion: failure`,
  2026-08-12 on `3c8095a`) failed **all four** suites, each with *"witnessed
  close group initial lookup found 5 peers, need 7"*; `evidence.txt` reads
  `e2e-devnet: FAIL … nodes=5 suites_run=4 failed=[S6-S8,S17,S18,S19]
  secs=1374`. Corrected 2026-08-17 to **14**, the count `scripts/e2e-devnet.sh`
  and `scripts/devnet/local-up` already default to. **This lane has never once
  been green on the remote, so the correction is a prediction until a run
  proves it** — the Q43/Q153 rule, on the row that most needs it. Take one
  `workflow_dispatch` run (or read the next weekly), record the verdict line
  verbatim, and read TWO numbers besides pass/fail: the **warm runtime** against
  the ≤ 15 min promote-to-required threshold (this run was **22.9 min**, already
  over it at 5 nodes), and whether 14 nodes fits the hosted runner's memory —
  P16 measured 10–14 nodes ≈ 0.5–1.7 GiB on a 2-core dev host and that figure
  has never been taken on `ubuntu-latest`.
- Accept:
  - One scheduled or dispatched run recorded with its `e2e-devnet:` verdict line quoted verbatim, naming the run id and the commit
  - `nodes=14` appears in that line — the override is proven to take effect, not merely committed
  - Warm runtime recorded and compared against the ≤ 15 min trigger, with the gap stated either way
  - If red, the failure is triaged per D52's rule and the tracking note lands in the same act
- Notes: The tree already carried the knowledge in two places and neither was
  consulted when D52 chose 5 — `docs/research/P16-devnet-feasibility.md:138-146`
  (*"10–14 nodes is the evidence-backed working size … 5 nodes … usable for
  smoke, not for the flagship suite"*) and `docs/devnet/local-devnet.md:51`
  (*"below CLOSE_GROUP_SIZE=7 — degraded quorums"*). The reduction saved
  **seconds** — boot 1.4 s at 5 nodes vs 6.2 s at 14 — against a 13 m 07 s cold
  build. Found 2026-08-17 by reading the evidence artifact after a maintainer
  question about artifact retention, not by any lane or checker.
- Notes: **[Registrar, 2026-09-13 — TICKED on D167 §1.1 / §2 R7.]** **Row 1**: `33616516399` (schedule, 2026-09-02, head `ae4e167`) → `e2e-devnet: PASS commit=ae4e167 nodes=14 suites_run=4 failed=[] pending=[] secs=1745 anvil=1.8.1 evidence=/home/runner/work/antseal/antseal/target/e2e-devnet/20260902T095317Z-ae4e167`; `34338263485` (schedule, 2026-09-09, head `3437890`) → `e2e-devnet: PASS commit=3437890 nodes=14 suites_run=4 failed=[] pending=[] secs=1721 anvil=1.8.1 evidence=/home/runner/work/antseal/antseal/target/e2e-devnet/20260909T100507Z-3437890`. Both lines were measured twice and independently — from the evidence artifacts by planning lane B and from the job logs by `scripts/check-devnet-ledger.py --append` — and are byte-identical to the artifacts' `evidence.txt` (183 B, sha256 `a44aaaba…7407` / `18ed8527…ba05`); they sit at `docs/testing/devnet-e2e-ledger.tsv`. Copied here verbatim because run records past retention are deletable from 2026-10-01. **Row 2**: `nodes=14` with the override proven, not inferred from the line (the script's default is also 14) — env echo `ANTSEAL_DEVNET_NODES: 14` and the launcher's 14-node boot line in both job logs; `git merge-base --is-ancestor 75a5f1a <head>` → 0 for both heads, 1 against the fix's own parent. **Row 3, each figure labelled**: COLD `secs=` 1745 / 1721 s, over the 900 s trigger by 845 / 821 s; NON-COMPILE runtime (secs minus cargo's `Finished … in`, 5 of 5 markers each) 413.59 / 440.28 s, under by 486 / 460 s — never a measured *warm* runtime, which this lane cannot have (D52 E3). **Row 4 not engaged**: `32221569300` and `32936547060` were refusals (one job, empty `runner_name`), recorded `refused` in the ledger. **Does NOT prove**: a cache-warm runtime; memory headroom (no RSS figure anywhere, only zero OOM signals); anything about the public 4-CPU runner class (both runs predate the 2026-09-12 flip; the first post-flip schedule is due 2026-09-16); any flake rate (the 95 % upper bound at n = 2 is 77.6 %).

### Q247 — The promote-to-required trigger needs twenty runs of evidence from a store that holds twelve

- Milestone: M4
- Size: S
- Deps: Q15, D52; Q246 (the lane must be able to pass before its evidence means anything)
- Spec: Milestones M4 (line 157)
- Do: `docs/ci-verification.md:1283` makes the `devnet-e2e-scheduled`
  promote-to-required trigger **"≥ 20 clean scheduled runs with warm runtime
  ≤ 15 min"**, and names the `devnet-e2e-evidence` artifact as where that
  evidence lives. **The arithmetic does not close.** The lane is weekly
  (`cron: "37 5 * * 3"`), so 20 runs span ≥ 133 days, while the artifact
  retention measured on this repository is **89 days** (`created_at`
  2026-08-12T07:19:54Z → `expires_at` 2026-11-10T06:56:33Z) — **at most 12 of
  the required 20 can coexist**, so the trigger is unreachable from its own
  named evidence store no matter how many runs go green. Rule the fix: either
  raise `retention-days` on the upload step to cover the window, or — better,
  and consistent with this project's own *evidence belongs in the row* rule —
  stop treating a 90-day expiring store as the home for evidence a permanent
  document cites, and append each run's one-line `e2e-devnet:` verdict to a
  committed file. The artifact then carries only the bulky diagnostic logs,
  which nothing cites and which may expire freely.
- Accept:
  - The trigger and its evidence store are arithmetically consistent — stated as a calculation, not an assertion
  - `docs/ci-verification.md`'s trigger text names the store that actually holds the evidence
  - ~~If a committed verdict file is chosen, it exists and the workflow appends to it, with the append proven by a run~~ **[RESTATED 2026-09-13 by D167 §2 R6 — a workflow that commits would be the repository's first `contents: write` grant, and its commits would trigger no CI.]**
  - **[D167 §2 R6]** The ledger exists; a committed script appends to it from the API at wave close with no workflow write grant; and `--against-api` was observed red naming real unrecorded runs and green after their append.
  - **[D145 §2 R4, resolved 2026-08-18, landed 2026-09-13]** The *"warm runtime"* clause names a runtime this lane can actually have, or the no-cache ruling (D52 E3) is revisited in the same act — an unreachable threshold is not made reachable by counting runs.
- Notes: Generalises past this lane. Any evidence a committed document cites
  must outlive the document's reader, and GitHub Actions artifacts cannot do
  that. Found 2026-08-17 alongside Q246.
- **[AMENDED 2026-08-18 by D145 §2 R4 — not ticked, not left. Three corrections, and a second unreachability this row never named.]**
  1. **Retention is 90 days, not 89.** This row measured from artifact *creation*; the clock runs from **run start** — `2026-08-12T06:56:32Z + 90 d = 2026-11-10T06:56:33Z`, matching `expires_at` to the second. 90 is GitHub's default.
  2. **13 can coexist, not 12** — a fencepost on intervals, present in this row *and* in the brief that re-measured it. The conclusion is unchanged: 20 weekly runs span ≥ 133 days.
  3. ***"Raise `retention-days`"* must be STRUCK rather than weighed.** 90 days is the **maximum a public repository may configure** (private/internal may reach 400), so the flip caps this exactly where it already sits and the option does not exist on the far side of the very decision this row waits behind.
  4. **The second unreachability, which this row never named**: the trigger reads *"≥ 20 clean scheduled runs with **warm** runtime ≤ 15 min"*, and this is the one lane that deliberately carries **no** `rust-cache` (D52 E3). **No run of it is ever warm**, so the trigger is unreachable on its runtime clause as well as on its count — two independent reasons, either sufficient.
  5. **A third arm this row never lists**: **daily cadence**, which the flip makes affordable because public repositories get free standard runners. That is the arm most likely to make the trigger reachable at all.
  Retention itself is now ruled at **30 days** by D145 §2 R1 — chosen by this workflow's own two-consecutive-reds triage rule (`devnet-e2e-cron.yml:27-29`), not by risk appetite: at weekly cadence R=7 keeps exactly **one** artifact alive, so the first red's diagnosis expires the day the second fires.
- Notes: **[Registrar, 2026-08-19 — dated addendum on a closed row, per the D140/Q24 precedent D150 §1.9 flags.]** This row's `Accept` clause *"both registered `OWED_PRESENCE` debts still print"* was **true at closure** and is **false as a live property from 2026-08-19**: D150 §2 R4 wrote both clauses into `README.md` and deleted both entries in the same act, leaving the register empty. The row is not re-opened and the clause is not rewritten — it recorded a fact about the tree at its own closure, and this line records when that fact stopped holding.
- Notes: **[D167, 2026-09-13, wave 35 — three corrections to this entry, and one misfiled addendum.]** **(1)** Retention is now **30 days from the upload** (D145 §2 R1 landed; artifact `10099660953` expires exactly 30 days after creation), and the repository's ceiling reads `{"days":90,"maximum_allowed_days":90}` — so at weekly cadence **5** artifacts coexist today and at most **13** ever could, against the 20 the trigger needs; this entry's `Do` still carries the superseded 89 days / 12 runs. **(2)** D52 never defines *warm*, and compile alone is **142–148 %** of the 900 s budget in both hosted greens, so D167 §2 R3 restates the clause as **non-compile runtime ≤ 15 min**, derived fail-closed at append time. **(3)** The count is **20 consecutive clean scheduled runs** (D167 §2 R4); refusals, classified by an empty `runner_name`, neither extend nor break a streak. **The dated addendum immediately above this note** (*"[Registrar, 2026-08-19 — dated addendum on a closed row …]"*) cites `Q248`'s `Accept` and calls the row closed — it belongs to `Q248` and was filed here; it is left in place and recorded, not moved, because other records cite this entry's text by position.
- Notes: **[2026-09-13, wave 35 — D167 §2 R1–R6 BUILT by lane W2; the row stays OPEN until `Accept` rows 1–2's `docs/ci-verification.md` text lands (the CI lane) and a commit births the ledger.]** The ledger is `docs/testing/devnet-e2e-ledger.tsv` (14 tab-separated columns, one line per completed run attempt, keyed on workflow file per D167 §4). The writer is `scripts/check-devnet-ledger.py --append`, read-only GETs only; **no workflow gained a write grant**. **Witness for the amended `Accept` row 3, on the real API, 2026-09-13**: header-only ledger → `--against-api` exit 1, `LAG` naming `31571938292`, `32221569300`, `32936547060`, `33616516399`, `34338263485`, with `STALE-SCHEDULE` silent; the first `--append` **refused** on a transient log-fetch failure and wrote nothing; the second exited 0 with 5 lines; `--against-api` then exited 0. The non-compile derivation reproduces D167 §1.3 (413.59 / 440.28 against 413.6 / 440.3 printed). D145 §2 R4's clause is satisfied by D167 §2 R3. Streak **2/20**. Gate lanes: `devnet-ledger-selftest` (92 arms: 69 planted faults, 16 green controls; the self-test itself proven by 32 mutations of its own branches) and `devnet-ledger`, which reds `UNBORN` until the commit that adds the ledger — so the ledger, the script and the gate wiring land in **one** commit. **Wave-close ritual (D167 §2 R2)**: `scripts/check-devnet-ledger.py --append`, then `--against-api` (must exit 0), commit the ledger, then run the gate. **Also recovered**: the 2026-08-12 red's log was still downloadable, so its full verdict line is recorded rather than `log-expired`.

### Q248 — The README front door contradicted the tree in three places, and Q29's landing falsified two more

- Milestone: M4
- Size: S
- Deps: after Q29 (the licence act whose landing falsified the README's licence section), D140 §2 R1/R7 (the ruling that puts the README edit here rather than on Q22); **precondition of Q65** (the publish-scope flip) and therefore of Q31/Q34
- Spec: Milestones M4 (line 157); positioning (line 28) — the README is `PRESENCE_SURFACES`' sole member in `scripts/check-copy-style.py`
- Discovered by: **D140's lane** (2026-08-17), §5 rows 1 and 4; the wrong-org rustdoc URL folded in under the Q65 precedent of folding at-the-flip items rather than minting a row apiece.
- Problem: **the first file a stranger reads was materially false, and one of its falsehoods was created by a commit made the same day.** B2 of the pre-public scrub named a false README front door as a blocker. Five sites: (1) the **licence section** told the reader *"no LICENSE file exists yet and no manifest carries a `license` field, so the source is under default copyright — all rights reserved"* — true when written, false from `1830618` onward, and D140 measured the window at **103 minutes**; (2) the **task count** *"648 tasks"* against a tree of 661; (3) `verifier-web/` described as **"(M3)"** while the page is deployed and live at `https://antseal.org/`; (4) three `cargo deny` **labels** in `CONTRIBUTING.md`, `.github/workflows/ci.yml` and `.github/workflows/advisory-cron.yml` still saying *"licenses stubbed until Q29"* after Q29 landed the licences lane; (5) `crates/antseal-net/src/wallet.rs:52`'s rustdoc link pointing at `https://github.com/antseal/antseal/…` — **an organisation nobody in this project owns**, in a publishable crate, so a docs.rs reader would be sent to a stranger's repository.
- Do: **fix the front door, and delete the count rather than update it.** Rewrite the licence section to state the dual `MIT OR Apache-2.0` grant *and* the distribution reality `COPYRIGHT` records (an `ant-backend` build — the only build that can seal — links copyleft crates), pointing at `COPYRIGHT` and D6. Delete the task count outright: it was wrong twice under two different lanes, it has no mechanical reader, and the sentence already names `python3 scripts/check-traceability.py` as the place it is right. Update the `verifier-web/` row to the live page. Correct the three `cargo deny` labels to name all four checks. Replace the `wallet.rs` absolute URL with the repo-relative idiom the other crate-source decision links use. **Add a `## Documentation` section** linking the six `docs/user/` pages and the threat model — that section is the mechanism by which Q24's *"linked from README"* clause, and the README halves of Q23/Q25/Q26/Q27, can be satisfied at all. **`README.md:7-9` must not be reworded**: it carries three satisfied positioning clauses against only two registered `OWED_PRESENCE` debts, so an edit there can silently lose a green clause.
- Accept:
  - Every false claim above is corrected at its own site, and the licence section names `COPYRIGHT` as the authority for the distribution obligations rather than restating them.
  - The task count is **gone**, not updated.
  - `README.md:7-9` is proven untouched by a byte comparison against the previous revision, not by inspection; both registered `OWED_PRESENCE` debts still print and the three satisfied clauses still satisfy.
  - `check-copy-style.py` and `check-traceability.py` are exit 0 with the real code read back from a file, and the workflow edits are proven by a **YAML parse** that reads the new step names out of the parsed structure — a line scanner cannot tell a parseable workflow from an unparseable one (D124).
  - The relative rustdoc link is resolved against the filesystem from the source file's own directory, not assumed.
- Notes: **Registered and closed in the same wave** (the U68 precedent). D140 §2 R7 overturned *"leave README to Q22"* as an **ordering contradiction**: `TODO.md` puts Q22 after Q30/Q31, and Q65 is a precondition of Q31 — so the row that owns the README is downstream of the publication a true README exists to protect. **This row discharges a precondition of Q65 and nothing more**: the two `OWED_PRESENCE` debts (`limit-exclusive-possession`, `compelled-disclosure`) stay open and stay Q22's.

### Q249 — Shipped rustdoc that goes nowhere, and nothing in the gate builds docs

- Milestone: M4
- Size: S
- Deps: after Q248 (which fixed the sibling link and established the relative-link idiom), Q31 (the release workflow that would publish the affected crates); relevant to Q241 (the publish blocker)
- Spec: Milestones M4 (line 157); the publishable-crate surface (P2's reserved names)
- Discovered by: **Q248's lane** (2026-08-17), while auditing the five relative decision links in crate sources to choose an idiom; re-measured centrally at registration.
- Problem: **a dead documentation link on a publishable crate, invisible because no lane in this repository builds documentation.** `crates/antseal-core/src/anchor/request.rs:2` reads ``//! [D59](../../../docs/decisions/D59-request-nonce-persistence.md)).`` The file sits at `crates/antseal-core/src/anchor/`, so `../../../` resolves to `crates/` and the target is `crates/docs/decisions/…`, **a directory that does not exist**; the correct prefix is `../../../../`. It is the only dead one of the relative decision links in crate sources, and it is **textually identical** to `crates/antseal-anchor/src/http.rs:2`, which is one directory shallower and therefore correct — the copy is wrong precisely because it is a faithful copy. `antseal-core` carries no `publish = false` (nor does the workspace table), so this is publishable surface. Second half: `antseal-net` declares `pub mod evm` behind the **non-default** `ant-backend` feature (`lib.rs:95-96`), while `wallet.rs` and `network.rs` are ungated — so a default-feature `cargo doc`, which is what docs.rs runs absent a metadata override, resolves intra-doc links at `wallet.rs:8,42,244,376` and `network.rs:14,24,261` against a module that does not exist. **Nine `crate::evm` intra-doc links, seven of them in ungated files.** All of it invisible: `cargo doc`/`cargo rustdoc` appears in **zero** workflow files and **zero** scripts.
- Do: Fix the `request.rs` prefix; resolve every relative link in crate sources against the filesystem in the same act rather than by eye.
