Decide the `crate::evm` links deliberately — either gate the referring doc lines behind the same feature, or add the `[package.metadata.docs.rs]` feature set that makes docs.rs build what the links assume. **Land a `cargo doc` check with the fix**, or the warnings return: the Q27 precedent is that a copy added without its lint is the drift the work exists to prevent. Decide its venue against D135's measured minute budget before adding a required context.
- Accept:
  - Every relative link in `crates/**/src/**` resolves on disk, asserted by a check that reads the filesystem, with a planted broken link watched red **by message naming the file**. **[MET 2026-08-22 — `doc-links`, the ninth check in `scripts/check-traceability.py` (D159 §2 R1).]** The red was witnessed on the **unfixed** tree, and the message names the file, the target as written and where it lands: *"`crates/antseal-core/src/anchor/request.rs:2`: the link target `../../../docs/decisions/D59-request-nonce-persistence.md` does not resolve — relative to this file's own directory it lands at `crates/docs/decisions/D59-…`, and no such file exists"*. R5's one-character fix then landed and was verified D159's way — `realpath -m` from the file's directory plus `test -e`, **never by counting `../` segments**, which is how the defect was written wrong. Green line: *"53 markdown link(s) with a relative `.md` target across 461 file(s) in 15 scan root(s), every one resolving on disk … 4 of them under `crates/*/src/`, against floors total >= 45 and crates-src >= 4"*, `REAL_EXIT=0`. Six R3 arms plus **8 logic plants, 2 live-corpus plants and 4 guard plants, zero uncaught**. **One gap, stated**: `--self-test` was not run in-wave (it stages a full tree copy and is banned mid-wave); it was run at the wave close on the quiet tree.
  - A documentation build runs somewhere a human or a lane will see it, and its warning count is asserted against a recorded number rather than eyeballed. **[REFUSED IN BOTH VENUES 2026-08-22 by D159 §2 R6, and this clause is ARMED rather than discharged or deferred — a THIRD state.]** Refused **hosted** because it cannot run and would not be evidence if it did (Q43's rule); refused in the **local gate** because after R2 nothing in the `crate::evm` class is left for it to catch, the six remaining broken intra-doc links cannot be enumerated this wave, and catching them needs `--all-features`, which breaches the containment `GATE_LIGHT_FEATURES` exists to hold. **The event that makes a documentation build load-bearing is PUBLICATION** — docs.rs runs a default-feature `cargo doc` on upload, and until an `antseal-*` crate is uploaded the output has no reader. **Live owners, both open rows**: `Q241` (the publish blocker) with `Q31` (the release workflow); `[package.metadata.docs.rs]` is decided in that act, not this one. This row ticks with the clause armed and owned, never silently.
  - The `crate::evm` decision is recorded — gated links or a docs.rs feature set — and the chosen route is stated at the site.
  - The check's venue and its weighted-minute cost are stated, because D135 §3 R6 makes raising a required context a decision act.
- Notes: **Corrects its own brief.** The wave-23 notes assign this subject two different ids in two places (`Q250` under Q248's findings, `Q249` in the final assignment table); the final assignment governs and this is **Q249**. *"Thirteen rustdoc warnings / twelve broken intra-doc links, six of them `crate::evm`"* is the lane's `cargo doc` measurement and was **not re-run at registration** (a sibling lane held the build lock); what is re-measured here is the link inventory — nine `crate::evm` links, seven reachable in a default build — and the zero-lane result, both of which stand on their own. The *"structural twin"* framing in the lane's report is also corrected: `http.rs:2` is a **textual** twin at a different directory depth, which is the mechanism of the defect rather than a precedent for the fix.

### Q250 — The threat model frames wallet exposure as a bundle-recipient exposure; the paying address reaches the storing nodes at upload time

- Milestone: M4
- Size: S
- Deps: after Q21 (which wrote §2.3), Q25 (whose page states the exception first); consumer of D37's payment shape
- Spec: Risks — wallet linkability (line 185); reveal `--include-receipt` (line 36); Milestones M4 (line 157)
- Discovered by: **Q25's lane** (2026-08-17), while checking its page claim-by-claim against §2.3; re-measured centrally at registration.
- Problem: **the section is scoped to what a bundle recipient learns, and the largest disclosure happens before any bundle exists.** `docs/threat-model.md` §2.3 (`:692-876`) opens *"Payment happens from an Arbitrum wallet, and the receipt names it. Including a receipt in a bundle (`reveal --include-receipt`, opt-in) exposes the paying wallet…"*, and its four subsections are chain-only observer, what the opt-in adds, funding hygiene, and whether the linkage is worth it. But `ant-protocol-2.3.0/src/payment/proof.rs:19-22` puts the on-chain **`tx_hashes`** inside the `PaymentProof` that travels **with the upload**, so every node holding your chunks is handed the payment transaction — and therefore the paying address — whether or not a receipt is ever included in any bundle. §2.3 never says this: the only storage-node mention in the section (`:786`) runs the opposite direction, describing what the *receipt* discloses about the network. `--include-receipt` is genuinely default-off (`cli.rs:387-390`, a bare `#[arg(long)] pub include_receipt: bool` with no `default_value_t`), which is exactly why the omission matters: a reader takes the opt-in to be the moment of exposure, and it is not.
- Do: Add the upload-time disclosure to §2.3 as a bounded exception, in the shape `docs/user/wallet-hygiene.md` §4 (`:199-208`) already uses — the address is semi-public from the **first seal**, not from the first receipt, and nothing configurable changes it. Cite `ant-protocol` by version and line as the user page does. Keep the section's conclusion intact: this **widens** the observability §2.3 already establishes rather than reversing it.
- Accept:
  - §2.3 states the upload-time disclosure with its upstream citation, and says plainly that leaving the receipt out hides the wallet from bundle recipients and not from the network.
  - The two documents agree: no claim in §2.3 contradicts `docs/user/wallet-hygiene.md` §4, checked claim by claim rather than asserted.
  - The §2.3 rewrite does not move the frozen assumptions block, and `check-copy-style.py` stays exit 0 on `docs/threat-model.md` (its one surviving P3 is the frozen one D139 §2 R3 rules untouchable).
- Notes: **Not a defect in the product** — the upstream shape is what pays for storage, and antseal cannot avoid it. It is a defect in where the fact is written down: **the user page has it and the threat model does not**, which is the inverse of the usual direction and is why it was found by comparing the two. The wave-23 notes assign this subject the id `Q250` under Q25's findings and the same id to Q249's subject under Q248's findings; the final assignment table governs.
- Notes: **[RE-HOMED 2026-08-22 by D159 — this block was misfiled under `Q249`, whose subject is rustdoc links. It discusses `wallet-hygiene.md`, `ant-protocol` and `build_paid_chunks`, and quotes THIS row's own `Do` verbatim; `Q250` is `[x]` while `Q249` is untouched, so a reader of Q249's detail entry saw a wave-26 execution note against a row nobody had executed. **Moved, not copied** — D159 measured it a MOVE rather than a delete (`grep -c 'wave 26 execution'` over Q250's span returned 0).]** **[Registrar, 2026-08-19 — wave 26 execution. One deviation from this row's `Do`, recorded rather than silently taken, and two upstream facts the row did not carry.]** **Deviation**: the `Do` says *"cite `ant-protocol` by version and line as the user page does"*; the lane brief said **version and symbol**, because line-number citations into upstream rot silently, and the lane followed the brief. The `Accept` row asks only for *"its upstream citation"*, which is satisfied — but `docs/threat-model.md` and `docs/user/wallet-hygiene.md` §4 now cite the same fact in two styles. **Fact 1**: antseal **cannot** omit the hashes — `build_paid_chunks` (`ant-core-0.5.0/src/data/client/batch.rs:300-305`) **hard-errors** on a non-zero quote whose tx hash is missing, so *"nothing you can configure changes that"* is true **by upstream refusal**, not by convention. **Fact 2**: the pinned `ant-node-0.15.0` **receives the hashes and never uses them** (`verifier.rs:836-853` logs the count at `debug` and verifies from the quotes and on-chain settlement) — they are **carried, not consumed**, which bounds what the *protocol needs* and bounds nothing about what a node *receives*. That asymmetry — a builder that refuses to omit a field a node never reads — is a live upstream-churn tripwire worth an S20-review note. **One breadth divergence left standing**: `wallet-hygiene.md` §4 says *"the nodes that store your chunks"*, while the proof rides **in** the PUT request, so every **offered** peer is handed it. Not a contradiction (stored ⊂ offered), but the user page is the one a stranger reads, and `docs/user/` was in no lane's write scope this wave.

### Q251 — `MVP-SPEC.md` line 143 diverges from shipped behaviour in more places than any one record collects, and the M4 gate has no place to read them

- Milestone: M4
- Size: S
- Deps: after Q21 (whose §2.1 re-measured two of the divergences), U6/U8/D40/D50/D72/C22 (the records that each hold one); **disposed at Q34**, the M4 gate row
- Spec: Vault (MVP-SPEC.md line 143) — the whole line; Milestones M4 gate (line 157), whose `Gate:` clause requires *"traceability matrix 100 % green"*
- Discovered by: **Q21's lane** (2026-08-17), §2.1 corrections 5 and 6, against the M0 stub's own scope lines; the collection defect found centrally at registration.
- Problem: **the spec is frozen and the protocol says the spec wins and the conflict is flagged — and the flags exist, one per local record, in five different files, so nothing collects them for the gate that has to certify the line.** Line 143 is one long paragraph and shipped behaviour parts from it in at least four places. (i) It says the AAD binding makes a parameter downgrade *"a detected authentication failure, not a silent weakening"*; shipped is a **pre-auth range rejection** — `KdfParams::check_ranges` (`vault/kdf.rs:280`, the Argon2id arm at `:288`) called from `decode` at `:438`, reached from `session.rs:424` one line ahead of key derivation at `:425-427` — with its own class `vault-kdf-params-out-of-range` and **exit 14** (`crates/antseal-cli/src/error.rs:375`). Stricter than the spec, and not the stated mechanism. (ii) It offers *"a high-entropy keyfile / OS-keystore wrap for `W`"*; **only the keyfile half ships** — wrap mode 2 is registered-but-unimplemented and refused with a distinct error at `session.rs:398-403`, class `vault-wrap-mode-unsupported`, **exit 19** (D50 ruled M1 keyfile-only; D72 answered the follow-up **no**). (iii) D40 recorded that line 143 *"names no scrypt r/p"* — an incompleteness, flagged at resolution and nowhere since. (iv) C21 found the HKDF/HMAC key state un-zeroizable on the pins, *"a live contradiction of MVP-SPEC.md line 143"*, dispositioned by C22/D88. **Each is flagged where it was found** — (i) at `tasks/U.md` in U6's 2026-08-01 execution note, (ii) at U8's re-scoping and D50/D72, (iii) at D40's register row, (iv) at C21/C22's rows — and **no document holds them together against the line**, so the gate's *"100 % green"* clause has nothing to read.
- Do: **Collect, do not re-litigate.** Produce one line-143 disposition — the obvious venue is a row in `docs/testing/verification-matrix.md` beside the existing spec-bullet rows, since that file is what `check-traceability.py`'s `[matrix]` check reads — naming each divergence, its shipped behaviour, its owning record, and its disposition (stronger-than-spec, deferred, incomplete, or accepted). Sweep line 143 for a fifth: the paragraph also mandates `zeroize` coverage, an own-sub-key wallet, and a nag on both failure modes, and each should be marked satisfied or not by measurement rather than by assumption. **No code change is licensed by this row.** Every divergence above is either stronger than the spec or a recorded deferral; the deliverable is the flag and its disposition.
- Accept:
  - ~~One document lists every measured divergence from line 143 with a `path:line` citation for the shipped behaviour~~ **[AMENDED 2026-08-19, wave 26 — the `path:line` clause is REFUSED FOR THIS VENUE on measurement, not on preference.]** One document lists every measured divergence from line 143, naming the shipped behaviour **by symbol** and citing the record that already flags it, and states the disposition of each. **Why the original could not be executed here**: `check-traceability.py --matrix` resolves only the `tests / evidence` cell and **never reads `notes`**, and a `path:line` in `tests / evidence` reds by construction — `PLAIN_PATH` is `^[\w./\-]+$`, so `:` is refused, proven by a planted specimen returning *"is not a recognised reference shape"*. A line number in `notes` would therefore be a pointer **nothing verifies**: strictly worse than either alternative rather than a compromise between them. **And this row supplied the argument itself**: of eighteen locators re-measured for the collection, **three were wrong the day they were written** — so a durable document adopting them would have published three wrong pointers as its first act. The dated `path:line` form stays in `TODO.md`'s register, where it is a timestamped claim about one tree state.
  - The list is **derived by re-reading line 143 clause by clause**, not by transcribing this row — a collection that only collects what was already known repeats the defect one level up.
  - Q34 cites it, so the gate's *"traceability matrix 100 % green"* clause has a named place where line 143's status is decided.
  - Nothing in `MVP-SPEC.md` is edited: the spec is frozen (`TODO.md`'s own rule — *"the spec wins; flag the conflict, don't silently diverge"*).
- Notes: **This row's brief was wrong about novelty and the correction is the point.** The wave-23 brief presented (i) and (ii) as an unflagged two-place divergence *"verified centrally"*. Measured at registration, **both are already flagged**: (i) at `tasks/U.md` in U6's execution note item 1, recorded 2026-08-01 as *"a deviation-in-mechanism, not in property"* with all three directions tested; (ii) throughout U8/D50/D72, with `tasks/U.md:128` quoting line 143's exact phrase as U8's own spec citation. Registering the row on the brief's framing would have published a false claim that nobody had noticed. What is genuinely missing is the **collection** — and the sweep that found the flags also found two further divergences the brief did not name, which is the argument for doing it clause by clause.
- Notes: **[Registrar, 2026-08-19 — wave 26. `Accept` row 1 is AMENDED on the implementing lane's measurement, and row 3 is CLOSED by the registrar in the same act.]** **Row 1's `path:line` clause is refused for this venue and the refusal is proven, not argued.** `check-traceability.py --matrix` resolves only the `tests / evidence` cell and **never reads `notes`**, and a `path:line` in `tests / evidence` reds by construction — `PLAIN_PATH` is `^[\w./\-]+$`, so `:` is refused, and a planted specimen returned *"is not a recognised reference shape"*. A line number in `notes` would therefore be a pointer **nothing verifies**, which is strictly worse than either alternative rather than a compromise between them. Row 1 now reads: *one document lists every measured divergence from line 143, naming the shipped behaviour **by symbol** and citing the record that already flags it, and states the disposition of each — the venue's own by-name rule governs, and the dated `path:line` form stays in `TODO.md`'s register, where it is a timestamped claim about one tree state*. **This row supplied its own argument for that**: of eighteen locators re-measured, **three were wrong the day they were written** — `error.rs:375` (exit 14 is `:401`), `kdf.rs:288` (the Argon2id arm begins `:283`), and D151's `D47:250-255` for §Spec-conformance (it is `:172-178`, sentence `:176`). A durable document that had adopted them would have published three wrong pointers as its first act. **Row 3 is closed here**: `Q34`'s `Do` and `Accept` now cite `docs/testing/verification-matrix.md` §"MVP-SPEC.md line 143" by name. **Two clause corrections carried from the collection**: clause (iv) is **closed history**, not a live contradiction — C22/D88 resolved C21 the same day, and the two greppable *"live contradiction"* strings are **conditional guard messages**, so grepping the phrase yields the opposite of the truth; and clause **(v)** (export and keyfile wrap mutually exclusive while line 143 offers both) was added on D151's ruling and verified independently.

### Q252 — The traceability self-test's resolved-with-no-record case disarmed itself when the register drained, and it reddened a required context

- Milestone: M4
- Size: S
- Deps: after Q13/Q145 (the checker and its self-test), D120 R6 (every arm is a delta against a baseline, not an absolute verdict); consumer of `985ad05`, which drained the open-decision set
- Spec: Milestones M4 gate — *"traceability matrix 100 % green"* (MVP-SPEC.md line 157)
- Discovered by: **the wave-23 gate run** (2026-08-17), which could not get `scripts/local-gate.sh` green; measured to pre-date the wave on a detached worktree.
- Problem: **a self-test case that built its own fixture out of live register state, and stopped being able to build it the moment the register was healthy.** The `decisions` red arm proved that a decision cited as normative with no record, no `DECISIONS_HOMED_ELSEWHERE` home and no open entry is caught. It constructed that fixture by **finding** an open decision row that was already cited, then mutating it to resolved. Wave 22's **`985ad05`** — *"M4's last four open decisions close in one round"* — took the open set **4 → 0** by closing D71/D72/D73/D134, so the case had no subject and **raised instead of running**. It is not cosmetic: `scripts/ci-lanes.sh:979` runs `python3 scripts/check-traceability.py --self-test` **first** inside `lane_traceability()` and returns 1 on failure, and `traceability` is one of the 19 required status contexts (`.github/workflows/ci-always.yml:36`, `:109-110`). So a green tree could not produce a green required lane. The general shape is worse than the instance: **a fixture derived from live state is a check that gets weaker exactly as the tree gets healthier**, and it fails loudly only by luck — a variant that found *no* subject and silently skipped would have been invisible.
- Do: Rebuild the fixture so it depends on no register state. The staging must respect two constraints that rule out the obvious shapes: `TODO.md` is **not** in `CITATION_SCAN`, so a citation and a register row cannot be staged in one file; and mutating `TODO.md` contaminates `decision-index` and every other reader of it. Take the arm that follows: **stage the citation in a swept file before the baseline is measured, and make the register row itself the mutation.** The check's own allocation bound (`n > max(allocated)` → skip, `scripts/check-traceability.py:778`) holds an above-ceiling citation inert, so the staged citation cannot perturb the baseline.
- Accept:
  - The case runs green in **any** register state, including an empty open set — proven by running it at the commit where the old one died, not by argument.
  - The staged citation is proven inert: all seven checks run with the plant present and absent, and the diff of the entire output is nothing but the swept-file counts.
  - The case list's growth is **pure insertion** — a `difflib` comparison over the case triples with `removed = NONE` — so no existing arm was traded for the new one.
  - Two faults planted **in the check** each redden exactly one new arm, each by its own message.
  - `./scripts/ci-lanes.sh traceability` — the exact required-context command, not a proxy — is exit 0 with the code read back from a file.
  - Any prose count in the file is **recounted**, not adjusted by arithmetic.
- Notes: **The lane overturned both the assertion's own lean and the orchestrator's proposed fix.** The case's lean was to retire itself; the brief proposed staging an open row plus a citation. Neither survived the two-file constraint above, and the arm taken was on nobody's list. **A second property gained coverage as a side effect**: the `still_open` exemption had been exercised only contingently — green whenever some open decision happened to exist — and is now its own green arm. **The prose-count sibling is recorded here because it is the same defect one level down**: the file carried *"23 of the 32 cases below are red and 9 are green"*, and **the lane's first correction of it was itself wrong** — it wrote 24 by arithmetic before recounting by AST, and says so at the site (`scripts/check-traceability.py:3076-3084`). A count in prose has no reader that can redden; the comment now instructs the next editor to recount.

### Q253 — D125 is allocated, recorded nowhere, homed nowhere and open nowhere, and only an unswept directory is holding the gate green

- Milestone: M4
- Size: S
- Deps: after D125 itself (the ruling), Q57 (the general form); **blocks nothing today and blocks the next `CITATION_SCAN` widening**
- Spec: Milestones M4 gate — *"traceability matrix 100 % green"* (MVP-SPEC.md line 157)
- Discovered by: **the wave-23 gate lane** (2026-08-17), while accounting for the `decisions` check's own summary line; re-measured centrally at registration.
- Problem: **one allocated decision id satisfies none of the three exemptions the check offers, and the check is green only because nothing that cites it is swept.** The arithmetic is exact and D125 is the sole instance: **140 allocated − 134 records − 5 `DECISIONS_HOMED_ELSEWHERE` = D125**, computed from the checker's own accessors. `check_decisions` (`scripts/check-traceability.py:776-787`) reds any cited id that is not recorded, not homed and not open — and D125 is none of the three. It stays green **solely because no swept file mentions it.** Measured, D125 appears in `TODO.md` (5), `docs/instrument-ledger.md` (3), `docs/decisions/D126-…md` (2), `docs/decisions/D73-…md` (1), `docs/ci-verification.md` (1), `docs/waves/wave-16-brief.md` (1) and `testdata/anchors/A25-wave16-cycle/CAPTURE.log` (1) — **and not one of those paths is in `CITATION_SCAN`**. **The exposure is wider than a marked-form citation.** Unlike the task-id check, which requires a citation to assert itself typographically, `check_decisions` matches a **bare** `\bD(\d+)\b` — so a plain `D125` in a Rust comment, a shell script or a JSON fixture is enough, in any file whose suffix is one of `.html .json .md .mjs .py .rs .sh` under `crates/`, `scripts/`, `docs/format/`, `docs/testing/`, `docs/user/`, `verifier-web/` or a swept root file. **This wave widened `CITATION_SCAN` once already**, admitting `docs/user`; the same move toward `docs/` or `docs/decisions/` reds a required context on contact, and five of the seven mention sites are exactly there. D125 is the **rule-8 starvation ruling** — a maintainer ruling made in session, executed by the protocol, and recorded only as a TODO.md register row and a protocol paragraph.
- Do: **Do not choose here — this needs a planning round, and both arms have consequences the register should weigh.** **Arm A: write the record.** `docs/decisions/D125-*.md` in the register's format, back-filled from the TODO.md row and protocol rule 8, which makes D125 ordinary and closes the hole permanently. Cost: it is a *retrospective* record for a ruling made in session, and the register has so far treated back-filled records as a distinct class; the wave-15 reconstruction precedent (D115 §4.1, twelve entries) is the one to read first, including its own provenance defect at **A134**. **Arm B: register it in `DECISIONS_HOMED_ELSEWHERE` with its real home.** That constant already carries five ids whose authority genuinely lives elsewhere, and D125's authority genuinely is `TODO.md`'s protocol rule 8 — the rule is executed from there and nowhere else. Cost: every existing entry points at a *document*, and pointing one at `TODO.md` makes the tracker its own authority, which is the shape rule 8 was written to avoid. **Whichever arm is chosen, state the general rule it sets**, because the next in-session ruling will land in the same position.
- Accept:
  - D125 satisfies one of the three exemptions by a recorded act, and the act names which arm was taken and why the other was refused.
  - The fix is proven against the failure it prevents: a bare `D125` planted in a swept file is watched **red by message before the fix and green after**, rather than the fix being asserted to work.
  - The general rule for in-session maintainer rulings is written down where the next one will be made — the protocol section, not only the decision record.
  - The arithmetic is re-derived at close: allocated − recorded − homed − open is **empty**, computed from the checker's accessors and not from this row.
- Notes: **A latent red, not a live one — and it is the Q57 failure sitting in the tree today, one scan root away.** Nothing is wrong with the gate; what is wrong is that its greenness rests on a directory not being swept rather than on the property holding. **Correction to the brief that minted this row**, verified at registration: it lists **five** citation sites and there are **seven**, the two omitted being **`TODO.md` itself** — where the ruling is recorded, and where any register maintenance could trip it if `TODO.md` were ever swept — and `testdata/anchors/A25-wave16-cycle/CAPTURE.log`. Three `testdata/acvp/*.json` files also contain the literal `D125` inside hex, but they are immune by regex rather than by design: `\bD(\d+)\b` fails on `07798DD125A2CEBB` only because the preceding character is a word character. That is luck holding a required context green, and it is worth stating in whichever record closes this.

### Q254 — The publish flip has no ordered execution checklist, and three of its steps are only correct in one order

- Milestone: M4
- Size: S
- Deps: after Q243, Q244; **with Q65**, **with Q242** — *(timing words restored 2026-08-19 by D154 §2 R1/R2 from D141 §2 R6's own prescription, which reads `Q65 (this row IS Q65's execution half), Q242 (with), Q243 (before), Q244 (before)`.)* **`Q65` is a partition, not a predecessor**: R6 gave it no timing word because this row IS Q65's execution half, and everything this row needs from Q65 already exists as a written record — Q65 finding 4, in Q65's own entry. **`Q242` is co-timed**: its `Accept` row 2 can only read true "in the same window as the visibility change", while this row's `Accept` is "the checklist is committed **before** the flip", so `after Q242` was an edge that could never be satisfied in the window this row needs. **Do not write `Q65 after Q254`** — measured 2026-08-19, it creates `Q65→Q254→Q65` plus three 3-cycles through Q242/Q243/Q244, each recorded `after Q65`; this is D141's own `Q31→Q65→Q244→Q31` shape on a second pair. `~~after Q65~~`, `~~after Q242~~` — **STRUCK 2026-08-19 by D154 §2 R2**; consumes D141 §2 R6.
- Spec: Milestones M4 (MVP-SPEC.md line 157); Risks — malicious verifier host (line 186)
- Discovered by: **D141 (2026-08-18, wave-24 planning round)**, while resolving the apparent Q65/Q22/Q31 ordering cycle. The cycle dissolved; the *sequencing of the flip itself* turned out to be the thing nobody owned.
- Problem: **Q65 rules what is published and D144 rules the machine-path precondition, but nothing states the order in which the flip is taken — and the order is load-bearing in at least three measured places.** (i) Q242 is **co-timed**, not prior: `PUT repos/aed900/antseal/private-vulnerability-reporting` returns 404 while the repository is private, so `SECURITY.md` ships naming a route that does not yet resolve and the gap between flip and enable is a window in which the only disclosure route is public. (ii) Q243 is **strictly prior**: the evidence artifact becomes readable the instant visibility changes, and D145 §2 R2 measured that the existing artifact predates the gate by five days. (iii) Q244's mitigations — private repo, no fork PRs, read-only token — are exactly what the flip removes, while `sha_pinning_required` stays `false` behind Q255.
- Do: write the checklist as an ordered, executable procedure in `docs/ci-verification.md`'s runbook, which is the venue Q65's own Accept row 3 names. Each step carries its verification command and expected output. Cover the four at-the-flip settings items Q65 finding 4 folds in (`homepage` and `topics`, both empty while `https://antseal.org/` is live and canonical; the eight expiring `fuzz-smoke-artifacts`; fork-PR approval, today not merely unset but **unqueryable** — `422 "Fork PR approval is not allowed for private repositories"`), the D61 §9 re-read, and the instruction to push `main` **by name**. That last is not stylistic: `git ls-remote` shows the remote carrying only `refs/heads/main` and `refs/tags/format-v1-freeze`, while this clone holds 35 unpushed branches, a `refs/original` filter-branch backup, 61 unreachable commits and 83 unreachable blobs — all local-only until someone runs `git push --all` or `--mirror`, at which point they are not. **[D153 §2 R8, 2026-08-19.]** Before the visibility flip, read `README.md`'s `<!-- BEGIN minisign-public-key -->` anchor. **If it carries a key, the flip is that key's first publication** and D71 §A R4's precondition applies to *this* step: `dig +short TXT antseal.org` must return the `antseal-minisign-key=` record first. If the anchor carries no key — its state on 2026-08-19 — record that read-back and proceed. This step exists because the publishing act and the key-publishing act are owned by different rows and only one of them knows about the pin.
- Accept:
  - The checklist is executable by a non-author, and every step names the command that reads back its own result.

  - It states that the flip is an **external action requiring express in-the-moment consent naming action, destination and account**, and that no prior consent carries forward to it.
  - `git push --all` and `git push --mirror` are named as forbidden, with the measured reason.
- Notes: minted by D141 §2 R6 rather than folded into Q65 because Q65 owns the *decision* and this owns the *execution*; the two have different Accept shapes and Q65's is already amended twice.
- Notes: **[Implementation lane, 2026-08-19 — wave 27. Landed at `docs/ci-verification.md:2825-3661`, additions only.]** See the `TODO.md` row for the full measurement. **Q65 is NOT ticked by this**: per D154 §2 R3 only `Accept` row 3's second conjunct is discharged; Q65's per-file `public`/`private`/`private-until-release` record and the D61 §9 re-read are still owed, and checklist steps A1/A7 say so explicitly. **Do not write `Q65 after Q254`** — D154 §2 R2/R3 forbid it.

### Q255 — The Pages action set is stale and its middle action carries a transitive moving tag into the only write-scope job

- Milestone: M4
- Size: S
- Deps: after Q244 (the pinning act), Q65; **before** any row that arms `sha_pinning_required` — this is that setting's hard blocker
- Spec: Risks — supply chain (MVP-SPEC.md line 186); Page provenance (line 139); Milestones M4 (line 157)
- Discovered by: **D143 (2026-08-18)**, as the arm no brief in the round had named.
- Problem: `actions/upload-pages-artifact@v3.0.1` is a **composite** action whose own `action.yml:77` reads `uses: actions/upload-artifact@v4` — a bare major tag one level below anything this repository can pin, inside `pages.yml`, the only job in the tree with write scope. GitHub's pinning policy traverses composites, so arming `sha_pinning_required: true` today refuses that workflow outright. Upstream fixed it in v5.0.0, which SHA-pins its own nested `uses:`.
- Do: upgrade and re-pin all three Pages actions — `configure-pages` v5.0.0 → v6.0.0, `upload-pages-artifact` v3.0.1 → v5.0.0, `deploy-pages` v4.0.5 → v5.0.0 — each to its resolved **commit** with an exact `# vX.Y.Z` comment, moving its `.github/action-pins.tsv` row with it, and read the release notes for input renames. Distinguish commit from annotated tag object by the two-endpoint test D143 established: `git/commits/<sha>` returns 200 only for a commit, `git/tags/<sha>` only for a tag object. Delete the `residual` ledger line **only after** confirming v5's nested `uses:` is SHA-pinned at the commit adopted. Fold in `persist-credentials: false` at `pages.yml:72`: the `pages: write` token currently lands in `.git/config` before 9 644 375 B of third-party bundled JS executes at `:77`, and the job's transitive script closure (31 scripts) contains zero networked git operations, zero `git+` sources in `Cargo.lock` and no `.gitmodules`, while all three Pages actions take the token as a declared input.
- Accept:
  - One green `workflow_dispatch` of `pages.yml` witnessing both changes — they share a witness, which is why they share a row.
  - `pages-publish.sh --build` green; the deployed hash recorded.
  - `check-action-pins.py` green **including rule P7**, which reds by design the moment a ledger row moves without the residual being re-taken.
  - The run id recorded in the row's Notes.
  - **[ADDED 2026-09-13 by D169 §2 R4.]** CodeQL default setup's accepted tag-resolution exposure (D143's adversary A2) is **re-decided in the act that ticks this row** — once this row lands, default setup is the last A2-exposed code on `main` holding cache write, so the acceptance D169 recorded no longer rests on a worse open path.
- Notes: `persist-credentials: false` **shrinks rather than closes** the blast radius — `id-token: write` is job-wide and remains in every step's environment. It is a behaviour change to a live deploy path, which is why it needs the witness rather than riding in on a pin bump.
- Notes: **[2026-09-13, wave 35 — LOCAL HALF LANDED (lane W1), `Accept` witness OWED; the row stays open.]** **Landed**: `pages.yml`'s checkout gains `persist-credentials: false` (with a comment that it narrows, not closes, the exposure — `id-token: write` stays job-wide); `configure-pages` → `45bfe0192ca1faeb007ade9deae92b16b8254a0d # v6.0.0`, `upload-pages-artifact` → `fc324d3547104276b827a68afc52ff2a11cc49c9 # v5.0.0`, `deploy-pages` → `368f82528645a54fb793d4d04e342629a3f51346 # v5.0.1`, each a lightweight tag whose SHA answered `git/commits` 200 and `git/tags` 404 (control: the annotated tag object `49a0bdc7…` inverted to 404/200); ledger rows moved to `resolved_utc 2026-09-13` / `next_review_utc 2026-12-12`; the `residual` line **deleted only after** `action.yml` was fetched at `fc324d35` and its sole nested `uses:` read back as `actions/upload-artifact@bbbca2ddaa5d8feaa63e36b76fdaad77386f024f # v7.0.0` (a commit, `node24`, no further `uses:`); the `# RESIDUAL` header kept. **DEVIATION from this row's `Do`: `deploy-pages` is adopted at v5.0.1, not v5.0.0** — v5.0.1 was released 2026-09-01, after this row was written, the major tag `v5` points at it, so D143 §2 R7's own re-resolve returns it and v5.0.0 would pin one patch behind the procedure's answer; its `action.yml` differs from v5.0.0 only in one input description. **`scripts/check-action-pins.py` had to change too, and the row's plan missed it**: the bump retired the literals its P1 and P7 self-test arms plant on (measured before repair: `self_test()` exit 1, both `NOT APPLIED`), so P1 now plants on the new `deploy-pages` line, P7 plants its own historical residual (a literal-only repair reds `[P3]` instead, measured), and a new arm covers P7's orphan-parent branch; self-test **10 arms, every one red by its own tag, 0 not applied**; flagless green; `check-ci-paths.py --self-test` green with its seven target files byte-identical before and after. **Corrections to this row's text**: the nested-pin fix landed in upstream **v4.0.0**, not v5.0.0; `upload-pages-artifact` declares no `token` input; the job executes **8** scripts, not 31, with zero networked git across all 41 scripts under `scripts/`. **`Accept` row 3 (P7 green) is met by the self-test alone** — the ledger holds no residual. **OWED — `Accept` rows 1, 2 and 4, as ONE consented act**: a push of `main`, then a `workflow_dispatch` of `pages.yml`, which redeploys `https://antseal.org/` (and repairs `R97`). Read-back plan, fixed before the act: (a) job steps > 0, every step `success`; (b) the verify step prints *served bytes match the built artifact*, because a green step alone can take the no-local-sums branch; (c) an independent fetch's sha256 equals that digest; (d) the checkout input echo reads `persist-credentials: false` against control run `34720769800`'s `true`; (e) no `extraheader` line at post-job cleanup, where the control shows one; (f) `Download action repository 'actions/upload-artifact@bbbca2dd…'` against before-control run `31873422229`'s `@v4`; (g) the run id and the deployed hash recorded verbatim here.
- Notes: **[2026-09-13, wave 35 — lane W1, phase 2 addendum.]** Phase 2 changed only `pages.yml`'s tier prose, at the same line count, so the pin lines (`:86`, `:87`, `:91`) did not move; the ledger's Pages rows are now `:15-17`. **Sequencing**: one consented push of `main` both witnesses `Q238` and puts this row's pins on `main`; this row's own witness is still the consented `pages.yml` dispatch after it.
- Notes: **[2026-09-13, wave 35 — WITNESSED; the row stays OPEN on ONE term: D169 §2 R4's re-decision, which this act turned into a measured question rather than a formality.]** Pushed and dispatched under the maintainer's express consent given 2026-09-13 in direct reply to a request naming each action, the destination `github.com/aed900/antseal` and the account `aed900`. **Run `34782645736`** (`pages.yml`, `workflow_dispatch`, `main` at `bfdc93e`), dispatched only after every hosted run on that commit was green. Read-backs, as planned: **(a)** job `publish` `success`, 12 steps, every step `success`, runner assigned. **(b)** `served bytes match the built artifact: b441bae727015ef2fb217fb071f7c6ae3fb31cde92f0af0900d146e472e00b1d`. **(c)** an independent fetch's sha256 is exactly that digest. **(d)** the checkout input echo reads `persist-credentials: false`, and the checkout step itself runs `git config --local --unset-all http.https://github.com/.extraheader` after the fetch. **(e)** at post-job cleanup the `--get-regexp …extraheader` command is followed by **no** header line (the control run `34720769800` showed one) — a bare count of the key string returns 6, every one a command line or the in-step removal, so the read-back is by position, not by count. **(f)** `Download action repository 'actions/upload-artifact@bbbca2ddaa5d8feaa63e36b76fdaad77386f024f' (SHA:bbbca2ddaa5d8feaa63e36b76fdaad77386f024f)` — the nested pin, where the before-control ran `@v4`. **(g)** run id and deployed hash recorded here and in `R97`. **`Accept` rows 1–4 are MET.** **HELD on the D169 §2 R4 term, because the re-decision is a real security question now**: default setup's tag-resolved actions hold Actions **cache write** on `main`, and `pages.yml` restores `Swatinem/rust-cache` inside its write-scope job; the obvious mitigation — the job's two-environment comparison — is **not independent of a poisoned crate cache**, because `scripts/reproducible-build.sh` copies the registry `cache` and `index` from build A's `CARGO_HOME` into build B (only `src` is re-extracted). Whether cargo re-verifies an already-cached `.crate` against `Cargo.lock`'s checksum, and whether a cache entry written by another workflow can match `pages.yml`'s restore key, are **not established**. Owner: the next planning round, briefed with these two measurements; timing: before this row ticks.

### Q256 — Six of the eight devnet evidence files are duplicated verbatim into the job log, where no gate can refuse them

- Milestone: M4
- Size: S
- Deps: after Q243 (the scan this bypasses), Q246; consumes D145 §2 R7
- Spec: Risks — key/secret handling (MVP-SPEC.md line 186); project rule 6
- Discovered by: **D145 (2026-08-18)**, and it is the measurement that decided that record.
- Problem: `scripts/e2e-devnet.sh:322,339` write through `tee`, so six evidence files land identically in the workflow log and in the artifact — measured per file: `local-up.log` 1076/1076, `suite-S6-S8` 589/589, `suite-S18` 110/110, `suite-S17` 64/64, `suite-S19` 17/17, `evidence.txt` 1/1. `redact()` is applied at `:361-362` to exactly the two artifact-only files. So Q243's scan gates the artifact and D145's `retention-days: 30` governs the artifact — together 525 954 of 620 748 B — and neither reaches the 94 794 B leaving through the log, which has its own retention and which the visibility flip exposes on GitHub's own statement that *"Actions history and logs will be visible to everyone"*. **Nothing has leaked**: the artifact scans `findings=0 verdict=CLEAN` and the log carries the same content. The *proof* is in the wrong place — the same defect Q243 was minted for, one channel over.
- Do: fix it at the **write**, not at the upload — pipe through the redactor before `tee` (`| redact | tee`) so both destinations receive redacted bytes. Then assert it rather than assuming it: a fault planted in `redact()` must redden a check that reads the log-bound stream, proven by message. Consider whether `--scan-evidence` should additionally be pointed at a captured copy of the log.
- Accept:
  - No evidence file reaches the job log unredacted.
  - A planted fault reddens by **message**, not by exit code — a crash exits nonzero too.
  - The two-sided property is preserved: contract addresses, blob digests and tx hashes still survive, per Q243's clean-fixture arm, so the fix cannot quietly become a blanket hex filter.

### Q257 — The reserved-placeholder vocabulary is enforced by a lint and written down nowhere a contributor reads first

- Milestone: M4
- Size: XS
- Deps: after Q65; consumes D144 §2 R8
- Spec: Milestones M4 (MVP-SPEC.md line 157)
- Discovered by: **D144 (2026-08-18)**, as an owed half of its own ruling.
- Problem: `check-traceability.py`'s `--machine-paths` check bans any absolute home path outside the closed vocabulary `(user, fixture, runner, u, x)`, and its remedy is deliberately **not** an exemption list — a contributor's only move is to rename their own placeholder. That is the right design and it is currently undiscoverable: the vocabulary lives in `RESERVED_PLACEHOLDER_NAMES` and in D144, and in neither `CONTRIBUTING.md` nor `docs/positioning-copy-style.md`. The first time anyone meets it is as a red gate on their own commit, citing a rule they have never seen.
- Do: state it in one line where a contributor meets it **before** the lint does. `CONTRIBUTING.md` is the likelier home, since the rule governs how paths are written in examples rather than product copy. Interpolate the names from the constant if the venue allows, so the document cannot drift from the check — the check's own four `::error::` messages already do this and therefore cannot go stale.
- Accept:
  - The vocabulary is stated in a contributor-facing file, naming all five reserved names.
  - The statement says an exemption list does not exist, so nobody looks for one.
  - A reader arriving from the lint's error message can act without opening a decision record.

### Q258 — Make the outstanding maintainer-only signing acts visible in the tracker
- Milestone: M4
- Size: S
- Deps: **with Q30** (this row surfaces Q30's outstanding acts; it is a predecessor of nothing). Consumes D153 §2 R4/R9, D71 §A R4/R5, D150 §4.2
- Spec: Milestones M4 "binary signing (sha256sums + minisign/cosign) + documented distribution" (MVP-SPEC.md line 157)
- Discovered by: **D153's lane** (2026-08-19) §2 R9 item 3, and asked for a wave earlier by **D150 §4.2**
- Problem: the remaining maintainer acts — **four** as of 2026-08-22, since D153 §2 R9 item 3's bundled *"§1a steps 1–2"* split when step 1 landed and step 2 was separately deferred — live only inside Q30's Notes, so a readiness survey that reads rows cannot see them — which is how the un-generated signing key went unnoticed for two waves until the maintainer asked. They also differ in kind and are routinely flattened into one "deferred" bucket: one is overdue and blocking, one is triggered by an act this project controls, and one is genuinely release-timed.
- Do: **[AMENDED 2026-08-22 by D157 §2 R5. Old text struck below, never deleted.]** Give the acts one visible home and **only one**. Status stays where D153 §2 R2 put it — `maintainer-key-procedure.md` §7 — which gains D157 §2 R4(a)'s routing table: per act, **who** takes it, **what is blocking it**, **when to take it**, and **what it releases**, by row id. This row's own body carries the same non-time-varying content and **no act status at all**, on both tracker surfaces, because `TODO.md`'s copy and `tasks/Q.md`'s copy of this row are already known to disagree **with the traceability gate green** (D157 §1.9). Do **NOT** introduce a class word: *overdue / triggered / release-timed* fuses two independent axes and means opposite things on §2's row and §3's (D157 §1.5) — and `ARMED` was refused for the same reason, being wrong in *direction* for §2, whose only predecessor is done. ~~give the three acts a visible home that states, per act, **who** takes it, **what unblocks when it lands**, and **whether it is overdue, triggered, or release-timed** — never a single "pending maintainer" bucket. Name §1a step 1's two offline backups as the one overdue act, with D153 §2 R4's finding that it gates §3 and not merely §4.~~
- Accept:
  - `maintainer-key-procedure.md` §7 carries D157 §2 R4(a)'s table with all **six** rows — §1, §1a step 1, §1a step 2, §2, §3 and §5 — each with its `blocked by` and `take it when` filled from the record that decides it (D71 §A R4 for §2, §A R5 clause 4 for §3, D153 §2 R4 for §1a step 1, D157 §2 R1 for §1a step 2). **No single class word appears.** *(Amended 2026-08-22 by D157 §2 R5; old text: "Each of the three acts is stated with its class (overdue / triggered / release-timed) and the record that classifies it.")*
  - The `releases` column names row ids — `Q30` `Accept` row 1 for §2, §3 and §5, **`R96`** as §5 step 2's write target, **`Q259`** as §1a step 2's blocker — and **§5 is marked NOT a maintainer act**, since `maintainer-key-procedure.md:3-5` names exactly three external acts and §5 is not one of them.
  - A reader who has read no decision record can act on **§2** — the only act with nothing blocking it — from §7's table alone, including that it needs the registrar account and **express in-the-moment consent naming action, destination and account**. Every decision cited in the table is cited as `D<id> §<section>`, never as a bare `§…`. *(The subject moved from §1a step 1 to §2 because step 1 is DONE: a readiness-from-this-row-alone standard aimed at a completed act cannot fail.)*
  - The exposure is stated **as it stands after 2026-08-22**: the bound is **D71** §2 R7.1's `-W` ban — the key is `Sc`-wrapped, so possession of a copy is not possession of the key — and the residue is **no longer deletion**, which two verified off-site backups make recoverable, but **loss or disclosure of the passphrase**, which now defends **three** copies of the key file rather than one. §1a step 2 addresses the first and `Q259` gates it; `key-custody.md` §7 is the response to the second.
  - **[NEW row, D157 §2 R5.]** No sentence in this row's body — **on either tracker surface** — changes when a maintainer act is taken. Read the body and name the sentence that would need editing the day §2 lands; **if one exists, the row is not done.**
- Notes: **This row takes none of the acts** — all three are the maintainer's, and §2 additionally requires express in-the-moment consent naming action, destination and account. Its deliverable is visibility, not execution.

### Q259 — The signing-key passphrase instruction has no referent, and its analogy inverts the risk model
- Milestone: M4
- Size: XS
- Deps: **with Q258**; consumes D153 §4.5
- Spec: Milestones M4 (MVP-SPEC.md line 157); key custody is D71's
- Discovered by: the maintainer's own execution of `maintainer-key-procedure.md` §1 (2026-08-19), recorded in Q30's Notes as *"The second is unfixed and needs a row."*
- Problem: four sites tell the maintainer to generate, store and record the signing passphrase *"the way the antseal vault passphrase is"* — `maintainer-key-procedure.md:54`, `:76`; `key-custody.md:55-56`, `:70`. **Measured 2026-08-19 as absent; re-measured 2026-08-22 and it EXISTS** — `docs/user/vault-theft.md:117-126`, linked from `README.md:84-85`, prescribes generation and storage for the vault passphrase, and `:123-124` calls forgetting it *"equally final"*. So the pointer is a **live mis-instruction with a known sign**, not a dangling one: it pushes the maintainer to replicate the one secret that must not be replicated. Sites B and D point at a referent that additionally **cannot be written** — antseal deliberately records the user's vault passphrase nowhere (`docs/user/vault-loss.md:163`, `:169-172`).md` or `MVP-SPEC.md`. And the analogy is inverted — `key-custody.md` §5 makes signing-key loss *"the cheap failure"*, recoverable by rotation and re-publication, while `docs/user/vault-loss.md:166` makes vault-passphrase loss **final**. A maintainer who follows the pointer therefore imports the *stricter* discipline's risk model onto the *cheaper* secret, or looks for a document that was never written, while holding live secret material.
- Do: replace both analogies with concrete, self-contained guidance that states what losing the signing passphrase actually costs and what storing it well means for **this** secret ~~— or write the referent the pointer assumes, and say explicitly which of the two was done~~ **[STRUCK 2026-08-22 by D157 §2 R3: §2 R1 rules the arm and §3.2 refuses the alternative — the referent exists, so "write it" no longer describes the tree. **Do** is now: splice D157 §2 R1's four exact replacements into sites A–D. Compose nothing.]**.
- Accept:
  - No instruction in the signing pages tells the maintainer to generate, store or record this passphrase by analogy to the vault's. **Measured as** `grep -nE 'passphrase is recorded|generated and stored the way|the way the antseal vault' docs/signing/*.md README.md`, **excluding `maintainer-key-procedure.md` from its `## 7. What has and has not been run` heading to end of file** — expected count **0**. Both exclusions are the point of the measurement, not conveniences: `docs/decisions/` is out of scope because a record is amended by dated addendum and never rewritten (D157 §2 R6), and §7 is out of scope because it **quotes** the old instruction in order to record why §1a step 2 was deferred — a lane "fixing" that quotation would delete the note explaining the deferral. **[REGISTRAR CORRECTION 2026-08-22, and it is the reason this row is written here rather than transcribed.]** D157 §2 R3 specified the pattern `'vault passphrase is|the way the antseal vault'`. **That pattern is falsified by D157 §2 R1's own prescribed text**: Site A wraps so that `passphrase` and `is` land adjacent at `maintainer-key-procedure.md:58`, inside the **contrast** sentence R1 mandates — so rows 1 and 2 could not both be satisfied by any one tree, since satisfying row 1 would mean re-wrapping text row 2 requires verbatim. Measured at registration: the specified pattern returns **1**, the pattern above returns **0**. The implementing lane found this and **refused to re-wrap**, on the ground that bending a ruled artifact to make an acceptance check green is the inversion this project names; the pattern is tightened instead. D157 §2 R3's `:258`/`:299` coordinates for §7 are also pre-edit — §7 now begins at `:282` and the file ends at `:344`, which is why the boundary is stated by **heading text** here and not by line number.
  - Each of the four sites — `maintainer-key-procedure.md` §1 (the passphrase paragraph) and §1a step 2, `key-custody.md` §2 (closing paragraph) and §4 (the passphrase bullet) — carries the replacement text of D157 §2 R1 **verbatim**. Stated as a presence assertion because row 1 alone is an absence check, and **an absence check goes green if a lane deletes the paragraphs instead of replacing them**.
  - The guidance states the signing key's own loss profile and cites `key-custody.md` §5 and §6 for it.
  - The distinction from the vault passphrase is stated **with its direction**: losing this one is recoverable and losing a vault passphrase is not, so this one is defended by **strength** rather than by **copies**. `docs/user/vault-theft.md` is not cited as guidance to follow anywhere in `docs/signing/`; the single link to `docs/user/` introduced by this row is to `vault-loss.md`, in a sentence that marks it a contrast.
- Notes: `docs/signing/` is Class P product copy — any replacement must keep `scripts/check-copy-style.py` green and obey D71 §2 R11's four prohibitions.

### Q260 — TICKED_WITH_RESIDUE: nothing reads a ticked row's residue marker

- Milestone: M4
- Size: S
- Deps: after Q253 (the traceability checker's shape and its self-test harness); with A139 (which discharges one of the three seeds)
- Spec: Milestones M4 (MVP-SPEC.md line 157); the tracker protocol is TODO.md's own rules 1, 2 and 7
- Discovered by: **D156 §2 R10** (2026-08-22). Two independent sweeps of D154 §4 item 4 — an instruction ordered a wave earlier and never executed — found the same class from opposite directions.
- Problem: **A `- [x]` row can carry a residue marker that no reader, human or mechanical, is required to see.** D156 §2 R1 rules that `after X` requires X's `Do` to have landed rather than X to be `[x]`, which makes ticked-with-residue a *correct* state rather than a defect — but only if the residue names a live owner and the marker is retracted when it is discharged. Neither obligation had a reader. Measured: four `🟡` glyphs outlived their residues by up to ten days, and every successor row that read them was reading a false statement; `R26`'s `STANDING DEBT` was retracted on two surfaces and left standing on `TODO.md`, which is the surface D156 itself then read, causing it to rule on a debt that no longer existed.
- Do: Add a check whose predicate is **a ticked row carrying an unretracted residue marker that is named in an open row's ordering run**, modelled on `ACCEPTED_NON_COVERED`'s three arms in `scripts/check-traceability.py`. Seed it with the three rows D156 §2 R10 names: **P17**, **A31**, **R26**. Register the retraction vocabulary so the check can tell a live marker from a retracted one.
- Accept:
  - The check reds on a ticked row whose residue marker is unretracted **and** whose id appears in an open row's ordering run, and prints the reading row's id — a finding that does not name its reader is not actionable.
  - **T4 is mandatory: the check does NOT fire on a marker-free untick.** Without it the predicate silently degrades into the 15-hit *ticked-behind-an-open-predecessor* measurement that D156 §2 R1 ruled **benign**, and the check becomes noise that trains readers to ignore it.
  - Four faults are planted and each reds **by its own message**, not by exit code: an unretracted marker on a read row; a retracted marker that must stay green; a marker on a row no open row reads (green); and T4.
  - The measured false-positive count over the real tree is recorded in the row at the act that lands it. D154 refused two checkers on yield (4 findings / 0 true, and 62 / 1) and this one is minted at **3 of 3** after the wave-28 retractions; if the landing measurement disagrees with that figure, the row records the new number rather than the expected one.
- Notes: The predicate is deliberately **narrower** than "a ticked row with a residue" — a residue nobody reads misleads nobody, and widening it re-creates the noise D154 refused. `A139` discharges the `A31` seed and `R26`'s marker was retracted at the wave-28 registration, so by the time this row runs its seed set may be **P17 alone**; that is the intended direction and not a reason to widen the predicate to keep the count up.

### Q261 — key-custody.md §10 says "Never edit a row" and nothing enforces it

- Milestone: M4
- Size: S
- Deps: with Q258 (both concern the signing-act record surfaces); the subject is `docs/signing/key-custody.md` §10
- Spec: Milestones M4 (MVP-SPEC.md line 157); key custody is D71's
- Discovered by: **D157 §2 R9** (2026-08-22) — the one checker of three candidates that survived measurement. The other two were refused on yield: a *status-claim-outside-§7* prose detector measuring **16 hits / 1 true / 11 false** (it reddens on the very pointer D153 §2 R2 mandates), and a copy-count detector at **33 / ~6 / 27**.
- Problem: `key-custody.md` §10 is an **append-only log** — it is the record of what was done to the project signing key and when, and its entire evidentiary value rests on rows never changing. The file says so in terms (*"Never edit a row"*). Nothing checks it. The failure is silent by construction: a row edited to say an act was taken reads exactly like a row that always said so, and the log is the only place that history lives.
- Do: Add a check that pins §10's existing rows against a committed baseline, so any change to an already-written row reds while a genuine append passes. Baseline at the file's birth commit and register the exceptions explicitly rather than by a fuzzy comparison.
- Accept:
  - A genuine **append** (a new dated row) passes.
  - An **edit to an existing row** reds, naming the row.
  - The planted fault D157 §2 R9 specifies is run and its message recorded: flipping the 2026-08-19 row's *"NOT yet made"* to *"made"* must give `::error:: row 1 EDITED` at `REAL_EXIT=1`, read back out of a file.
  - The registered-exception count is stated in the row. Baselined at the file's birth commit it is exactly **one**, which §11 already documents; if the landing measurement gives a different number, the row records the measured one.
- Notes: Measured at **0 false positives** on the real tree with `REAL_EXIT=0` before the plant. This is the one place in the corpus where an append-only claim is load-bearing *and* unenforced — the goldens and the freeze boundary already have their own guards. **This row takes no maintainer act**: it checks what is written about the key, never the key.
- Notes: **[WAVE 33, 2026-09-03 — the residue BIT, exactly in its recorded shape, and its local half is CLOSED.]** The 2026-08-27 history rewrite gave every commit in its range a new SHA, so this checker's pinned birth commit stopped existing and it went red BY DESIGN (baseline drift, `REAL_EXIT=1`) — and sat red, unseen, for SIX DAYS, because the lane ran in no ritual: found by a wave-33 planning sweep, not a gate. Repaired by deliberate re-derivation (`git log --diff-filter=A` returns exactly one commit, `af38d18`; the drift arm re-proven red on a planted bogus pin AFTER the fix, message-verified), and `run custody-log` is wired into `local-gate.sh` beside `package-smoke` in the same commit, green on arrival per D158 (the composite self-test + check read back `REAL_EXIT=0`). The class was swept: exactly ONE live repo-SHA pin exists under `scripts/` (this one; 3 fixture literals excluded by inspection). WHAT STAYS OWED: the hosted home — `check-ci-paths.py`'s UNREACHED entry moves to FOLLOW_EDGES only in the commit that wires the lane into the traceability JOB, owner a CI lane, timing with the first green hosted run after Maintainer actions (11). Ledger entry 252.

### Q262 — The pre-flip key-publication check reads README only, and the page footer can now carry a key

- Milestone: M4
- Size: S
- Deps: with Q258 (the maintainer signing-act surfaces); **before Q65** — this is a pre-flip check and must be right before the flip runs, which is an ordering word and not an `after` edge
- Spec: Milestones M4 (MVP-SPEC.md line 157); key publication is D71's
- Discovered by: **R96's implementing lane** (2026-08-22), reporting a hazard **its own change created** rather than one it found lying there — which is why it is recorded here rather than in the ledger.
- Problem: `docs/ci-verification.md`'s pre-flip step **A6** exists, in D153 §2 R8's words, *"so the flip cannot become a key's first publication by accident"*. It greps the `minisign-public-key` marker pair in **`README.md` and nothing else**. That was complete **by accident**: until 2026-08-22 the verifier page footer had no key element and no marker, so the README was the only tree-resident place a key could sit. `R96` closed that gap, and the footer is now the sharper case of the two — **the page is already live and public at `https://antseal.org/` while the repository is still private**, and `scripts/pages-publish.sh` contains **zero** occurrences of `key`, `signing` or `minisign`. So a key placed in the footer reaches the world through a publishing path that never touches `Q65`'s flip at all. D71 §A R4 makes **first publication** the event that starts the DNS `TXT` pin's clock and calls that pin *"the one that should not be deferred"* — this route would start that clock silently, with no row observing it.
- Do: Widen A6 so it reads **every** tree-resident publication location the signing procedure names, not just the README — at minimum `README.md` and `verifier-web/index.template.html`, taking the list from `maintainer-key-procedure.md` §5 rather than hard-coding it, so a fifth location added later is covered without a second edit. Then decide, and record, whether `scripts/pages-publish.sh` gains its own guard: the page publishes independently of the repository flip, so an A6 that only runs pre-flip does not cover the page's own release path.
- Accept:
  - A6's command reads every location `maintainer-key-procedure.md` §5 names, and the list is derived rather than transcribed — a location added to §5 without touching A6 must still be checked.
  - A planted key-shaped run in the **page footer** reds A6 by message, naming the file. A planted key-shaped run in `README.md` still reds. Both are proven, not assumed.
  - The page's own publish path is dispositioned in writing: either it gains a guard, or the reason it needs none is recorded with the row that owns the residue.
  - The row states plainly that at the time of writing the footer carries **no** key, so this is a hazard and not a live leak — a future reader must not mistake it for an incident.
- Notes: **This row takes no maintainer act and publishes nothing.** It changes what is *checked* before a publication, never what is published. The interlock with `R96` is deliberate: R96's own test asserts the footer block is in exactly one of two states — the placeholder sentence, or a key-shaped run — so the moment §5 step 2 fires, that test flips to the key-shaped arm and this check must already be in place. **`Q258` is the readiness surface** that must show this, which is why the two are co-timed rather than ordered.


### Q263 — `.gitignore` declares deny-by-default for secret material and does not deliver it: five of seven secret-shaped names are not ignored, going into the flip
- Milestone: M4 — **before the visibility flip**, because that is when the hole stops being local. Not a flip blocker in the Phase-A sense (nothing is currently mis-tracked); a hole in the one mechanism that defines the `private` class.
- Size: S
- Deps: none. It is a `.gitignore` edit plus its own check; it touches no product code and no other lane's surface.
- Spec: Risks — key/secret handling (MVP-SPEC.md line 184); Milestones M4 (line 157)
- Discovered by: **D161 §2 R9, 2026-08-22**, re-probing scrub blocker **S2** while ruling `Q65`'s `private` class. S2 was recorded 2026-08-16 in `docs/reviews/pre-public-scrub-worktree.md` and **the scrub's own §8 never minted a row for it** — `grep` over `TODO.md` and `tasks/` returns zero rows naming `credentials.json`, which occurs in exactly one file in the tree: the review that found it.
- The defect, precisely: `.gitignore`'s own header states a deny-by-default rule for secret material — *"no secret material may ever be committable by default … extend them freely, never narrow them"* — and the patterns do not implement it. Probed 2026-08-22 with `git check-ignore -q` against names that **create no files**, so the probe is free and repeatable:

  ```
  NOT-IGNORED  .env  .env.local  secrets.env  id_rsa  credentials.json
  IGNORED      foo.key  .secrets/a
  ```

  **Five of seven.** This is the `private` class's only enforcement mechanism (D161 §2 R2 adopts `.gitignore` by reference as that class's register), and `secret-guard` (Q2) is a *content* scanner over what is already staged — it is the second line, not this one. The two are complementary and neither substitutes for the other.
- Do: Extend `.gitignore` so the declared rule holds for the dotfile and keyfile families the probe names — at minimum `.env` and its `.env.*` variants, `*secrets*.env`, the OpenSSH private-key basenames (`id_rsa`, `id_ecdsa`, `id_ed25519`, and their `*_rsa`/`*_ed25519` forms), and `credentials.json`. **Widen, never narrow**: the header's rule forbids removing an existing pattern to make room, and any pattern added must not shadow a tracked file. Then add the probe as a **check**, so the rule is enforced rather than asserted — the natural venue is a new arm on an existing instrument rather than a new script, since the project's rule is that a copy added without its lint is drift.
- Accept:
  - Every one of the seven probe names resolves the way the header's rule says it should, verified by `git check-ignore -q` on names that **create no files**, with the before/after counts recorded in this row's Notes (5 of 7 not-ignored → 0 of 7).
  - `git ls-files -i -c --exclude-standard` returns **0** after the change — no pattern added here shadows a file already tracked. Measured **0** before, so this is a *preserved* invariant and the row says so rather than claiming a repair it did not make.
  - The check **plants a fault per rule arm** and asserts on the **message** that fault produces, never on exit status (Q149); the fixture count is **derived from the fixture list**, not written as a literal (Q245); and the arm's subject is **constructed** rather than read out of the live `.gitignore` (Q252), because a fixture whose subject is state the project is driving to zero disarms itself the moment the project succeeds.
  - The green arm is proven too: at least one name that is *correctly* ignored today (`foo.key`) and one that must *stay* trackable (a real tracked file, e.g. `README.md`) are asserted, so the check is not merely "ignore everything".
- Notes: **This row publishes nothing and takes no maintainer act.** It does not claim that any secret was ever committed — `docs/reviews/pre-public-scrub-history.md` measured that question separately and found zero across 3 450 blobs, with 37 of 37 detector patterns fired on 44 planted canaries before any zero was believed. What this row buys is that the *next* one cannot be committed by accident. **It is deliberately NOT closed inside D161**: that record adopts `.gitignore` by reference precisely so the register has one truth, and a decision record that edits the register it adopts becomes a second one.
### Q264 — Execute the success-metric cadence after the ship: outreach, weekly review, and declaring the count met
- Milestone: M4
- Size: S
- Deps: Q35 (the plan and the instrument this row executes), Q34 (the ship — this is the one row the `after Q34` term was ever true of), U76 (the `metric-vault-fp` route that makes `vault_fp` fillable)
- Spec: Milestones M4 success metric (MVP-SPEC.md line 157); Product definition MVP user (line 26)
- Discovered by: **D163 §2 R6/R7** (2026-08-22), which SPLIT `Q35` rather than reword its ordering term. Id assigned centrally by the registrar; the record deliberately did not number its own discovery.
- Problem: `Q35` carried clauses on both sides of the ship in one row, so it was **untickable in both directions** — `after Q34` made the plan unwritable before release, and `before Q34` would have made the post-release cadence a claim nobody could own. D163 §2 R5 measured that `after Q34` was never a typo: `git log -S` traces it to one authoring act (`0efdeee`, the founding tracker) and it sits beside an `Accept` conjunct that agrees with it.
- Do: **after the release ships**, execute the cadence `Q35` planned: run the first outreach batch, review progress **weekly**, record each completion into `docs/success-metric.md` using D73 §4 R7's row shape, and **declare the count met** when the termination condition fires. Reporting stays D73's **two numbers, never one** — `n_evidenced` and `n_claimed` — and the metric stays **zero-telemetry**: the evidence is the participant's submitted `.sealproof`, verified offline by the maintainer, never a beacon or a callback.
- Accept:
  - Progress reviewed **weekly post-release**, with each review dated in `docs/success-metric.md`'s header — `Q35` `Accept` row 2 conjunct 2, moved here by D163 §2 R7 as the only clause `after Q34` was ever true of.
  - The first outreach batch is **executed** (not merely scheduled — scheduling is `Q35`'s), and the weekly cadence runs until the termination condition fires.
  - **The count is declared met**, gated on all three of: `U76`'s `metric-vault-fp` route available, **X2** fired, and the **X7** planted-fault record present in the header — D73 §7 item 6 calls that trio *"a concrete pre-condition of declaring the count met"*. `legs_1_2` stays the only cell permitted to read VERIFIED.
  - The row terminates: **≥10 completions, or a recorded abandonment with its date and reason.** Stated so this row is bounded and tickable, which is why D163 §2 R7 keeps it out of the Continuous section.
- Notes: **Bounded, not continuous** (D163 §2 R7). This row takes **no agent act before the ship** and several of its clauses are maintainer-facing (outreach to real people, offline verification of submitted bundles); it is listed as agent work only for the bookkeeping half. `Q35` is its sole predecessor for the plan and the instrument — `docs/success-metric.md` must exist, with every number at zero, before this row has anywhere to write.

### Q265 — Arm the promoted reproducibility lane as a required status context, once a hosted run has reported it green on `main`
- Milestone: M4
- Size: S
- Deps: after **Q238** (which builds the filtered lane this arms) **+ one hosted run reporting the new context `success` on `main`**; **before Q34** — a DEADLINE, not a gate on starting, on the D156 §2 R8 reading D163 §2 R4 applied to `Q1`. **No `after Q65` edge is minted**: D164 §2 R2 measured that the flip is neither necessary nor sufficient for this act, and writing that edge would re-import the term the same record just struck. Co-owned with `Q258` and with Maintainer actions entry **(8)** — it is the *same* GitHub write as `Q1`'s branch-protection residue, not a second one.
- Spec: Page provenance — reproducible build and published hash (MVP-SPEC.md line 139); Risks — malicious verifier host (line 186); release (line 156)
- Discovered by: **D164 §2 R3** (2026-08-27), which SPLIT `Q238` on the D163 §2 R6/R7 precedent rather than rewording its ordering term. Id assigned centrally by the registrar; the record deliberately did not number its own discovery.
- Problem: `Q238` carried an agent obligation and a maintainer obligation under one timing word, and the timing word named the wrong event. Building the lane needs no network and is available today; **arming a required status context is a GitHub API write no agent may take**, and it is genuinely gated — but on *a hosted run having reported the context green on `main`*, not on the repository's visibility. Conflating the two is what produced three different timing words across four surfaces for one row.
- Do: **Decide the arming mechanism, then arm it.** (a) Rule **classic branch protection with `enforce_admins: false`** versus a **ruleset** — D164 §2 R6 records that the register has never distinguished them and that they differ on exactly the case `Q238`'s shape creates (a docs-only direct push with the context unreported). (b) Read the check-runs and confirm the promoted context has reported `success` on `main` at least once — **read it, never expect it**. (c) Arm it, and **regenerate** the required-context payload rather than incrementing a copy (D164 §2 R7; C2's own rule). (d) Read the armed state back.
- Accept:
  - The arming mechanism is ruled, in writing, with the `enforce_admins`/ruleset difference stated rather than elided.
  - A hosted run is cited by id showing the promoted context `success` on `main` **before** any arming call is made.
  - The context is armed and read back, **or** a recorded refusal with its reason — either outcome ticks this row; an indefinite wait does not.
  - The required-context payload is regenerated from `scripts/check-ci-paths.py`'s `REQUIRED_CONTEXTS`, and the runbook's stale prose count (19 contexts from 17 jobs against `ci.yml`'s 15 job ids) is corrected or explicitly left to `Q56` by name.
- Notes: **This row exists so that `Q238` can tick on work an agent can do.** D164 §2 R4 makes the binding predicate a branch the runbook carries, on the **A6** precedent (*"BEFORE, and it is a branch"*): if the context reports green before `B0`, arming is a **Phase A** step and the maintainer's *"before public"* wish is met literally; if it has not, arming becomes a **Phase C** act tied to `C4`, and the flip proceeds carrying a **recorded exposure** in `B6`'s shape — dated and owned, never silent. **Nothing here asserts that the flip restores CI**: D164 §2 R5 rules that no row or step may assert either branch of the refusal disjunction, and the cut was placed at the green-run predicate precisely so this row does not depend on that question. **`docs/ci-verification.md` C2's *"arming early is actively unsafe"* is UNPROVEN** and was the reason this obligation had been pushed after the flip — see D164 §2 R2 and the `enforce_admins: false` bypass the same file prescribes at `:277` and describes at `:292-294`. This row takes an external action and **no agent may take it**; it needs express in-the-moment consent naming action, destination and account.
- Notes: **[2026-09-13, wave 35 — two changes to what this row arms, and the question that must be ruled before any arming call.]** **(1)** The regenerated payload now carries **20** contexts (D168 adds `reproducible-build`), and both of this row's deadline conditions are measured met for the 19 existing ones: `ci` green on `4a7f524` (run `34719132556`), `ci-always` green on `ef5f6d9` (run `34720769800`); protection is **available and unarmed** (`branches/main/protection` → 404 *Branch not protected*, `rulesets` → 0). **(2) Clause (a) cannot be ruled as an API detail.** With one admin who pushes directly and **zero** pull requests ever, classic protection with `enforce_admins: false` and a ruleset with an admin bypass both constrain **nobody** — every admin push bypasses, so D164's docs-only-push case is not special — while a ruleset with no bypass rejects **every** direct push (a fresh commit has no checks) and forces a pull-request-first workflow. **What "required" binds is the maintainer's to rule first** (D168 §4): *nominal for the admin* → a ruleset with an explicit admin-role bypass, contexts pinned to the GitHub Actions app, created disabled, read back, then activated; *binding on everyone* → pull-request-first, taken as its own consent. The arming calls and read-backs are drafted in wave-35 planning lane C's report and are **not executed**.
- Notes: **[2026-09-13, wave 35 — the maintainer DEFERRED the ruling.]** Asked in direct reply to a named question — on a one-admin, zero-pull-request repository, should a required status check be nominal for the admin (a ruleset with an explicit admin bypass) or binding on everyone (pull-request-first)? — the maintainer answered **"Decide later"**. Nothing is armed; the payload stays regenerable at 20 contexts; the first hosted green of the 20th context (`reproducible-build`, run `34781450856`) now exists, so the row's only remaining precondition is the ruling itself.

### Q266 — `scripts/scrub-history.sh` exists and nothing runs it, so history coverage is current as of one commit and stale after the next
- Milestone: M4
- Size: S
- Deps: after **Q65** finding-2 (the pre-public scrub this makes repeatable) and the wave-31 delta scan that built the script; **before Q65's flip** — a DEADLINE, because the flip publishes history and a scan nobody re-runs is a scan whose answer expires. Sibling of `Q2` (`secret-guard`), which has the same defect for a different corpus.
- Spec: Risks — malicious verifier host (MVP-SPEC.md line 186); release (line 156)
- Discovered by: the wave-31 history-delta lane (2026-08-27), which built the script and then reported that it had nowhere to run. Id assigned centrally by the registrar.
- Problem: **the only history scan this project had was a dated document, and the replacement for it is a script no gate invokes.** `docs/reviews/pre-public-scrub-history.md` was measured on 2026-08-16 over 7 935 objects; by 2026-08-27 the store held **8 439** — **+504 objects, +299 blobs, +20 commits** that no scan had ever read — and nothing detected the drift, because nothing was looking. `scripts/scrub-history.sh` now closes the *measurement* gap (11 rules, 12 fixtures, 3 arms, `--self-test` green, both plants red by message) but not the *recurrence* gap: `grep -rn 'scrub-history' scripts/ .github/` finds it in no lane and no workflow. `secret-guard` is the precedent and carries the same hole — it is **not in `local-gate.sh`** either, and it excludes `.git` outright (`scripts/ci-lanes.sh:1406`), so no committed instrument reads history at all. Cost is not the obstacle: the delta run is **10.9 s** and a full-store run is under two minutes.
- Do: Wire the scan so it re-runs without anyone remembering. Decide the venue **on measured cost**, not by preference — a `ci-lanes.sh` lane, a `local-gate.sh` step, or both — and register the edge in `scripts/check-ci-paths.py` (`FOLLOW_EDGES` once it is invoked from a job, `UNREACHED_EDGES` until then, which is the classification a sibling lane had to add mid-gate one wave ago). Prefer `--since <last-scanned-rev>` for the routine arm and keep a full-store arm available, and record which rev the routine arm compares against so the answer is never silently narrower than it looks.
- Accept:
  - `scrub-history.sh` is invoked by a committed lane or gate step, and the invocation is registered in `check-ci-paths.py` under the correct edge class.
  - The gate step's failure is proven by a planted fault, red **by its own message**, not merely by exit status.
  - The routine arm names the revision it scans from, in its own output line, so a narrowed scan cannot look like a full one.
  - `secret-guard`'s exclusion of `.git` is stated in one place as deliberate-and-covered-by-this-lane, so the two instruments' domains are written down rather than inferred.
- Notes: **This row does not claim a secret was ever committed** — the wave-31 delta found none, and neither did the 2026-08-16 review.
