It claims that the *property* of having looked expires, and that a dated document cannot carry it. **The one finding the delta reversed belongs to `Q268`, not here.** Rule 8 was considered and refused: the subject is the pre-public scrub, a ship-path obligation of `Q65`, not the apparatus that checks the apparatus.

### Q267 — the `advisories` half of the `audit-deny` gate lane is RED on a live RUSTSEC advisory, and the minimal fix ripples into a target this host cannot test
- Milestone: M4
- Size: S
- Deps: after nothing; **before Q31 and Q34** — a DEADLINE, because a release workflow and a ship gate both run this lane, and a cryptographic verifier should not publish with its own advisory lane red. Sibling of `P13`/`Q10` (the advisory-triage path protocol rule 5 names).
- Spec: Supply chain and dependency policy (MVP-SPEC.md release, line 156); Risks (line 186)
- Discovered by: the wave-31 `Q241` lane (2026-08-27), while running `D158 §2 R4`'s step 3; confirmed independently by the orchestrator. Id assigned centrally by the registrar.
- Problem: **`./scripts/ci-lanes.sh audit-deny` exits 1 — `advisories FAILED, bans ok, licenses ok, sources ok`.** `RUSTSEC-2026-0258` (*h2 unbounded empty DATA frames*, low severity, patched in **0.4.16**) fires on `h2 0.4.15`, reached via `ant-core 0.5.0 → axum → hyper` and `reqwest → alloy-provider`. **It is not this wave's doing** — `git show HEAD:Cargo.lock` carries `h2 0.4.15`, and the lane was green at wave 30's close, so the advisory landed in the database between the two. **The obvious fix was attempted and REFUSED on measurement**: `cargo update -p h2 --precise 0.4.16` succeeds, but the resolution is **not minimal** — it also moves `windows-sys` **0.61.2 → 0.52.0/0.59.0/0.48.0** and `socket2` **0.6.5 → 0.5.10**. Those are Windows-target crates that do not compile on this host at all, and the 3-OS required suite that would catch a regression **cannot run**: every hosted job since 2026-08-16 is refused. Shipping an unverifiable lockfile change into the lane that gates a release is the trade this row refuses to make silently. The lockfile was restored **byte-identically** (`cmp` 0) and `touch`ed.
- Do: Clear the advisory without shipping an unmeasured ripple. In preference order: **(a)** find a resolution that moves `h2` alone — pin the transitive versions that drifted, or bump the parent that pulls the newer `windows-sys`, and show the diff is `h2`-only; **(b)** if no minimal resolution exists, take the full resolution **and get it witnessed on Windows and macOS** before it counts, which makes this row depend on CI being unblocked and should be said rather than assumed; **(c)** if neither, record a **priced exception** in `deny.toml` with an expiry date and the reasoning, on the `ACCEPTED_NON_COVERED` model — never an open-ended `ignore`.
- Accept:
  - `./scripts/ci-lanes.sh audit-deny` exits 0, **or** carries a dated, reasoned, expiring exception whose entry names this row.
  - If the lockfile moved, the diff is stated crate by crate, and any crate that this host cannot compile is named as unwitnessed rather than passed over.
  - `Cargo.lock`'s change (or its absence) is read back, and the wasm32 and `--locked` builds are re-run.
  - The advisory's applicability to *this* product is assessed in writing — antseal is an HTTP **client** here, and an unbounded-empty-DATA-frame denial of service is primarily a server-side exposure; that assessment is the difference between a real risk and a lane colour.
- Notes: **The exit code was not trusted on its own.** `cargo deny` exits 2 for a bans failure and 1 for an advisories failure, and the sub-check the `Q241` plant targeted (`bans`) was green throughout — which is why it was run isolated as the primary evidence. Protocol **rule 5** names an advisory on a pinned crypto crate as a decision trigger; `h2` is not a crypto crate, so this is filed as a row rather than a decision, and if the exception arm (c) is taken it becomes one.

### Q268 — the pre-public scrub reports published the maintainer's personal address into the history they were certifying clean
- Milestone: M4
- Size: S
- Deps: **before Q65's flip** — a DEADLINE and a maintainer decision, because the flip publishes history and there is no rollback after it. After the wave-31 delta scan that measured it.
- Spec: Risks — malicious verifier host (MVP-SPEC.md line 186); Positioning and disclosure (line 28)
- Discovered by: the wave-31 history-delta lane (2026-08-27), as finding **N-1** — the one measurement of the 2026-08-16 review that **reversed**. Id assigned centrally by the registrar.
- Problem: **the 2026-08-16 history review certified the maintainer's personal email address at zero occurrences, in bold. It is now in 15 blobs across 4 paths, all four live in `HEAD` — and every single occurrence is a scrub report quoting the address as its own search term.** The instrument wrote its subject into the corpus it was measuring. Paths and counts at `HEAD`: `docs/reviews/pre-public-scrub-github-side.md` ×3, `docs/reviews/pre-public-scrub-history.md` ×3, `docs/reviews/pre-public-scrub-worktree.md` ×3, `tasks/Q.md` ×1. **No instrument can see it**: `secret-guard` excludes `*.md`, and `machine-paths` scans neither `docs/reviews/` nor `tasks/`. Severity is low — a contact address, not a credential, and one already reachable through other public channels — but it is **the maintainer's to accept, not a lane's to decide**, and the asymmetry matters: redacting `HEAD` is four small edits, while the 15 historical blobs are removable only by a rewrite, and `format-v1-freeze` is a pushed tag that a rewrite would invalidate.
- Do: Put the choice in front of the maintainer with both costs attached, and execute whichever is chosen. **(a) Accept**: record the decision, and redact the literal from the four `HEAD` files anyway so the count stops growing — the search term can be named without being spelled. **(b) Rewrite**: price it honestly against the pushed `format-v1-freeze` tag and the 533 commits the remote already carries, and note that a rewrite re-opens every digest the freeze pins. Whichever is chosen, add a rule that a scrub report **names its search terms without reproducing them**, so the next review does not re-introduce what it is looking for.
- Accept:
  - The maintainer's choice is recorded with its date and its reason.
  - If (a): the four `HEAD` occurrences no longer contain the literal, and the reports still say what they searched for.
  - The no-reproduce rule for scrub reports is written where the next scrub author will read it.
  - `scripts/scrub-history.sh` is re-run and its finding count for this class is stated after the change, so the claim is measured rather than asserted.
- Notes: **This row deliberately does not reproduce the address**, and neither does the delta record that found it — the same discipline whose absence created the row. Both new wave-31 files were scanned through the real code path and return `findings=0 verdict=CLEAN`, so neither poisons future runs. **The commit identities are clean**: all 20 new commits and all 300 delta commit objects carry only GitHub noreply identities, so this is a documentation exposure and not an authorship one.
- Notes: **[EXECUTED 2026-08-27, wave 31 — the maintainer chose (a) ACCEPT-AND-REDACT, in session, and the `HEAD` half is done in the act that records it.]** Instruction, given in session: remove the personal email address **and every reference to the second GitHub account, including any prefix variant of its name**. **Scope widened by that instruction beyond this row's original subject**, which named only the address. **Redacted at `HEAD`, 16 occurrences across 6 files**: the address → `maintainer-email` (3 in `pre-public-scrub-github-side.md`, 3 in `-history.md`, 3 in `-worktree.md`, 1 in `tasks/Q.md`); the detector NAME (which embedded the mail provider) → `email-maintainer` (2 in `-worktree.md`), because the name leaked the provider even where the address did not; the username stem → `maintainer-handle` (2 in `D145-devnet-evidence-retention.md`), **found only on a second sweep** because the first searched for the full address and the stem is a different string; and the second GitHub account name (1 in `TODO.md`'s Maintainer-actions block, 1 in `docs/ci-verification.md`'s `gh auth status` transcript at step A8). **Residual sweep for the address, the mail provider, the username stem and the account name returns **0 files** — run **match-level**, not line-level, for a reason recorded at the ledger: an exclusion applied per LINE let one benign `substitutable` on a very long line suppress two real hits on that same line.** `check-traceability.py` 10/10, `check-copy-style.py` and `secret-guard` all `REAL_EXIT=0` after the edits; **no checker ever depended on these literals** (verified before editing — the only script hits were `substitutable` matching `tuta`). **The rule this row asked for is now in force at both redaction sites**, stated where the next reader will meet it: a scrub report **names its search terms without reproducing them**, and the `gh auth status` step records *"do not restore it"* with the reason — the check it prescribes is *"is the active account `aed900`?"*, which `Active account: true` answers alone, so the other name was never an input. **WHAT REMAINS, AND IT IS THE HALF THAT MATTERS: the remote already carries all of it.** The four commits that introduced the address (`1830618`, `496f1a6`, `4cae455`, `dee7b2a`, all 2026-08-17) are **ancestors of `origin/main` (`c2709ea`)** — measured by `git merge-base --is-ancestor`, YES on all four. The repository is **private**, so it is not publicly visible, but the redaction above is a **`HEAD`-only** change and does **not** remove the string from history, local or remote. **Consequence for the flip, stated plainly so nobody re-derives it**: making this repository public exposes the address in history **unless** the history is rewritten *and* force-pushed first — and a rewrite invalidates the pushed `format-v1-freeze` tag and every digest pinned to it. That is the decision this row still carries. `D144`'s ruling on `/home/deb` is the precedent and it cuts the same way: *"scrubbing HEAD moves the string from visible in `git grep` to visible in `git log -S`, one command later"*. **This row does NOT tick on the `HEAD` redaction alone.**
- Notes: **[HISTORY REWRITTEN 2026-08-27, wave 31 — the maintainer escalated from accept-and-redact to REMOVE COMPLETELY, and the recorded price of doing so was WRONG.]** Instruction: remove the address and every reference to the second account from anywhere, **before** going public. **The blocker this register had recorded for three waves does not exist.** `docs/reviews/pre-public-scrub-history.md`, `D144` and this row all priced a rewrite as invalidating the pushed `format-v1-freeze` tag. **Measured**: the tag is at `d3345e1` (**2026-07-28**), and the earliest commit carrying either identifier is `873a1cf` (**2026-08-08**) — `git merge-base --is-ancestor format-v1-freeze 873a1cf` returns **YES**, so the tag **predates the entire rewrite range** and its SHA, and every digest pinned to it, is untouched. **Verified after the fact: the tag's commit is byte-identical before and after.** The inherited price was reasoning about `/home/deb`, which *is* in the initial commit — and it was carried over to a different subject without re-measuring. **Scope, measured before acting**: exactly **7 paths** ever held any form, and exactly **one ref** — `refs/heads/main` — ever reached them; the other 35 branches, the leftover `refs/original/refs/heads/m2w4-kappa` and both tags are clean. So the rewrite ran over `873a1cf^..main`, **72 commits**, and touched nothing else. **Executed**: `git filter-branch` with an `--index-filter` over those 7 paths and a `--msg-filter`, both driven by one 8-rule substitution list ordered longest-first, so the full address is consumed before the bare stem. **Commit messages were in scope and had to be** — three carried the address. The substitution list was proved not to touch `substitutable`, which contains the mail provider's name as a substring and had already caused one false-clean sweep. **Then the objects were actually removed**, which is the step that is easy to skip: the `refs/original` backup ref was deleted, **the stale `refs/remotes/origin/main` (still pointing at the pre-rewrite `c2709ea`) was deleted** — it alone was keeping every old blob alive, and a first purge reported the identifiers still present because of it — reflogs expired, `gc --prune=now` and `prune --expire=now`. **Verification, exhaustive and match-level**: every one of **3 689 blobs** and every one of **713 commit messages** in the whole store scanned for all four forms → **0**. `git fsck` clean, `unreachable=0`, store 8 439 → **7 993** objects. `HEAD`'s tree is byte-identical to pre-rewrite (`ceded50f`), working tree clean, 36 branches intact. `check-traceability` 10/10, `check-copy-style`, `check-ci-paths`, `secret-guard` and the wave's own `scrub-history.sh` all confirm the identifiers absent. **WHAT REMAINS, AND IT IS THE ONLY THING**: `origin` still holds the **old** history. The remote is unchanged and this row does not tick until it is. Removing it there is a **force-push over `main`**, an external action requiring express in-the-moment consent naming action, destination and account — **no agent may take it**, and none has been given. Until then the identifiers remain on GitHub's servers in a **private** repository. **The repository must not be made public before that force-push lands.**
- Notes: **[FORCE-PUSH LANDED 2026-08-27T23:5x, under express in-session consent naming action, destination and account — AND IT IS NOT ENOUGH. This row still does not tick.]** `git push --force origin main`: `+ c2709ea...68d7a88 main -> main (forced update)`, `PUSH_REAL_EXIT=0`. Remote head now matches local; **a fresh `git clone --mirror` of the remote scans to 0 occurrences of all four forms.** Pre-push checks re-verified at the final commit: active account `aed900`, remote `private` with **0 forks**, **one** distinct author/committer identity across all commits, clean tree, `secret-guard` and `check-personal-data` both `REAL_EXIT=0`, and the full gate **36 PASS / 0 FAIL / 0 SKIP / 0 `::error::`**. **WHAT A FORCE-PUSH DOES NOT DO, measured rather than assumed.** GitHub does **not** delete the unreferenced objects. `gh api repos/aed900/antseal/commits/c2709ea…` still returns that commit, and `…/commits/496f1a6…` — one of the four that introduced the address — **still returns 8 occurrences of the identifier**, with the file at that ref returning **3**. The objects are unreachable from any ref and invisible to `clone`, but they are alive and fetchable **by SHA**. **AND THE NEW HISTORY PUBLISHES THE SHAs.** This is the part that would have escaped notice: `496f1a6` appears in **3 tracked files**, `4cae455` in 5, `1830618` in 6, `dee7b2a` in 4, `c2709ea` in 2 plus 2 commit messages — written by this very row's own records, which cite the old commits as evidence of when the disclosure entered. So a public reader would not need to guess a 40-hex SHA: **the repository would hand them the pointer, and the pointer still resolves.** The rewrite removed the identifiers from the branch and left a working index to them in plain sight. **Consequence, and it is the operative one: the repository MUST NOT be made public until the unreachable objects are gone.** Two acts, neither of which an agent can take: **(a)** ask GitHub Support to run garbage collection on `aed900/antseal` — this is the only act that actually deletes them, and it is the fix; **(b)** optionally strike the old SHAs from the tree, which removes the index but not the objects and is therefore **defence in depth, never a substitute for (a)**. Doing (b) alone would be the worst outcome available: it looks like closure and changes nothing about retrievability. **Re-measure after (a) with the exact command that proved the exposure** — `gh api repos/aed900/antseal/commits/496f1a6…` — and require a **404**. A clean `clone --mirror` is **not** the test; it was already clean the moment the push landed, which is precisely why it is the wrong predicate to close on.
- Notes: **[GC RAN, AND THE MEASUREMENT CLOSES THE ROW — 2026-09-02, wave 33. The old objects are gone by every route an attacker would use, and the closing predicate needed one correction on its way to being met.]** GitHub executed the maintainer’s 2026-08-28 Support request. **Controls before predicates**, because on a private repository an auth failure also reads as not-found: the live head `ae4e1673…` fetched clean in full and short form (`REAL_EXIT=0` both, re-read from files). Then the ruled command on the verbatim route: `gh api repos/aed900/antseal/commits/496f1a60773713033bd82b8849b664290ea40e01` → `REAL_EXIT=1`, *"No commit found for SHA: 496f1a60… (HTTP 422)"* — **not the ruled 404**, and **byte-identical in form to a never-existed control**: a syntactically valid 40-hex SHA that was never in any repository, fetched the same minute, answers the same 422 and the same sentence. The GC’d object is now indistinguishable from one that never existed — a stronger statement than the 404 the ruling asked for. The four sibling SHAs the records cite (`c2709ea`, `4cae455`, `1830618`, `dee7b2a`) answer identically. The two OTHER direct-SHA routes measured live on 2026-08-27 both now return the **literal 404 the ruling named**: the file-at-old-ref fetch (`contents/docs/reviews/pre-public-scrub-history.md?ref=496f1a60…` → *"No commit found for the ref … (HTTP 404)"*, where it had served the file with 3 occurrences) and the tarball route (`tarball/496f1a60…` → HTTP 404). **The predicate’s status-code error is recorded at the instrument ledger** — closure is taken on the message plus a live control, never the code alone. **GC touched only the unreachable**: `git ls-remote origin` shows `main` at `ae4e1673…` and `format-v1-freeze` intact (`5b72bb0` → `d3345e1`), so the pushed freeze and every digest pinned to it are unchanged, and the repository read back `private` with **0 forks** the same morning. **The tracked-file citations of the old SHAs deliberately stay**: they are dated evidence in removal records, and the pointer they hand a reader now dereferences to nothing — striking them was ruled defence in depth, never a substitute, and is moot with the objects gone. **What this closure does not cover, stated so nobody over-reads it**: GitHub’s internal storage beyond its serving surfaces (unqueryable from outside; the row’s threat was the public reader, and every serving route measured is dead); the browser UI route (unmeasured — the API is the recorded route and the recorded predicate); and any copy taken by an authenticated collaborator while private (0 forks throughout). Exposure between force-push and GC was NIL, as priced. **Consequence: the last pre-flip blocker is discharged — everything that now stands before B0 is the maintainer’s alone** (B0 consent, B1 flip, the billing act, the DNS TXT pin, P17’s funding), and the standing *the repository must not be made public yet* note dissolves with this row.

### Q269 — The personal-data guard: a checker that holds digests instead of identifiers, and a hook that refuses the commit before the disclosure is expensive
- Milestone: M4
- Size: S
- Deps: after **Q268** (the disclosure that motivated it, and the source of its deny table); **with Q2** (`secret-guard`, whose blind spots — `*.md` and `.git` — are half the reason this exists). **before Q65's flip** — a DEADLINE: the guard is worth most while the repository is still private and a mistake is still cheap to undo.
- Spec: Risks — malicious verifier host (MVP-SPEC.md line **186**); Positioning and disclosure (line 28)
- Discovered by: the maintainer, in session 2026-08-27, after `Q268`: *"ensure my personal email, unrelated github usernames or home directories or any other unnecessary personal information will not be shared again in the future."* Id assigned centrally by the registrar.
- Problem: **nothing in this repository could see personal data, and the one instrument that came closest wrote the disclosure itself.** `secret-guard` excludes `*.md` and `.git`; `machine-paths` polices absolute PATHS rather than identities, and its own header says in terms *"THIS IS NOT A PRIVACY CONTROL"*. So a personal email address sat in four tracked files for ten days, entered sixteen historical blobs and reached the remote, and every check stayed green. **The obvious fix is itself the defect**: a checker that greps for a forbidden address must contain that address, which makes the checker the next leak.
- Do: Build `scripts/check-personal-data.py` so that it **holds SHA-256 digests of the forbidden strings and never the strings**, extracts candidate tokens from the tracked set, hashes each and compares — so it can name the **class** and the **file:line** of a hit while being physically unable to print or leak the value it matched. Two arms, because a denylist only knows what it has been told: **ARM 1 DENY**, exact and hashed, zero false positives by construction; **ARM 2 CLASSIFY**, heuristic and allow-listed, so every email address must be a noreply, an RFC 2606 documentation domain, or registered **by digest** with a reason, and every `/home/<name>`, `/Users/<name>` and `C:\Users\<name>` must be a reserved placeholder or registered **per name with a count**. Wire it into `scripts/local-gate.sh` (self-test first, as every checker there) and add `.githooks/pre-commit`, armed by `git config core.hooksPath .githooks`.
- Accept:
  - The checker contains **no personal identifier of its own**, including in its self-test fixtures, and passes when run over itself.
  - Every arm is proven able to red by a **planted fault**, confirmed **by message**, and the DENY arm's output is proven **not to contain the literal it matched**.
  - Home-directory names are counted **per name** against registered ceilings, not as one total.
  - The gate lane and the hook run the **same** checker, and the reason the hook is not sufficient alone is written where a contributor reads it.
  - `CONTRIBUTING.md` states the rule — *name the class, never the literal* — and the one command that arms the hook.
- Notes: **Per-name counting is the load-bearing design choice, and it was not the first one tried.** A single total is an assertion that cannot fail in the way that matters: a new personal username can appear while an accepted one loses an occurrence, and the sum never moves. Per name, an **unregistered** name reds on its first occurrence regardless of anything else. **The maintainer's own home path is registered at its measured ceiling of 131** with D144's ruling attached and the maintainer's 2026-08-27 re-affirmation — accepted, but it cannot grow silently. **Two defects were found in the guard by testing it rather than reading it**, and both are recorded because they are the same defect the guard exists to prevent, committed by the guard: its self-test fixtures were literals, so it **flagged itself** the moment it was tracked (caught by running a real `git commit` through the hook, not by the self-test, which passed happily); and its own docstring named the accepted home path, adding **+1 to the very count it polices** and reddening it against its own ceiling. Every fixture is now assembled at runtime from fragments.


### Q270 — ci.yml's `test` job builds no verifier page, so `the_built_page...` (R96) fails on every clean hosted runner (ci red since ~2026-08-16)
- Milestone: M4
- Size: S
- Deps: **before Q65's flip** — a DEADLINE and a maintainer concern: a public repo must not show a red `ci`.
- Spec: Verification / CI (MVP-SPEC.md M0 — "wasm32 build in CI"); Positioning (a broken-looking public repo)
- Discovered by: the wave-33 post-push hosted verification (2026-09-03) — the first real `ci` execution after the billing block cleared. Run `33756110493`, `test` job `steps=8` FAIL. Recorded at `docs/instrument-ledger.md` entry 256. Id assigned centrally by the registrar.
- Problem: `.github/workflows/ci.yml`'s `test` job runs `cargo test --workspace --locked` with **no verifier-page build step**, so on a clean hosted runner `target/verifier-web/index.html` does not exist and `the_built_page_carries_the_footers_signing_key_element_byte_for_byte` (`crates/antseal-wasm/tests/page_template.rs`, R96 Accept row 2) **panics** — it reads the BUILT artifact and, by design, **refuses to skip** (a skip would be "R96's own defect reproduced"). `ci` was green through 2026-08-15 and red every run since 2026-08-16 (18 successes, all pre-Aug-16). The register had attributed all post-Aug-15 `ci` reds to the CI **billing** block, but the step count separates them: `steps:0` is billing, `steps>0` is a real failure — and this one is `steps>0`. **Pre-existing**: `ci.yml` and the test are untouched by the session's commits. The **local gate masks it** — `local-gate.sh` runs `cargo test` (line ~283) before `verifier-page-build.sh` (line ~730) and lives off a leftover `target/verifier-web/` from a prior run; `cargo clean && local-gate.sh` would red locally too (the test file's own header documents this cross-lane bootstrap).
- Do: Build the verifier page in `ci.yml`'s `test` job **before** `cargo test --workspace`, adding the wasm toolchain to that job, mirroring `.github/workflows/verifier-page.yml`'s `browser` job — **not** softening or moving the R96 assertion. Classify the new `ci.yml`→script edges in `check-ci-paths.py` in the same commit.
- Accept:
  - A hosted `ci` run shows the `test` job **GREEN**, with the page-build steps executing before `cargo test --workspace` — and the run id is recorded here.
  - `check-ci-paths.py` classifies every new edge (green flagless run); `check-action-pins.py` unaffected (no new `uses:`).
  - `crates/antseal-wasm/tests/page_template.rs` is unchanged — the built-artifact assertion still refuses to skip (R96).
- Notes: **[FIX LANDED 2026-09-03, wave 33 post-push — implemented by a seal lane, verified by the orchestrator; does NOT tick until the hosted witness is green.]** The `test` job gained two `run:` steps after `Swatinem/rust-cache` and before `cargo test --workspace --locked`: `./scripts/wasm-tools-provision.sh` (installs the pinned `wasm-pack` + `wasm-bindgen-cli`; the image ships neither) and `./scripts/verifier-page-build.sh --build-only` (its `stale_guard` rebuilds the module via `wasm-pack-build.sh --build-only`, then `verifier-page-pack.mjs` writes the single `target/verifier-web/index.html` — so one command subsumes the module build). `rust-toolchain.toml` already declares `targets = ["wasm32-unknown-unknown"]`, so the existing `rustup show` provides the wasm target. **9 new edges classified in `check-ci-paths.py`**: 3 FOLLOW (`verifier-page-build.sh`→`wasm-pack-build.sh`, →`verifier-page-pack.mjs`; `wasm-pack-build.sh`→`wasm-imports.mjs`) and 6 UNREACHED (prose/comment mentions and code past the `--build-only` early return). **0 new `uses:` actions** — both steps call committed scripts, so `action-pins.tsv` is untouched. Static checks green: `check-ci-paths`, `check-ci-shell`, `check-action-pins` all `REAL_EXIT=0`, YAML parses. **Added CI cost**: the `test` job now provisions the wasm tools and builds the page — roughly +3–8 weighted min cold (the `cargo install` of the tools), ~+1–2 min warm once `rust-cache` is populated; the workflow's `ANTSEAL_NO_REAL_ANCHOR_NETWORK=1` is inherited so the build stays offline. **What holds the tick: the hosted witness.** Per D166's discipline, an unwitnessed CI change is not a landed one — this row ticks only when a hosted `ci` run shows the `test` job green.
- Notes: **[TICKED 2026-09-03 — WITNESSED. The fix is proven on the hosted runner.]** `ci` run **`33808001048`** on `faf270a`: **overall `success`, all 15 jobs `success`.** The `test` job grew from `steps=8` (the failing run `33756110493`) to `steps=10`, and its step list confirms the order: `5. install the pinned wasm tools this image does not ship` → `6. build the verifier page the workspace suite asserts against (R96, ledger 256)` → `7. Run cargo test --workspace --locked`, every one `success`. So `the_built_page_carries_the_footers_signing_key_element_byte_for_byte` now reads a **built** `target/verifier-web/index.html` instead of panicking on a cold runner. **`ci` is GREEN on `main` for the first time since 2026-08-15** — which is also the closing predicate several M4 hosted-witness rows named (assess per-row in the go-public-prep wave; a green `ci` is a witness, not an automatic tick). Row CLOSED.
### Q271 — CodeQL default setup, switched on by the flip, reports 66 alerts (44 critical) against a cryptographic library and nobody has triaged one of them
- Milestone: M4
- Size: M
- Deps: after Q65 — the flip is what provisioned the scanner; the row could not exist before it.
- Spec: Verification / CI (MVP-SPEC.md M4 — hardening); Threat model (a hardcoded value that is actually a key is the worst defect this repository could carry)
- Discovered by: the wave-34 flip act itself (2026-09-12). `B8` enabled code scanning; CodeQL default setup provisioned and its first scan settled at **66 alerts — 44 `critical`, 21 `high`, 1 `medium`** across 6 rules. Not predicted by any row: the register had costed `B8` as four switches to set, not as a scanner that would produce findings.
- Problem: A count is not a triage, and these alerts are **neither self-evidently noise nor self-evidently real**. `rust/hard-coded-cryptographic-value` **44**, `rust/cleartext-logging` **13**, `py/clear-text-logging-sensitive-data` **5**, `rust/weak-sensitive-data-hashing` **2**, `js/unvalidated-dynamic-method-call` **1**, `js/identity-replacement` **1**. Three measurements were taken at mint so the next lane starts from evidence: **(1)** `weak-sensitive-data-hashing` is a **justified false positive** — both hits are ESSCertID v1 cert hashing (`crates/antseal-core/src/anchor/ess.rs:281` plus a test) and the code already answers it in a comment, *"v1 has no algorithm field: RFC 2634 §5.4 fixes SHA-1"*; there is no algorithm to choose. **(2)** `cleartext-logging` is **sink-side and unresolved** — the flagged lines are the shared `Ui::line` helper (`crates/antseal-cli/src/commands.rs:112`/`:114`, `eprintln!`/`println!`), so one sink is reported once per tainted source; one named source, `passphrase_source`, is a **closure** (`impl FnOnce() -> Result<SecretBuf, CliError>`, `crates/antseal-cli/src/vault/export.rs:1018`) that *produces* a secret rather than logging one, which is a name-driven false-positive shape — **but that is 1 of 13 paths and the other 12 are unfollowed. A crypto tool that logs a passphrase is a real defect and the repository is now public, so this must be settled path by path, never by analogy from the one path that was checked.** **(3)** `hard-coded-cryptographic-value` **splits 20 test / 24 non-test**; the 20 are golden vectors this project ships deliberately, and the 24 concentrate in `crates/antseal-core/src/crypto` (10), `src/anchor` (7), `antseal-anchor/src/submit` (7), `src/tsa` (6) — most likely domain-separation strings, HKDF `info` constants, protocol magic and pinned TSA roots, some **frozen by `format-v1`**, but *most likely* is not a finding.
- Do: Triage all 66 by rule. Dismiss **per class with a recorded reason**, never per alert and never by disabling a rule to reduce a count. Follow each `cleartext-logging` path to a source and say for each whether a secret can reach the sink. Split the hardcoded-value set into frozen-constant / test-vector / real-key classes and fix anything in the third. Then decide whether CodeQL **default setup** stays on or is replaced by a pinned workflow, and write the answer down either way — noting that `D141`/`Q244` require every `uses:` pinned to a 40-hex commit while **default setup is a GitHub-managed workflow that no `action-pins.tsv` row governs**, a new exposure of the class `Q255` already tracks.
- Accept:
  - Every one of the 66 alerts reaches a state with a reason recorded against its **class**; the count is not reduced by disabling a rule.
  - The 13 `cleartext-logging` paths are each resolved to a source, with a stated verdict on whether a secret can reach the sink; the `passphrase_source` closure finding is cited as evidence for the paths it actually covers and no others.
  - Any real defect has a test that **fails before the fix and passes after**, per the standing plant-a-fault rule.
  - The `uses:`-pinning question for GitHub-managed default setup is answered in writing, and if default setup stays, the exemption is recorded where `Q244`'s reader will meet it.
- Notes: **Not world-visible, measured** — anonymous `GET https://api.github.com/repos/aed900/antseal/code-scanning/alerts` returns **401**, so only write access sees these and there is **no public-exposure clock**; the urgency is correctness, not embarrassment. Do not let the 44 `critical` labels set the priority: CodeQL's severity is a property of the rule, not of this codebase, and a cryptographic library is the single most false-positive-prone subject for `hard-coded-cryptographic-value`. The dependency graph also parsed root `requirements-crosscheck.txt` as a **pip manifest** (run *"Graph Update: pip in /."*), which is not what that file is — currently 0 Dependabot alerts, so it costs nothing today, but a spurious pip advisory would arrive with no Python in the project.
- Notes: **[2026-09-13, wave 35 — D169 RULED; the local half LANDED (lane W3), re-verified from source with no planning-lane verdict overturned; the row does NOT tick.]** **Census** (a fresh read at 2026-09-13T01:41:16Z matched the planning capture exactly): 66 = 64 open + 2 fixed, 0 dismissed. By class — H-OUTBUF 4 (false positive) · H-FIXTURE 27 (25 used in tests + #24/#48 already fixed) · H-NONCE 13 (used in tests) · L-PROD 3 (false positive) · L-TEST 10 (used in tests) · P-NAME 5 (false positive) · W-PROTOCOL 2 (#21 false positive, #22 used in tests) · J-DRIVER 1 (false positive) · J-TRUE-NOOP 1 (true, fixed in code) — **14 false positive + 49 used in tests + 2 fixed + 1 true = 66.** **`Accept` row 2 (the cleartext paths) — MET**: 29 (alert, source) pairs, not 13 paths; #19's sink `Outcome::raw(` has one call site, in `verify`, which opens no vault; #18/#20 print `ImportSummary` (a path, a count, two booleans); every secret type prints redacted; the `passphrase_source` finding is cited only for the `vault/export.rs` flows it covers. **`Accept` row 3 — no security defect**; #6's no-op removed from `scripts/verifier-page-pack.mjs` and the refusal loop it sat in proven for the first time: two self-test arms planted in the template (`'unsafe-inline'` and `'unsafe-eval'`), each red by its own message, an emptied refusal list turning the self-test red (*the assertions stayed GREEN … they are not checking anything*, exit 1), a new guard reporting an arm whose plant changes nothing, and the real build still byte-identical. **D169 §2 R3 LANDED**: `crates/antseal-wasm/tests/page_template.rs` enforces D129 §7 (*all bundle-derived text reaches the DOM by `textContent`*) by refusing `innerHTML`, `outerHTML`, `insertAdjacentHTML` and `document.write` across the whole template, excusing only a pinned sentence per match; four plants red by message (the brief's `el.innerHTML = x`, a sink dropped from the list, a per-line exemption, `//` treated as a comment inside a string); 11 passed, clippy clean. **Blind spot, stated at the test**: computed names, aliases, parsers outside the list, and the wasm-bindgen glue. **`Accept` row 4 — MET** by D169 §2 R4. **Record**: `docs/reviews/codeql-triage-2026-09-13.md` (per-class and per-alert tables, the 40/4 correction, the nondeterminism re-measured). **REMAINS — the state half of `Accept` row 1: 63 consented dismissals** (not 64 — #6 is fixed in code, #24/#48 already fixed), each citing the review record, the three L-PROD comments stating the source counts they cover (3, 11, 3). **Answered, no row minted**: `metric_vault_fp`'s salt reader accepts an all-zero salt, but D73 §4 R7 governs how the salt is generated and kept secret, not its value, and nothing has been published with it — its natural home is the salt-generation step, not a new row.

The ~2 000 lines of new Rust landed this wave added no alert. **Watch**: CodeQL is nondeterministic on identical code (D169 §1.6), so an alert may reappear; it is re-dismissed by its class rule and never read as a regression or a fix.

### Q272 — The threat model has no storage-network adversary, although an eclipse or a single hostile peer can take a payment without storing, or make a stored work look absent
- Milestone: M4
- Size: S
- Deps: after S39 and S40 (the behaviour it describes), Q21 (the threat model); before Q34
- Spec: Risks (MVP-SPEC.md line 186); threat model (`docs/threat-model.md`)
- Discovered by: wave-35 planning lane G, 2026-09-13. Id assigned centrally.
- Problem: offline verification never trusts Autonomi and is unaffected, but three properties are: **under an eclipse** the attacker is the whole close group, prices within the total shown at consent, takes the payment and can accept a PUT without replicating it, undetectably from the eclipsed vantage (a false *already stored* is caught at finalize, because the attacker cannot serve bytes it never received); **under withholding**, restore and the live check get a false absence — and at ant-core 0.5.0 no eclipse is needed, since one hostile close-group peer returning bad bytes can abort a sweep; and **privacy**: §2.3's bound (*it is the address, not the work*) assumes independent close groups, which an eclipse breaks. Upstream's client routing is permissive against Sybils, and upstream's peer cache would make an eclipse persist across runs and leak into another product's directory. No section says any of this, and the enumeration check requires only that ten section names are present.
- Do: **rule the placement first** — decision owed **(D-e)**, planned 2026-09-13: recommended a new §2.11 with a correction note on §2.3 — then write it: rank peer sources by provenance, cover withholding (including the one-peer abort), payment misdirection and non-replication, state that antseal adopts no upstream peer cache, and extend the section-enumeration check.
- Accept:
  - The section exists with each named property stated in user-facing terms and the §2.3 correction dated.
  - The enumeration check reds on the new section's deletion, planted and watched by message.
